# Changelog Archive — 2026 H2 Part 1 (2026-07-01 through 2026-09-16)

Entries from [`docs/CHANGELOG.md`](../CHANGELOG.md) dated 2026-07-01 through 2026-09-16 (129
entries) moved out here verbatim on 2026-09-30 to keep the live changelog under its size budget
(see `docs/CONVENTIONS.md` "Doc size budgets" for the reasoning). Order and content are unchanged
from the live file: reverse chronological (newest first within this range, 2026-09-16 down to
2026-07-01), each entry exactly as originally written. For entries from 2026-09-19 onward, see
`docs/CHANGELOG.md`. For entries from 2026-01-01 through 2026-06-30 (the H1 half), see
[`docs/changelog/2026-H1.md`](2026-H1.md).

---

## 2026-09-16 — Doc archival restructure: CLAUDE.md / CHANGELOG.md / SESSION_STATE.md split for context budget

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- Mechanical, verbatim archival split of the three docs loaded into every session and every
  background pipeline agent's context. Nothing was rewritten or summarized away — every moved
  word survives verbatim in its new home, with a pointer left in the file it moved out of, per
  this project's own "record, don't rewrite history" discipline.
  - **`CLAUDE.md`** (150,894 → 57,674 bytes): the `## Tech Stack (Locked In So Far)` section's 16
    dated "direction" blockquotes (everything after the tech-stack table), the
    `## Immediate Next Actions for Claude Code` preamble paragraph, item 1's build-by-build
    progress narrative, and items 2/3 (both fully resolved, no forward-looking content left) all
    moved verbatim to the new `docs/DIRECTION_LOG.md`. Each is replaced in `CLAUDE.md` with a
    short pointer — a one-line date+subject list for the 16 blockquotes, an inline pointer
    sentence for the other three. `## Current State` and the remainder of `## Immediate Next
    Actions` (items 4–6, and the "still open"/guidance tails of items 1 and the preamble) are left
    as-is, judged current-state description and active guidance rather than closed historical
    record — trimming them risked losing meaning for a size saving that wasn't needed once the
    Tech Stack cut alone brought the file under budget.
  - **`docs/CHANGELOG.md`** (1,073,889 → 734,963 bytes, before this entry): every entry dated
    2026-01-01 through 2026-06-30 (102 of 230 entries — in practice 2026-05-21, the project's
    first entry, through 2026-06-30) moved verbatim, in the same order, to the new
    `docs/changelog/2026-H1.md`; a pointer line added near the top of the live file. Entries from
    2026-07-01 onward (128 entries, now 129 with this one) stay.
  - **`docs/SESSION_STATE.md`** (194,520 → 32,264 bytes): kept the header/parallel-thread preamble
    plus the two newest dated sections (both 2026-09-16: "Console S7 and decision D5" and
    "Console S5.1.d–e and the draft"); the other 18 dated sections moved verbatim, newest first,
    to the new `docs/handoffs/2026-09.md`, with a one-line-per-section pointer list left behind.
  - Added `scripts/doc-budget.py` (stdlib-only, no dependencies) — checks the byte size of the
    three files above against a budget (60 KB / 60 KB / 800 KB) and exits non-zero if any is
    over — and a new `docs/CONVENTIONS.md` §4 "Doc size budgets" recording those numbers and why
    `docs/CHANGELOG.md`'s is well above the ~250 KB a first pass might suggest (recent entries are
    individually large enough that the kept 2026-07-01-onward content alone is ~718 KB).
  - Drive-by fix: `docs/CONVENTIONS.md` §3's footnote calling `SESSION_STATE.md` "gitignored" was
    stale — it has been committed since the 2026-08-04 "Solo mode retired" CHANGELOG entry.
  - Corrected a live counter drift `doc-stats.py --check` surfaced mid-pass and unrelated to the
    restructure itself: a sibling agent's concurrent work in this same session added tests to the
    collected suite (3131 → 3145); `CLAUDE.md`'s three "pytest tests" claims were updated to match.

**Why:**
- These three files are loaded into the context of every background agent and every session
  turn. Measured before this change: `CLAUDE.md` 145 KB (~36k tokens) × 44 agent runs in one
  session. Shrinking them is a permanent, per-run saving, and the pattern (verbatim extraction +
  a dated archive + a pointer, proven lossless by diffing the set of non-empty lines removed from
  the source against the set added to the archive) is one this project can reapply the next time
  any of the three grows past budget again.

**Affected docs:**
- `CLAUDE.md` — `## Tech Stack (Locked In So Far)` trimmed to the table + a "Direction log"
  pointer subsection; `## Immediate Next Actions for Claude Code`'s preamble + item 1 + items 2/3
  trimmed to pointers.
- `docs/CHANGELOG.md` — pointer line added near the top; 2026-01-01..2026-06-30 entries removed.
- `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` — trimmed to header + two newest sections + an "Archived sections"
  pointer list.
- `docs/CONVENTIONS.md` — new §4 "Doc size budgets"; stale `SESSION_STATE.md` "gitignored"
  reference corrected.
- New: `docs/DIRECTION_LOG.md`, `docs/changelog/2026-H1.md`, `docs/handoffs/2026-09.md`,
  `scripts/doc-budget.py`.

**Verification:** every moved block's non-empty lines were diffed as a *set* against its archive
file's non-empty lines (zero found in neither, across all three splits); the four sub-line/
whole-paragraph surgical edits in `CLAUDE.md` (which a naive whole-line diff can't verify, since
the "before" line and "after" line differ by construction) were each checked directly — the moved
substring present verbatim in the archive, the kept substring present verbatim in `CLAUDE.md`;
`python3 scripts/doc-stats.py --check` is clean; `python3 scripts/doc-budget.py` exits 0 (all
three files under budget); the pre-existing `tests/test_pipeline_scripts.py` suite (14 tests)
passes unchanged.

## 2026-09-16 — Console S7 BUILT and D5 EXECUTED: the plugin frames the Console instead of re-implementing it, and its Imprints screen is deleted (iteration 10 of ten — the work order has no open slice left)

**Authority:** **implementation (Claude Code)** for the build and for the seven calls below, each open
to veto. **D5 is the user's ruling** (2026-09-11, `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §8.1): retire the
plugin's Track C Imprints screen at S7, keep "Deploy as twin" as a slim plugin action. The run's order
and the commit policy are the user's (2026-09-11, 2026-09-15). **With this slice
`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` has no open slice left — S1–S7 and S5.1 a–e are all built**; what
remains of the ten-iteration run is iteration 11's follow-ups (GAP-087..GAP-090) and the finale.

**Version-locked: nothing moved.** No `IR_SCHEMA`/`PLANNER`/`OP_CATALOG`/`VALIDATOR`/`ADAPTER`/
`IMAGE_MANIFEST` change, no new op type, no catalog rule, no schema bump, **no API change**. Op types
**40**, catalog rules **114**, pytest **3119 passed / 12 skipped, 3131 collected** — unchanged: the only
Python edit is three DTO **comments** recording that the fields they describe no longer have a UI
consumer. Nearly all of the slice is in `o2/grafana/plugins/axellio-axrange-control-panel/`, a separate
npm package.

**What changed:**

- **D5 executed — the Imprints screen is deleted.** `ImprintsScreen.tsx` (646 lines) and
  `ImprintTopologyPanel.tsx` (183) are gone, with the `initialView` panel option and its in-panel
  toggle, and with the imprint reads and types that existed only for them. **Verified by sweep:** no
  reference survives anywhere under `o2/grafana/`, provisioning and dashboards included. It is deleted
  because of what it rendered: a tolerant API's placeholder plus qualifier, shown as a confident `0`
  (`docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` **GAP-083**, now Resolved).
- **The embed: a panel that frames the Console at its own origin.** New
  `src/components/console/{ConsoleEmbed.tsx, consoleUrl.ts}` and two panel options —
  `consoleBaseUrl` (required) and `consoleImprintId` (optional deep link). It is an **iframe at the
  Console's origin, not the SPA mounted inside the plugin**, and the component says why: the SPA calls
  **same-origin `/api`**, Grafana's origin implements no CORS, so a shared React root would put every
  read on an origin the API refuses. `consoleUrl.ts` is pure and strict — it refuses a relative base, a
  non-http(s) scheme, a query or fragment, and embedded credentials, each with the reason — and it
  **does not restate the SPA's URL grammar**, which has exactly one home
  (`o2/console/src/model/zoom.ts`): the plugin builds `<base>` and `<base>/imprints/<id>`, and anything
  deeper is reached by navigating inside the frame.
- **"Deploy as twin" survives as a slim action** (`src/components/deploy/DeployTwinAction.tsx`): pick an
  imprint → **preview** through the open, pure `POST /api/imprints/{id}/plan` (validate + whole-DAG
  build, no `state.db` write, no cluster call) → an **in-panel confirmation** restating the imprint, the
  op count and the target's **`api_url`** → and only then `POST …/deploy`. It renders **no belief-store
  measurement in any state** — picked, previewed or deployed — because GAP-083's defect must not survive
  the screen it lived on.
- **One 401 message** (`src/io/errors.ts`), now used by every write surface **and** by the four read
  surfaces that had their own copies. It names the datasource, the `Authorization: Bearer` header and
  `AXRANGE_API_TOKEN`, because a 401 through the datasource proxy has exactly one cause here: reads are
  open, writes are token-gated.
- **The `(unset)` target placeholder is no longer selectable** (`src/io/targets.ts`).
  `GET /api/targets` publishes `node: "(unset)"` when the adapter config declares an API URL but no
  default node; both pickers now render that option **disabled, with its reason**, instead of letting a
  placeholder string be sent as `{"target_node": "(unset)"}` on a real deploy.

**The calls (implementation (Claude Code), each open to veto):**

1. **An iframe, not a shared React root** — forced by CORS, not chosen. §3 S7 offered both shapes; only
   one works, and the reason is recorded in the component rather than left for the next reader to
   rediscover.
2. **`initialView` is deleted with no replacement.** A dashboard can no longer *open* on the Console and
   needs one click. Re-adding a pin under a new name would be re-adding what D5 deleted.
3. **Console and Deploy are two views**, so the frame gets the whole panel rather than sharing it.
4. **An in-panel confirmation, not `window.confirm`** — a browser dialog can be suppressed and says
   nothing about the destination.
5. **A preview is a required gate:** the deploy affordance does not exist until a plan for *that*
   imprint and *that* target does.
6. **A preview that lands after the picker moved is discarded and announced**, rather than locking the
   pickers during a plan that takes seconds and cannot be cancelled.
7. **The 401 helper was extended to read surfaces too** — same gate, same cause, same fix, one wording.

**The defect trail — three findings, and what each would have cost:**

- **The stale preview, on the one screen whose job is to state the blast radius.** A plan resolving
  after a selection change overwrote the shown plan, so the confirmation could state one imprint's op
  count while deploying another — **measured at 695 ops from the wrong cast**, and in the sharper case
  naming a cluster the plan was never built against, immediately before real VMs are created. Fixed
  with a **live-pair gate on the side-effecting tail** (LES-274's shape), keyed on the *state* rather
  than on a picker's `onChange`, because the target can also arrive as a prop.
- **The framing note named the wrong knob.** Grafana's `[security] allow_embedding` governs Grafana
  **being embedded**, never Grafana **embedding another origin** — so an operator would have flipped it,
  seen no change, and wrongly ruled the cause out. The note now names, in likelihood order: the
  **browser's** own reachability of the Console origin (this frame is the one call in the plugin the
  *browser* makes; everything else goes server-side through the datasource proxy), **mixed content**
  (an https Grafana framing an http Console — the shape of the example URL), Grafana's
  `content_security_policy` `frame-src`, and last the Console origin's own `X-Frame-Options` /
  `frame-ancestors`, marked **inert** because `axrange serve` sends neither.
- **A placeholder string in an identifier field** was selectable and would have been sent as deploy
  **placement data** — the mirror image of GAP-083 and worse, because it is not merely displayed
  (**LES-342**).

**Gates (no live-verify — nothing here deploys or touches a cluster; what only a live Grafana can
confirm is listed in §21.5 and in `docs/SESSION_STATE.md`):** plugin `npm run build` exit 0; plugin
`npm run test:ci` **68 passed / 5 suites**; plugin `npm run typecheck` **339 → 244** errors — all of the
pre-existing TS2686 baseline (LES-042), the drop being **exactly the two deleted files**, **no surviving
file's per-file count moved**, and **none of the 244 in a file this slice created or touched**. No
cross-damage: Console typecheck clean, Console Vitest **1018 passed / 8 skipped**, `pytest -q` **3119
passed / 12 skipped**. **42 of 42 plugin cases show a visible `1 passed` when run alone** — which is how
LES-339 was found.

**Honest limits recorded** (`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §21.5): **none of the embed is testable
here** — that the frame renders rather than being refused, that the SPA inside it reaches its
same-origin `/api` from Grafana's page context (third-party storage partitioning can break the
pending-edit draft in a cross-site frame **even when the frame renders**), that the new options appear
and persist in a saved dashboard and that dashboards saved with the removed `initialView` still load,
the datasource-proxy path end to end (including a genuinely missing bearer header, whose 401 wording was
only checked against a synthetic error), a real deploy through the action, and plugin signing/loading —
all of it needs a rebuilt, redeployed plugin against a live Grafana. Two new gaps came out of the
slice: **GAP-097** (`schema_version` is rendered unchecked by both the plugin and the SPA, against
design note 15 §9 seam 1) and **GAP-098** (the previewed plan is not pinned at deploy — the server
re-plans, and agreement rests on identical inputs plus a deterministic planner).

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` — status header (**S7 BUILT; no slice open**); §3 S7 marked BUILT;
  §20.6's "S7 is the only open slice" corrected; new **§21 "S7 built as, 2026-09-16"**.
- `docs/design/15-console-canvas.md` — §9 (the embed as built: an iframe at the Console's origin, and
  why; the plugin is no longer "a read-only viewer until it embeds this SPA"), status and §13.
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — **GAP-083 ✅ Resolved (built)**, with the stronger closure recorded; new
  **GAP-097** and **GAP-098**.
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — **LES-339..LES-342** new.
- `CLAUDE.md` — the Console blockquote.
- `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` — iteration 10 of ten, with a section on what only a live Grafana can confirm.
- The plugin's own `README.md` and `CHANGELOG.md` (0.3.7) were written by the build.

**Affected code:**
- `o2/grafana/plugins/axellio-axrange-control-panel/src/components/console/{ConsoleEmbed,consoleUrl}.*`,
  `src/components/deploy/DeployTwinAction.*`, `src/io/{errors,targets}.ts` (**new**);
  `src/components/{AxrangeControlPanel,ControlToolbar,NodeInspector}.tsx`,
  `src/components/twin/TwinTopologyScreen.tsx`, `src/io/api.ts`, `src/module.ts`, `src/plugin.json`,
  `src/types.ts`; **deleted** `src/components/imprints/{ImprintsScreen,ImprintTopologyPanel}.tsx`.
- `axrange/api/dtos.py` — three comments only (the retired screen was the reason three fields are
  non-null; the rule stands, the consumer is gone).

## 2026-09-16 — Console S5.1.d–e BUILT: pending work is visible, counted by gesture, undoable whole — and it survives the browser (GAP-094 closed). **S5.1 is complete, a–e** (iteration 9 of ten)

**Authority:** **implementation (Claude Code)** for the build and for the six calls below, each open to
veto. **The slice's scope is the user's** (`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §3 S5.1, the post-build
amendment of 2026-09-11), and so is the run's order (the ten-iteration Console run, whose **iteration 9
is S5.1.d–e**) and the commit policy (unattended local commits for iterations 7–11, 2026-09-15).
**With this slice the S5.1 amendment is complete — a, b, c, d and e — and `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md`
has exactly one open slice left: S7, the Grafana embed.**

**Version-locked: nothing moved.** Frontend-only — everything is under `o2/console/`, **no Python was
touched**. No `IR_SCHEMA`/`PLANNER`/`OP_CATALOG`/`VALIDATOR`/`ADAPTER`/`IMAGE_MANIFEST` change, no new
op type, no catalog rule, no `scenario_schema_version`, state-store or `PLANE_EPOCH` bump, **and no new
record kind**. Op types **40**, catalog rules **114**, pytest **3119 passed / 12 skipped, 3131
collected** — all unchanged.

**What changed:**

- **Pending work is visible on the map (S5.1.d).** A **placed** clone keeps drawing hatched at its pin;
  an **unplaced** added segment — the site `+`'s, or a generated one — is **appended to its own site
  column** through the same slot rule a clone's proposal uses, and, when the column has no room, parked
  in a lane **clear of every box the plane draws**. At **level 1** a pending segment draws as a hatched
  spine with its hosts as **hatched chips**, in a band reading `added here · N · pending (browser
  only)` — and a host added to an *observed* segment is a hatched chip beside that segment's observed
  bands. The four level-0 goldens stay **byte-identical**: an added tile takes no grid slot, displaces
  no drawn tile and changes no budget.
- **The change list counts gestures (S5.1.d).** `src/edit/gestures.ts` (new, pure) groups entries by
  their `GestureTag` into one row in §3's own shape — `clone of 10.80.20.0/24 → 10.80.31.0/24 · 1
  segment · 17 hosts · 2 conduits · pending` — expanding to its records, which stay individually
  removable. **A solitary record is still one plain row** (most gestures are one record, and ceremony
  would make the list harder to read), and **a group's state is the weakest of its records'**, so a
  clone half-re-staged never reads "saved". **Whole-gesture undo** removes exactly that batch, **drops
  the pin** of a segment it takes out of play, **re-saves once** when saved records were involved, asks
  first when the undo would strand a record outside the gesture, and leaves the document
  **byte-identical to before the gesture**.
- **S5.1.e.** *Add host here* on a level-1 spine prefills the segment and **the next free address** —
  following it as records are staged and removed, and never overwriting what the operator typed.
- **The draft closes GAP-094** (`src/edit/draft.ts`, new). Pending records are persisted per imprint in
  `localStorage` and survive a **reload**, an **imprint switch**, the **breadcrumb**, the **outline's
  imprint list** and the **browser's Back**; they are discarded on save. **Restoring is reconciliation,
  never a replay:** a record the file now holds **identically** is dropped as redundant, a **different**
  record under the same key says exactly that, the restore runs **the same cross-record refusals the
  staging paths run** (a draft is the only way a record reaches the store without passing a gesture),
  and a shape check refuses what this app could not have produced — deliberately **looser** than the
  server, which stays the authority (LES-271). A **world-dependent** refusal *keeps* the draft so the
  operator can resolve it and get their copy back; a **permanent** one drops it. The **discard**
  control is offered whenever a notice is shown, including when nothing could be restored. A blocked or
  full store is **said once**, names what is at risk, and never takes the app down — and the write
  token is still never persisted, proven by a test that greps every storage write in the source.
- **The recompile disclosure.** The restore waits for the **cast** to resolve or fail, reports "the
  bundle has been recompiled since this draft was taken" when the build moved, and **says the check
  could not be made** when the cast read failed — LES-273's third outcome, rather than silence that
  reads as "unchanged". The draft keeps **its own** `code_fingerprint` until a **save** supersedes it.
- **The guards.** One guard on **`go`** — the single function every in-app navigation passes through —
  firing only when a navigation *leaves* the imprint; **Escape** keeps its own refusal with no
  one-click exit (a keypress is cheap to make by accident, LES-324); **`beforeunload`** only while
  there is unsaved state. **The browser's Back is deliberately not intercepted** — nothing is lost, and
  the honest substitutes are the guard's own copy ("the work is kept as a draft on this browser"), a
  **destination notice** naming the imprint left behind, its count and the way back, and the
  **catalog's** "N imprints on this browser have unsaved drafts" for a cold start.
- **Gesture ids are unique per session** (`crypto.randomUUID()`, with a random-prefixed counter as the
  fallback — never a bare counter), because they are now persisted in the draft.

**The calls (implementation (Claude Code), each open to veto):**

1. **Two different escapes, one geometry source.** A clone **proposal** may overlap and **discloses
   what it covers** (the placement step exists to move it); an **appended** tile is drawn until the
   operator places it and therefore parks **clear of everything the plane draws**. One `drawnBoxes` set
   feeds both the parking lane and the "what does this cover" report, under one rule: **you do not
   cover the box you are inside**.
2. **A stale draft is restored-and-disclosed, never merged** (LES-251 holds): world-dependent refusals
   **keep** the key so the operator can resolve them, permanent ones **drop** it.
3. **One guard on `go`**, not one per link — a new link cannot forget it — with **Back un-intercepted**
   and the destination and catalog notices as the honest substitute.
4. **A gesture's deploy tag is the weaker of the two:** a batch holding any `override` reads as the
   fresh-deploy tag, never as `te deploy --update`.
5. **The fingerprint stamp advances only in `performSave`'s success continuation** — not in any effect
   keyed on an etag, which is what destroyed the evidence a restore compares against.
6. **`invalidStoredRecord` is a shape check, not a re-validation:** it refuses what this app could not
   have produced and lets everything else through to the server, which remains the authority.

**How it was verified — and what the rounds caught.** Code-writer → **tester and reviewer in
parallel** → a combined fix round (**3 must-fix, 9 should-fix, 3 nits**) → a bounded re-probe (which
found a regression and a half-closed item) → a four-item round → a one-line round. **Every fix carries
a revert proof.**

- **The gesture-id collision, found independently by both verifiers.** Ids were minted from a
  `useRef(0)` counter that resets on every workspace remount — and the draft persists each record's
  whole tag. A clone staged, reloaded, then followed by a second clone produced **two batches under one
  id**: one change-list row labelled with the first, counting both, whose single undo removed **all 47
  records and both pins**. The id also keys the grouping, the expander and `data-gesture`, so the
  collision was wrong in four places at once (**LES-332**).
- **The parking lane landed on the O7 unplaced tile while reporting `covers: []`.** The lane cleared
  the *columns* rather than every box the plane draws, and the coverage report was computed from a
  different set than the lane — so the one tile it always hit was the one it never named (**LES-334**).
- **The recompile check was skipped by a race, and its evidence re-stamped.** The restore compared the
  draft's fingerprint against a cast that had not arrived, which looked exactly like a bundle that
  publishes none — and the draft-write effect then stamped the *new* fingerprint over the draft,
  destroying the comparison for every later restore too (**LES-333**).
- **An undo was disabled with an untrue reason.** A pending-only gesture re-saves nothing, so requiring
  the save metadata made the control read "you must fill in `source` first" for an operation that never
  touches the file; `metadataReady` is now required **only where a saved record is actually involved**
  (**LES-336**'s neighbour, recorded in the work order's §20.4).
- **A kept draft with no discard control.** A draft kept because the *world* refused it showed a notice
  with no way to get rid of it; the offer now stands **whenever a notice is shown**, including when
  nothing restored.
- **The leave guard acted instead of dismissing.** Undoing the last gesture under an open prompt
  navigated the operator somewhere they had not agreed to go: a prompt whose reason has gone must be
  **dismissed**, never answered (**LES-336**).

**Three verification lessons this iteration earned** (all in `docs/LESSONS.md`):

- **A test that passes in its file and fails alone is a scheduling coincidence.** `waitFor` asserts a
  value *became* right, not that it *is* right, and a later effect had re-stamped it. Every test added
  or changed in the last two rounds was **also run alone**, and a sweep of all 44 tests in
  `s51d.ui.test.tsx` individually found no other order dependence (**LES-337**).
- **A regression test for "the second one collides with the first" must restore and then create a
  second.** One that restores and stops passes on the collision.
- **A browser probe pointed at the checked-in `twin-configs-cast/` writes into the repo.** A placement
  pin reached `corpapcn-twin/layout.json` and tripped
  `tests/test_api_console.py::test_the_real_bundles_have_no_operator_files_and_reading_them_creates_none`.
  Probes belong on a **scratch copy** (**LES-338**). The stray file was removed; a backup sits in the
  session's scratch.

**Gates (no live-verify — nothing here deploys or touches a cluster):** Vitest **1018 passed / 0 failed
/ 8 skipped (1026)** (iteration 8 ended at 924 / 8); `npm run typecheck` clean from zero; `npm run
build` to a scratch `--outDir` exit 0; pytest **3119 passed / 12 skipped, 3131 collected** (the
one-failure run during the round was the stray `layout.json`, now removed). `doc-stats.py --check`
clean.

**Honest limits recorded** (`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §20.5): a **kept** draft is replaced by
the next pending write in that imprint — the stored draft mirrors what is pending, which is what makes
it self-healing — and the discard offer stands throughout; a tile **dragged onto another** still
overlaps by design and names what it covers; **Back is not intercepted** (nothing is lost, and the
notices say where the work is); and the two behavioural tests for the fingerprint race are **decisive
only in isolation** — a **structural** guard (no effect keyed on an etag may stamp the draft) is what
holds in a full-suite run, and the test says so. One new gap: **GAP-096** — a placement pin is written
to the server at **stage** time while its segment is still pending.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` — status header (**S5.1 complete, a–e; S7 is the only open
  slice**); §3 S5.1.d and S5.1.e marked BUILT; new **§20 "S5.1.d–e built as, 2026-09-16"** (what
  landed, the calls, a clause → proving-test table, the rounds, the limits, what remains).
- `docs/design/15-console-canvas.md` — §8 (the draft and its reconciliation; gesture entries and
  whole-gesture undo; the guard model), §4 (how pending is drawn — placed, appended, parked, held),
  status and §13.

## 2026-09-16 — Console S5.1.b–c BUILT: a clone carries a segment's hosts to a new network, and a fresh segment can be born with them (iteration 8 of ten)

**Authority:** **implementation (Claude Code)** for the build and for the seven calls below, each open
to veto — call 1 (exact-versus-overlap) is the ruling `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §3 S5.1.b
asked for and §18.6 carried into this iteration. **The slice's scope is the user's** (§3 S5.1, the
post-build amendment of 2026-09-11), and so is the run's order (the ten-iteration Console run, whose
**iteration 8 is S5.1.b–c**) and the commit policy (unattended local commits for iterations 7–11,
2026-09-15).

**Version-locked: nothing moved.** No `IR_SCHEMA`/`PLANNER`/`OP_CATALOG`/`VALIDATOR`/`ADAPTER`/
`IMAGE_MANIFEST` change, no new op type, no catalog rule, no `scenario_schema_version`, state-store or
`PLANE_EPOCH` bump. Op types **40**, catalog rules **114**. **No new record kind** — a batch is N
records of the ruled kinds, each still built by `edit/records.ts`'s `buildRecord`. The Python side is
one generator, its drift test and two acceptance tests: pytest **3131 collected** (was 3122), the run
**3119 passed / 12 skipped**. The four level-0 goldens are **byte-identical**: an added segment takes
no grid slot and reflows nothing.

**What changed:**

- **The batch path — one gesture, many records, all or nothing.** New `o2/console/src/edit/batch.ts`
  (`buildBatch`) checks a whole batch before a single record is staged: `buildRecord` per record, then
  the store's identity rule, then `crossRecordConflict` against what is staged **and** the batch's own
  earlier records; the first refusal refuses everything, naming the record by position and gesture.
  The reducer gains a **`stageBatch`** action, and the identity rule behind it is the exported pure
  `batchRefusal(entries, records)` — so the pin, the placement and the selection a gesture also
  commits hang off **the same decision the reducer makes** rather than a parallel one. Each entry
  carries a **`gesture` tag** (`{id, label, kind, source?}`): browser knowledge only — the assertions
  file has no gesture concept, so a tag survives a save and re-read only while the record on file is
  still, **by value**, the record the gesture staged.
- **Clone and drag (S5.1.b).** **Alt-drag** a tile — the source stays put, a ghost follows the pointer
  — or press *Clone this segment…* and place it; a **tap** places it too, so the gesture is complete
  by pointer, keyboard and touch. The drop position is committed as an **S4 pin on the clone's final
  CIDR**, through the same writer a drag uses, so the plane stamp's rule still holds (one gesture
  authors exactly one pin, LES-296). The clone form carries §3's whole table — CIDR, name, zone type
  (defaulting to the **source's** declared zone), hosts to bring (*all* / *by role* / *first N by
  address*), address mapping (*same host part* / *renumber from .10*), copy rules, mirror conduits, and
  one required note propagated to every record as **`cloned from <cidr>: <note>`**. Its refusals are
  stated, never silent: **hostname- and MAC-keyed hosts are listed as *not cloned*** with the reason (a
  hostname names one machine), a selection of only such hosts is refused rather than staging a bare
  segment, a mapped address the export or the file already holds is refused **with a *renumber* offer**,
  the CIDR must be the network address, and a source conduit whose peer **is** the clone's own network
  is listed as *not mirrored* rather than dropped.
- **Generated hosts (S5.1.c).** The site `+` form gains a **hosts block**: a count, a role, and a
  listen set prefilled from the persona library, with addresses allocated **from `.10` upward** —
  skipping every address the export carries **and every `address:` key the file holds in any block,
  `suppress` and `override` included**. It emits `add: segment` + N × `add: host` through the same
  batch path. With no count it stages exactly one record, as S5.1.a did.
- **The per-role listen table is generated, not hand-written.** `scripts/gen_role_listen.py` reads
  `axrange/personas/catalog/*.yaml` and writes `o2/console/src/model/roleListen.generated.ts`;
  `tests/test_console_role_listen.py` regenerates it and fails on drift, with an independent
  cross-check that every entry is an emit the catalog really names, on the **schema's own** transport
  rule (`Emit.effective_transport()`, never restated). **The rule is drive-aware:** a `real-service`
  persona **serves** its emits (its own id is the listening role) and every other drive **dials** (the
  `peer_role` listens) — the first cut read every drive as dialing and had `corp-workstation`
  "listening" on 53/88/389/135/123. `loadgen-*` personas are excluded (they characterize capture
  throughput and had given every workstation four 999x/udp ports and invented two roles), and reserved
  peer tokens are skipped **on the dialing side only**, so a daemon serving `internet` keeps its port.
- **Pending clone tiles are drawn.** New pure `o2/console/src/layout/added.ts` places a **placed**
  added segment at its pin (or this session's placement) — hatched for *pending*, outlined for *saved*
  — takes no grid slot, is selectable, is **held and disclosed** when its group is collapsed (R6's
  rule for a pin on a hidden tile), and **names every drawn tile it covers**, since an absolute pin may
  overlap by design but a silently-unclickable tile underneath is not a design.
- **A drag no longer sweeps text.** `usePinDrag` cancels `selectstart` **only while a gesture it began
  is active** — measured 364 characters selected across tile labels by one drag before, 0 after — and
  deliberately does not `preventDefault` the `pointerdown`, which would take focus-on-press away from
  controls that must keep it.
- **Three disclosures the review added:** the listen prefill's caveat at the point of use (below);
  a pin naming no segment in play is **named in the pin bar** rather than counted as a placed tile; and
  a failed `PUT …/layout` is **raised as a visible alert** plus "placed in this browser only" on the
  tile, instead of being discoverable solely in an `aria-label`.

**The calls (implementation (Claude Code), each open to veto):**

1. **Exact-versus-overlap for a clone's CIDR** — the ruling §3 S5.1.b asked for: an **exact** match
   with an observed segment (whatever its standing against the cast), a segment the cast declares, or a
   pending/saved/applied add is **refused** — a clone creates a *new* segment, and reaching an existing
   one is *Add host here*; an **overlap only warns** (NET-010, which a relocated cast makes moot),
   exactly as S5.1.a's site `+` does. Note the deliberate asymmetry with the site `+`: S5.1.a *allows*
   an exact add of an observed segment the cast does not declare, because that add **confirms** it; a
   clone of it would not.
2. **The drive-aware listen rule**, its `loadgen-*` exclusion, and the **reserved-token asymmetry** —
   skipped when dialing, kept when serving, so `dmz-web` keeps 443 for internet clients.
3. **Disclosure, not a heuristic, for direction.** Five roles' tables carry ports the role **dials** —
   `web-tier` (3306), `db-replica` (3306, 5432), `protocol-gateway` (502, 4840), `egress-proxy`
   (53/udp), `monitoring-collector` (9100, 161) — because a `real-service` persona's emits are filed
   under its own id with no served-versus-dialed split. The generated header names the five, the form
   repeats the caveat **where the default is offered** (naming the typed role first when it is one of
   them), and `check_dialed_caveat()` fails loudly if a named role stops being a `real-service` persona
   of the catalog. **The real fix belongs in the persona catalog — an emit has no direction** —
   registered as `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` **GAP-095**.
4. **A batch never replaces an existing record** — stricter than single-record staging, whose
   documented re-stage rule replaces. One record of sixteen silently overwriting a saved host is not a
   gesture anyone meant.
5. **Hostname- and MAC-keyed hosts are not clonable**, and a selection naming only such hosts is
   **refused** rather than quietly staging a bare segment.
6. **Ports use the compiler's canonical order**, and `image`/`resources` travel only from an
   **asserted or overridden** value — an image nothing asserted is the compiler's to choose for the new
   host too.
7. **The placement clamp tests the column's own bottom edge**, not the east-west rail (below).

**How it was verified — and the two measurement disputes that shaped it.** Code-writer → tester and
reviewer in parallel → one combined fix round (**1 must-fix, 7 should-fix, 5 nits**) → a bounded
re-probe → a final two-line fix. **Every fix carries a revert proof.** The tester walked both real
bundles in headless Chromium with PID ownership proven (LES-315) and caught a **stylesheet `fill`
overriding the hatch pattern** — a rule jsdom cannot see, so no unit test could have.

- **Dispute 1, and the slice's sharpest finding: both parties were right.** The reviewer measured a
  clone proposal crossing **six asserted arcs**; the code-writer could not reproduce it and measured
  **38 units of clearance**. Five of `corpapcn-twin`'s six columns carry a `+N more` box, which reserves
  a row, so their box bottom is **680** and the proposal sits inside them — but `site:misc` has exactly
  six segments and therefore **no** `+N more`, so its box ends at **568** and the same proposal falls
  100 units outside its own column, through the corridor the arcs anchored on that group drop into. The
  first clamp tested the **east-west rail**, which calls that case clear (668 < 706). It now tests the
  **column's own bottom edge**, and the escape moves the proposal to the right of the column instead.
- **Dispute 2:** the tester's first band measurement used **arc bounding boxes** rather than the rail,
  and the reading was withdrawn.
- **What hid it:** a layout probe that does not run through the live seam (`realGraph` +
  `level0OptionsFor`) is not measuring the map the operator sees — LES-311's class, one iteration on.

**Gates (no live-verify — nothing here deploys or touches a cluster):** Vitest **924 passed / 0 failed
/ 8 skipped (932)** (iteration 7 ended at 822 / 8); `npm run typecheck` clean from zero; `npm run
build` to a scratch `--outDir` exit 0; pytest **3119 passed / 12 skipped, 3131 collected**.
`doc-stats.py --check` clean.

**Honest limits recorded** (`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §19.5): the placement **escape can
overlap the next column** on a narrow plane — accepted, because the placement step exists to move it
and a placed tile names what it covers; a proposal for a column **pinned below the band** follows its
column rather than teleporting away from the segment being copied; a **removed clone's pin lingers**
in `layout.json` (inert, now disclosed in the pin bar — cleanup is S5.1.d); and level-1 hatched chips,
the per-gesture change list, whole-gesture undo and **draft persistence** remain S5.1.d (GAP-094).

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` — status header; §3 S5.1 (S5.1.b–c BUILT; the exact-versus-overlap
  ruling recorded where the slice asks for it; S5.1.d gains dead-pin cleanup); new **§19 "S5.1.b–c built
  as, 2026-09-16"** (what landed, the calls, a clause → proving-test table, the rounds and the two

## 2026-09-15 — Console S5.1.a BUILT — selection drives editing (iteration 7 of ten)

**Authority:** **implementation (Claude Code)** for the build and for calls 1–6 below, each open to
veto. **The slice's scope is the user's** — `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §3 S5.1, the
post-build amendment of 2026-09-11 — and so is the run's order (the ten-iteration Console run of
2026-09-11, whose **iteration 7 is S5.1.a**). **Call 7, the commit policy, is the user's too
(2026-09-15).** The slice **resumed from a rate-limit interruption**: its first code-writer was cut
off mid-draft with two of its own acceptance tests red, and a resumed code-writer finished it.

**Version-locked: nothing moved.** No `IR_SCHEMA`/`PLANNER`/`OP_CATALOG`/`VALIDATOR`/`ADAPTER`/
`IMAGE_MANIFEST` change, no new op type, no catalog rule, no `scenario_schema_version`, state-store or
`PLANE_EPOCH` bump. Op types **40**, catalog rules **114**. The slice's only Python is **one additive
API field**, `CastSummaryDTO.segment_roster` (below), and its tests: pytest **3122 collected** (was
3112), the full run **3110 passed / 12 skipped**. **No record kind changed** — every gesture still
builds its record through `o2/console/src/edit/records.ts`'s `buildRecord`, and the S2 reducer is
untouched.

**What changed:**

- **Selection drives editing.** The selected element opens in the inspector with its facts and its
  actions (`o2/console/src/views/ElementActions.tsx`, new). A **level-0 tile** is selected through its
  select control (the ✎, now a stroked SVG path icon with a 24 × 24 hit target; clicking the tile
  itself still drills), a **level-1 spine** is selected in place, and a **chip** drills to its host's
  route — for a host, the URL's subject IS the selection. A **segment** offers *Add host here*, *Clone
  this segment…*, *Add conduit to…* and *Suppress hosts…*; a **host** offers *Change role*, *Override
  image / resources* and *Suppress*. *Clone this segment…* is present but `aria-disabled`, with a plain
  reason (it is S5.1.b) — an absent action and an unbuilt one read identically otherwise. **The S5
  `EditPanel`'s free-standing forms are gone**; what remains there belongs to the file: the change
  list, the save metadata, Save and Apply. A segment R6's budget collapsed has no tile but reaches the
  same actions from the outline or search, because opening an element selects it
  (`views/selection.ts` `resolveSelection`: an explicit in-place selection wins, else the element the
  URL names).
- **A segment is added from a `+` on the site it is born in.** Each map site header and each outline
  site row carries one, with one accessible name from one helper; the outline root's `+` is the
  site-less add, which lands in `misc`. The form prefills **the next free `/24` in the site's
  two-octet prefix** (`edit/allocate.ts` `nextFreeSlash24`, new — free means no overlap with any CIDR
  in play, at any prefix length), and **the prefill never proposes an observed CIDR**, including one
  the cast does not declare. The CIDR field **follows the suggestion until it is typed in**; one
  canonical `strictCidr` value feeds every note and the Stage refusal, and `model/keys.ts` `parseCidr`
  now reads a **digits-only** prefix length. Stage:
  - **refuses** a CIDR the cast declares — joined **by name** through the new segment roster, with
    renames mapped from this file's saved and pending records — saying *"already declared in the cast
    — select it and use Add host here"*; and refuses a CIDR a **pending or saved** add already holds;
  - **allows** an observed CIDR the cast does not declare under that name, saying *"renamed or pruned
    (the cast joins by name). An add sets its name and zone type, but the compiler keeps a segment only
    when a host is placed on it: select it and use Add host here"*;
  - **only warns** on an overlap, naming NET-010 and `--relocate`.

  The **name prefill** follows aximprint's `segment_name` (`seg-` plus the dashed network address) and
  disambiguates a clash with `-<prefix length>`, then a counter; Stage refuses a name another network
  holds (STR-095). The **zone type** starts at the site's dominant declared zone, then the cast's, then
  `simple`, and the form says which.
- **An added segment is selectable before the map can draw it.** A pending or saved `add: segment`
  the export does not observe is listed in the outline under the site it would group with, badged with
  its state; a direct URL selects it while the map says plainly it is not drawn yet; it offers *Add
  host here* and *Add conduit to…*; and every conduit picker offers it, labelled as an add.
  **Removing** an `add: segment` that is the **only home** of staged hosts or conduits asks an inline
  confirmation listing them, with each kind's real outcome — a host left without it would **fail the
  apply**, a conduit would be **reported ineffective** and dropped (`edit/dependents.ts`, new). A
  record with another home (an observed segment, or another add) is not a dependent, so an add of an
  observed segment goes alone. Records whose segment has no home are **flagged as orphans** against the
  observed segments plus this file's adds — never the cast's relocated roster.
- **A selection lives exactly as long as the pathname.** It resets on a pathname change only — not
  on `?changed=1`, a view flag or a dimming filter, each of which had dropped the selection and
  whatever was typed under it. `api/hooks.ts` `useResource` now returns data **only for the subject it
  was read for**, and `views/useGraph.ts` holds the last graph across an apply's cast re-read keyed on
  the **input objects** it was built from, never on an id label. **Escape** clears an explicit
  selection first and returns focus to the control that opened it (else the map pane, never `body`);
  from inside the actions frame it steps out to the map; and at the network level **with unsaved edits
  it does not navigate** — it announces *"N unsaved edits — save or discard them before leaving"* in a
  live region. A bundle with no topology export (a 404) reads as **absent**, not failed, and a host
  route keeps its actions, including through an apply. The selection is marked `aria-current` on one
  element per view — at level 0 the tile itself, never also its select control.
- **The cast publishes its segment roster** (additive and not version-locked, on R5's
  `conduit_roster`/`routers` precedent): **`CastSummaryDTO.segment_roster: [{name, cidr, zone_type}]`**
  in the twin config's own order, `len(segment_roster) == segments` on every bundle. `cidr` is the
  **deployed** network (relocated on a `--relocate` cast, which is why the join is by name) and
  `zone_type` is `null` where the config declares none, never the effective zone. Mirrored in
  `o2/console/src/api/types.ts` (`CastSegment`) and fed through `useGraph.castInputFrom`; a server
  without the field reads as *not published by this server*, never as a cast that declares nothing.
  **It fixes a defect the fixture had hidden:** the inspector's deployed-CIDR and zone facts read "not
  declared" on **every real segment**, because only a test fixture's direct maps had ever supplied
  them. A checked-in `o2/console/test/fixtures/real-segment-rosters.json` feeds the real-bundle
  allocator tests and is pinned to the real `<id>.yaml` files by a Python drift test; the opt-in
  `live-api` test now expects the field, so it fails against an older server.
- **Code organisation.** New `src/edit/allocate.ts`, `src/edit/dependents.ts`,
  `src/views/ElementActions.tsx` and `src/views/paneParts.tsx` (the shared `Row`/`Fact`/`Field`).
  `layout/sites.ts` publishes **`Site.prefix`**, so nothing re-derives a prefix by parsing a label; the
  four level-0 goldens gain **13 `site.prefix` leaves (0 removed, 0 changed)**, and the level-1 and
  matrix goldens are byte-identical.

**The calls.** Calls 1–6 are **implementation (Claude Code)**, open to veto; call 7 is the user's.

1. **A pruned or renamed observed CIDR is re-addable, and the note states what the compiler really
   does** — an add of an observed CIDR *confirms* rather than creates, and a host placed on it is what
   keeps the segment. The first wording ("restores that segment") rested on
   `docs/design/14-operator-assertions.md` §3's worked example and was **wrong**; the reviewer settled
   it by running aximprint's real `compile_twin`. Design note 14 carries a dated correction.
2. **A pending or saved added segment is selectable**, restoring S5's
   host-and-conduit-on-an-added-segment capability so that S5.1.a ships alone. Drawing pending tiles on
   the map stays S5.1.d.
3. **Only a name held by a DIFFERENT network collides** — an add of an observed segment under its own
   observed or cast name names nothing new.
4. **The zone default is site → cast → `simple`**, never `vlan`: `simple` is the twin-config schema's
   own default, one `vlan` segment makes the whole twin a VLAN zone, and a VLAN zone rejects SNAT
   egress. (The S5 form defaulted to `vlan`.)
5. **Escape never discards unsaved work** — the unsaved-edits guard at the network level.
6. **`segment_roster` is added to the API**, rather than the Console guessing whether the cast
   declares a segment.
7. **Commit policy (Authority: user, 2026-09-15): unattended LOCAL commits for iterations 7–11.** A
   push and any change to the standing `:8080` serve still check back.

**How it was verified — the defect trail, by class.** A resumed code-writer; a tester and a reviewer
in parallel; fix round 1; a re-verify; fix round 2; a light re-probe; a tiny fix round 3. **Every fix
carries a revert proof.** The tester walked the two real bundles (`corpapcn-twin`, pit) in headless
Chromium against its **own** scratch server, with PID ownership proven (LES-315).

- **Fix round 1 — honest facts, honest claims, a selection that lasts.** (A1) the inspector's cast
  facts read "not declared" on every real segment, masked by fixture-only maps → the roster;
  (A2) Stage refused every observed CIDR as already "in the twin", unable to tell a declared segment
  from one the cast does not carry; (A3) removing the free-standing forms had removed the only path to
  hosts and conduits on an added segment; (A4) the name prefill could duplicate a segment name — a
  `/23` or `/25` at an observed network address inherited its `/24`'s name; (B1) the live note and the
  Stage check read different values, and `Number("")` turned a half-typed `10.80.31.0/` into a `/0`
  that "overlapped" everything; (B2) `?changed=1`, view flags and dimming filters reset the selection
  and its typed input — on **every apply that changed anything**; (B3) Escape left a level before
  clearing a selection; (B4) a missing export rendered as a failed read; (B6) the landing note promised
  a map placement the map cannot draw, and counted staged adds as siblings; (B8) a prefix re-derived
  from a site label, and a network-mask rule written twice; (B5/C) text-glyph controls without 24 × 24
  targets, the map's and outline's `+` under different names, focus falling to `body`, and one site's
  typed value reaching another site's form.
- **Fix round 2 — isolation, and saying what really happens.** **(MF1, the must-fix)** switching
  imprint A → B, directly or via the catalog, drew **A's tiles under B's heading and let a gesture
  stage A's segments into B's file**: `useResource` cleared stale data in an effect, leaving one mixed
  render, and round 1's graph hold — keyed on the imprint **id label** — made that render persistent.
  (SF1) a segment renamed by a saved add read as "pruned"; (SF2) removing an added segment silently
  orphaned the hosts and conduits staged on it; (SF3) an apply's cast re-read turned a no-export bundle
  into a failed read and unmounted the host's typed input; (T5) Escape at the network level discarded
  pending edits; (ZT) the zone defaulted to `vlan`; plus four nits (Escape spending a press on the URL's
  own subject, `body` recorded as an opener, a re-staged add replacing silently, a vanished conduit
  peer still staged) and a **circular test** — the real-bundle sweep expected back the very pruned list
  it was built from (LES-187's class), now fed from the drift-tested roster fixture instead.
- **Fix round 3 — the dependents' key space.** The re-probe's case: SF2 counted an add of an
  **observed** segment as the home of the hosts on it, and counted the cast's relocated roster as a
  home when flagging orphans. Both now follow the compiler's own resolution set — observed segments plus
  this file's adds (aximprint `known_cidrs`).

**Gates (no live-verify — nothing here deploys or touches a cluster):** Vitest **822 passed / 0
failed / 8 skipped (830)** (R6, the last committed baseline, was 647 / 8); `npm run typecheck` clean
from zero; `npm run build` to a scratch `--outDir` exit 0; pytest **3110 passed / 12 skipped, 3122
collected**. `doc-stats.py --check` clean.

**Honest limits recorded** (`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §18.5): **navigation other than
Escape still discards pending edits** — the breadcrumb, the outline's imprint links, the browser's
Back and a reload — because `edit/store.ts` has **no draft mechanism**, although S5.1's acceptance text
describes one; it is S5.1.d's, registered as `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` **GAP-094**. A rename made through
an `override` record (a hand-edited file only) is not mapped and honestly reads "renamed or pruned".
MF1's input-keyed hold is a second wall that no test can isolate while the first stands (LES-313).
Cosmetic: the remove-confirmation's buttons are crowded, and Escape's focus return scrolls the map
sideways. `corpapcn-twin`'s six "pruned" segments come from an **older** compiler — the current one
prunes none on a real PIT, so any apply brings them back regardless (GAP-087's class). And the
compiler half of call 1 is aximprint's: an `add: segment` on an observed-but-uncarried CIDR with no
host is a **silent no-op**, reported neither `ineffective` nor `unmatched`, and its conduit remedy
text points at that same gesture — **GAP-093**. **Carried to iteration 8:** S5.1.b must rule
exact-versus-overlap before reusing `claimOn`, and must handle a clone dropped into a collapsed group.

## 2026-09-15 — Console level-0 R6 BUILT: a tile says how much it carries, a group draws its busiest six, and the collapse discloses what it hides — every rendering slice is now built

**Authority:** **implementation (Claude Code)** for everything below — `columns.ts`'s three rules
(ranking, budget, column width), `SITE_BUDGET = 6`, the decision to keep the **host-count** ranking
rather than rank by bytes, the `PLANE_EPOCH` bump, the arc-folding rule that keeps a collapsed
segment's links on the map, the whole disclosure round, and every honest limit named. **The run's
scope and order are the user's** (2026-09-11): build the whole Console — the level-0 order's R1–R7,
then `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §3 S5.1 (a–e), then S7 — as a ten-iteration run whose
**iteration 6 is R6**; level-0 D1–D3 take the order's v1 defaults and D5 is ruled. **With this
slice every RENDERING slice in that order is BUILT** (R1–R7); iteration 7 moves the run from drawing
to editing (S5.1.a).

**Version-locked: nothing moved.** Frontend-only — everything is under `o2/console/`, **no Python was
touched**. No `IR_SCHEMA`/`PLANNER`/`OP_CATALOG`/`VALIDATOR`/`ADAPTER`/`IMAGE_MANIFEST` change, no new
op type, no catalog rule, no `scenario_schema_version` or state-store bump. Op types **40**, catalog
rules **114**, pytest **3094 passed / 12 skipped** (`-m "not slow"`), all unchanged. **`PLANE_EPOCH`
goes 2 → 3** — R6 changes *which tiles exist*, which is exactly the case R3's rule reserved the manual
epoch for (`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_level0_rendering.md` §12.3).

**What changed:**

- **A tile now says what it holds, and a group draws only its busiest six (closes §2 F5).** Two new
  pure pieces: `o2/console/src/layout/columns.ts` (`orderSegments` — placed-host count desc then
  CIDR, with `?order=cidr` keeping the address order; `budgetColumn`; `siteColsFor`; `columnWidthFor`)
  and `SITE_BUDGET = 6` in `src/model/budgets.ts`, beside the other budgets rather than at its use
  site. The tile carries **`CIDR · hosts · bytes (north-south and east-west) · up to 3 role badges ·
  evidence ring`**; a group over budget collapses the rest into one **`+N more`** tile that
  `?expand=<site key>` (repeatable, in the URL) lifts; and a group of **≤ 4 shown** tiles is laid out
  **two** columns across instead of three, so a small group stops reserving a width it cannot fill.
  `layout/level0.ts` publishes `overflow: OverflowTile[]` and `hiddenSegments`; `model/graph.ts` gains
  `GraphSegment.eastWest` **derived from `trafficPairs` rather than recounted**, so a tile's byte line
  and an arc's width can never disagree about the same pair (LES-025); `model/zoom.ts` gains
  `?order=hosts|cidr` and the repeatable `?expand=`; `layout/infra.ts` moves to per-column widths;
  `layout/plane.ts` hashes the three new budget constants **and** takes the epoch to 3. On the real
  `corpapcn-twin` bundle the map draws **18 tiles for 32 segments** (14 collapsed, and **32 again**
  with every group expanded) — and the 19-segment `10.80` group on the pit bundle goes from **seven
  tile rows to two plus the overflow tile**, taking the plane's height from **1282 to 834**.
  **Collapsing is a projection of the map, never a filter:** a collapsed segment keeps its outline
  row, its matrix axis, its search hit and its drill-down.
- **The ranking stays host-count, deliberately, and ranking by bytes was refused.** A level-0 tile is
  mostly a statement about *population* — where the twin's hosts are — and a byte ranking would
  collapse exactly the segments that hold it (a quiet 30-host workstation segment sorts below a
  one-host egress talker). `?order=cidr` is the other real question ("where is 10.20.195.0/24") and is
  offered beside it; the byte figure on the tile is what carries volume.
- **Two defects found by MEASUREMENT rather than by assumption, both fixed in the build.**
  **(1) The east-west band's top was measured from the lowest *tile*, and a column can end below its
  last tile** once the `+N more` box takes a grid slot — so the band ran **201.8 units against its
  140-unit bound**. It is now measured from `columnsBottom`; reverting turns **9 tests red across 6
  files**. **(2) A collapsed segment has no tile, so every arc anchored to one would have silently
  vanished** — measured before the fix: **24 of 40 pairs surviving on the pit bundle and 16 of 31 on
  `corpapcn-twin`**. The byte loss is **twin-specific**, which is the part a single-bundle check would
  have missed: pit keeps **98.9 %** of its east-west volume because its dominant pair has both ends
  drawn, while `corpapcn-twin` keeps **4.6 % and loses its widest arc entirely**. A collapsed
  segment's arcs now **fold onto its group's `+N more` anchor and merge** (`ArcAnchor.key`, one curve
  per *anchor* pair, `Arc.pairs`, `foldedPairs`, and `internalPairs` for a pair whose two ends fold to
  the same anchor — not drawable, counted rather than dropped in silence); reverting turns **6 tests
  red**.
- **The disclosure round — the slice's real lesson.** The review's headline was that **the collapse
  hides most of a group's volume and nothing said so**, and the per-tile byte line structurally cannot
  reveal it because a collapsed segment has no tile. Measured on the shipped bundles: pit `10.20`
  hides **4 of 10** segments carrying **75.1 % of that group's north-south bytes** (including
  `10.20.195.0/24` — **1.77 GB from one host**, the bundle's largest egress talker); pit `10.80` hides
  **13 of 19** carrying **65.5 %**; `corpapcn-twin`'s `10.20` hides **4 of 10** carrying **99.9 % of
  its east-west volume**. So: the **hidden volume** now appears in the `+N more` tile's hover text, in
  its accessible name and in the tier's own budget note, **all from one helper** (LES-307's rule, third
  instance) and **omitted when the collapsed set carried nothing** — "0 B and 0 B" is noise, and its
  absence is not a claim. Two further disclosures landed with it: **`· N held for collapsed tiles`**
  beside the pin count, because a pin on a collapsed tile is *kept but not drawn*; and a **merged arc
  now states that its protocol mix is unavailable** ("protocol mix is per link; this curve merges 2,
  so the matrix has each one") rather than silently dropping the clause.
- **The golden account is the RE-KEYED one, not the raw one.** The raw diff reads **366 / 374 changed
  leaves and 326 removed** on the two level-0 goldens, which reads as a rewrite. Re-keyed by identity
  (tiles by CIDR, arcs by pair, devices by id) it is **69 changed and ZERO removed** — nothing was
  lost; the raw count is the ranking permutation re-indexing embedded `hosts[]` arrays. Three causes,
  in order of size: **(a)** the plane narrowing from `siteColsFor` making a ≤ 4-tile group two across
  — `width` **2374 → 1514** and **2932 → 1900**, the largest contributor and the one missing from the
  first account; **(b)** the band-top fix, `rails[4].box.y` **470 → 482**, exactly `SITE_PAD`; **(c)**
  the permutation plus the new keys (`overflow`, `hiddenSegments`, `foldedPairs`, `internalPairs`,
  `arcs[*].pairs`, `segments[*].eastWest`). **Every box that moved moved in `x` only; `y` is invariant
  at 348.** Three level-1 goldens gained **+2 leaves each** (`segment.eastWest`, because `layout1`
  embeds `GraphSegment` — LES-300's third instance), both matrices are **byte-identical**, and **two
  new goldens** (`level0-collapsed`, `level0-collapsed-cidr`) document both orders.

**Why:**

- F5 was the last open defect of the §2 review and the only one that was about the *tile* rather than
  about a connector: every tile the same size, none of them saying how much it carried, and a
  19-segment group drawn as seven tile rows that ran off the bottom of any window. The budget is what
  stops the plane's height being a function of the busiest group, and the byte figure is what makes
  §1 property 3 ("magnitude visible before any label") true of the tiles as well as the connectors.
- The disclosure work is the slice's substance, not its polish: a projection that hides 75 % of a
  group's traffic while showing a tidy six tiles is a *more* confident wrong picture than the
  unreadable seven-row column it replaced. Generalized as **LES-319**.

**Gates (no live-verify — nothing here deploys or touches a cluster):** Vitest **647 passed / 8
skipped** (from 594/8 at iteration 5); `npm run typecheck` clean from zero; `npm run build` exit 0;
`pytest -m "not slow"` **3094 passed / 12 skipped**, unchanged. `doc-stats.py --check` clean. The
standing `axrange serve` is still deliberately **not** restarted (`docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-084), so
the live Console at `10.80.72.52:8080` still shows pre-R1 code.

**Honest limits recorded** (`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_level0_rendering.md` §8, an R6 block):
**the map does not fit at 1600 px and that is now inherent — accepted.** Measured: the plane is
**2030 × 834** against a ~1091 px pane at a 1600 px window (**939 px off-screen**), and fits at 2600
with 61 px to spare. R6 did its job on *height*; the residual is **width**, and it is structural —
three groups at six tiles across is `3 × 558 + 152 + 214 + 24 = 2030`, and even `SITE_BUDGET = 4`
gives 1548. **Column wrapping (site columns onto a second row) is named as the follow-up slice**, with
R1's off-screen readout covering it honestly meanwhile. Also recorded: a **pin on a collapsed tile** is
kept, not drawn, not dropped, and re-applied at its saved coordinates on expand (a *site* pin carries
the `+N more` box with its column); and the **no-reflow pin sweep**, published with its grid —
`x ∈ [140,1400]` step 20 × `y ∈ {620,300}` = **132 positions per column**: `site:10.80` **89 clean /
23 trunk / 36 stub**, `site:10.20` **89 / 31 / 31** (the per-family counts are not a partition — one
pin can violate in two families). **Every pre-R6 pinned coordinate had to be re-aimed because the
plane narrowed**, so the violating ones are now *derived* from the layout while the clean ones stay
literal on purpose — a future plane change falsifies them loudly instead of silently re-aiming them
(**LES-317**). **No new gap registered**: the width residual is carried as a named follow-up slice
rather than a `PRODUCT_GAPS` entry, since it has an owner and a shape.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_level0_rendering.md` — status header (**every rendering slice BUILT**;
  column wrapping named as the follow-up); §2 **F5** closed; §3 R6 marked BUILT **with its acceptance
  wording corrected** (the literal "6 tiles + one `+10 more`" assumed a 16-segment site — true of
  `corpapcn-twin`/`corpapcn-golden`, while the pit bundle has **19** and yields `+13`; the rule is
  `shown === min(SITE_BUDGET, total)` and `hidden === total − shown`, the same correction R3's badge
  rule got); §5 **D2** gains the leg-vs-arc width contrast; §8's R6 block; new **§14 "R6 built as,
  2026-09-15"**.
- `docs/design/15-console-canvas.md` — §3's level-0 row (the tile's byte figure, the per-group budget
  and the two orders; the element count reads *18 drawn of 32 segments*), §4's "segments as tiles in
  CIDR order inside each group" amended to the ranking + budget, §12's status (**all seven rendering
  slices built**).
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` — status header (R6 BUILT, the rendering order closed); §3
  S5.1's sequencing note (a clone dropped into a collapsed group is now a real case); §15.2's
  re-anchoring note gains the budget's consequence.
- `o2/console/README.md` — R6 shipped; `columns.ts` in the layout module list; the real-bundle
  paragraph reads 18 drawn tiles of 32 segments; **all four rendering slices done — S5.1 and S7
  remain**.
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — **LES-315..LES-321**.
- `CLAUDE.md` — the Console blockquote.
- `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` — iteration 6 of ten.

**Affected code:**
- `o2/console/src/layout/columns.ts` (**new**, pure); `src/model/budgets.ts` (`SITE_BUDGET`,
  `siteCollapses`); `src/layout/{level0,arcs,infra,plane}.ts`; `src/model/{graph,zoom}.ts`;

- `o2/console/test/r6.{columns,ui}.test.*` (new); the two new goldens
  `test/layout/level0-collapsed{,-cidr}.golden.json`; `test/{layout.golden,layout.real,model,zoom,
  views,r2.acceptance,r2.trunk,r3.acceptance,r3.infra,r3.ui,r4.acceptance,r4.arcs,r4.ui}.test.*`,

## 2026-09-15 — Console level-0 R4 BUILT: the east-west band becomes volume-weighted arcs, and an arc drops into the band before it curves

**Authority:** **implementation (Claude Code)** for everything below — `arcs.ts`'s geometry and its
**two departures** from the slice's implementation sketch (the drop-before-curve and the
`max(bandTop, both ends)` depth anchor), the **ACCEPT ruling on the inert top-K budget**, the two
evidence corrections, and every honest limit named. **The arcs ruling itself is the user's**
(2026-09-11, `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_level0_rendering.md` §4.1 — east-west links are volume-weighted
arcs anchored at the site group's bottom edge, **superseding** the orthogonal one-channel-per-conduit
lanes ruled in `docs/design/15-console-canvas.md` §4/§5 on 2026-09-04, which are **amended to the
arcs model in this commit** as §4.1 requires). **The run's scope and order are the user's**
(2026-09-11): build the whole Console — the level-0 order's R1–R7, then
`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §3 S5.1 (a–e), then S7 — as a ten-iteration run whose
**iteration 5 is R4**; level-0 D1–D3 take the order's v1 defaults and D5 is ruled.

**Version-locked: nothing moved.** Frontend-only — everything is under `o2/console/`, **no Python was
touched**. No `IR_SCHEMA`/`PLANNER`/`OP_CATALOG`/`VALIDATOR`/`ADAPTER`/`IMAGE_MANIFEST` change, no new
op type, no catalog rule, no `scenario_schema_version` or state-store bump. Op types **40**, catalog
rules **114**, pytest **3094 passed / 12 skipped** (`-m "not slow"`), all unchanged. **`PLANE_EPOCH`
stays 2** and `plane.ts` is not in the changeset — no tile, site, trunk or device coordinate moved, so
R4 invalidates no pin.

**What changed:**

- **The east-west band is arcs, and `lanes.ts` is DELETED (closes §2 F2 and F4).** A new pure module
  `o2/console/src/layout/arcs.ts` — modelled on R2's `trunk.ts` and R3's `infra.ts`, pairs and
  already-placed boxes in, curves out — draws each segment pair as a cubic anchored at **the group
  box's bottom edge** at its own tile's x. `model/graph.ts` gains `TrafficPair` / `ProtoShare` and
  `trafficPairsOf`: unordered pairs of **placed, local** segments carrying `bytes`, `flows` and a
  `protoMix` in descending order; same-segment, non-local and unplaced ends are excluded (north-south
  is R2's). `layout/level0.ts` publishes `arcs` + `hiddenPairs` with a `showAllPairs` option;
  `geometry.ts` gains `ARC_DEPTH_MIN 28` / `ARC_DEPTH_MAX 120` / `ARC_DEPTH_PER_DX 0.05` /
  `ARC_BAND_PAD 20` / the sampling constant; `model/zoom.ts` gains **`?ew=all|top`** as a *view flag*
  (correctly outside the dimming set and preserved by `clearFilters`); `shell.css` swaps `.lane` for
  `.arc` / `.is-asserted` / `.is-silent`. **F4 is closed rather than measured**: the blue lane lines
  that dropped from a row-1 tile through every row beneath it are gone, and the band is
  **height-bounded by construction** — `ARC_DEPTH_MAX + ARC_BAND_PAD` whatever the pair count (proven
  at 300 pairs), where 40 lanes cost 440 px.
- **An asserted conduit is drawn as an arc in the edit colour, dashed when silent.** That is the one
  property the retired lane geometry had — one lane, one conduit — preserved for the set where it
  means something, so a **declared-but-silent** conduit is visibly different from an
  **observed-but-unasserted** flow. A pair that is **both** is drawn **once**, as asserted, carrying
  its observed volume (two identical curves on the same two anchors are one curve plus a misleading
  count). Arcs are **not** protocol-coloured — `15` §5 stands, colour on the Console is evidence — so
  the dominant protocol and the mix live on hover (`arcTitle`) and in the inspector.
- **DEPARTURE 1, forced by measurement: an arc drops to the band top BEFORE it curves.** The slice
  text specifies a cubic straight from one group edge to the other, and built literally that
  **measurably crossed tiles**: a cubic reaches only **three quarters** of the way toward its control
  points (its lowest point is `0.125·(y₀+y₃) + 0.75·y_c`), and site columns differ in height, so an
  arc between two *short* columns sags through the lowest tiles of any *tall* column between them —
  **1 crossing at K=12 and 6 under `?ew=all`** on the pit bundle, all six through `10.80.79.0/24`.
  Each end now descends at its own tile's x (inside its own column's x-span, which no other column
  occupies) to the band top, and curves from there — putting the whole curve where no tile is, by
  construction rather than by luck. **The §4.1 ruling is honoured in substance**: every arc still
  *originates at its group's bottom edge*, asserted for all 40 on the real bundle. The departure is
  from §3 R4's implementation *sketch*, not from the user's ruling. Both geometries were measured
  independently by the tester (0/0 shipped versus 1/6 literal; six tests red without the fix).
- **DEPARTURE 2: the control depth is anchored at `max(bandTop, both endpoints)`, not `bandTop`
  alone.** Provably identical to the sketch on the pure layout (`bandTop = lowestTileBottom + 26`
  while the tallest column's bottom is `+12`), and golden-pinned to prove it. They differ only under
  a **pin** that drags a group below the band, where the literal form puts both controls *above* both
  endpoints and bulges the curve back **up through the tiles of the column that was dragged** — the
  F4 defect re-entering through the geometry that retired it.
- **The top-K budget (K = 12) is implemented, tested, and INERT on live content — ruled ACCEPT.**
  §4.1's own rule draws every *asserted* conduit while the budget bounds only the *observed* set, and
  both shipped casts declare a conduit for essentially every pair they observed: the pit bundle
  publishes **40 conduits for its 40 observed pairs** (40 asserted arcs, `hiddenPairs === 0`, and no
  *show all* control rendered at all), `corpapcn-twin` **22 against 31** (22 asserted + 9 observed,
  still under K). **Budgeting by volume was rejected**: a declared-but-silent conduit carries
  `bytes: 0`, sorts last, and would be hidden **first** — precisely backwards for the element §4.1
  exists to distinguish. A *separate* budget for the asserted family was rejected as a second control
  and a second projection that would hide the twin's **declared structure**. The band is
  height-bounded by construction, so the cost of inertness is **density, not layout**. Stated
  plainly as a consequence: `?ew=all` is **unreachable through the UI** when nothing is hidden, so
  D2's "3.06 px among 12 versus 4.74 px across 40" describes a view no operator can currently open.
- **Evidence correction 1 — the real-bundle budget tests described a view that cannot occur.** The
  pit bundle's own YAML carries 40 conduits and the API publishes them, but those tests built the
  graph **without the roster**. They are kept (they exercise the budget branch), retitled
  `[no-roster construction]` and commented at the construction, and a new `describe` covers the
  **live shape**: 40 conduits for 40 observed pairs (0 undeclared), 40 asserted arcs,
  `hiddenPairs === 0`, no crossings, and `?ew=all` a no-op **including every stroke width**.
  Generalized as **LES-311**.
- **Evidence correction 2 — a pin-sweep figure is only meaningful with its grid.** The
  pinned-column-crosses-another-column sweep is now published **with the grid it was measured on**
  (`x ∈ [140, 1100]` step 20 × six y values = **294 positions per column**): `site:10.20` crosses at
  **129**, `site:10.80` at **85**. An independent re-run by the tester over a *different* (coarser)
  grid with an arcs-only filter measured **146** and **84** — which is exactly why the grid travels
  with the number rather than the number travelling alone. Both pinned coordinates (violating
  `(400,620)`, clean `(140,620)`) reproduce under both agents.

**Why:**

- F4 was the last of §2's crossing-connector defects and the only one R2 could merely *measure*
  rather than fix: `noConnectorCrossesATile` passed on the shipped fixtures while the lane geometry
  that would break it was untouched, so the claim was bounded to those fixtures. Arcs make §1
  property 2 structural — an arc starts *outside* every box by construction — and make §1 property 3
  ("magnitude is visible before any label") true of the east-west band for the first time, since 40
  lanes drew a 2.2 GB link and a 5 MB link at the same stroke.
- The asserted/silent distinction is what keeps the lane model's one real virtue after aggregation by
  volume takes its place: a conduit the twin **declares** and the wire never carried is a fact about
  the twin, and it must not look like an absence.

**Gates (no live-verify — nothing here deploys or touches a cluster):** Vitest **594 passed / 8
skipped** (581 at build, 588 after the tester's 7, 594 after the fix round); `npm run typecheck`
clean from zero; `npm run build` exit 0; `pytest -m "not slow"` **3094 passed / 12 skipped**,
unchanged. Goldens: the build round moved the level-0 pair by **2 changed** leaves (`height`
555 → 561.3 and `rails[4].box.h` 61 → 67.3 — **only the east-west band grew**), **58 added**
(`arcs`, `hiddenPairs`) and **33 removed** (`lanes`); the fix round renamed **3 keys each**
(`arcs[i].observed` → `hasTraffic`) with **0 values changed**. The other five goldens are
byte-identical throughout — **including all three level-1 goldens**, because `trafficPairs` hangs off
`ConsoleGraph` rather than the embedded `GraphSegment` (LES-300, predicted before the regeneration
and confirmed by it). The standing `axrange serve` is still deliberately **not** restarted
(`docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-084).

**Honest limits recorded** (`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_level0_rendering.md` §8, an R4 block in R2's
idiom): the drop-before-curve and depth-anchor departures above; the **inert** budget, recorded
where a reader would otherwise meet it as a bug ("the band draws 40 arcs and no *show all* control
appears"); and **a pinned column drags its arcs across another column's tiles** — the same no-reflow
consequence R2 records for the bus and R3 for the stubs, now for the third connector family, swept
rather than sampled (LES-305) and quoted with its grid.
