## 2026-09-14 — Console level-0 R3 BUILT: each infrastructure device sits over its own group with a short stub, and a tile names its roles instead of drawing an unreadable bar

**Authority:** **implementation (Claude Code)** for everything below — the infra placement rule and
its row grid, the content-derived band height and the `PLANE_EPOCH` bump it forces, the role-badge
**reservation** rule (a deliberate, recorded departure from the slice's literal wording, §3 R3
corrected in the same pass), the top-N disclosure, and every honest limit named. **The run's scope
and order are the user's** (2026-09-11): build the whole Console — the level-0 order's R1–R7, then
`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §3 S5.1 (a–e), then S7 — as a ten-iteration run whose
**iteration 4 is R3**; level-0 D1–D3 take the order's v1 defaults and D5 is ruled.

**Version-locked: nothing moved.** Frontend-only — everything is under `o2/console/`, **no Python
was touched**. No `IR_SCHEMA`/`PLANNER`/`OP_CATALOG`/`VALIDATOR`/`ADAPTER`/`IMAGE_MANIFEST` change,
no new op type, no catalog rule, no `scenario_schema_version` or state-store bump. Op types **40**,
catalog rules **114**, pytest **3112 collected** — all unmoved (`pytest -m "not slow"` 3094 passed /
12 skipped, byte-identical before and after).

**What changed:**

- **Each device sits over its own group, and the diagonals are gone (closes §2 F3).** A new pure
  module `o2/console/src/layout/infra.ts` — modelled on R2's `trunk.ts`, ids in / boxes and
  polylines out — places an infra device at **the centre of the site column that holds its
  segment**, in role order, and drops a **short vertical stub** to that column's group header.
  Nothing has to be routed around because nothing is in the way: the header spans the column's full
  width, so a device inside that width lands on it. On the pit bundle the seven devices distribute
  **2 / 3 / 2** — two resolvers over 10.20, both domain controllers plus `10.80.64.2` over 10.80,
  two singleton-site resolvers over `misc` — and **no connector touches a tile**. A stub ends at its
  header's **nearest** horizontal edge (a shared `geometry.nearestEdgeY`, now used by R2's legs and
  R3's stubs rather than written twice), and an **unplaced** device keeps its centred, dashed-outline
  treatment with **no stub at all** — there is no group to point at, and a stub to the nearest column
  would assert a membership the export does not support. `layout/level0.ts` places devices through
  the module, renames `DevicePlacement.tieToCidr` → **`siteKey`**, publishes
  **`Level0Layout.infraStubs`**, and re-derives both on pin, so a dragged column takes its devices
  and their stubs with it (LES-266's re-anchoring rule, third consumer).
- **A tile names its roles (closes part of §2 F5).** `GraphSegment.roleBadges` carries up to three
  buckets of the existing `roleHistogram`, drawn as `DC 2` / `EP 14` badges with a display
  abbreviation map and a **total** `roleBadgeLabel()` (an unknown role derives initials or a
  four-glyph stem rather than being dropped; an empty `role: ""` falls back to the unroled label).
  `RoleHistogram` is **removed** from `mapParts.tsx` — the tile was its only caller — because at 140
  units across it encoded the same numbers in a channel nobody can read: a stack of coloured slivers
  says *mixed*, where `DC 2` says which roles and how many. The hue is kept, so a role still reads as
  the same colour everywhere; `Outline.tsx` still renders the model's full histogram.
- **The badge ruling, recorded with its cost (a departure from the slice text, corrected in §3 R3).**
  The order says badges are "up to three, **highest count first**", and its own acceptance says
  `10.80.20.0/24` carries a `DC` badge with count 2. **On the shipped data both cannot hold**: pure
  count ranking yields `EP 9 / WS 7 / NONE 5` and hides **both domain controllers** (and
  `dns-resolver` on the twin bundle) behind two buckets that are *absences of a fingerprint* rather
  than roles. R3 therefore **reserves slots for `INFRA_DEVICE_ROLES`** — deliberately the same set
  that earns a box in the infrastructure band, not a second vocabulary — then fills by count, with
  the fixed role order as the tie-break. **The reviewer ruled KEEP, measured:** the reservation
  changes the badge set on **4 of 39** pit segments and **3 of 32** corpapcn segments, and across
  both displaces a role that is *not* `generic-endpoint`/`unroled-endpoint` **exactly once**
  (`10.80.20.0/24` on corpapcn, where `corp-workstation 3` gives way to `DC 1` + `DNS 1`). Widening
  to four badges was **refused** on arithmetic: badge width is `(140 − 2·4)/3 = 44` units at 9 px,
  and a fourth drops it to 32, where labels overflow.
- **The projection discloses itself, in the accessible name too.** Because the badges are
  deliberately *not* a ranking, three badges must never read as the whole answer: the tile now says
  so twice from **one helper** (`badgeRowTitle`) so the two cannot drift — a `<title>` on the badge
  row ("3 roles shown of 5 on this segment — +2 more in the inspector") and ", +2 more roles"
  appended to the tile's `aria-label`. The full histogram remains the record in the outline tree and
  the inspector.
- **`PLANE_EPOCH` 1 → 2, and the rule it establishes for R6.** The INFRASTRUCTURE band's height is
  now **content-derived** (`TIER_INFRA_H` became a floor; a column whose devices overflow its width
  wraps, and the band grows to hold it rather than overlapping the tier beneath), which pushes the
  segment tier and every tile down. **No constant moved, and a hash over constants cannot see a rule
  that reads the data** — the same argument `sites.ts`'s bucketing and R6's budgets get — so the
  manual epoch is the only carrier. The cost is stated rather than hidden: no shipped bundle wraps,
  so for every bundle in the tree R2's plane and R3's plane are identical and the bump makes the app
  say "these pins may no longer line up" where in fact they do. That is the **safe** direction of a
  question one global token cannot answer per bundle (a false flag is a sentence the operator
  dismisses; a missing one is the silent misplacement the stamp exists to stop, LES-289), and blast
  radius is zero today — the only `layout.json` in the tree holds `"pins": {}`. **The rule for R6:
  any geometry that reads the data rather than only the constants bumps the epoch.**

**Why:**

- F3 is the last connector family on level 0 that crossed tiles by construction, and §1 property 2
  ("nothing crosses a tile") is one of the three properties this order is judged on. R2 made that
  property *measurable* (`noConnectorCrossesATile` on every level-0 golden); R3 is what brings the
  infrastructure band inside it — the tie-lines were deliberately absent from R2's helper because the
  **view** derived them and the layout published nothing to check (LES-304).
- A 156-unit tile cannot carry a legend, so its three badges are its answer to "what is this segment
  **for**" — and on a corporate twin the count-ranked answer is `generic-endpoint` on nearly every
  segment, which is the same answer everywhere and therefore no answer at all.

**Gates (no live-verify — nothing here deploys or touches a cluster):** Vitest **532 passed / 8
skipped** (from 475/8 at iteration 3); `npm run typecheck` clean from zero; `npm run build` exit 0;
`pytest -m "not slow"` **3094 passed / 12 skipped**, unchanged. **Ten revert proofs across the
slice, all RED.** Goldens regenerated **once** in the build round and **not at all** in the fix
round: `level0` 18 changed / 69 added / 7 removed, `level0-added-column` 18 / 77 / 7 — of the 18
changed, **15 are a 3-element permutation** (the device array is now column-major) and **3 are
`box.x`**; **no `y` moved anywhere** and nothing outside `infraDevices` changed. Three level-1
goldens gained `roleBadges` only; both matrices byte-identical. Screenshots were deliberately **not**
re-shot after the fix round, with the reason stated (nothing in that round can change the drawing);
the iteration-4 shots remain accurate. The standing `axrange serve` is still deliberately **not**
restarted (`docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-084).

**Honest limits recorded** (`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_level0_rendering.md` §8, an R3 block in R2's
idiom):

1. **A column pinned over another column drags its stubs through that column's tiles** — inherent to
   an absolute override with no reflow. The *evidence* matters more than the limit: the widened
   crossing assertion passed at the one sampled coordinate (`x=900`), which turned out to be a
   **lattice coincidence** — device pitch 168 against tile pitch 172 leaves an ~8-unit clean window
   that **slides** as the pin moves, and a sweep of `x ∈ [140,200]` at `y=620` violates at **13 of
   16** positions. Both a violating and a clean coordinate are pinned as tests (with the delete-me
   message) and the reason is in `infra.ts`'s docstring.
2. **Headroom is zero spare slots, not one.** `devicesAcross(524) = 3` and both real bundles' busiest
   column holds exactly 3 — the earlier prose claiming "four fit across" was **wrong**, and it was
   the claim that made the epoch bump sound harmless. Now pinned as a relation in
   `layout.real.test.ts` (`max(perColumn) === devicesAcross(columnW)`), so it fails the day a bundle
   grows a fourth device in one column — the same day the plane moves.
3. **Three device rows lose the stub clearance.** Two rows are guaranteed clear
   (`offset = PITCH/2 = 84 > DEVICE_W/2 = 75`, independent of column width); at three, rows 1 and 3
   share a parity. Carried as a measured limit with a delete-me assertion — it needs 5+ devices in
   one column against a real maximum of 3.
4. **An unplaced device no longer overlaps a placed one** — fixed *structurally* rather than
   asserted: `infraBandRows()` gives unplaced devices their own row below every column's, with
   placement top-aligned to one shared `gridTop`. Stated cost: a twin *with* an unplaced device draws
   its tiles one row lower. No shipped bundle has one, so no golden moved.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_level0_rendering.md` — status header; §2 **F3** fixed and **F5**
  partially; **§3 R3 marked BUILT and its badge wording CORRECTED** to reserved-then-by-count with
  the reason; §8's four new limits; new **§12 "R3 built as, 2026-09-14"**.
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` — status header (R3 BUILT); §15.2's tie-line note, which R3
  replaced with a published stub.
- `docs/design/15-console-canvas.md` — §3's level-0 row (role badges, not a histogram bar) and §4's
  "the infrastructure devices are tied to the segment that holds them", now true **positionally**.
- `o2/console/README.md` — R3 shipped; the layout module list gains `infra.ts`.
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — LES-302..LES-307.
- `CLAUDE.md` — the Console blockquote.
- `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` — iteration 4 of ten.

**Affected code:**

  `model/{graph,roles}.ts`, `views/{Level0,mapParts}.tsx`, `theme/shell.css`.
- `o2/console/test/r3.{infra,acceptance,ui}.test.*` (new), `test/layout/helpers.ts`,

## 2026-09-14 — Console level-0 R2 BUILT: the trunk — Internet → tap → WAN → one leg per site, width by north-south volume — and the plane stamp that keeps an absolute pin honest

**Authority:** **implementation (Claude Code)** for everything below — the trunk geometry, the
north-south aggregation and the inferred-WAN-edge rule, the shared magnitude scale and its
normalisation range, the plane stamp's shape and its write rule, and every honest limit named. **The
run's scope and order are the user's** (2026-09-11): build the whole Console — the level-0 order's
R1–R7, then `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §3 S5.1 (a–e), then S7 — as a ten-iteration run whose
**iteration 3 is R2**; level-0 D1–D3 take the order's v1 defaults and D5 is ruled.

**Version-locked: nothing moved.** No `IR_SCHEMA`/`PLANNER`/`OP_CATALOG`/`VALIDATOR`/`ADAPTER`/
`IMAGE_MANIFEST` change, no new op type, no catalog rule, no `scenario_schema_version` or state-store
bump. Op types **40**, catalog rules **114**. The pytest counter moves **3101 → 3112 collected**
(3100 passed / 12 skipped) — R2's only Python is the additive `plane` field on `axr-layout/1` and the
tests that pin it.

**What changed:**

- **The path exists (closes §2 F1).** A new pure module `o2/console/src/layout/trunk.ts` returns the
  connected geometry level 0 was missing: Internet pill → **spine** (carrying the tap badge) → WAN
  edge → a short **stem** → a horizontal **bus** → **one vertical leg per site group**. A leg ends at
  its group header's **nearest horizontal edge and enters no box** — normally the top edge, but a
  group a pin has dragged *above* the bus is approached from below and stops at its **bottom** edge,
  because ending at the top would run the leg through the header it points at. `layout/level0.ts`
  gains `trunk` and `tap`; the bus sits inside the existing WAN→infrastructure tier gap, so **the
  trunk adds no height and moves no tile**. `applyPins` re-runs the trunk over **moved** headers
  (the spine and the tap do not move — they are chrome, not content), so a dragged group's leg
  follows it for free, the same re-anchoring LES-266 established for lanes.
- **North-south volume, and an honest WAN edge.** `model/graph.ts` gains
  `GraphSegment.northSouth: {bytes, flows}` — summed over edges with **exactly one known-local end**
  — plus `Site.northSouth` (`layout/sites.ts`) and `ConsoleGraph.wanEdge` / `.vantage`. The WAN box
  is now keyed on `graph.wanEdge`, which exists when **either** a router is published (R5's
  `routers`) **or** crossing traffic is observed; an edge with no published router is
  `inferred: true` and is labelled ***egress (inferred from observed traffic)***, never "gateway".
  **`inferred` keys on whether a router was PUBLISHED, not on whether it has legs** — a cast
  declaring a legless `gw` has declared a gateway, and crediting the observation for it would be the
  wrong claim.
- **The tap badge (closes §2 F8).** `Level0Layout.tap` carries the export's own `vantage` and
  `windows`, drawn **verbatim beside** the badge rather than parsed into a shape: the two real
  bundles disagree (`axellio-hq/corp-apcn-rdr1:12` over 9 windows on the pit bundle,
  `https://10.80.65.7:0-11` over 11 on the golden), and a parser would have to guess which is the
  host and which the window count.
- **Magnitude is visible, and the scale's range was decided in this slice (R4 inherits it).** New
  pure `layout/scale.ts` holds `1 + 5 · normalised log10(bytes)` once. The decision: **normalise over
  the DRAWN set's own log range**, not against an absolute zero. Measured on the pit bundle —
  legs **6.00 / 5.38 / 1.00** where an absolute-zero scale gave 6.00 / 5.82 / 4.57; the twin bundle
  6.00 / 5.08 / 1.00; and **R4's future top-12 east-west pairs now span 2.06 px where they spanned
  0.66 px**, which is §1 property 3 failing across eleven of twelve arcs versus holding. A
  **zero-volume leg keeps `HAIRLINE` (0.5) as the CLAIM** while the view marks it `.trunk.is-silent`
  (dashed, 1 px), so "no traffic observed in this window" is visible rather than a quarter-pixel;
  the same treatment covers a declared-but-silent spine, stem and bus. Every group gets a leg — "no
  traffic was observed here" and "this group is not attached to the edge" are different statements,
  and only the first is ours to make.
- **`noConnectorCrossesATile` (§1 property 2) is now a computed assertion**, in
  `o2/console/test/layout/helpers.ts` and called by **every** level-0 golden from R2 on (R3's stubs
  and R4's arcs join it as they land). Liang–Barsky against the closed box plus a strict-interior
  midpoint test, so a connector that *ends* on an edge or runs *along* one is correctly not a
  crossing; it returns the **named offenders** rather than a boolean, so a failure reads
  "trunk leg site:10.80 crosses tile 10.80.72.0/24".
- **The plane stamp — R1's deferred decision, implemented (§8, LES-289).** New pure
  `layout/plane.ts` mints `plane: "l0-<epoch>-<fnv1a8>"`, hashed over **exactly the constants that
  position a PINNABLE element** (content origin, the tier stack above the tiles, the site/tile grid)
  plus a **manual epoch** for a plane change no constant expresses (R6's per-group budgets; and any
  change to `sites.ts`'s grouping or `compareCidr`'s ordering — a hash over numbers cannot see a
  change in a comparator). R2 itself moves no tile, so **R2 invalidates no pin and the stamp must not
  say it does**. `axrange/api/console_files.py` / `dtos.py` / `service.py` round-trip it as an
  **opaque, shape-checked token** (≤64 chars, `[A-Za-z0-9._:-]`) and never interpret it — the plane
  belongs to the code that draws it. **Absent stays absent** (a legacy file read and re-saved
  unchanged stays byte-identical; `plane: null` is never invented), and an absent stamp means
  *legacy*: the pins are accepted, flagged, and stamped on the next qualifying write.
- **The stamp's WRITE half, which took three rounds to get right.** A drag commits the *whole* pin
  set, so stamping "current" on every save re-certified N inherited pins off one gesture — the
  warning vanishes for ever while N−1 pins are still wrong, i.e. LES-289's own corruption re-entering
  through its own mechanism (review must-fix M1). Fixed by `planeForSave(pins, authored,
  currentPlane)`, one pure rule beside the status reader it must agree with: the stamp advances only
  when the set is **wholly** re-authored (a reset, vacuously, or every surviving pin re-placed).
  The final review round then found the session record was keyed by pin **name**, so a
  412 → reload → drag sequence could certify a concurrent writer's coordinates; re-authorship is now
  identified **by value** — the record says what *this session wrote*, and a key counts only while
  the pin's current coordinates still equal it. The record lives in `ImprintWorkspace` beside the
  document it describes (it previously died on every drill to level 1), and the on-screen note counts
  progress ("re-placed 1 of 2") using the same rule that clears it.

**The goldens, accounted for.** **Cumulative versus pre-R2: 0 leaves changed and 0 removed — every
difference is an addition.** `level0.golden.json` +80 leaves, `level0-added-column` +99 (`trunk`,
`tap`, `northSouth`), and **three level-1 goldens +2 each** because `layout1` embeds `GraphSegment`,
which now carries `northSouth` — a model field moves whichever goldens the layouts *embed* it in, not
the one layout you edited (LES-300). Both matrix goldens are byte-identical, and the final fix round
moved no golden at all.

**Why:**

- R2 is the slice that turns the grid into a network diagram: §1 makes "there is a path" and
  "magnitude is visible" two of the three properties level 0 is judged on, and R1's §8 limit 2
  (the INTERNET and WAN EDGE rails reading as empty on a narrow window) was explicitly deferred to
  the trunk that gives that chrome something to be part of.
- The scale's normalisation range had to be settled **here**, not in R4, because R4's arcs reuse the
  module verbatim; deciding it against only the trunk's three legs would have handed R4 a scale that
  fails on twelve arcs.
- The stamp had to land in the slice with the whole series in view (R2/R3/R4/R6 each move the plane
  again, and R6 changes which tiles exist), which is precisely the call R1 recorded rather than
  migrating four times.

**Gates (no live-verify — nothing here deploys or touches a cluster):** Vitest **475 passed / 8
skipped**; `npm run typecheck` clean from zero; `npm run build` exit 0; full `pytest -q` **3100
passed / 12 skipped (3112 collected)**. **7 revert proofs plus 3 more for the fix rounds**, each RED
and each naming the test it is meant to protect; the two-argument save seam is proven by a mutation
that fails exactly 1 of 475. The standing `axrange serve` is still deliberately **not** restarted
(`docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-084).

**Honest limits recorded** (`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_level0_rendering.md` §8): a site pinned
**across** the bus still makes the trunk cross tiles (inherent to an absolute override with no
reflow — measured clean at pin y=620/300 below the bus and y=138/24 above it, violating at y=100,
and pinned by a test that tells the next reader to delete it if it ever empties); the two-argument
save seam is **not compiler-enforceable** (TypeScript's parameter-arity rule accepts a one-argument
function at that prop, so the contract is visible in the types and guarded by a test); and stroke
width is **relative to the drawn set** (a pair at 3.06 in the top-12 view is 4.74 under `?ew=all`) —
recorded beside D2, where the top-K budget is chosen, so an operator never reads a width change as a
data change.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_level0_rendering.md` — status header; §2 F1/F2/F4/F8; §3 R2 marked BUILT;
  §5 D2's width-is-relative note; §8's three new limits; new **§11 "R2 built as, 2026-09-14"**.
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` — status header; **S1.6**'s `axr-layout/1` document shape now
  carries the optional `plane` stamp; §15.4's pins limits point at it.
- `docs/design/15-console-canvas.md` — §4's pins paragraph and the layout-document shape.
- `o2/console/README.md` — R2 shipped; the layout module list gains `trunk.ts` / `scale.ts` /
  `plane.ts`.
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — LES-296..LES-301.
- `CLAUDE.md` — the Console blockquote; the pytest counter 3101 → 3112.
- `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` — iteration 3 of ten.

**Affected code:**
- `o2/console/src/layout/{trunk,scale,plane}.ts` (new, pure), `layout/{level0,sites,pins,geometry}.ts`,
  `model/graph.ts`, `api/types.ts`, `views/{Level0,MapPane,mapParts,usePinDrag}.tsx`, `App.tsx`,
  `theme/shell.css`.
- `axrange/api/{console_files,dtos,service}.py` — the opaque `plane` round-trip.
- `o2/console/test/layout/helpers.ts` (new), `test/r2.{trunk,ui,acceptance}.test.*`, the golden and
  real-bundle suites, `tests/test_api_console.py`.

## 2026-09-12 — Console level-0 R1 BUILT: the map is drawn at 1 layout unit = 1 CSS px and the tiers are named — width, rails, and the filter taxonomy stated once

**Authority:** **implementation (Claude Code)** for everything below — the viewport module and its
0.8 floor, the rail geometry and its content-derived heights, the chrome collapse, the taxonomy
refactor, the plane-stamp decision scoped to R2, and every honest limit named. **The run's scope and
order are the user's** (2026-09-11): build the whole Console — this order's R1–R7, then
`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §3 S5.1 (a–e), then S7 — as a ten-iteration run whose
**iteration 2 is R1**; level-0 D1–D3 take the order's v1 defaults and D5 is ruled.

**Version-locked: nothing moved.** Frontend-only, entirely under `o2/console/`. No `IR_SCHEMA`/
`PLANNER`/`OP_CATALOG`/`VALIDATOR`/`ADAPTER`/`IMAGE_MANIFEST` change, no new op type, no catalog
rule, no `scenario_schema_version` or state-store bump — and **no Python touched**: `pytest -m "not
slow"` is byte-identical before and after (3083 passed / 12 skipped), and the policed counters
(3101 collected, 40 op types, 114 rules) do not move.

**What changed:**

- **Width — the map is drawn, not fitted (closes §2 F6, "illegible").** A new pure module
  `o2/console/src/layout/viewport.ts` states the rule **once**: `BASE_SCALE = 1` (one layout unit =
  one CSS px, the default, with the pane scrolling in both axes), `MIN_FIT_SCALE = 0.8` as the floor
  a *fit width* may never cross, and `fitScale` / `scaleFor` as the only arithmetic either mode uses.
  `theme/shell.css`'s `svg.level0` drops `max-width:100%; height:auto` and takes a **12 px** root
  font — which is what makes the floor a legibility guarantee (12 px × 0.8 = 9.6 px is the smallest
  type the map can ever draw); **level 1 and the matrix keep the old fit-to-pane rule**, deliberately.
  `views/Level0.tsx` measures the pane and sizes the SVG `width`/`height` = units × scale, publishing
  the result as `data-scale` while **the viewBox stays in pure layout units** — so the scale is a
  view concern and the coordinate goldens cannot move with the window (LES-268). A `1:1 | fit width`
  control carries a readout that states what is cut rather than leaving the scrollbar as the only
  tell: `· N px off-screen — scroll, or fit width`, and, when fit itself cannot show the whole plane,
  `· 80% floor — scroll for the rest`. The pre-R1 build fitted a ~2,800-unit plane into a ~700 px
  pane at ~25 %, rendering 11 px type at under 3 px.
- **Tier rails (closes §2 F7).** `layout/geometry.ts` gains `RAIL_W = 128` / `RAIL_GAP_X = 16`;
  `layout/tiers.ts` gains `TIER_LABELS` (INTERNET / WAN EDGE / INFRASTRUCTURE / SEGMENTS /
  EAST-WEST), beside `TIERS` because the ordered vocabulary and its words are one thing; and
  `layout/level0.ts` gains `Level0Layout.rails`, **built by mapping `TIERS`** so "in `TIERS` order"
  is structural rather than a convention a later edit can break. The content origin shifts right by
  `RAIL_W` (one fixed left margin, no relative geometry changed) and the top chrome now centres over
  the **content** area rather than the whole canvas, so the Internet pill is not pulled left onto the
  rails. **Rail heights are content-derived:** a cast with no router draws no WAN EDGE rail and one
  with no infrastructure devices draws no INFRASTRUCTURE rail, because a band's *reserved* height is
  not evidence that anything is in it — the same overclaim the two lower tiers already avoided.
  Rails are pre-pin extents: `applyPins` deliberately does not move them, since a rail that chased a
  dragged tile would stop describing its tier.
- **Chrome gives the drawing its pane back.** The summary strip (`CastNumbers` + `EvidenceMix`, an
  8-tile grid that cost the top half of the pane) collapses to a **one-line bar with a digest**,
  expanded by `?summary=1` **in the URL, not a store**, so it survives a reload and rides the one
  navigation path every drill already threads. The outline pane collapses too (`localStorage`, every
  access guarded — it is a per-viewer convenience about this window, not a description of what is
  being looked at, so a pasted link must not carry it), and is **forced open at the catalog level**,
  where it is the only navigation — without touching the stored preference, and with the toggle
  hidden rather than left as a control that visibly does nothing.
- **One filter taxonomy, stated once.** `model/zoom.ts` now names the three categories in one place
  — **dimming filters** (`ghosts` / `class` / `occupancy_lt`), the **emphasis flag** (`changed`), and
  **view flags** (`summary`) — and publishes `anyDimmingFilter` + `clearFilters`; `model/graph.ts`
  and `views/Filters.tsx` derive from them instead of restating membership at three sites. This is
  what keeps the new `summary` flag from dimming a tile, emptying itself on *clear*, or resetting the
  operator's open drawer: the selection reset is keyed on the **scope-bearing** part of the URL only.
  `parseFilters`'s three boolean readers collapse into one `readFlag`.
- **Also:** an SVG `<desc>` naming the drawn tiers for assistive tech (the rail groups themselves are
  `aria-hidden`, or forty tiles' worth of labels would be read in document order); and a literal
  **NUL byte** in `model/graph.ts` — a map-key separator committed long before this slice — rewritten
  as an escape, because `file` classified the module as binary data and GNU grep had been silently
  skipping it (LES-295).

**The goldens, accounted for.** Regenerated **once**, in this slice, and only two of the seven:
`level0.golden.json` and `level0-added-column.golden.json`. Both trees were machine-walked rather
than eyeballed — **31 and 35 changed leaves, every one of them an `x` or a `width` and every one
exactly +128 (`RAIL_W`); no `y` changed anywhere; `rails` is the only new key.** The other five
goldens are byte-identical. That walk is the evidence that the rail gutter is a pure translation of
the content plane and not a relayout.

**Why:**

- R1 is the half-day that fixes "I can't read it", and it precedes R2–R4 because every later slice
  draws into the geometry it establishes. The order's own §1 makes legibility and the tier rails two
  of the three properties level 0 is judged on.
- The floor is what turns the worst case into a scroll gesture instead of an unreadable page, and
  putting it in one pure module is what stops "fit means this, actual means that" being open-coded in
  a view.
- A view flag that rides the `Filters` type is enrolled in every consumer that reads that type as a
  whole; deriving the taxonomy once is the only way the next flag joins the right set (LES-288).

**Honest limits, recorded rather than papered over** (`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_level0_rendering.md`
§8):

1. **A pin stored as absolute coordinates silently re-points by the +128 shift.** Blast radius today
   is zero — the only `layout.json` in the tree holds `"pins": {}` — so R1 accepts and publishes it
   rather than migrating. **Decision, scoped to iteration 3: R2 adds a plane/epoch stamp to
   `axr-layout/1`** so a client can offset or refuse stale pins, because R2, R3, R4 and R6 each move
   the plane again and R6 changes which tiles exist (LES-289).
2. **At 1246–1600 px the INTERNET and WAN EDGE rails read as empty**, because the centred chrome sits
   at layout x≈1141 on a 2374-unit plane and needs panning. Deferred deliberately: R2's trunk is what
   makes that chrome read as a path and R6's per-group budgets are what narrow the plane, so
   re-anchoring now would reverse the ratified anchoring decision (LES-261), churn the goldens twice
   and look wrong once the trunk exists. Scrolling to content on first paint was considered and
   **refused** — it would scroll away the rails this slice delivers. The cheap honest mitigation
   shipped instead: the off-screen readout.
3. The `atFloor`/`belowFloor` knife-edge (a plane fitting at exactly 0.8) is not separately
   revert-provable with the shipped fixture; the change is strictly more correct and the surrounding
   readout is pinned.

**Gates (no live-verify — nothing here deploys or touches a cluster).** Vitest **398 passed / 8
skipped** (from 325/8 at iteration 1: R1 added tests, the bounded re-test added 34, the fix round 12);
`npm run typecheck` clean from zero; `npm run build` exit 0; `pytest -m "not slow"` 3083 passed /
12 skipped / 0 failed, byte-identical before and after. **Ten revert proofs red.** `doc-stats.py
--check` clean.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_level0_rendering.md` — status header, §2 F6/F7, §3 R1 marked BUILT, §8
  (three new honest limits), new **§10** ("R1 built as")
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` — status header; §15.3's `max-width:100%` premise dated to S4
- `o2/console/README.md` — status paragraph, the `src/layout/` tree (`viewport.ts`)
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — LES-288..LES-295; LES-253's `Scope` extended in place
- `CLAUDE.md` — the Console blockquote
- `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` — the 2026-09-12 section

**Affected code:** `o2/console/src/layout/viewport.ts` (new), `layout/{geometry,tiers,level0}.ts`,
`model/{zoom,graph}.ts`, `views/{Level0,MapPane,Filters}.tsx`, `App.tsx`, `theme/shell.css`; tests
`test/{r1.acceptance.test.tsx,r1.goldens.test.ts,viewport.test.ts}` (new) and
`test/{layout.golden,model,shell,views,zoom}.test.ts(x)`; goldens `test/layout/level0.golden.json`,
`test/layout/level0-added-column.golden.json`.

## 2026-09-11 — Console level-0 R5 + R7 BUILT: the cast publishes its conduit roster and routers, and ONE addressing id per bundle — the refusal lifted exposed a plan-build bug that had made "deploy as twin" dead on every real cast bundle

**Authority:** **user** for the run's scope and order (build the whole Console: level-0 R1–R7, then
`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §3 S5.1 a–e, then S7; level-0 D1–D3 take the order's v1 defaults),
for **D3** ("tolerate on read" — the GAP-082 rule) and for **D5** (retire the Grafana plugin's
Imprints screen at S7, keeping "Deploy as twin" as a slim plugin action). **implementation (Claude
Code)** for everything else below: the field names, the id classification, the shared path helper, the
number rule, the cache and its key, and every honest limit named.

**Version-locked: nothing moved.** No `IR_SCHEMA`/`PLANNER`/`OP_CATALOG`/`VALIDATOR`/`ADAPTER`/
`IMAGE_MANIFEST` change, no new op type, no rule, no `scenario_schema_version` or state-store
`SCHEMA_VERSION` bump. Two **additive** DTO fields on an existing route, one new leaf module
(`axrange/jsonnum.py`), and a shared helper in `axrange/twinconfig/registry.py`.

**What changed:**

- **R5 — the cast publishes its conduits and routers (closes `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-079).**
  `CastSummaryDTO` gains **`conduit_roster: [{from, to}]`** and **`routers: [{name, segments}]`**,
  both defaulting to absent. **Two deliberate name changes from the work order** (implementation
  calls): the roster is `conduit_roster`, **not** the order's `conduits`, because that name is already
  the shipped **count** and reusing it would break a published field; and the router legs are one
  entry **per expanded router**, named as deployed (`gw01`, `gw02`) from the expander's own ids,
  **not** a flat `router_segments` — a twin with two gateways has two leg sets and a flat list cannot
  say which is which. The SPA mirrors both (`o2/console/src/api/types.ts`, LES-258), deletes the
  hard-coded empty `LIVE_CONDUITS`, and keeps *absent* distinct from *empty* (`graph.castOmits`) so
  the UI says "this server didn't publish the roster" rather than "the twin declares none". The WAN
  label reads `gateway · N leg(s) · SNAT` for exactly one router (N counting only legs on **drawn**
  tiles), `gateway · SNAT` when none is drawn, and `K routers · legs on M segments` for several.
- **R7 — one addressing id, one guard, on every route (closes GAP-082).** The canonical id (directory
  name = `twin_config.id` = URL id) is **the** addressing id; every other id-shaped field is
  classified by one question — *may a legitimate producer write a different value?* — and the answer
  decides refuse vs warn (the classification lives in `axrange/api/imprints.py`'s module docstring,
  field by field; LES-278 generalizes it). A **normalized** sidecar's `imprint_id` still **refuses**
  when it names a different imprint (LES-195's copy-paste protection) and **warns** when missing; a
  canonical sidecar declares none; the topology export's baked `imprint_id` and any `pit_id` are
  published and warned about, **never compared**. One guard (`check_imprint_addressable`) now runs on
  **every** imprint route — `/cast`, `/provenance`, `/topology`, `/assertions`, `/layout`, apply and
  diff — so a bundle one route refuses, every route refuses.
- **Two corrections to the record.** GAP-082's refusal was a **400, not a 500** (the level-0 order's
  F9 says 500), and it hit **every** cast bundle, not only one with a differing baked id — because the
  *normalized* sidecar's id check was being applied to *canonical* sidecars, which declare no imprint
  id at all (LES-281: detect the shape, then enforce that shape's precondition).
- **The headline bug (review must-fix M2), found because R7 lifted the refusal in front of it.** The
  API resolved a twin config's relative `scripts/…` paths against the **materialized tempfile's**
  directory, so plan build 400'd on **every real cast bundle** and "deploy as twin" was dead on real
  content; the ordinary `config:` API path had the same bug. The CLI had fixed it locally long ago
  (LES-037's class). Now one shared `twin_config_source_dir()` / `resolve_twin_config_path()` in
  `axrange/twinconfig/registry.py`, used by the CLI and **both** API resolvers. Real-content proof,
  pinned in `tests/test_api_console.py`: plan previews of **695 / 1077 / 1227** ops
  (`corpapcn-golden` / `corpapcn-twin` / `pit-395a61f04e-1780405200`) and **72** for
  `config: svc-verify`.
- **Robustness.** New `axrange/jsonnum.py` — **one** bounded-number rule for anything read from a
  bundle artifact and republished (whole numbers `0 .. 2**53 - 1`, finite floats, **refusal naming the
  field, never clamping**), replacing three drifting copies. Sidecar preconditions are **shape-first**,
  with a new `unrecognized` shape meaning *present but not consumed* (distinct from `absent`).
  Mistyped nested export values answer **400, not 500**. One confidence classifier serves both the
  rows and the mean (a non-numeric confidence is **absent and counted**, which retires a typed 400 on
  `/provenance`). The materialized IR tempfile is deleted on the plan and deploy paths.
- **Performance.** The new guard had made every route parse the twin config, and that parse is
  **214.7 ms** on the pit bundle — paid on every pin drop. A stat-keyed parse cache at the one loader
  (key: resolved path + inode + size + mtime + ctime; **stat before read**; failed parses never
  cached; bounded and locked, since sync routes run on a threadpool) leaves the guard at **4.48 ms**
  (~48× cheaper than the parse it replaces), `GET /layout` at **9.2 ms** (from 223.5), `GET
  /assertions` at **9.6 ms** (from 244.2), a Console open at **637 ms / 0 parses** (from 2522 ms / 8)
  and a plan preview at **1737 ms / 1 parse** (from 2234 / 3). The cold first parse per bundle per
  process is unchanged — that is stated, not averaged away.
- **The cache hands out a per-caller DEEP COPY, on the hit path as well as the miss.** `frozen=True`
  was **not available**: `axrange/twinconfig/expand.py:677` mutates a twin-config model in place
  during IP allocation (`leg.ip = allocs[leg.segment].next()`), so freezing would refuse a working
  path — and `frozen` blocks attribute assignment **only**, so it would not have stopped
  `tc.nodes.pop()` or a nested model's field being rewritten, both of which the new test exercises.
  The copy costs **4.3 ms** on the pit bundle (109 nodes) and **1.6 ms** on `corpapcn-twin` (62
  nodes), against ~210 ms and ~65 ms to parse — ~2 % of the parse, buying isolation between callers
  (LES-284, LES-287).
- **Six further fidelity-reader fields typed**, each refusal naming the field: `segments[i].entities`
  (a bounded whole number), `segments[i].coverage_source`, `nodes[i].role`,
  `nodes[i].role_evidence_class`, `nodes[i].segment_evidence_class`, `nodes[i].locality`. **`role`
  was the live hazard** — it is a dict key in `axrange/fidelity/rows.py`'s `_group_key`, so a list or
  dict `role` was the same unhashable-key 500 already fixed for `segment`/`cidr`. Consumers were
  checked: these flow into DTOs, dicts and sets that already tolerate `None`, so only a wrongly typed
  value refuses, and it refuses earlier (LES-285). A module-docstring **carve-out** in
  `axrange/api/imprints.py` records the paired decision — a canonical row's non-finite `confidence` is
  ignored, counted and reported by the one rule both readers share — ending with "Do not restore that
  400."

**Verification.** **pytest 3089 passed / 12 skipped / 0 failed (3101 collected)**; **Vitest 325
passed / 8 skipped (333)**; `npm run typecheck` clean from zero; `npm run build` exit 0; the seven
layout coordinate goldens **byte-identical** (neither slice is layout). **15 revert proofs** were run
across the three fix rounds. No live-verify: nothing here deploys or touches a cluster — and the
standing `axrange serve` deliberately was **not** restarted (see GAP-084; that decision is the
orchestrator's).

**Why:**

- GAP-079 left the Console's east-west band empty against a real bundle: the layout was computed and
  golden-proven, only the wire was short a field. R5 is that field, and it unblocks R4's arcs.
- GAP-082 made the summary route refuse **every** real cast bundle, which took "deploy as twin" with
  it (its pre-check runs the summary). R7 was scoped as "unblocking only" and behaved exactly as
  LES-276 now warns: relaxing a refusal exposed what had been sitting behind it, and the newly
  reachable path failed on real content the suite had never driven.
- The robustness and performance work is not gold-plating: a guard on every route inherits the hottest
  route's call rate (LES-282), and a number republished to a browser must satisfy the browser's range
  or the Console draws every host as a ghost (LES-279/LES-280).

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_level0_rendering.md` — status header; §2 F9 (400, not 500; every bundle);
  R5 (real field names, BUILT); R7 (BUILT under D3); §5 D1–D3 taken; new §9 "R5 + R7 built as"
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` — §8.1 (D5 **ruled**; D3(a) superseded); status blockquote
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — GAP-079 ✅, GAP-082 ✅ (+ the 400-not-500 correction); **GAP-083..GAP-092**
  new
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — **LES-275..LES-287**
- `o2/console/README.md` — the "One live-app gap" paragraph is retired
- `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/SESSION_STATE.md`

**Affected code:** `axrange/api/imprints.py`, `axrange/api/dtos.py`, `axrange/api/service.py`,
`axrange/api/topology_export.py`, `axrange/twinconfig/registry.py`, `axrange/fidelity/reference.py`,
`axrange/cli/main.py`, **new** `axrange/jsonnum.py`; `o2/console/src/{api/types.ts,model/graph.ts,
layout/level0.ts,views/{useGraph.ts,Level0.tsx,MapPane.tsx}}`; tests in `tests/test_api.py`,
`tests/test_api_console.py`, `tests/test_imprints_reader.py`, `tests/test_twinconfig_registry.py`,
**new** `tests/test_jsonnum.py`, `tests/test_imprint_routes_adversarial.py`, and
`o2/console/test/castinput*.test.tsx`.

**Supersedes:** `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §8.1's implementation call **D3(a)** — "`GET
/{id}` stays strict". The summary route no longer refuses a bundle over a producer-baked id; it
refuses only what the addressing rule owns, and warns about the rest. The tolerant-listing half of
D3(a) stands unchanged.

**Links:** `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_level0_rendering.md` §5 (D1–D3), §9 (built as);
`axrange/api/imprints.py` module docstring (the per-field id classification);
`axrange/jsonnum.py` module docstring (the number rule).

## 2026-09-11 — Console S6 acceptance CLOSED: a real-compiler apply through the SPA's own HTTP path writes the authored key into the new sidecar

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code) — for running the proof and for the six findings it
produced. The acceptance item itself is `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §13.2/§17.5 (Authority:
user, via design note 14 §3's determinism rule).

**What changed:**

- **`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §17.5 is CLOSED.** The residual carried since S1 — *nothing
  in the suite proves the **real** compiler writes an authored key into the **new** sidecar on an
  apply; the first live apply through the SPA is that proof* — is **proven**. A real-compiler apply,
  driven through the same HTTP calls the SPA client makes, PASSED all five checks on
  `twin-configs-cast/corpapcn-golden` (suppressing `address:10.80.34.10`): the new sidecar carries the
  key in `population.suppressed` with **`matched: true`**; `GET …/provenance` agrees; the diff's
  `suppressed_keys` lists it; the twin config no longer places the host (**node_specs 88 → 87**); and
  a second identical apply is **byte-identical** (`1de327650032c7ce`). Apply took **2.6–2.8 s**.
  Evidence: `/tmp/claude-1000/-home-rocky-axRange/99fbb50e-2bf2-481b-a118-41544f6745ac/scratchpad/apply-proof/evidence/summary.json`.
- **Six findings registered.** Four are axRange's and are scheduled as **iteration 11** of this run:
  **GAP-087** (a compiler upgrade rides along silently on the first apply — aximprint HEAD had renamed
  11 node groups, so the recompile differed by **22 hosts added / 23 removed** while the Console
  reported "1 element changed", because it joins on observed identity; on a **deployed** twin those
  renames would replace VMs on `te deploy --update`), **GAP-088** (the apply response's
  `code_fingerprint_before/after` is copied from the PIT row and did **not** move, while the real build
  hash — `cast_invocation.code_fingerprint` in the topology export — did; the `dtos.py` /
  `recompile.py` docstrings calling it "the compiler's build hash" contradict the data), **GAP-089**
  (`app.py`'s `since` parameter description names a value that 404s — the value that works is
  `fingerprint_before`), and **GAP-090** (the server's no-op warning is an ambiguous "either … or …";
  the SPA's own branch is fine). Two are aximprint's, recorded here because axRange is the consumer
  that hits them: **GAP-091** (`install-cast` never deletes stale files, so superseded scripts and
  personas accumulate in a bundle — and would be committed with a tracked one) and **GAP-092** (the
  sidecar records the assertions file's **absolute** path, so two checkouts applying identical
  assertions produce different bytes and fingerprints).
- **Live-environment fact recorded:** `aximprint cast` opens the IR store **read-write** (a duckdb
  connect plus `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`), so the proof ran against a **scratch copy** of the
  store rather than the real one.

**Why:**

- §13.2's settlement (a suppressed-key **set delta**, a per-removal `authored_key` column refused) was
  argued from the producer's source, and the tests doctor the sidecar at the correct seam — but
  neither is the compiler actually doing it. This was the one claim in the S5/S6 loop that only a real
  recompile could settle, and it is the claim the whole edit→apply→refresh loop rests on.
- It is also the first time the apply path ran against a **tracked, shipped** bundle rather than a
  fixture, which is what surfaced GAP-087/GAP-091/GAP-092 — none of them visible from a green suite.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` — §17.5 closed (proof, numbers, findings); status blockquote
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — GAP-087..GAP-092 new
- `o2/console/README.md` — the outstanding-proof sentence removed
- `docs/SESSION_STATE.md`

**Affected code:** none — a verification run, not a code change.

**Links:** `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §13.2 (the settlement being proven), §17.5;
`docs/design/14-operator-assertions.md` §3 (the determinism rule).

## 2026-09-11 — Console S5.1 (post-build amendment to the built S5): selection-driven editing, clone-and-drag a segment with its hosts, host generation on a new segment

**Authority:** user

**What changed:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §3 gains **S5.1** (a–e), OPEN, over the S5 that shipped
  2026-09-10 (§16) — its record layer, reducer, states, change list and save path stand; the
  inspector's edit actions follow the selected tile / spine / chip; the `EditPanel`'s context-free
  "Add a segment" / "Add a conduit" forms are removed; "add a segment" moves to a site-header `+` prefilled with the next free
  `/24`; **Alt-drag a tile (or *Clone this segment…* + placement) clones it** — one `add: segment`
  plus one `add: host` per chosen host with the address re-based to the new CIDR (same host part,
  or renumbered), role/listen/dial copied, conduits optionally mirrored, one note propagated to
  every record; the add-segment form can **generate N hosts** of a role with a catalog-default
  listen set; pending gestures render hatched on the map and appear in the change list as one
  gesture expanding to its records, with whole-gesture undo.
- `docs/design/15-console-canvas.md` §8: two gesture rows added; principle 5 refined — a gesture
  may emit a **batch** of the ruled record kinds, never a new kind.

**Why:**
- S5 as built is confusing in use (user, on the live canvas): forms unrelated to the selection,
  add-host one at a time from the inspector only, no pending state on the level-0 map, drag meaning
  only "pin". The operator's actual task — a new segment with hosts behind it —
  had no path. The assertions vocabulary (`axr-assertions/1`) already expresses a clone as
  `add` records, so this is an interaction model, not a schema change; hostname-keyed hosts are
  refused as clone sources because a hostname names one machine, and nothing observed is copied as
  evidence — every cloned record is `asserted` with the operator's note.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` — §3 S5.1 (new), status blockquote
- `docs/design/15-console-canvas.md` — §8 amendment + two table rows

**Supersedes:** nothing ruled; refines `15` §2 principle 5 ("every gesture maps to one record kind")
to "one gesture, one batch of ruled kinds".

**Links:** `docs/design/14-operator-assertions.md` §3 (record shapes),
`axrange/api/schemas/axr-assertions-1.schema.json`.

## 2026-09-11 — Console level 0 re-scoped: the canvas must read as a network — trunk, rails, arcs (not lanes), width; seven slices commissioned after S6

**Authority:** user

**What changed:**
- New `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_level0_rendering.md` (OPEN, R1–R7), a post-build follow-on to S3/S4 of
  `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md`, commissioned after S6 and beside S7. R1 width + tier rails; R2 the trunk (Internet → tap badge →
  inferred WAN edge → one leg per site group, width by north-south bytes); R3 infra devices over
  their group with orthogonal stubs + role badges on tiles; R4 the east-west band as **volume-
  weighted arcs from the group's bottom edge**, top-K on the map, full set in the matrix, asserted
  conduits as distinct edit-colour arcs; R5 closes GAP-079 (additive `conduits` + `router_segments`
  on `CastSummaryDTO`); R6 tile content, ranking and per-group budgets; R7 the new GAP-082.
- **Ruling: arcs, not orthogonal lanes** for the east-west band — supersedes the 2026-09-04 clause of
  `docs/design/15-console-canvas.md` §4/§5 ("every conduit runs as an orthogonal lane … one channel
  each"). `15` §4 carries the amendment; `o2/console/src/layout/lanes.ts` is retired in R4.
- New `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` **GAP-082** (the summary route refuses a cast bundle whose export bakes
  a different `imprint_id`); GAP-079 cross-referenced to R5.

**Why:**
- A review of the live canvas against the corp-apcn report at `:8088` found the design's tiered
  *path* diagram never made it into the build: no trunk or WAN box (the live `CastInput` carries no
  router legs), no rails, tie-lines drawn diagonally across tiles, lanes that would drop through
  tiles once conduits arrived, uniform tiles that hide magnitude, and a ~60 % render scale that makes
  11 px type illegible. The topology export already carries everything the report draws (4,157
  edges with bytes/flows/proto and segment membership), so these are renderer problems.
- Orthogonal one-channel-per-conduit lanes cannot scale past a handful of pairs (a 440 px band at
  40 pairs) and cannot avoid crossing tiles when the endpoint sits inside a multi-row grid; the
  decision was taken on a side-by-side of three routings drawn on one fixture (that order's §4.1).
- "Better than the report" = the report's path-and-volume reading **plus** the evidence channel,
  the stable layout and the editable drill-down the Console already has — none of which is visible on
  a grid with no path.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_level0_rendering.md` — new
- `docs/design/15-console-canvas.md` — status note; §4 amendment; §5 marker; §12 ruling
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` — status blockquote
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — GAP-079 scheduled note; GAP-082 new

**Supersedes:** the orthogonal-lane clause of the 2026-09-04 Console design ruling (`15` §4/§5);
`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §14.5's "starts drawing lanes with no further UI change" plan.

**Links:** `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_level0_rendering.md` §2 (findings), §4.1 (the ruling), §5 (D1–D3).

## 2026-09-10 — Console S6 BUILT: apply and refresh close the loop on the operator canvas — one review must-fix restores an honest "can't tell" outcome

**Authority:** **implementation (Claude Code)** — for the build, the must-fix and the two
should-fixes below, and the kept-vs-deferred calls. The design this implements is **Authority: user**:
`docs/design/15-console-canvas.md` §8 (apply = save + recompile + refresh; the five-second highlight)
and `docs/design/14-operator-assertions.md` §3–§4 (the determinism rule), with
`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §3's S6 acceptance and §13.2's residual.

**Version-locked: nothing moved.** No `IR_SCHEMA`/`PLANNER`/`OP_CATALOG`/`VALIDATOR`/`ADAPTER`/
`IMAGE_MANIFEST` change, no new op type (**40**), no rule (**114**), no `scenario_schema_version` or
state-store `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump. The change is **entirely frontend** (`o2/console/` only) — the
pytest suite is untouched (**2705** collected). Vitest **288 passed / 6 skipped** (was 275/6 at S5).

**What changed:**

- **Apply is wired over the real S1 `POST /api/imprints/{id}/apply` endpoint** — the aximprint gate
  was lifted at S1 (§12.8); S6 is the SPA's consumer of a built endpoint, not a stub. Clicking Apply
  **saves any pending edits first** (the same `PUT …/assertions` gate Save uses — apply *is* save +
  recompile, `15` §8), POSTs a full recompile, and resolves one of three outcomes:
  - a **compile failure** surfaces the compiler's OWN stderr verbatim, never a generic error;
  - a **byte-identical recompile** ("no change") shows a determinism note with **no highlight** — the
    check that catches a compiler that is not actually deterministic (`14` §3);
  - a **real change** reloads the cast/provenance/assertions, diffs against the pre-apply
    `code_fingerprint`, and **glows the touched map elements for five seconds** while a `?changed=1`
    filter keeps them reachable once the glow fades (`15` §12's O5 default).
- **The change-set reduction is a new pure module, `src/model/changes.ts`**, joining the diff back to
  drawn map elements on **OBSERVED identity** — observed CIDRs, observed node ids, and authored
  **suppressed keys** (from `ImprintDiffDTO.suppressed_keys`, §13.2) — never the twin-side ids the
  diff also carries, which name no element the map ever draws.
- **Built on the pre-existing `useApply`/`getDiff` client** (S1/S2); `src/edit/store.ts` and
  `src/layout/` are untouched, and the seven layout coordinate goldens are **byte-identical** (apply
  is not layout).

**One review must-fix and two should-fixes, all FIXED with revert-proven tests:**

- **(must-fix) An apply that reports `changed: true` but whose diff cannot be computed now renders an
  HONEST "the post-apply diff couldn't be computed" outcome, distinct from "nothing on this map
  moved."** Two producer signals say "changed, but I can't tell you what changed": `history_written:
  false` (carried in the apply response's own `warnings`) and a `getDiff` call that itself fails on
  the follow-up read. The pre-fix UI read only the happy-path field and collapsed both into the
  **same silent no-highlight render** as a genuine no-op apply — the same don't-know-vs-no collapse
  LES-240 (the wire contract) and LES-253 (the render contract) already named, surfacing here a third
  time, at the point an already-fetched result becomes a highlight-or-not decision. Fixed the same
  way: read the qualifying field explicitly and render the can't-compare state, carrying the server's
  own `warnings`.
- **(should-fix) A stale apply's continuation no longer force-navigates the operator.** A `liveRef`/
  `pathnameRef` **still-current** guard now gates **only** the trailing `go(...)` navigation call in
  the apply continuation; the state refresh (cast/provenance/assertions/diff) still lands wherever the
  operator has since navigated, rather than the whole continuation short-circuiting or yanking them
  back to the imprint they applied from.
- **(should-fix) `changes.ts`'s join predicate (`changeSetSize`/`isNodeChanged`/`isSegmentChanged`) is
  now reused at its five call sites** instead of inlined `.has()`/`.size` checks — making the module's
  exports live code rather than a second, silently-drifting copy, and clearing the same dead-export
  class the tester flagged on `twinChangeCount` (removed during the interrupted build below rather
  than left as an orphan).

**Process note, worth recording on its own.** This slice's implementation was interrupted mid-build
by an **Opus session rate-limit**; the tester, reviewer, fix and doc stages that finished it ran on
**Sonnet** as the fallback (per this project's working agreement: implementation work runs on Opus
subagents, with Sonnet as the fallback when Opus is unavailable). A `/dev` pipeline run can span a
model-tier boundary mid-slice without losing the thread, provided every remaining stage still gets a
full pass — which this one did: **288 passed / 6 skipped**, one must-fix and two should-fixes found
and fixed, each revert-proven, goldens byte-identical.

**Honest limit — carried forward, not closed.** `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §13.2's
residual — proof that the **real** compiler writes an authored key into the **new** sidecar on an
apply — is **still open**: it is a real local side effect (a genuine recompile against the live
bundle), so it is tracked as a standing S6 acceptance item and run separately from this doc-only
pass, not folded into the frontend build's own gate. **S7 (the Grafana embed) is the last slice**, and
is next.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/CHANGELOG.md` — this entry.
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` — STATUS header flipped to "S6 BUILT 2026-09-10 (§17); S7
  OPEN"; new §17 "S6 built as"; §11 sequence updated (S7 is next, deliberately last).
- `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` — new 2026-09-10 Console S6 section; next step = S7 + the §13.2 live-apply
  proof.
- `docs/design/15-console-canvas.md` — built-status touch (S6 built); §13 progress.
- `o2/console/README.md` — Status line + "what it does not yet do" now say apply is live.
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — LES-273 (a "could not compare" signal must reach the outcome-rendering branch,
  or "don't know" collapses into "no"), LES-274 (an async write-flow's stale-continuation guard must
  gate only its side-effecting tail call, not the whole continuation).

**Links:** LES-273..LES-274. Same family as LES-240/LES-253 (don't-know vs no) and LES-251 (a
refusal's etag is for a reload, never adopted).

## 2026-09-10 — Console S5 BUILT: editing and save are live on the operator canvas — seven gestures, one record each, three visible states

**Authority:** **implementation (Claude Code)** — for the build, the two review should-fixes and the
N1 fix below, and the kept-vs-deferred calls. The design this implements is **Authority: user**:
`docs/design/15-console-canvas.md` §8 (the gesture → record table, the three visible states, save vs
apply) and `docs/design/14-operator-assertions.md` §3 (the `axr-assertions/1` record kinds and their
closed sets), with `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §3's S5 acceptance.

**Version-locked: nothing moved.** No `IR_SCHEMA`/`PLANNER`/`OP_CATALOG`/`VALIDATOR`/`ADAPTER`/
`IMAGE_MANIFEST` change, no new op type (**40**), no rule (**114**), no `scenario_schema_version` or
state-store `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump. The change is **entirely frontend** (`o2/console/` only) — the
pytest suite is untouched (**2705** collected), and the seven layout coordinate goldens are
**byte-identical** (editing is not layout; the S2 reducer and `src/layout/` are untouched). Vitest
**275 passed / 6 skipped** (was 219/6 at S4).

**What changed:**

- **Seven edit gestures, each producing EXACTLY ONE `axr-assertions/1` record** — add-segment,
  add-host (role + listen set), override-role, suppress-host, override-image, override-sizing
  (`resources`), add-conduit — mapping to the `add` / `override` / `suppress` kinds of `14` §3
  (`15` §8's table); the inspector's remove-assertion deletes a record. The pure heart is
  `o2/console/src/edit/records.ts` (`buildRecord`, `deployConsequence`, `crossRecordConflict`,
  `strictCidr`, `metadataReady`), whose client pre-flight **mirrors the server's
  `axrange/api/console_files.py` refusals rule-for-rule**: a non-empty `note` required on all three
  kinds, the closed `override.field` set, `suppress` host-keys-only, stable entity keys, the two
  cross-record conflicts (a key in both `add` and `suppress`; a duplicate authored key), and the
  canonical conduit-key half-ordering.
- **Three visible states (`15` §8), driven through S2's untouched pure reducer:** *pending* (browser
  only, hatched), *saved* (written to the YAML, outlined), *applied* (present in the cast, rendered
  `asserted`).
- **A change-list panel** tags each entry with its deploy consequence **derived from the record kind
  alone** — `add`/`suppress` → `te deploy --update`, an `override` of an existing node → a fresh
  deploy (`docs/WORK_ORDER_twin_incremental_update.md` §3.1) — which is what the bundle supports,
  since it publishes no map from an authored key to the cast node spec it landed in.
- **Save = `PUT …/assertions`** (a merge, the file authoritative), keyed on the **canonical** imprint
  id, with the stale-etag **412** surfacing a reload prompt and **never adopting the server etag**
  (LES-251) — the pending edit stays intact and a reload must happen before the next save.
- **Apply is deliberately NOT built — that is S6.** The `POST …/apply` endpoint already exists and is
  proven against the real compiler (S1.5, §12.8); S6 is the SPA apply + refresh slice over it. A
  clean seam remains — the change-list already tags consequences and the store already tracks
  pending → saved → applied.

**The route reconciliation (a supersession to record).** `docs/design/14-operator-assertions.md`
§3's normative rule (line 132, refined 2026-09-04) **refuses a `route` record by name** — a twin
config has no route object, and conduits are how reachability is authored. §3's earlier "first cut"
edit-set prose (lines 190, 267) still read **"conduits and routes,"** the pre-refinement list. The S5
build follows the refined rule: there is **no route gesture**, and the "seven gestures" are the
no-route set above. Note 14's two stale prose mentions are corrected to "conduits" in this pass;
`CLAUDE.md`'s golden-twin blockquote "conduits/routes" is left as a dated historical record (the
"record, don't rewrite history" discipline), superseded in direction here.

**Two review should-fixes and one nit (N1), each fixed with a revert-proven test:**

- **(S1) Removing a saved row no longer commits unsaved pending edits.** The removal document is now
  built from **saved + applied** entries only, holding the "pending = browser only" boundary
  (`15` §8) — a "collect every entry" serializer (`documentFrom`) had turned a **scoped** write (a
  removal) into a **whole-store** commit (LES-272).
- **(S2) The remove-that-re-saves path now shares Save's `source`/`as_of` metadata gate** (a shared
  `metadataReady` predicate) instead of PUTting metadata the server refuses.
- **(N1) `strictCidr` rejects leading-zero IPv4 octets inline** (matching the server's
  `ip_network(strict=True)`), instead of letting the value through to a delayed server 400.

**Honest limits (recorded, not defects):**

- **N3 — registered as `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-081 (Low).** A *pending* `suppress` re-staged over
  a key the standing cast **already** suppresses can flip to *applied* on the next provenance load,
  hiding the unsaved indicator (`hasUnsavedEdits` counts only pending). Narrow and self-correcting;
  the clean fix needs editing the deliberately-untouched S2 reducer, and the effect-level alternative
  has a stale-read / re-render-loop problem — left as a known limitation.
- **N2 is a documented non-issue.** `records.ts` refuses `{cpu: ""}` — the one place the client is
  *stricter* than the server — but the form pre-filters empty fields, so it is UI-unreachable
  (LES-271 is the direction rule this exception is checked against).
- The frontend round-trip test asserts the **client's JSON document**; the on-disk YAML byte-identity
  is owned by S1's `tests/test_api_console.py` — a boundary, not a gap.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/CHANGELOG.md` — this entry.
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` — STATUS header flipped to "S5 BUILT 2026-09-10 (§16); S6–S7
  OPEN"; new §16 "S5 built as"; §11 sequence updated (S6 is next).
- `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` — new 2026-09-10 Console S5 section; next step = S6.
- `docs/design/15-console-canvas.md` — built-status touch (S5 built); §13 progress.
- `docs/design/14-operator-assertions.md` — §3 prose "conduits and routes" → "conduits" at two spots
  (lines ~190, ~267), aligning the first-cut prose with §3's own normative refusal of `route`
  (line 132).
- `o2/console/README.md` — Status line + the "what it does not yet do" note now say editing/save is
  live.
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — LES-270 (a `*/` in prose/glyph inside a block comment terminates it), LES-271
  (a mirrored client validator may be looser than its server, never stricter — the direction rule),
  LES-272 (a "collect every entry" serializer turns a scoped write into a whole-store commit).
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — GAP-081 (N3, the pending-suppress applied-flip).

**Links:** LES-270..LES-272; GAP-081. Same family as LES-251 (a 412 etag is for a reload, never a
retry) and LES-258 (the hand-written Python↔TypeScript DTO mirror can drift).

## 2026-09-10 — Console S4 BUILT: pins are live on the operator canvas — absolute overrides with no reflow, and two review-fixed drag bugs

**Authority:** **implementation (Claude Code)** — for the build, the two review-fixed drag bugs
below, and the kept-vs-deferred calls. The design this implements is **Authority: user**:
`docs/design/15-console-canvas.md` §4 (a deterministic identity-keyed layout with **optional pins**
over a fully-pinned one) and `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §3's S4 acceptance.

**Version-locked: nothing moved.** No `IR_SCHEMA`/`PLANNER`/`OP_CATALOG`/`VALIDATOR`/`ADAPTER`/
`IMAGE_MANIFEST` change, no new op type (**40**), no rule (**114**), no `scenario_schema_version` or
state-store `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump. The change is **entirely frontend** (`o2/console/` only) — the
pytest suite is untouched (**2705** collected), and the seven layout coordinate goldens are
**byte-identical** (a pin is an absolute override *applied on top of* the pure layout, so the
no-pins render is unchanged — clearing pins is byte-identical to the pure function). Vitest **219
passed / 6 skipped** (was 191/6 at the S3 drill-down fix).

**What changed:**

- **Pins are live.** An operator drags a **segment tile** or a **whole site column** on the level-0
  canvas to reposition it. A pin is an **ABSOLUTE OVERRIDE with NO reflow** (`15` §4): the pinned
  element ignores its computed slot and lands at its pinned top-left, and **every unpinned element
  keeps its computed place** — it does not shift to fill the gap or make room. So an unpinned
  neighbour never moves when you pin its sibling, and *reset pins* is exactly "call the layout with
  an empty set", byte-identical to the pure no-pins render (the property that lets a deterministic
  layout win over a fully-pinned one).
- **Persistence.** Pins persist to `<imprint>.layout.json` (`axr-layout/1`) via `PUT …/layout`,
  kept in a `layout.json` doc **separate** from the assertions/edit store (pins are layout, never
  evidence). The save is **optimistic** with an etag reconcile: the pinned/reset position shows at
  once, then the PUT's authoritative etag is adopted; a stale etag (a concurrent writer) triggers a
  fresh **reload** before the next drag, never a silent retry that would clobber the other write
  (LES-251). Pins are keyed on the **CANONICAL** imprint id the PUT path is keyed on — the same id
  drill-down uses, never a producer-baked one (LES-263) — so they survive reload and apply.
- **Reset** returns the canvas to the pure function; **hosts are never pinnable** — no drag
  affordance in the UI (infrastructure devices, the Internet pill and the *unplaced* tile carry
  none), and the server `_validate_pin_key` refuses a host key by name.
- **The load-bearing internal fix — lane re-anchoring.** S3 moved tiles but not the east-west
  conduit lanes anchored to them. `applyPins` now **re-derives** a moved tile's lanes by re-running
  the same pure `layoutLanes` primitive over the **moved** tile positions with a **fixed band axis**
  (`laneBandTop`), so a lane whose endpoint a pin moved tracks the tile while the band stays put — and
  a pair neither pin touched recomputes **byte-identical** (unmoved bottom-centres, same channel
  order), leaving locality intact (LES-266). An infra device's tie-line picks up the moved tile for
  free because the view draws it off `l0.tiles`.
- **viewBox growth stays in the VIEW.** The canvas viewBox grows to *contain* a tile/site a
  **committed** pin pushed past the pure canvas — computed in `Level0.tsx` (`frameFor`), framed to
  **`savedPins` only, never the live drag** — so the pure `layout0` output (and the goldens) is
  untouched and empty-pins is an exact no-op (LES-268).

**Two review-caught drag bugs, each fixed with a revert-proven test:**

- **(1) The post-drag click suppressor is now TIME-bounded (250 ms), not "expected-click"-bounded.**
  A drag uses `setPointerCapture`, which routes the compatibility `click` after `pointerup` to the
  capturing `<svg>` (not the tile's `onClick`) in several browsers — so a flag cleared only by "the
  awaited click arrived" stays **armed** and eats a **later** legitimate drill click. Suppression is
  bounded by time instead (LES-269).
- **(2) The client→layout scale is captured ONCE at gesture start**, and the viewBox is framed to
  **committed** pins only. Re-reading a live CTM mid-drag drifts: the level-0 SVG is
  `max-width:100%`-scaled, so a viewBox that grows during the drag rescales the CTM and the tile
  **accelerates** away from the cursor. Capturing the scale once (via `getScreenCTM().inverse()`,
  with a boundingRect ÷ viewBox fallback and identity last resort) keeps the tile 1:1 under the
  cursor even when the diagram is wider than its container (LES-267).

**Honest limits (recorded, not defects):**

- **Server pin-key CIDR asymmetry (pre-existing, newly reachable by S4)** — registered as
  `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` **GAP-080**. `_validate_pin_key` accepts a *loose* `segment:` CIDR while
  tiles are keyed on the strict network form (a mismatched key silently matches no tile), and
  `_SITE_KEY_RE` rejects `/`, so a `site:raw:<cidr>` key (mintable by `sites.ts` for a **non-RFC1918**
  twin) would be 400-refused and the optimistic pin silently revert. **Not** reachable with today's
  all-`/24` RFC1918 content — but S4 is the first code to emit site keys, so it is a real latent gap.
- **Save is not serialized**: a rapid second save during an in-flight PUT can 412 → roll back →
  reload and drop the second edit (it converges via reload; matches the no-queue design).
- **Dead pins accumulate** in `layout.json` for since-removed segments/sites (harmless — `applyPins`
  ignores an unmatched key).
- **Drag is pointer-only**; the site header has no keyboard activation (a minor a11y gap).

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/CHANGELOG.md` — this entry.
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` — STATUS header flipped to "S4 BUILT 2026-09-10 (§15); S5–S7
  OPEN"; new §15 "S4 built as"; §11 sequence updated (S5 is next).
- `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` — new 2026-09-10 Console S4 section; next step = S5.
- `docs/design/15-console-canvas.md` — built-status touch (S4 built).
- `o2/console/README.md` — Status line + the "Pins are …" note now say pins are live.
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — LES-266 (re-derive a moved connector from moved coords via the pure
  primitive), LES-267 (capture the SVG client→user scale once at gesture start), LES-268 (viewBox
  growth in the view, never the pure layout), LES-269 (a post-drag click suppressor must be
  time-bounded).
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — GAP-080 (the server pin-key CIDR/site-key asymmetry).

**Links:** LES-266..LES-269; GAP-080. Same family as LES-261 (pins are the escape hatch for a
column-count reflow) and LES-251 (a 412 etag is for a reload, never a retry).

## 2026-09-09 — Console S3 drill-down id fix: navigation uses the canonical imprint id, never the export's baked `imprint_id`

**Authority:** **implementation (Claude Code)** — a same-day follow-up to the "Console S3 BUILT"
entry directly below, found in **live use immediately after S3 shipped**.

**Version-locked: nothing moved.** No `IR_SCHEMA`/`PLANNER`/`OP_CATALOG`/`VALIDATOR`/`ADAPTER`/
`IMAGE_MANIFEST` change, no new op type (**40**), no rule (**114**), no `scenario_schema_version`
or state-store bump. The change is **entirely frontend** (`o2/console/` only) — the pytest suite is
untouched (**2705** collected), and the seven layout coordinate goldens are **byte-identical** (this
is a navigation-**id** change, not coordinates).

**What changed:**

- The canvas built **every** drill-down route (a segment/host tile click, the outline links, a
  search hit) from `graph.imprintId`, which `buildGraph` derived from the **topology export's baked
  `imprint_id`** field — *not* the canonical URL id the API is keyed on (bundle directory name =
  `twin_config.id` = the id in the page URL). When the baked id disagreed with the canonical id, a
  drill navigated to a **non-resolving id (400/404)**.
- **Fix:** `CastInput` gains a **REQUIRED** `imprintId` (the canonical id, **no fallback**);
  `useGraph` threads its URL `imprintId` into `buildGraph`; and `ConsoleGraph.imprintId` is set from
  it. The baked `imprint_id`/`pit_id` remain as **display/provenance data** but drive **no** route.
- **4 revert-proven regression tests** in `o2/console/test/model.test.ts` use a fixture where
  **canonical ≠ baked** (`corpapcn-twin` vs `pit-63994c9543-…`) — the disagreement path the
  self-consistent goldens never ran. Vitest now **191 passed / 6 skipped** (was 187/6 at the S3
  build).

**Why / blast radius:**

- It hit **two of three** imprints: the deployed disco twin `pit-395a61f04e-1780405200` (baked
  `corpapcn-disco`) and — **pre-existing and untouched by S3** — `corpapcn-twin` (baked
  `pit-63994c9543-…`). Only `corpapcn-golden` worked, and only because it is self-consistent
  (baked == canonical), which is exactly why the goldens missed the bug.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/CHANGELOG.md` — this entry.
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` — new §14.6 (post-ship fix note); S4 remains next.
- `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` — the 2026-09-09 Console S3 section (post-ship fix + serve state).
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — LES-263 (the canonical-vs-baked-id navigation trap), LES-264 (the
  multibyte-`§` locale-grep trap that hid the original assignment during investigation), LES-265
  (a stale Vite/Vitest cache masking a revert proof).
- `o2/console/README.md` — one line under "The URL is the zoom" naming the drill id as canonical.

**Links:** LES-263/LES-264/LES-265; same family as LES-262/LES-259 (a mono-value fixture certifies a
bug green) and LES-195 (a catalog reader must publish the id its own lookup resolves by).

## 2026-09-09 — Console S3 BUILT: the map pane draws — levels 0–2 + the matrix over pure, golden-tested layout modules

**Authority:** **implementation (Claude Code)** — for the build, for the three review should-fixes
below, and for the kept-vs-deferred calls. The design this implements is **Authority: user**:
`docs/design/15-console-canvas.md` §3–§7 (the three levels, the deterministic prefix-grouped layout,
edges as bundles + a matrix) with the §7 v1 defaults O1–O7 (`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §7).
`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §3's S3 slicing and §5's layout contract were already recorded
there as implementation calls open to veto; §14 (new) is the work order's own account of what the
build **changed about them**, which is the part §3 cannot tell a later reader.

**Version-locked: nothing moved.** No `IR_SCHEMA`/`PLANNER`/`OP_CATALOG`/`VALIDATOR`/`ADAPTER`/
`IMAGE_MANIFEST` change, no new op type (**40**), no rule (**114**), no `scenario_schema_version`
or state-store `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump. The change is **entirely frontend** — `o2/console/` plus one
`docs/WORK_ORDER_console_canvas.md` §5.2 doc edit — so the pytest suite is untouched (**2705
collected / 2693 passed / 12 skipped**).

**What changed:**

- **The map pane now DRAWS.** S2 was the scaffold that rendered only the cast's live summary; S3
  renders the twin's structure at all three levels plus the matrix, against the **real**
  `twin-configs-cast/corpapcn-twin/` bundle (786 nodes / 1437 edges / 32 segments): **32 level-0
  tiles, 22 east-west conduit lanes, six infrastructure devices**, a level-1 spine with role bands
  and rung-collapse, the level-2 host facts table, and the roles × roles matrix.
- **A PURE `src/layout/` module set** — `geometry` / `tiers` / `sites` / `level0` / `level1` /
  `matrix` / `lanes`, plus a `pins.ts` seam left for S4 — with **no React, no DOM, no API import**
  and no `react-flow`/`d3-force`/`dagre`. Input is the joined graph; output is coordinates. This is
  the module boundary `15` §9 and the work order §4 require, and it is what makes the layout
  golden-testable off a machine.
- **A React- and API-free `model/` layer:** `graph.ts` joins the topology export and the cast
  summary into one `ConsoleGraph`; `roles.ts` (the fixed role order + the infra-device set),
  `budgets.ts` (per-level budgets + the O6 collapse rule), `search.ts` (name / address / role / CIDR
  → a zoom). The `views/` — `Level0` / `Level1` / `Level2` / `Matrix` plus the outline, legend,
  filters and search — compose `model` + `layout` and touch nothing else.
- **The §7 v1 defaults O1–O7 are all consumed as ruled.** O1 site-bucketing (two octets for RFC1918
  /24s, longest common prefix otherwise); **O2** the six-class evidence palette ships from the
  mockup hues as **swappable `theme/tokens.css` tokens** (the `dataviz` pass's starting point, not
  its verdict); **O3** the matrix at level 0 **plus** hosts × hosts inside a segment, which cost
  nothing extra; O5 the five-second post-apply highlight seam; **O6** the level-1 rung-collapse for a
  band over 15 chips (the 27-host `generic-endpoint` regression band, LES-185); **O7** the 49
  unplaced + 596 non-local entities counted in the outline root and reachable, nothing silently
  invisible. **Every element's evidence class is the export's own `*_evidence_class`, never
  inferred.**
- **The §5 layout contract is enforced by committed coordinate goldens** (seven), produced from a
  checked-in trimmed fixture and regenerated **only** by `npm run layout:golden` (never on a plain
  test run), plus a `layout.real.test.ts` that runs the §3 count checks against the real bundle — a
  hand fixture cannot prove the layout works on what ships.

**Three review should-fixes (each a real defect the build's first green suite had certified):**

- **(A) The evidence-mix ring/dot now orders by the defined `precedenceOf`, not alphabetically.** A
  real display bug: for a multi-class mix the "dominant" swatch named the **wrong** class, because
  the two orders disagree (`imputed` sorts before `observed` alphabetically but ranks *below* it in
  precedence). The all-`observed` fixture never ran the sort and hid it (LES-262).
- **(B) The §5.2 locality guarantee was corrected to be honest.** Determinism + locality hold for a
  **column-preserving** edit (add/remove a host, or a tile that fits an existing row grid). A
  **column-count** change (a new row, or a new/promoted `/16` that adds a column) reflows the
  width-centered chrome and every downstream column — that is a deliberate consequence of ordered
  columns (`15` §4) and is **S4-pins territory**, not a wider guarantee from the pure function. The
  chrome keeps its width-centered aesthetic by design; the churn is documented; a second
  `level0-added-column` golden pins the baseline so S4 has a reviewed reference (LES-261).
- **(C) `cellDetail` no longer rebuilds the O(1437-edge) matrix on every Inspector render** — it
  reuses a memoized `MatrixLayout` (a perf fix on the hot selection path). Two nits closed in the
