## 2026-09-05 — Fidelity scorecard BUILT (spec 13 §6's *certify* box, S1–S4)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code) for the build, for the **row-5 denominator refinement**
(§3.2's "the observed listen set" narrowed to observed *service* listens, with every exclusion
published) and for the five corrections below; the row set, gate set, six-class evidence discipline
and the deployment-scope consequence it builds against are **Authority: user**
(`docs/design/13-golden-twin-fidelity.md` §3/§3.3/§3.4/§8, `docs/design/14-operator-assertions.md`
§2, and the rulings recorded in `docs/WORK_ORDER_fidelity_scorecard.md` §9.6 — 9.1 resolved by note
14's six-class model, 9.2(a)/9.3(a)/9.4(c) taken as implementation calls open to veto, 9.5 ruled
with the §3.4 deployment-scope variant).

**What changed:**

- **`axrange/fidelity/` (new leaf package, 3,938 lines across six modules)** — the fidelity
  scorecard: spec 13 §3.1's eleven rows under §3.3's gate set, §4's evidence-class rule and §8.3's
  provisional flagging, written beside the twin config it certifies and served over the API. A leaf
  like `axrange/measurement/`: it imports `planner` (`inventory`/`state`/`opview`), `twinconfig`,
  `manifest` and — from `runner.py` only — `adapter_proxmox`; it imports neither `o2control` (role
  overrides arrive as an argument) nor `aximprint`, ever (it reads three files on the cross-repo
  seam).
  - **`reference.py`** — the cast-bundle reader. Loads `<id>.yaml` through the **one** twin-config
    loader pinned to the bundle directory (never a first-hit catalog chain, LES-198), plus
    `<id>.topology.json` (the observed superset) and, when present, `<id>.provenance.json`. It
    publishes **`bundle_id`** — the directory name *its own lookup resolved by* — and cross-checks
    the content-declared `twin_config.id` against it (LES-195), while **`pit_id` is published and
    never cross-checked**: it is a point-in-time id by the producer's design and is not the
    addressing id. `evidence_source` is `"re-derived from topology export"` or `"sidecar fields"`,
    and the canonical `axr-cast-provenance/1` sidecar is consumed for per-fact classes when present.
  - **`schema.py`** — the output dataclasses: `EvidenceMix` over note 14's **six classes**
    (`asserted`/`imported`/`observed`/`referenced`/`imputed`/`compiler`) plus an `unclassified`
    bucket that is a *counter*, not a seventh class (a class the reference declared nothing for is
    never silently promoted to `observed`); a `not-computed` verdict that **refuses construction
    without a reason**; §4.1's rollup (`golden` iff every gating row passes · `not-golden` iff every
    gating row is pass|fail with at least one fail · **`incomplete` iff any gating row is
    `not-computed`** — an unevaluable gate is never a failure, and no build is golden on a scorecard
    with an uncomputed gate); and `gate_set()`.
  - **`rows.py`** — the eleven row computations, **pure** over `(reference, reproduced, probe)` so
    they are unit-testable with no DB and no cluster, the way `planner/plandiff.py` is. Rows 1 and 2
    return **`not-computed` on an empty denominator** rather than a vacuous pass (LES-101); row 1 is
    group-granular with the OS-family clause reported **imputed-from-role**, never scored; row 2's
    reproduced side is cross-checked against the deployed twin's actual attachment set; row 3 is
    declared-vs-applied over **`COMPLETED` unconditional** op rows only (LES-095/LES-113/LES-159);
    row 5 distinguishes **`unresolved`** (an observed fact with no twin host at all → fail) from
    **`unmeasured`** (a twin host that could not be asked → blocks a pass, never asserts absence);
    and rows 1/2/3 score only `vm_status == "completed"` hosts, publishing `planned_not_deployed`
    beside the result.
  - **`probe.py`** — pure render + parse for the in-guest protocol probe, stdlib-Python-3 only:
    dns, http, https, ntp, krb (AS-REQ), ldap (rootDSE), smb (SMB2 NEGOTIATE), rdp (X.224) and —
    added by the denominator ruling below — **cldap, nbns, dhcp INFORM and dcerpc EPM bind** (with
    `ldaps` rounding out the LDAP pair: **thirteen** protocol-aware methods in all), plus the bare
    `connect`/`udp-connect` fallbacks, which are labelled *not protocol-aware* and can never count
    as `pass`. Every method is **wire-tested against a canned responder**, never asserted on its
    rendered bytes alone (LES-221). Containment
    is enforced **at render**: every peer address is an orchestrator-resolved literal, the property
    `drive: command` is built around (LES-201/LES-202).
  - **`runner.py`** — the one place holding a `GuestAgent`. Peer selection is **Linux-only**, read
    from `wait-os-ready`'s recorded `os_family`; `_require_python3` and the render both sit **inside
    the per-peer `try`**, so one unusable guest yields `unmeasured` facts rather than losing the
    whole document (LES-089's refusal, scoped to the peer, not the run); the temporary script is
    removed in a `finally`. `scorecard_for_deploy` is the **one** helper the CLI and the API both
    call (LES-037).
- **CLI `axrange te scorecard [DEPLOY_ID]`** — a standalone deploy-scoped verb outside the six-verb
  grid, following `te inventory`'s precedent, with `--json --reference --out --no-write
  --include-deferred --no-probe --attest-visual --fail-on-verdict`. Exit **0 whenever a document was
  produced** (the verdict is data, not a tool failure); 3/4 on `not-golden`/`incomplete` **only**
  under `--fail-on-verdict` (§9.4(c)); `--json` emits nothing but JSON on stdout on success *and*
  failure, diagnostics to stderr (LES-083). The artifact is written to
  `twin-configs-cast/<id>/<id>.scorecard.<deploy_id>.json` by default — keyed on `deploy_id` so a
  re-certification is a **new** document beside the old, not an overwrite.
- **API `GET /api/deploys/{deploy_id}/scorecard?probe=&include_deferred=&reference=`** with a
  `ScorecardDTO` — a peer of `/inventory` and `/access`, field-for-field with `Scorecard.to_dict()`,
  and calling the same `scorecard_for_deploy` helper so CLI and API can never diverge (the exact
  divergence `inventory.py` records as a past review finding).
- **`axrange.twinconfig.instance_ids` made public** (was `_instance_ids`) — row 3 compares a cast
  group's declared installs against op rows keyed by exactly these ids, and a second copy that
  drifts by one character silently reads a correct twin as broken.
- **Fixtures and tests (137 cases across four files).**
  `tests/fixtures/cast-bundles/mini-cast/` mirrors the *real* cast's degenerate shape — a router
  legging every segment, `count:`-collapsed groups, a synthetic segment, a gated-out node and
  segment, a Windows node — because a fixture that does not mirror that shape cannot prove row 1
  works (LES-185); `mini-cast-prov/` carries a canonical-shape sidecar; and
  `tests/fixtures/scorecards/mini-cast.scorecard.json` pins the published document **byte-for-byte**
  so its shape cannot drift silently.

**The row-5 denominator refinement (Authority: implementation (Claude Code)).** §3.2 read "the
observed listen set"; as built the denominator is the observed **service** listens on **deployed**
hosts **at or above the deploy threshold**, with every exclusion published with its reason and its
count rather than silently dropped — an unexplained filter is as dishonest as a vacuous pass, so
LES-101 cuts both ways. Three classes are excluded: dynamic/ephemeral ports (≥ 49152) and Windows
Delivery Optimization 7680 as **`not a service`** (a once-seen RPC endpoint-mapper allocation is an
artefact of the capture window, and scoring the twin for not reproducing `57086/tcp` is scoring
noise); proprietary 9997 and 3389/udp as **`no protocol-aware probe`** (RDP-UDP is negotiated inside
an established TCP session; a port that can only ever return `inconclusive` blocks a pass for a
reason that has nothing to do with the twin); and facts **`below deploy threshold`** — reported,
never failed (§3.4). Motivation: 13 of the standing twin's 25 observed port pairs had no
protocol-aware probe at all, which made `golden` structurally unreachable on row 5; with cldap,
nbns, dhcp and dcerpc added, **every remaining fact in the denominator is protocol-aware**.

**Five corrections during the build (Authority: implementation (Claude Code)), each a real defect
the green suite had certified:**

1. **Linux-only peer selection.** The injector-selection shape was reused from
   `o2control/taps.py::_select_verify_injector` without its OS/capability filter; against the
   standing twin it would have peered **28 of 45 targets to Windows guests** and aborted the run on
   a missing `python3`.
2. **Row 5 honours the deploy threshold.** A gated-out host's listen fact resolved to no twin
   candidate and read as a reproduction **failure** — the exact inversion of §3.4's ruling, which
   says a below-threshold fact is out of deployment scope and is *reported*.
3. **Rows 1 and 2 return `not-computed` on an empty denominator**, not `pass` (LES-101).
4. **The re-derivation warning is attached to the derivation, not to the sidecar's absence.** As
   first written it was emitted from the "sidecar missing" branch, so it would have vanished the day
   the sidecar landed while classes were still being re-derived for the facts it does not cover.
5. **Planned-but-never-cloned hosts are excluded from the reproduction denominator** and published
   as `planned_not_deployed`, rather than counted as reproduced.

**Three code-writer deviations from the work order, recorded rather than quietly taken:**
`expanded_nodes` (138) and `expanded_hosts` (137) are **both** published rather than one reconciled
number; the "below threshold" state is named from the gate that produced it, so a gate-less twin
reads `not_evaluated_below_bar` and a gated one reads `below deploy threshold`; `to_dict()` emits
**every** key including empty ones, so two scorecards diff cleanly; a missing deploy raises
`InventoryError` (HTTP 404), reusing the inventory endpoint's own contract; and the second fixture
bundle deliberately keeps the golden document on the **sidecar-absent** shape, which is run 1's real
state.

**Why:**

- Spec 13 §6's lifecycle has one box that does not exist besides *drift*: **certify**. Without it
  "golden" is an assertion, and the CorpAPCN loop's closing finding — that a traffic-shape
  fingerprint is neither this platform's purpose nor a proxy for it — has no replacement measure.
- A certification artifact is exactly where a vacuous pass is most expensive, which is why
  `not-computed` is a first-class verdict with a mandatory reason, why `incomplete` can never
  collapse into `not-golden`, and why every row publishes its evidence mix instead of one number.
- The probe lives in-guest over the existing QGA channel because twin segments are isolated SDN
  networks with no route from the orchestrator; "read-only" for run 1 is defined as no package
  install, no op row, no state-store write and the temporary script removed — materially the action
  `verify-capture` already performs on every tapped deploy (§9.2(a)).

**Run 1, measured on the standing `te corpapcn-twin-0e4f7d58` (138 VMs, dev45; read-only,
`--no-probe`):** verdict **`incomplete`** — which is itself the acceptance criterion for §4.1's
rollup, since row 5 gates and is uncomputed without the probe. **Row 1 FAIL**, naming the five
role-less high-occupancy hosts absent from the cast (`10.20.193.95`, `10.20.205.56`, `10.80.20.67`,
`10.80.72.13`, `192.168.0.149`). **Row 2 FAIL** — 6 of 32 observed segments have no twin segment, 6
of 31 observed local cross-segment pairs touch one of them, 0 unrouted among the rest. **Row 3
PASS** over 11 declaring groups → 22 hosts → 27 applied actions. **Row 5 `not-computed`** with a
real denominator: 31 facts across 10 hosts, plus 10 published exclusions (7 *not a service*, 2 *no
protocol-aware probe*, 1 *below the bar*). Rows 4/6/7 **deferred**; rows 8/9/10 **provisional
`not-computed`** pending the fingerprint seam; row 11 **`not-attested`**;
`provenance_sidecar: absent` with its re-derivation warning. This is the scorecard the spec
predicts — one calling this twin golden would have been the bug.

**Scope not built:** **S5** (rows 8/9/10 from `<id>.fingerprint.json`, work order §8.2) is out of
scope for this build and is the field `reference.fingerprint: "absent"` already fills. S4's deferred
`deploy_gate` inventory key is n/a here — the scorecard calls `confirm_deploy_gate` directly. The
subject does **not** publish a per-host `os_family`: it is read for peer selection only, and the
inventory endpoint already carries `os` — a recorded non-publication, not a gap.

**Nothing version-locked moved.** No `IR_SCHEMA`/`PLANNER`/`OP_CATALOG`/`VALIDATOR`/`ADAPTER`/
`IMAGE_MANIFEST` change; **op count stays 40**, **catalog rule count stays 114**, no state-store
schema bump. The scorecard is a pure read over existing artifacts plus one in-guest probe over the
existing QGA channel. **Full suite: 2262 passed / 12 skipped / 0 failed, 2274 collected.**

**Live-verify:** the gated **read-only** `dev45` run (work order §7, steps 1–6 — including step 2's
probe pass, expected to flip row 5 to `fail` and the verdict to `not-golden`) has **NOT** run.

- `docs/WORK_ORDER_fidelity_scorecard.md` — status → BUILT; new §9.7 "Built as"; §3.1 row 5 and §3.3

- `docs/design/13-golden-twin-fidelity.md` — §3.1 row 5 (the denominator refinement as built); §6

- `CLAUDE.md` — golden-twin blockquote (scorecard BUILT, run 1 measured, dev45 probe run pending);

## 2026-09-04 — Deploy gate BUILT (spec 13 §3.4, the axRange half)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code) for the build and for the two cascade corrections below
(both refine `docs/WORK_ORDER_deploy_gate.md` §4.3 step 3 — the work order's own mechanism, not any
user ruling); **user** for the policy this implements, ruled in that work order's §11.1 and recorded
in `docs/design/13-golden-twin-fidelity.md` §3.4 (the 0.8 default kept, `unroled-endpoint`, the
fixed occupancy ladder with a group carrying its minimum member's numbers, `role_confidence` absent
where it does not apply, compiler-class entities carrying no block).

**What changed:**

- **`axrange/twinconfig/gate.py` (new, 559 lines)** — the deploy gate. `DEPLOY_MIN_OCCUPANCY`
  (default **0.8**, the same bar spec 13 row 1 judges against) and `DEPLOY_MIN_ROLE_CONFIDENCE`
  (default **0.0**, off) are ordinary twin-config params: `deploy_thresholds` resolves them out of
  the already-resolved map and raises `TwinConfigParamError` on a non-numeric or out-of-range value
  rather than comparing a string falsy. `check_observation` implements §3.3's six rules plus §3.2's
  occupancy checksum (`|occupancy - windows_seen/windows_total| > 1e-6` is a hard error naming the
  entity — the counts are the carried occupancy's checksum, not a second source), and **refuses a
  `role_confidence` on a segment block** (a segment has no role; parsing-then-ignoring is the
  false-affordance shape LES-172 exists to close). `gate_verdict` is the §4.2 predicate with a
  `1e-9` boundary epsilon, returning a typed `GateVerdict(metric, reason)` so a caller offers the
  remedy for **the clause that actually fired**. `exact_param_value` renders a threshold a printed
  remedy can be pasted back verbatim. `normalize_declared_thresholds` coerces the two declared
  defaults to `float` so a config writing the natural `DEPLOY_MIN_ROLE_CONFIDENCE: 0` (an int) does
  not make `--param …=0.9` fail as "not a valid int" — fixed narrowly, for these two keys, never by
  widening `params._coerce_like`. `DeployGateReport` renders the summary/detail/warning lines (the
  summary says **"effective"** thresholds, because a `--param` override is invisible in the declared
  `params:` block printed above it) and `to_dict()` is what `twin-config show --json` publishes.
  `DeployGateRecord` + `confirm_deploy_gate` / `deploy_gate_report` are the §6 recovery surface.
- **`axrange/twinconfig/schema.py`** — `TwinConfigObservation(TCBase)` (`windows_seen`,
  `windows_total`, `occupancy`, optional `role_confidence`) and an `observation:` field on
  `TwinConfigNode` and `TwinConfigSegment` — the same two models `when:` sits on. The three numeric
  fields are declared `Optional` only so a partial block is reported against the entity **by name**
  instead of a pydantic list index; all three are required in practice.
- **`axrange/twinconfig/expand.py`** — the gate and its cascade inside `_build_ir`, which now
  returns `(ir, report)`. Order: `when:` (authored intent) first, unchanged; then the node gate;
  then the segment gate + cascade; then conduits, then the `identity:`/`data:` cross-check.
  `scenario.labels` records the effective thresholds — emitted **only when a block exists**, merged
  into any existing labels map, and therefore folded into `ir_hash`, which is what turns threshold
  recovery into a *proof* rather than a guess. New `expand_twin_config_raw_with_gate` returns the
  typed report; `expand_twin_config_raw` now **produces into the `warnings` out-parameter** — its
  first producer since the persona check was removed on 2026-08-04, and the reason that channel was
  kept. Refusals raise the new `DeployGateRefusalError(TwinConfigShapeError)` (`errors.py`), a
  subclass so every existing `except TwinConfigShapeError` is unchanged while a surface can still
  tell "the gate refused this threshold" (turn the dial) from "this config does not expand" (a
  content bug).
- **`axrange/cli/main.py`** — `--param` on `twin-config show` (D4, the one genuinely new flag: it
  makes `show` the "preview a threshold before deploying it" verb); an uncapped `deploy gate:`
  section with distinct **REFUSED** vs **UNAVAILABLE** framing and a `not applicable` line for a
  blockless config; the `params:` line annotated with the overrides actually in force; `show --json`
  carries a `deploy_gate` key. `_resolve_scenario_or_config`'s `quiet` mode now sends gate warnings
  to **stderr instead of dropping them** — `quiet` exists to keep stdout parseable, not to hide that
  a gate removed nodes.
- **`axrange/api/service.py` + `dtos.py`** — `imprint_plan` threads expansion warnings (an imprint's
  `<id>.yaml` *is* a twin_config, so its plan preview runs the gate); additive
  `PlanSummaryDTO.config_warnings` with a `[]` default, so every existing construction and consumer
  is unchanged and the scenario-sourced path stays empty.
- **Tests** — `tests/test_twinconfig_gate.py` (**87 cases**) over a new fixture cast
  (`tests/fixtures/twin-configs/gate-cast/gate-cast.yaml`) whose `gw` legs **every** segment, as the
  shipped cast's does; `test_twinconfig_catalog.py`'s `plan_id` pin extended from five shipped
  configs to **six**, adding the cast at `corpapcn-twin-b6aaa3c5`, plus a threshold-invariance test
  (expansion at `1.0` is byte-identical to the default for a blockless config) and a
  `"labels" not in scenario` assertion that pins the *mechanism*, not just its consequence;
  `test_twinconfig_cli.py` +11; `test_api.py` +3. Suite: **2125 passed / 12 skipped / 0 failed,
  2137 collected**.

**Two corrections to the work order's cascade (`docs/WORK_ORDER_deploy_gate.md` §4.3 step 3):**

1. **A router follows its segments.** Router legs count neither as `legged` nor as `occupied`; a
   segment the gate or the cascade removes has its router legs pruned in the same pass; a router
   stranded of every leg is dropped and reported; and the "gated out from under an emitted node"
   hard error fires only for a **non-router** node. Without this, every real cast refused: the
   compiler emits a single `gw` legging all 27–33 of its segments and exempt from the node gate, so
   counting its legs made every segment permanently "occupied" (the emptiness cascade could never
   fire) and turned every below-bar segment into the hard error. Found independently by the
   aximprint deployment-scope build and by code review.
2. **The emptiness cascade removes a segment only when the gate emptied it** — `occupied_before`
   (non-router occupants before gating) vs `occupied_after`, dropping iff before is non-empty and
   after is empty. A router-only segment (a router↔router transit link, a WAN uplink) has no host
   occupant *by construction* and so is never dropped by the cascade; only its own `observation:`
   block can remove it. Found by the tester against shipped content: a bare emptiness test cost
   `dmz-perimeter` its `internet` uplink and `datacenter --param FABRIC=true` its `fab-link` plus
   both fabric routers, from a single annotated host that gated nothing out. Both cases are now
   pinned as tests.

**Why:**

- Spec 13 §3.4 moves the deployment decision from compile time to deploy time: the cast annotates,
  the deploy selects. Rows 1 and 2 of the fidelity spec are unjudgeable while the compiler has
  already discarded everything below its own bar — "is every observed host above the bar placed?" is
  vacuous against a pre-filtered list. With the gate, the deployable set and the set row 1 expects
  are one set by construction, and **one cast serves every threshold**.
- Recovery had to be a proof, not a recomputation: a `--param` override is persisted nowhere, so
  reading a config's declared `params:` at scorecard time can never distinguish it from the
  default. Emitting the effective thresholds into `scenario.labels` folds them into `ir_hash`, so
  `confirm_deploy_gate` either reproduces the deploy's recorded hash — confirming the threshold
  **and** making the gated-out set exactly reproducible — or returns `None`, which a caller renders
  as `threshold: UNCONFIRMED`. Never a number nobody can stand behind.

**Measured on the real fresh cast** (`corpapcn-scope-check` — 33 segments, 88 node specs, 115
observation blocks; the aximprint deployment-scope build's output, not a fixture):

- `--param DEPLOY_MIN_OCCUPANCY=0.0` → **157 nodes / 33 networks**; the default **0.8** → **80 nodes
  / 24 networks** (64 observed + 15 internet-sink + 1 `gw`).
- The `gw`'s leg count equals the surviving segment count at **every** threshold — correction 1
  holding on real content.
- `axrange te validate --config corpapcn-scope-check` passes at 0.8.
- Under one pinned `plan_id`, the op-id set difference is **`0.5 → 0.8` removed-only** and
  **`0.5 → 0.0` added-only, 0 mutated** — the work order §5 claim that a threshold change is exactly
  the whole-node add/remove subset `te deploy --update` implements, and never trips its in-place
  mutation refusal. Now a test, not a claim.

**Scope note:** S1–S3 are complete; **S4 is partial** — `scenario.labels` and `confirm_deploy_gate`
are built, the optional `deploy_gate` key on `TwinInventory`/`TwinInventoryDTO` is **deferred**
(there is no producer for it without a second CLI surface, and shipping a key nothing populates is
LES-172's shape; the fidelity scorecard calls `confirm_deploy_gate` directly).

**Nothing version-locked moved:** no `IR_SCHEMA` / `PLANNER` / `OP_CATALOG` / `VALIDATOR` /
`ADAPTER` / `IMAGE_MANIFEST` change, no `scenario_schema_version` bump, no state-store
`SCHEMA_VERSION` bump. **40 op types, 114 catalog rules — both unchanged.** `axrange/twinconfig/` is
content-side, the numeric cousin of the existing `when:` gate. `Scenario.labels` — free-form,
documented unvalidated, and with zero consumers since it was defined — **gained its first
producer**.

**Gated `dev45` live-verify:** none of its own, per the work order §9 — a pure expander change whose
entire output surface is an IR dict the unit suite asserts byte-for-byte, and whose `plan_id` pin
over six shipped configs is a stronger guarantee than a cluster run. Two checks fold into the fresh
cast's own (already gated) deploy: the printed `deploy gate:` summary must match `te status` /
`te inventory` node counts, and `confirm_deploy_gate` must return **confirmed** at the threshold the
operator passed.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_deploy_gate.md` — status → BUILT; new §11.2 "Built as"; §4.3 step 3 amended (the
  original text kept and marked superseded).
- `docs/design/13-golden-twin-fidelity.md` §3.4 — the two cascade rules and the `scenario.labels`
  threshold record, as built.
- `CLAUDE.md` — golden-twin blockquote; test counter 2113 → 2137.
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — LES-210 … LES-215.
- `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` — handoff.

## 2026-09-04 (evening) — Three more drafts (deploy gate, reference ingest, deployment scope); rulings: 0.8 kept, `unroled-endpoint`, the occupancy ladder; the declared-transformation rule; the Console SPA starts clean

**Authority:** user, 2026-09-04, for the three rulings and the Console call; implementation (Claude
Code) for the drafts, the reconciliation and the calls marked as taken.

**What changed:**

- **Three work orders DRAFTED (Opus), OPEN:** `docs/WORK_ORDER_deploy_gate.md` (axRange — the
  `observation:` block, the `DEPLOY_MIN_OCCUPANCY` gate and cascade, never-silently-skip through the
  expander's dormant `warnings` channel, threshold recovery, the `plan_id` pin); aximprint's
  `docs/WORK_ORDER_reference_ingest.md` (the six-class model in its IR spec, the precedence
  resolver, the sidecar as a versioned schema with `overrides`/`confidence`/`signal` and the
  installer copy, the assertions file as input 1, the LDIF export as input 2 with its allowlist and
  the lab-realm mapping, the hand-backs) and `docs/WORK_ORDER_deployment_scope.md` (emit-all, the
  block, `unroled-endpoint`). Both aximprint files sit in the `/home/rocky/aximprint-axrange`
  worktree, switched to `wip/golden-twin` for the purpose.
- **Rulings on the drafts:** the **0.8 default is kept**, knowing it deploys ~64 observed hosts
  against 122 placed today (the old cast had no occupancy threshold); role-less hosts are emitted as
  **`unroled-endpoint`** (image rule, no persona), not the inferred `generic-endpoint` 102 hosts
  already hold with a persona; the cast's group key gains a **fixed occupancy ladder** (core ≥ 0.8 /
  regular 0.5–0.8 / transient < 0.5) with the block carrying the group's minimum member's numbers —
  over the draft's exact-`windows_seen` key (43 → 87 groups, every group renamed on every
  re-imprint). Recorded in spec 13 §3.4's refinement and §8.6.
- **Adopted from the reference-ingest draft into design note 14:** the **declared-transformation
  rule** (a compiler replacement for deployability — a relocated CIDR, the lab realm — is applied
  after resolution, records the original, and is not a precedence event; the scorecard scores it as
  reproduced under a declared transformation), the assertions schema's missing fields (`segment:` on
  a host add, `name:` on a segment add, `conduit` as a record, `route` refused by name, a closed
  per-kind `override.field` set), the file's location beside the IR store (no "imprint directory"
  exists in aximprint's CLI), and the limit that an assertions-only imprint cannot be materialized
  in v1.
- **Implementation calls taken, open to veto:** deploy gate D1 (c) `scenario.labels` as the
  threshold carrier (no `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump), D2 hard error, D3 confidence gate now / off, D4
  `--param` on `twin-config show`; reference ingest D-a..D-i as recommended (one IR bump `1.4`
  naming both changes; the sidecar versioned; routes refused; an in-repo SD parser; `bundle_schema`;
  `--max-identities` uncapped; LDIF-confirmed unseen hosts recorded, not placed; three IR tables).
  Corrections made to the drafts on review: two shipped scenario IRs do set `labels` (the
  deploy-gate draft said none); the services draft's "topology.json 1.3 → 1.4" becomes a
  `bundle_schema` key, since the export carries no version of its own.
- **Hand-back to axRange (new):** `TwinConfigIdentityUser` has no `delegation_to` passthrough, so
  constrained delegation cannot reach a twin from any source.
- **Console canvas — discussion opened; the SPA starts clean (Authority: user).** A React/TypeScript
  SPA under `o2/console/`, served by `axrange serve`'s existing `AXRANGE_SPA_DIR` mount, not a
  Grafana plugin; three semantic-zoom levels, a deterministic identity-keyed layout, edges in
  bundles and a matrix, coordinated panes with the URL as the zoom, edits as assertion records with
  pending/saved/applied states; the plugin's inspector and deploy tree are not ported. New API
  needed: imprint topology, cast bundle + fingerprint, assertions GET/PUT, apply, layout pins, a
  post-apply diff. **Ratified later the same day (Authority: user)** with both forks ruled — the SPA
  starts clean; deterministic prefix-grouped layout with optional pins — as
  `docs/design/15-console-canvas.md`; a mockup and the Console work order (separate) follow.

**Why:** the three rulings each chose the option that keeps the twin exactly what the wire
certifies (0.8), keeps an inferred role's meaning intact (`unroled-endpoint`), and keeps group
names stable across re-imprints so the drift lifecycle's `--update` stays incremental (the
ladder). The declared-transformation rule was forced into the open by the lab-realm ruling: a
`compiler`-class value replacing an `imported` one would otherwise invert the precedence order.

**Affected docs:** `docs/design/13-golden-twin-fidelity.md` (§3.4, §8.6); `docs/design/14-operator-assertions.md`
(§2, §3, §8); `docs/WORK_ORDER_deploy_gate.md` (new; §11.1); `docs/WORK_ORDER_protocol_faithful_services.md`
(§6.1 carrier); aximprint `docs/WORK_ORDER_reference_ingest.md` (new; §11) and
`docs/WORK_ORDER_deployment_scope.md` (new; §9); `CLAUDE.md`; `docs/SESSION_STATE.md`.

## 2026-09-04 (later) — Spec 13 §7 sequence ruled; deployment scope §3.4 (the cast describes, the deploy selects); canvas save/apply, drift flag-only, first edit set

**Authority:** user, 2026-09-04, on the four items the morning entry left open; the
occupancy-not-confidence gate and the 0.8 default were proposed by implementation (Claude Code) and
accepted the same day.

**What changed:**

- **§7 sequence — RULED, with a variant.** The reference channel (assertions first, LDIF second)
  and firewall policy follow the first golden, as read. The variant: the fresh cast's rows 1 and 2
  are closed **not by assertions and not by a cast-policy guess** but by a **deployment-scope
  change** — the cast stops dropping role-less hosts (emitted as generic endpoints) and
  low-occupancy segments, emits an **observation block** per node and segment (windows seen /
  total, occupancy, role confidence), and the **deploy** gates on it: a twin-config param
  `DEPLOY_MIN_OCCUPANCY`, **default 0.8, the same bar row 1 uses**, so the deployable set and the
  set row 1 expects are one set by construction. Gate on **occupancy, not confidence** (a
  present-but-unroled host is a fixture and goes in; a one-window host is a transient and stays
  out); confidence shades the canvas and is an optional second gate. Rows 1 and 2 judge against the
  deployable set at the recorded threshold; below it is *observed, below deploy threshold* —
  reported, never failed. New spec §3.4; §3.1 rows 1 and 2, §7 and §8.5/§8.6 amended; scorecard
  §9.6 updated (S2 reads the threshold and gains the new state). The cast's two existing
  compile-time filters (aximprint D5 role-bearing-only, D11 `--min-occupancy`) become annotation —
  an aximprint hand-back; the gate is an axRange `twinconfig/` change, the numeric cousin of the
  existing `when:` gate, nothing version-locked. One cast now serves every threshold; changing it
  changes the `plan_id`, correctly.
- **Canvas loop (design note 14 §4, §7) — RULED:** **save** writes the assertions file only;
  **apply** saves, recompiles and refreshes (API: `PUT …/assertions`, `POST …/apply`); assertion
  drift is **flag-only** (an assertion keeps winning until the operator changes it); the **first
  edit set** is add segment / add host with role + listen set / change role / suppress host /
  image and sizing overrides (those facts become `asserted`, hence scored) / conduits and routes —
  identity facts deferred to the LDIF export. The apply recompile mechanism (subprocess to
  aximprint's CLI in v1, a watcher later) is the one remaining implementation call, open to veto.
- **Still open** in spec §8.5: the v1.1 numbers, whether row 11 ever gates, the second golden's
  experiment class. Nothing in design note 14 §7 remains open beyond the veto.

**Why:**

- The user's reasoning as given: the cast should emit everything and carry an indicator that lets
  axRange's deploy ignore nodes, rather than either baking a different drop policy into the cast or
  making the operator assert what the vantage merely saw too rarely; and a save should not
  re-shape the twin — only an explicit apply should.

**Affected docs:** `docs/design/13-golden-twin-fidelity.md` (new §3.4; §3.1 rows 1–2; §7; §8.5;
§8.6); `docs/design/14-operator-assertions.md` (§4, §5, §7); `docs/WORK_ORDER_fidelity_scorecard.md`
(§9.6); `CLAUDE.md` (golden-twin blockquote); `docs/SESSION_STATE.md`.

**Affected code:** none yet. **Supersedes:** the §7 order recorded as an implementation reading in
the 2026-09-03 §8 entry and restated in this morning's entry; the morning entry's "still open"
list for design note 14 §7.

## 2026-09-04 — Operator assertions and the canvas loop (design note 14); six-class evidence model; sidecar shape; `App.listen` wired; real DC under the lab realm; row 1 rule; two work orders drafted

**Authority:** user, 2026-09-04, for every ruling below; implementation (Claude Code) for the two
drafts, the seam reconciliations, and the calls marked as taken. All in one design session
(Fable); the drafts were produced by Opus subagents per the user's routing rule of 2026-09-03.

**What changed:**

- **New design-of-record `docs/design/14-operator-assertions.md` (ratified).** Motivated by the
  user: a single vantage cannot discover whole categories of structure, so the operator must be
  able to add missing components and override discovered evidence, and that interaction should be
  driven through the visual layer. The note defines (1) a **six-class evidence model in precedence
  order** — `asserted` (operator, confidence 1.0) > `imported` (out-of-band snapshots: LDIF export,
  host inventory, device configs) > `observed` > `referenced` (second-hand from inside the capture,
  meaning unchanged) > `imputed` > `compiler` — with the house rule generalized to "no class
  masquerades as a higher one; an override is visible, never an erasure"; (2) the **assertions
  file** `<imprint>.assertions.yaml` (add / override / suppress, keyed by the stable entity keys of
  the topology export), ingested through the same file-drop door as the LDIF export and promoted to
  the **first** reference ingest to build; (3) the **Console canvas loop** — display (six-class
  legend, confidence shading, ghosts for dropped hosts and pruned segments, overrides kept visible,
  assertion drift flagged), edit (first cut: add segment, add host with role + listen set, change
  role, suppress host), save → recompile → refresh, with the YAML as the contract the UI is thin
  over; (4) the **evidence-mix guardrail**: every scorecard row publishes how many facts of each
  class it rests on, which is what lets `asserted` carry full confidence without the scorecard
  certifying its own inputs.
- **Two findings behind the model, verified read-only against the CorpAPCN belief store.**
  `defaulted` is not an IR evidence class at all (aximprint's `SPEC_network_ir.md` excludes it):
  it lives only in the cast provenance sidecar and marks nine kinds of compiler choice (image,
  sizing, zone type, relocated CIDRs, the synthetic gateway and internet-sink nodes,
  `default_agents: false`, persona seeds) — renamed `compiler`, kept unscored. And `referenced`
  meant *in-capture second-hand* evidence (DNS answers, SPNs; the class behind `ir_unseen`), which
  spec 13 §3 had stretched over out-of-band imports that sit on the other side of `observed` in
  trust — hence the split into `referenced` and `imported`.
- **Sidecar shape (scorecard WO §9.1) — RULED:** the real compiler's shape (path-addressed fields
  plus the `population` block) is the contract; axRange's imprint reader adapts it for the
  Console; aximprint adds `confidence` / `signal` per-field extras; the Track-C fixture is
  regenerated from a real cast. Found on the way: **no compiler sidecar exists anywhere on disk** —
  `install_cast_as_twin.py` never copies it (a hand-back to aximprint).
- **`App.listen` — RULED wired** as the twin's declared listen set, plus a twin-config `listen:`
  passthrough. A version-locked `IR_SCHEMA` touch with **no version bump**: the field exists with
  exactly this shape (`axrange/ir/schema.py:184`/`:193`, `docs/IR_SCHEMA.md:362`), has zero
  consumers, and no shipped content sets it. Guard: every shipped twin config keeps a
  byte-identical `plan_id` / `ir_hash`.
- **Real domain controller — RULED now, under the lab realm `corp.axr.lab`.** The observed realm
  was offered — `AXELLIO.DOM` sits in the belief store's *observed* Kerberos rows and
  `axdc01.Axellio.dom` in its NTLM rows, so the aximprint premise that "nothing in a capture
  supplies an AD forest name" (`roles.yaml:80-94`) is wrong today — and **not chosen**: the twin's
  forest name is `imputed`, users and passwords are lab defaults marked `imputed`. Reading: the DC's
  short hostname may still follow the observed `axdc01`.
- **Row 1 rule — RULED:** the occupancy clause is evaluated at (role, segment) group granularity,
  naming every high-occupancy host the cast dropped (the cast keeps no per-host identity); the
  OS-family clause is reported `imputed`-from-role and not scored until the reference channel
  lands (the wire carries no OS attribute).
- **Two work orders DRAFTED, OPEN:** `docs/WORK_ORDER_fidelity_scorecard.md` (spec §6's certify
  box: eleven-row computation, the §3.3 gate set, an output document whose rollup distinguishes
  `incomplete` from `not-golden`, `axrange te scorecard` + `GET /api/deploys/{id}/scorecard`, a new
  leaf module `axrange/fidelity/`, a READ-ONLY dev45 run 1 against the standing twin) and
  `docs/WORK_ORDER_protocol_faithful_services.md` (the row-5 server half: real servers per observed
  listen set — a real DC, bind9, nginx, chrony, dhcpd — configured from observed data, with the
  echo responder retired to a labelled fallback). **Seams reconciled:** the probe belongs to the
  scorecard and its protocol set follows the observed AD profile (krb/ldap/smb/rdp handshakes
  added — without them row 5 could never pass on the one host carrying 12 of the observed ports);
  `topology.json` 1.4's `services[]` (+ `windows_seen`) is specified once, in the services draft;
  the sidecar copy is one change. Spec 13 §3.3's wording (row 9) and §6 (the recapture the certify
  box never named) were tightened.
- **Run 1 expectation corrected:** scored today the standing twin fails rows **1, 2 and 5**, not
  only 5 — five role-less high-occupancy hosts dropped by the cast and six pruned segments. Both
  are cast-policy artifacts the assertions channel is designed to fix.
- **Implementation calls taken (Claude Code), open to veto** — services D3 (b) runtime servers +
  baked client tools, D5 (a) now / (b) later, D6 (a), D7 (a), D8 (a) echo as a `compiler`-class
  fallback behind a render-time guard; scorecard 9.2 (a) probe on the standing twin under a stated
  read-only definition, 9.3 (a), 9.4 (c) exit 0 with `--fail-on-verdict`.
- **Still open:** spec §8.5 (incl. the §7 re-sequencing, scorecard §9.5); design note 14 §7's four
  items, each with a v1 default named. **Hand-backs to aximprint** (not yet recorded there): the
  wrong `roles.yaml:80-94` premise; the port-389 `serves` tie-break in `synth/personas.py` broken
  by row order; the uncopied sidecar.

**Why:**

- The user's reasoning, recorded as given: the amount of structure discoverable from one tap point
  is bounded, so the platform needs a way for the user to add missing components and override
  discovered evidence; the directory export is one such path; hand-authored input should be its own
  class with the highest standing; and the interaction should be visual — discovered structure
  displayed with evidence class and confidence, edited on a canvas, saved into the YAML, recompiled,
  refreshed — so a hand-authored file need not exist before the first compile but does after it.
- The lab realm over the observed one, and row 1's scoring, were the user's choices without a
  stated rationale beyond the selection; the alternatives are recorded beside each.

**Affected docs:** `docs/design/14-operator-assertions.md` (new); `docs/design/README.md` (index);
`docs/design/13-golden-twin-fidelity.md` (§3 channels + class sentence, §3.1 rows 1 and 11, §5
item 5, §8.6); `docs/WORK_ORDER_fidelity_scorecard.md` (new; §9.6); `docs/WORK_ORDER_protocol_faithful_services.md`
(new; §10.1); `CLAUDE.md` (golden-twin blockquote); `docs/SESSION_STATE.md`.

**Affected code:** none yet — design and work-order drafts only. No version-locked schema,
op count or rule count moves; `App.listen`'s first consumer is authorized but unbuilt.

**Supersedes:** spec 13 §3's "aximprint's existing three" evidence-class sentence and its
`referenced`-for-imports channel wording (the 2026-09-03 ratification); the services draft's D4
recommendation (observed realm) — the user chose the lab realm.

## 2026-09-03 — Golden-twin spec §8 ruled: first golden gates on the structural + behavioral rows; standing twin scored first; tolerances provisional to v1.1; directory export = owner-run LDIF file drop

**Authority:** user, 2026-09-03 — the four rulings, given in a Claude Code session against
`docs/design/13-golden-twin-fidelity.md` §8 as it stood at `97fd3346`. The consequences drawn
from them below are implementation (Claude Code) readings, marked as such in the spec, and are
what the work orders build against unless corrected.

**What changed:**

- **§8.1 — the first golden's gate set is by category, not by experiment class.** Ruling: *"Let's
  focus on all structural and behavioral category dimensions to start with"*; *"rows 4/7 are
  operational, you can ignore them for now."* Rows 1 asset, 2 topology, 3 configuration, 8
  dependency (structural) and 5 protocol, 9 temporal, 10 behavioral (behavioral) gate the first
  golden; 4 state and 7 security are deferred by name, 6 performance by the category rule; 11
  visual is reported (reading). New spec §3.3 tabulates it. Supersedes the spec's §4 proposal
  (attack path plus detection validation, rows 7/5/3/1/2 gating) — a proposal inside the entry
  below, not a separately ratified decision; §4's table stays the model for later goldens.
- **§8.2 — the standing twin is scorecard run 1; the fresh cast is the first golden.** Ruling:
  *"Score the standing twin first; the fresh cast is the first golden."* Two row rules amended in
  the same ruling: **row 3** — *"role-level install lists will suffice"* (v1 rule: each placed host
  carries its inferred role's install list from the role catalog, reported as imputed; the
  inventory-backed ≥ 90 % rule waits for the host-inventory reference ingest); **row 5** —
  *"implement realistic protocol clients and servers based on the observed data. avoid using
  synthetic data generation where possible"* (real clients — `drive: command`, built — and real
  **servers** on twin hosts and at the aximprint sink, configured from observed data, with §5.6's
  wire-derived principals giving a real DC its identities; filler only where nothing was observed).
  Reading: the protocol-faithful traffic work order gains a server half, and the Windows-responder
  problem returns to the critical path as a real DC, not a listener.
- **§8.3 — rows 6/8/9/10 tolerances stay provisional until scorecard run 1, then are fixed as spec
  v1.1** (the recommended option). On run 1 they are reported, not gating, and flagged provisional
  in the scorecard's output.
- **§8.4 — the directory export is an LDIF file drop run by the reference owner, with no
  credential or network reach from our side** (the recommended option): built-in
  `ldifde`/`ldapsearch` with a supplied attribute allowlist so secret-bearing attributes never leave
  the reference network; the security-descriptor attribute included for ACL edges; one parser that
  later takes a live read-only bind unchanged; BloodHound JSON as a possible second format.
- **Re-sequencing (reading, not ruling):** scorecard run 1 and the row-5 client+server build first,
  in parallel; spec v1.1; the fresh cast and first certification; *then* reference ingest and the
  firewall-policy IR extension, since both serve the deferred operational rows. Spec §7 carries the
  new order; the previous order had reference ingest first.
- Still open, recorded in spec §8.5: the v1.1 numbers; whether visual ever gates; the second
  golden's experiment class; confirmation of the re-sequencing.

**Why:**

- The user's rulings are recorded verbatim; no rationale was stated beyond them. Read together
  they certify first on what the wire plus the cast pipeline can already deliver (structure and
  behavior), with the protocol row raised from "answers on the port" to "real client, real server,
  observed content," and sequence the reference channel — the operational half — behind that
  first proof rather than in front of it. Deferring the four tolerances follows the spec's own
  argument: the only numbers available today come from a twin with 12 of 18 local-edge keys
  structurally absent, a ceiling rather than a target.

**Affected docs:** `docs/design/13-golden-twin-fidelity.md` — STATUS, §3.1 rows 3 and 5 (rules
amended), new §3.3, §4 (first-golden paragraph superseded), §5 item 1 (mechanism), §7 (order),
§8 (rewritten as the ruling record); `CLAUDE.md` (golden-twin blockquote: a parenthetical correcting "not yet written", plus an appended ruling summary);
`docs/SESSION_STATE.md` (next steps and open questions).

**Affected code:** none — a design ruling. The standing twin and its `tap`/`tfc` deploys are
unaffected. No version-locked schema, op count or rule count moves.

**Supersedes:** the "first golden is certified for attack path plus detection validation"
paragraph of spec §4 (proposed in the 2026-09-03 ratification entry below; §8 had explicitly left
it open).

## 2026-09-03 — Golden-twin fidelity: the direction ratified, CorpAPCN loop closed at iteration 19

**Authority:** user, 2026-09-03.

**What changed:**

- The CorpAPCN twin-fidelity loop (aximprint `docs/JOURNAL_corpapcn_fidelity.md`) is **closed** at
  iteration 19. Evidence: the deciding axis `role_edges_local` read 0.595 / 0.618 / 0.742 / 0.589 /
  0.533 / 0.593 across iterations 14–19 — a band, not a convergent trend; 12 of 18 source
  local-edge keys are **structurally absent** from the twin (a Windows DC that answers nothing, no
  workstation-to-workstation traffic — see aximprint `docs/JOURNAL_corpapcn_fidelity.md`'s recorded
  structural ceilings, restated in `docs/SESSION_STATE.md`'s 2026-08-31 history in git); and the
  fingerprint measures traffic **shape**, which is neither this platform's goal nor a reliable
  proxy for what that goal depends on.
- Direction ruled: fidelity against a **closed spec** becomes the goal — "golden twin" — not
  fidelity against one capture's traffic shape. **Ten dimensions** (asset, topology, configuration,
  state, protocol, performance, security, dependency, temporal, behavioral) plus **visual**, each
  tagged with its observation channel (wire / reference / operator / console) and evidence class
  (observed / referenced / imputed), weighted by **experiment impact** — what the platform is
  actually for (attack/defense validation, `CLAUDE.md` "What This Project Is"), not by how
  measurable an axis happens to be from a capture alone.
- The **operational half** (config/state/dependency-shaped dimensions) is an observation gap the
  wire cannot fill; aximprint gains a **reference-ingest channel** (directory exports first) to
  close it. Posture is injected into a twin; secrets never are.
- **Work-order triage under the new direction:** Option A (`--reapply`, see the entry immediately
  below) is kept as the ongoing maintenance path; protocol-faithful traffic (`drive: command`, see
  the entry below that) is promoted; the Console (`docs/WORK_ORDER_aximprint_console.md`) is kept
  as the golden-twin UI; `docs/WORK_ORDER_o2_sensor_rules.md` is promoted; **per-rule firewall
  policy** (`CLAUDE.md` "Immediate Next Actions" item 5, open since the router slice landed
  2026-06-10) is promoted — the one reproduction gap injection alone cannot fill, since routers
  ship a permissive default-forward and the twin-config `conduits[].policy` field that once modeled
  per-rule policy was found inert and removed 2026-08-04 (CHANGELOG 2026-08-04 "Role and persona
  become `tfc`-owned"; the sibling IR-level dead fields from that same audit, `Activity.adversary`/
  `Network.isis_area`, are tracked separately at `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-049 since `IR_SCHEMA`
  is version-locked and those two were reported rather than removed).
- Three new work orders are scoped but **not yet written**: a fidelity **scorecard** (axRange,
  scoring a twin against the ten-dimension spec), a **firewall-policy IR extension** (axRange), and
  **reference ingest** (aximprint).
- **Branch plan:** fast-forward both repos' `master` (this branch, `wip/aximprint-integration`, is
  strictly ahead of `master`), then open `wip/golden-twin` in both. The design record for this
  decision lands as `docs/design/13-golden-twin-fidelity.md` on that branch immediately after this
  documentation pass — referenced here as forthcoming, not yet written.
- The standing `te corpapcn-twin-0e4f7d58` (138 VMs, dev45) **stays up**: it is the scorecard's
  first subject and Option A's live proof.

**Why:**

- Six iterations of tuning a traffic-shape fingerprint plateaued in a band rather than converging,
  and the plateau is **structural** (missing source local-edge categories the twin cannot reproduce
  without content it does not have), not a tuning problem — iteration 19 itself is a recorded
  **trade** (`role_edges` improved 0.878 → 0.406, but `role_edges_local` and
  `fan_out_degree_local` both regressed slightly). A platform whose stated purpose is validating
  attack/defense controls needs fidelity on the dimensions that purpose depends on (configuration,
  protocol, security posture, dependency graph), not primarily on aggregate traffic-shape
  statistics.

**Affected docs:** `CLAUDE.md` (new blockquote); `docs/design/13-golden-twin-fidelity.md`
(forthcoming, `wip/golden-twin`); `docs/SESSION_STATE.md`.

**Affected code:** none directly — a direction decision. The standing twin and its `tap`/`tfc`
deploys are unaffected.

## 2026-09-02 — Content-only reapply for `--update` (`--reapply`, responder op-row drift, Option A)

**Authority:** user, 2026-09-02 — ruling on `docs/WORK_ORDER_responder_op_row_drift.md` §4, over
that work order's own options B (a state-store schema bump that would hard-fail every `state.db`,
including the one managing the standing 138-VM twin) and C (an `OP_CATALOG` outputs change for
`OP-APP-030` to record a content hash it does not today).

**What changed:**

- `axrange te deploy --update` gains an opt-in `--reapply` flag (update-only; refused with the same
  "only makes sense with `--update`" posture as `--allow-destroy`). It carves **one** narrow
  exception out of `--update`'s Mutated hard-refusal: a mutated `run-script` (`OP-APP-030`) op
  whose **only** differing resolved input is `script_content` re-executes through the normal
  executor/config-exec path instead of being refused, then **persists** the new content so a later
  `--update` classifies the op `Unchanged`. Implements exactly one row of `docs/PLANNER.md` §9.2's
  deliberately-unbuilt redo-rules table (`apps[*].install` → "redo affected install action ops") —
  built for one op/field pair, not the general table.
- Mechanism, in `axrange/planner/plandiff.py`: a pure predicate pair, `reapplyable_ops` /
  `reapply_ineligibility_reason`, is the **single source of truth** for both the gate (which
  mutated ops qualify) and the refusal message (why a given one doesn't) — one function feeds both,
  so the check and its explanation cannot drift apart. `render_value(..., max_len=)` /
  `content_digest` print a **sha256 fingerprint** of script content instead of the text itself,
  everywhere a diff is shown — CLI stderr and API JSON error bodies alike (a length heuristic:
  short content still prints verbatim).
- `force_reset_ops` flips a covered op's status back to `pending`; the pre-existing attempt-start
  hook re-derives fresh inputs from the on-disk plan, so a successful re-apply persists the new
  content and a failed one leaves the row retryable with a non-zero exit — no new state machine.
- `provision-app` / `pkg:` (the `run-script` sibling with the same shape) is **deliberately out of
  scope**. Every other mutated field, on every other op, is still a hard refusal naming
  op + field + old→new value, unchanged.
- No `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump; `OP_CATALOG` (40 ops) and `VALIDATOR` (114 rules) counts unchanged.
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` **GAP-054** registers the underlying defect (persisted op inputs can
  diverge from deployed guest content after an out-of-band push) regardless of which option was
  chosen, per the work order's own §8 instruction — `--reapply` gives a supported path **going
  forward**; it does not retroactively repair a row that drifted before it existed. The standing
  twin's 19 out-of-band-pushed responder guests (iterations 16/18 of the CorpAPCN loop,
  `docs/WORK_ORDER_responder_op_row_drift.md` §1) are exactly such rows — and a further re-push
  (iteration 19, commit `128f498c`, 2026-09-02) added to the drift rather than exercising the new
  supported path, since `--reapply` covers only same-op-different-content mutation, not the
  personas/tfc-tier iteration this run actually was.

**Why:**

- The alternative to a supported path was a full 138-VM redeploy to change five shell scripts, or
  the out-of-band QGA push that created the drift `docs/WORK_ORDER_responder_op_row_drift.md`
  documents. `run-script` already declared `redo_on=("apps[*].install",)` in the op catalog — the
  trigger existed; the machinery to consume it didn't. Options B and C were both rejected on
  standing-twin cost: B needs a state-store schema bump that hard-fails every existing `state.db`
  (`StateStoreSchemaError`), including the one managing the twin this feature exists to help; C
  needs a content hash the system has never recorded, so it would only help ops deployed **after**
  the change.

**Affected docs:** `docs/PLANNER.md` §9 (blockquote + §9.2 redo-rules table `apps[*].install` row),
`CLAUDE.md` (W2 blockquote gains the carve-out), `docs/WORK_ORDER_responder_op_row_drift.md`
(STATUS → BUILT, option A), `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` (GAP-054, new).

**Affected code:** `axrange/planner/plandiff.py` (`reapplyable_ops`, `reapply_ineligibility_reason`,
`render_value`, `content_digest`), `axrange/api/service.py` (`resolve_deploy_plan(..., reapply=)`,
`DeployResolution.reapply_ops`, `_mutation_refusal_message`), `axrange/cli/main.py` (`--reapply`
flag, diff printer), `axrange/api/dtos.py` (`CreateDeployRequest.reapply`).

**Links:** commit `4c2b7679` (build), merged at `f2162e55`.

## 2026-09-02 — `drive: command`: a protocol-faithful traffic driver for personas

**Authority:** user, 2026-09-02 — accepting `docs/WORK_ORDER_protocol_faithful_traffic.md` as
proposed, including its version-locked `PSN-030` enum widening.

**What changed:**

- A fourth persona `drive:` kind, `command`, joins `scripted` / `replay` / `real-service`: at each
  emit's firing instant it runs a **real protocol client tool** (`dig` / `curl` / `ntpdate` /
  `ping`) instead of opening a socket and pushing tagged filler bytes at a port. Covers five v1
  protos (dns/http/https/ntp/icmp) via a driver-owned per-proto argv template table (work order
  §3.2 option (a) — personas stay portable; an unmapped proto is a fail-loud authoring error, never
  a silent substitution).
- Reuses the existing pipeline verbatim: the persona-document schema (no new field), orchestrator-
  side peer resolution (`o2control.traffic._resolve_peers`), the transient-`systemd-run` launch
  primitive (never `nohup`, LES-086), the instance lifecycle helpers, the QGA delivery path
  (base64-in-command, LES-002), and the `get_driver` dispatch — adding one `_DRIVERS` entry was the
  entire execution-side wiring change; `o2control/traffic.py` needed no edit.
- **Containment is structural, not conventional** — the load-bearing safety property. Every peer
  address is resolved to a concrete twin host IP in the orchestrator and baked into the rendered
  client as a literal; `_check_containment` refuses to render any argv whose address token is a
  bare hostname with no `--resolve`/`--connect-to` binding, so the guest can never be made to
  resolve a name.
- `volume:` resolves to invocation count (`messages`) + inter-invocation sleep (`gap_s`); `bytes:`
  is spent only by `ping -s` and is otherwise announced-once-and-ignored at arm time (never
  silently dropped). An unmapped proto and a missing in-guest binary both fail loud (probed at
  start, guarded a second time per-fire). **Linux only** — a Windows `command` persona is a loud
  `PersonaDriverError`, not a silent no-op (no PowerShell template table exists, and no Windows
  responder exists to answer one either). No shipped catalog persona uses `drive: command` yet; the
  tool set belongs in an image bake (`docs/IMAGE_BUILD.md`), not a standing twin's `install:` list
  — adding one there trips `--update`'s mutation refusal (see the entry immediately above).
- **Review round (`a0f6bb67`, 2026-09-03) closed four gaps the first cut left open:** an authored
  `emits[].transport:` is now honoured (dig gains `+tcp`) or loudly refused when a template's tool
  genuinely can't drive it (ntp+tcp, any explicit transport on icmp) — a derived (unset) transport
  is never consulted; a template/peer-consistency assert confirms every containment-accepted argv
  actually addresses the peer it claims to, resolving **through** a `--resolve`/`--connect-to`
  binding rather than false-positiving on the legitimate escape hatch; the tool probe's
  missing-binary lines are now tagged (`MISSING:`) and filtered on the tag, so a login shell's
  profile/banner text can't be misread as a missing tool; and `ScriptedDriver`/`CommandDriver`'s
  near-duplicate `_start_linux` bodies were factored into `drivers.base.start_linux_python_client`
  (LES-025 reuse). Eight new tests cover curl's `-q`/`--noproxy`/no-`-L` containment surface,
  bound-to-non-IP refusal, zero-address/wrong-peer template-bug refusals, explicit-transport
  honouring/refusal, and the login-shell-banner fix.
- **Version-locked, additive:** `docs/VALIDATOR.md` `PSN-030`'s drive enum widens to
  `scripted | replay | real-service | command` — rule id/category/severity/pass/count all unchanged
  (114, the `PSN-100`/`PSN-110` precedent). No `IR_SCHEMA`/`OP_CATALOG`/`PLANNER`/`ADAPTER`/
  `IMAGE_MANIFEST` change; no new op type; no schema-version bump.
- **Deliberately deferred, gated:** the gated `dev45` live-verify is **not** run against the
  standing `corpapcn-twin` (retrofitting `dnsutils`/`curl`/`ntpdate` onto it would itself trip the
  mutation refusal, work order §5.2) — it is gated additionally on the aximprint companion (the
  internet sink becoming real services, work order §5.1), and will run against a small
  purpose-built twin with the tools baked in. See `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-056.

**Why:**

- Detection rules match protocol **semantics** (a DNS query name, an RC4 TGS-REP), never a port
  number. The `scripted` driver's filler-byte traffic can never trigger one and therefore can never
  produce a realistic false-positive rate either — half of what a detection-validation range is for
  (`CLAUDE.md`'s stated primary purpose). This does **not** move the CorpAPCN fidelity loop's
  current axes (they count edges, not protocols — GAP-C20, aximprint) but is a precondition for the
  platform's actual purpose, independent of that loop.

**Affected docs:** `docs/VALIDATOR.md` (PSN-030 row), `docs/OP_CATALOG.md` (OP-TFC-010
description), `docs/design/07-traffic-personas.md` (already updated in the diff — the drive-kind
table gains a fourth row), `CLAUDE.md` (new blockquote), `docs/WORK_ORDER_protocol_faithful_traffic.md`
(STATUS → BUILT, axRange half; live-verify gated).

**Affected code:** `axrange/personas/schema.py` (`DRIVE_KINDS`),
`axrange/personas/drivers/command.py` (new), `axrange/personas/drivers/base.py`
(`start_linux_python_client`), `axrange/personas/drivers/__init__.py`,
`axrange/validator/catalog.py` (PSN-030 message template).

**Links:** commits `a48d4e5d` (build), `a0f6bb67` (review-round fix), merged at `4ebdf49c`.

during the 2026-08-28 → 2026-08-31 CorpAPCN fidelity-loop session, which closed two previously-

doc-writer catch-up pass, dated to when the events actually happened.

## 2026-08-27 — Logical/Physical semantics restored (aximprint topology-export wired) + code-review fixes (M1/M2/M3) + twin-configs search path

**Authority:** user (the semantic restoration decision, the multi-dir search-path direction, and the
three MUST-FIX findings below are all user/code-review directed) + implementation (Claude Code) for
the concrete fixes. Directed by the same `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_selector_and_stale_deploys.md`
session as the entry immediately below, later the same day, after a concurrent code review and the
aximprint side landing its own half of the contract.

**Supersedes:** the "Console selector unstuck..." entry immediately below's Logical/Physical framing
(NOT its defect-1/defect-2 content, which stands) — see that entry's own corrected `Supersedes:` note.

**What changed:**

1. **Logical/Physical restored to their ORIGINAL, user-authored meaning** (the "Twin Topology view
   (first cut)" entry two below): **Logical** = the aximprint IR's `segments`+`edges` (the conversation
   graph); **Physical** = the aximprint IR's full observed node census (every node fully AND partially
   discovered — a deployed twin is a scaled-down SUBSET). A shipped `twin_config` is explicitly NOT an
   acceptable substitute for either (a lossy projection, no traffic edges at all) — the earlier same-day
   entry's substitution of the twin-config's own structure for both tabs is reversed. That content
   SURVIVES as a new **fourth tab, "Structure"** (the axRange twin's own declarative structure +
   `/api/deploys/{id}/inventory` placement facts) — useful on its own, never again presented as Logical
   or Physical. **Deployed** is unchanged throughout (structure + inventory + live op status).
2. **New read surface: the aximprint cast-topology-export artifact** (`<id>.topology.json`, emitted by
   `aximprint-axrange/scripts/install_cast_as_twin.py` BESIDE the twin-config/imprint it casts from —
   `twin-configs-cast/corpapcn-twin/corpapcn-twin.topology.json` is the first real instance: 786 nodes /
   32 segments / 1437 edges, 356 role-classified). New `axrange/api/topology_export.py` (mirrors
   `axrange.api.imprints`'s posture exactly — reads a checked-in/located FILE only, never imports
   aximprint or touches its duckdb store) + `TopologyExport*` DTOs (`dtos.py`) +
   `GET /api/scenarios/{id}/topology-export?source=twin-config|imprint` (`service.topology_export`,
   `app.py`). Resolution piggybacks on `axrange.twinconfig.registry.load_twin_config_raw`'s REAL
   (non-materialized) source path — `materialize_twin_config`'s tempfile has no sibling anything — so no
   new catalog-directory concept was needed; the SAME twin-configs/imprints catalogs already in place
   serve it. 404 (not an error) when no sibling file exists, which is every twin-config/imprint today
   except `corpapcn-twin`; `TwinTopologyScreen`'s Logical/Physical tabs render the exact wording
   "the imprint's conversation graph is not exported yet" for that case, never a blank canvas, never a
   silent fallback to Structure's data. New `src/components/twin/TopologyExportView.tsx` renders it —
   TABULAR (segments/edges/nodes tables with an `evidence_class` badge on every row — constraint 2, work
   order: render observed vs. imputed distinctly, never flatten), a deliberate scope call recorded in
   that file's own docstring: `~/apcn/topology`'s tiered/arc/sankey renderer is a bespoke layout engine
   built for one capture's traffic volumes; a sortable table gives full, honest access to the SAME
   `TopologyExport` model today, and a future diagram can read it unchanged.
3. **M1 (code review) — the scenario/twin-config/imprint fallback chain silently hijacked a
   cross-catalog id collision.** `corp-enterprise` exists in BOTH the shipped twin-configs catalog (33
   nodes, `domain-controller`) and the Track-C imprints fixture (43 nodes, `dc01`); the id-only chain
   (twin-config tried before imprint) always resolved the twin-config even when a caller meant the
   imprint — the WRONG graph rendering with no error at all, worse than a 404 (LES-196's blank canvas is
   at least honestly broken). Fixed: `GET /api/scenarios/{id}/topology` and `.../nodes/{node_id}` gained
   an explicit `?source=scenario|twin-config|imprint` (`service._resolve_read_ir_path`, now returning
   `(path, is_temp)`), pinning resolution to exactly one catalog; omitted, the original chain runs
   unchanged (back-compat). `TwinTopologyScreen`/`ControlToolbar` both now pass it (`sourceKind`,
   tracked since the picker already needs to know which catalog an id came from to disambiguate the
   SAME collision in the option list — see M2). Regression-pinned against the real fixture content
   (`dc01` vs `domain-controller`), not a shape both branches share.
4. **M2 (code review) — the picker listed ids its verbs rejected.** `ControlToolbar` used to hand-roll
   its own two-catalog (scenario+twin-config) fetch instead of reusing `io/sources.ts:listAllSources`
   (whose own docstring already, wrongly, claimed it as a consumer) — now it does, gaining imprints too.
   Every option's value is `kind:id`, not a bare id (mirroring `TwinTopologyScreen`'s own fix for the
   same collision). Deploy now dispatches `{config: id}`/`{imprint: id}`/`{scenario: id}` correctly (the
   backend `CreateDeployRequest` has supported all three since aximprint Track C landed; only the
   frontend never sent the right field). Validate/Plan are honestly DISABLED for a non-scenario pick
   (with an explanatory tooltip) rather than left to reliably 404 — `/api/validate`/`/api/plan` have no
   `config`/`imprint` source field on the backend at all today, unlike Deploy; extending them is real,
   separate backend work, out of this pass's scope and recorded here rather than silently left broken.
5. **M3 (not a code change) — a concurrent fidelity-experiment file in this shared tree
   (`twin-configs-cast/corpapcn-twin/corpapcn-twin.yaml`) must never be swept into a commit alongside
   this work.** Recorded as a git-staging discipline note for whoever commits this: stage files
   explicitly, never `git add -A`/`commit -a` in this tree.
6. **S7 — twin-configs search path (the concrete "not sufficient" gap in the entry below's
   `--twin-configs-dir` flag).** `axrange.twinconfig.registry` now accepts a colon-separated STRING of
   multiple directories wherever it previously took one (`search_dirs`, mirroring
   `AXRANGE_PERSONAS_DIR`'s own colon-separated idiom) — `axrange serve --twin-configs-dir
   "twin-configs:twin-configs-cast"` lists the shipped catalog AND a cast/working catalog together,
   instead of one replacing the other. A single directory with no `:` round-trips unchanged (every
   existing caller, test fixture, and the CLI's historical single-dir flag are unaffected). On an id
   collision across directories the LATER one in the search path wins (same override direction as
   personas). `list_twin_configs`/`load_twin_config_raw`/`load_twin_config` all inherit this for free —
   the API and CLI (`--twin-configs-dir`) never needed their own change, since both already just thread
   the configured string straight through. Live-verified against the real dev45 content: a scratch
   `serve --twin-configs-dir "twin-configs:twin-configs-cast"` listed all 6 configs (5 shipped +
   `corpapcn-twin`) and served its real topology-export end-to-end.
7. **S8 (code review) — the twin-config/imprint catalog fallbacks in `_resolve_read_ir_path` had no
   path-traversal guard**, unlike `ApiSettings.scenario_path`/`axrange.api.imprints._imprint_dir`, which
   already enforce one — `axrange.twinconfig.registry` trusts its caller and has none of its own. Fixed
   with a shared `_is_safe_catalog_ref` (service.py), applied to both branches and to the new
   topology-export resolver.
8. **S2 (code review) — `materialize_twin_config` never deleted its own tempfile.** Every
   topology/node-detail read against a twin-config or imprint id leaked one (~13 KB, ~150 ms to create)
   forever. `_parse_ir_only` now deletes it in a `finally` block when `_resolve_read_ir_path` reports
   `is_temp=True`; a REAL scenario catalog file (`is_temp=False`) is never touched (regression-pinned
   both ways).

**Not done this pass (recorded, not silently dropped):** Validate/Plan's own `config`/`imprint`
backend support (see M2 above); S3 (`o2/grafana/dashboards/deploy-op-status.json`'s `?status=all` need
and the `/api/deploys/latest` unfiltered-by-design decision); S9 (`docs/design/11-api-ui.md` still
documents the pre-`?status=`/`?source=` API contract). Each is a scoped, well-understood follow-up, not
an unknown.

**[Corrected 2026-09-03, Authority: implementation (Claude Code):** this work sat uncommitted for a
week and landed as commit `fa0520d4` (2026-09-02), which also closed S3 and S9 above in the same
pass — the dashboard's three `/api/deploys` queries now carry `?status=all`, and
`docs/design/11-api-ui.md`'s endpoint table documents `?status=`, `?source=`, and the new
topology-export route. Validate/Plan's `config`/`imprint` backend support (the one item M2 left open)
remains genuinely not done. See `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` and LES-197 for how a "code-complete,
verified" change sat uncommitted long enough for a sibling commit (`6b340504`, 2026-08-28) to sweep
half of it in, leaving HEAD unable to start `axrange serve` until `fa0520d4` restored the rest.]

**Status:** unit/integration-verified — Python 1941 passed / 12 skipped (+25 from this pass, zero
regressions); plugin jest 26/26, webpack build clean, `tsc --noEmit` diffed against a stashed baseline
(LES-042) — zero new finding types beyond the pre-existing `TS2686`/JSX-runtime-config-mismatch noise.
The full chain (multi-dir search path -> twin-config resolution -> topology-export read -> DTO) was
live-verified against dev45's REAL `corpapcn-twin` content via a scratch `serve` instance on a spare
port; the shared live instance on :8080 was never restarted or touched by this work (it was
independently restarted by someone else mid-session, observed but not acted on). No gated `dev45`
live-verify of the PANEL itself (Grafana + a browser) has run.

**Files:** `axrange/api/topology_export.py` (new), `axrange/api/dtos.py` (+`TopologyExport*`),
`axrange/api/service.py` (`_resolve_read_ir_path` returns `(path, is_temp)` + `source=`,
`_is_safe_catalog_ref`, `topology_export`), `axrange/api/app.py` (`?source=` on topology/node-detail,
new `/topology-export` route), `axrange/twinconfig/registry.py` (`search_dirs`, multi-dir
`list_twin_configs`/`load_twin_config_raw`/`load_twin_config`);
`o2/grafana/plugins/axellio-axrange-control-panel/src/io/api.ts` (+`TopologyExport*` types,
`getTopologyExport`, `source` params, `CreateDeployRequest.config`/`imprint`),
`src/components/ControlToolbar.tsx` (listAllSources reuse, `kind:id` dispatch, Validate/Plan gating),
`src/components/AxrangeControlPanel.tsx` (`scenarioKind` tracking), `src/components/twin/
{TwinTopologyScreen,twinViewState}.ts(x)` (4-tab restructure), `src/components/twin/
TopologyExportView.tsx` (new); tests: `tests/test_api.py`, `tests/test_twinconfig_registry.py`,
`o2/grafana/.../src/components/twin/twinViewState.test.ts` (rewritten for the 4-state model).
