## 2026-08-27 — Console selector unstuck + `/api/deploys` status filter + Twin Topology Logical/Physical wired live

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code). Directed by `docs/WORK_ORDER_console_selector_and_stale_deploys.md`
(Authority: user, incl. its "SCOPE EXPANSION" addendum, same date) — a live-panel bug report against
`corpapcn-twin-580f4c94` on dev45.

**Supersedes:** the "Twin Topology view (first cut)" entry immediately below (same date, earlier same
day) — **correction, 2026-08-27, Authority: user:** the paragraph that used to stand here claimed the
"first cut" entry's Logical/Physical framing was "more conservative than necessary." That was wrong.
The first-cut semantics (Logical/Physical = the aximprint IR's conversation graph / full observed node
census; a shipped `twin_config` is explicitly NOT an acceptable substitute for either) were a
deliberate, user-authored decision, not an overcautious placeholder — reversing it without confirming
was itself a Design Document Maintenance violation (`CLAUDE.md`: a decision reversal is a confirm-first
trigger). The user caught this same-day and directed a restore; see the entry immediately ABOVE this
one ("Logical/Physical semantics restored...") for the correction and the topology-export read surface
that makes the restored semantics non-empty. This entry's OTHER content (defects 1/2, the selector fix,
the status filter) stands unchanged and un-superseded.

**What changed** (two measured defects + a scope expansion the user raised while reviewing the fix):

- **Defect 1 — the Console's scenario selector was structurally empty.** `axrange/api/service.py`'s
  `_resolve_read_ir_path` (the read-only topology/node-detail resolver LES-196 introduced for imprint
  ids) now ALSO falls through to the twin-configs catalog before imprints — `scenario → twin-config →
  imprint`, mirroring `create_deploy`'s own three-way source split. `GET /api/twin-configs` already
  existed (contrary to the work order's initial belief that no twin-config route existed at all — see
  "found wrong" below) but had no way to point a running `axrange serve` at a non-default catalog dir;
  `--twin-configs-dir` is now a real `axrange serve` CLI flag (`cli/main.py`, `api/serve.py`), mirroring
  `--scenarios-dir` exactly (single configurable dir, not a union of several — matches every other
  `*_dir` setting in `ApiSettings`).
- **Defect 2 — `GET /api/deploys` returned destroyed deploys with no filter.** New `status` query param
  (`app.py`/`service.list_deploys`): unset → DEFAULT excludes `destroyed` (an unfiltered Console shows
  only live deploys); `?status=all` → full history; `?status=<one value>` → exactly that status.
  `StateStore.deploys_matching` gained a `status:` filter param (additive; `planner/state.py`) so
  filtering happens IN SQL before `LIMIT`, not after — a destroyed row's `updated_at` advances every
  time one is torn down, so a naive fetch-then-filter would silently under-return once a burst of
  recent destroys crowds a small page (regression-pinned in `tests/test_planner_state.py`).
- **Scope expansion — Twin Topology's Logical/Physical tabs, previously stubbed "pending aximprint
  topology-export," are wired to data that already existed.** `Logical` (any of the three source kinds,
  deployed or not) and `Physical` (vmid/PVE-node/vnet-bridge/NIC — needs a live deploy) both reuse
  `DeployedTopology`/`computeLayout` unchanged (it already tolerated `inventory=null`), fed by
  `GET /api/scenarios/{id}/topology` (now three-way-resolving) and `GET /api/deploys/{id}/inventory` —
  no new backend route. New `src/io/sources.ts` (`listAllSources`) is the ONE fetch merging
  scenarios+twin-configs+imprints for `TwinTopologyScreen`'s picker (view-only — never a
  lifecycle-verb mismatch risk, unlike `ControlToolbar`'s Validate/Plan/Deploy-oriented picker, which
  deliberately stays scenario+twin-config only: imprints deploy through their OWN dedicated
  `/api/imprints/{id}/deploy` flow, not the generic `scenario`/`config` body fields). New
  `src/components/twin/twinViewState.ts` (`resolveTwinViewRenderState`) is the pure "what does this
  tab render" decision — split out and unit-tested (14 cases) the same way `topology/layouts/canvas.ts`
  is, specifically to guarantee constraint 1: every non-diagram case names itself
  (`empty-no-source`/`loading`/`error`/`not-applicable`+reason) — there is no code path that falls
  through to a blank render. `AxrangeControlPanel.tsx`'s canvas-tab topology load also gained an
  explicit `topologyError` (previously a 404/failure there was swallowed silently).
- **Found live-testing, fixed in the same pass:** this repo's OWN fixtures collide — `corp-enterprise`
  is both a shipped twin-config AND the aximprint Track-C fixture imprint. A plain `<option value={id}>`
  couldn't disambiguate which one the operator picked (the backend always resolves deterministically
  either way, so this was a messaging/UX bug, not a wrong-data bug) — fixed by encoding
  `kind:id` in the picker's option value instead of the bare id.

**Why:** "the panel asks the operator to choose from a list that structurally cannot contain the thing
they deployed" (defect 1) and "the state store is correct — nothing to purge" but the endpoint
"faithfully returns rows the caller never wanted" (defect 2) are both API-shape problems, fixed at the
API so every consumer (CLI `list-deploys` unaffected — it never filtered — and every future panel)
inherits the fix rather than re-implementing the same predicate. The scope expansion's own instruction
("the architectural lesson matters more than the visuals") is why this does NOT attempt an apcn-topology
port: apcn's tiered/arc/sankey renderer is traffic-volume-driven (built from a real capture's byte
counts), and axRange's declarative IR carries no observed traffic at all — there is nothing for an
apcn-style arc band to plot. What travels instead is the lesson: one composed model per view, reused
published endpoints, explicit non-diagram states.

**Decided (defect 2 default):** `active`-by-default at the API — `?status=` unset means live-only.
Considered "the panel filters instead" and rejected it: `TwinTopologyScreen`'s pre-existing `deployed`
tab already had its OWN client-side `.filter((d) => d.status !== 'destroyed')`, which is exactly the
"each consumer re-implements the same predicate" the work order warned against — proof, not just
theory, that a client-side filter drifts. That redundant filter is now dead code removed by the same
change (the endpoint's default supersedes it).

**Status:** unit/integration-verified (Python: 1916 passed / 12 skipped, +10 new; plugin: jest 22/22,
webpack build clean, `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` diffed against a stashed baseline per LES-042 — zero new
finding TYPES in either, only pre-existing systemic noise (`TS2686`/`react/react-in-jsx-scope` from the
plugin's automatic-JSX-runtime vs. standalone-tsc/eslint config mismatch, and `react-hooks/set-state-
in-effect` from this plugin's already-established data-loading-effect idiom) — see LES-042's
generalization note below). Both API fixes were ALSO spot-verified against dev45's real, live 43-row
`state.db` and real `target-environments/`/`twin-configs/`/imprint-fixture content via a scratch
`axrange serve` instance on a spare port — the shared live instance on :8080 (serving the concurrent
`corpapcn-twin` fidelity experiment) was never restarted or touched. No gated `dev45` live-verify of the
PANEL itself (Grafana + a browser) has run; that is real UI, not curlable.

**Files:** `axrange/api/service.py` (`_resolve_read_ir_path` twin-config fallback, `list_deploys` status
filter), `axrange/api/app.py` (`/api/deploys?status=`), `axrange/api/serve.py` +
`axrange/cli/main.py` (`--twin-configs-dir` on `axrange serve`), `axrange/planner/state.py`
(`deploys_matching(status=)`); `o2/grafana/plugins/axellio-axrange-control-panel/src/io/{api,sources}.ts`,
`src/components/{ControlToolbar,AxrangeControlPanel}.tsx`,
`src/components/twin/{TwinTopologyScreen,twinViewState}.ts(x)` (new: `sources.ts`, `twinViewState.ts`);
tests: `tests/test_api.py`, `tests/test_planner_state.py`,
`o2/grafana/.../src/components/twin/twinViewState.test.ts` (new).

## 2026-08-27 — aximprint integration — Twin Topology view (first cut): Deployed tab from te config; Logical/Physical pending the aximprint topology-export

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- New **Twin Topology** screen in the Grafana control-panel plugin (`o2/grafana/plugins/axellio-axrange-control-panel/`) — a third top-level view alongside Topology/Imprints (`AxrangeControlPanel.tsx` toggle now Topology/Twin/Imprints), with three source-mapped tabs: **Deployed** ← te config, **Logical** + **Physical** ← aximprint IR.
- **Deployed tab BUILT + live** (`src/components/twin/DeployedTopology.tsx`): an apcn-*style* (not a port) deterministic SVG — WAN → router band → segment columns → host chips — with per-host role/IP and a **live deploy-status dot**, sourced from `GET /api/scenarios/{id}/topology` (structure + status) enriched by a new `GET /api/deploys/{id}/inventory` client method + `TwinInventory` types (`src/io/api.ts`). Structure-only by design: a te twin has no traffic at deploy time, so apcn's traffic weighting is a deferred later overlay (from the twin's own tap/tfc telemetry, NOT aximprint).
- **Logical/Physical tabs render an honest "pending aximprint topology-export" state** (`src/components/twin/TwinTopologyScreen.tsx`), not a fabricated graph — they need a NEW aximprint emit (the shipped `twin_config` is a lossy projection that drops exactly the partially-discovered hosts the Physical census is about, and carries no traffic edges).

**Why:**
- User request (2026-08-27): topology views for a given twin modeled on `~/apcn/topology`'s logical/physical/deployed split. Source mapping decided by the user — logical + physical from aximprint's IR (the full observed node census, fully AND partially discovered from the tap vantage), deployed from te config (a scaled-down SUBSET of it). aximprint is **L3-only by design** (no L2/switch fabric — Explore of `aximprint-axrange/src/aximprint/ir/`), so "physical" here means observed-node completeness, not switch-level; confirmed with the user after an initial mis-scoping.

**Status:** first cut. **Builds clean** (webpack + jest 8/8; no new type errors beyond the documented TS2686 React-UMD baseline, LES-042). Plugin TS is outside pytest (**1918** unchanged). **Not yet** through the tester/reviewer stages for the twin components; no live-verify. The linchpin follow-up is the cross-repo **aximprint topology-export contract** — an apcn-model-shaped JSON artifact projecting `ir_entity` + `ir_unseen` + `ir_edge` + `ir_segment` + `ir_identity` — which unblocks Logical + Physical and lets them reuse apcn's traffic-rich renderer. Coexists with the old bake-off "Topology" tab for now (consolidation TBD).

**Files:** `src/components/twin/{TwinTopologyScreen,DeployedTopology}.tsx` (new), `src/io/api.ts` (+`TwinInventory`/`HostInventory`/`HostNet` + `getDeployInventory`), `src/components/AxrangeControlPanel.tsx` (twin view wired into the toggle).

## 2026-08-25 — aximprint integration — Track C slices 2+3: "deploy as twin" backend action + Grafana Imprints Console UI

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code). Design-of-record: `docs/design/aximprint-integration/`
(`WORK_ORDERS.md` Track C) + `docs/WORK_ORDER_aximprint_console.md`. Follow-on to Track C slice 1
(entry immediately below, same date, committed at `ed79cdc`).

**What changed:** the other two of Track C's three §2 scope items — slice 1 covered only **read +
provenance**; this pass builds **deploy action** (PART A) and **UI** (PART B):

- **PART A — backend "deploy as twin" (`axrange/api/service.py`, `dtos.py`, `app.py`):**
  - `imprint` becomes a THIRD mutually-exclusive deploy source in `create_deploy`, alongside
    `scenario`/`config` (`sum((has_scenario, has_config, has_imprint)) > 1` → 400; none set → 400;
    `params` now valid with `config` OR `imprint`, previously `config`-only).
  - New `_resolve_imprint_scenario_path` — the imprint-sourced sibling of the existing
    `_resolve_config_scenario_path` (LES-037's expander-reuse rule): an imprint's `<id>.yaml` is
    byte-for-byte a `twin_config` document at the identical `<catalog_dir>/<id>/<id>.yaml` subfolder
    shape `axrange.twinconfig.registry` already discovers, so "deploy this imprint" is
    "expand+validate+plan+deploy this catalog entry" through the exact same `materialize_twin_config`
    call — only the catalog directory differs. It calls `axrange.api.imprints.load_imprint_summary`
    FIRST to get the established 404 (`ImprintNotFoundError`) / 400 (`ImprintDataError`) split
    (reusing slice 1's M2 id-cross-check, LES-195) instead of letting an unknown/malformed imprint
    fall through to `materialize_twin_config`'s own flat-400 `TwinConfigNotFoundError`.
  - New thin wrappers: `imprint_plan` (pure preview — validate+plan only, no state.db write, no
    lease, no execution; calls the SAME `_validated_plan_for_path` the ordinary `plan()` calls) and
    `imprint_deploy` (a straight pass-through to `create_deploy(dtos.CreateDeployRequest(imprint=
    imprint_id, ...))` — NO second lease/resume/`--update`/executor implementation anywhere on this
    path).
  - New endpoints `POST /api/imprints/{id}/plan` (open, matching `/api/plan`'s pure/no-auth posture)
    and `POST /api/imprints/{id}/deploy` (write-token gated, matching every other
    `/api/deploys`-class write) — both share `create_deploy`'s existing `runner`/`background` test
    seams one-for-one.
  - New DTOs `ImprintPlanRequest`/`ImprintDeployRequest` (mirror `CreateDeployRequest`'s `--update`
    fields one-for-one; the response IS the existing `CreateDeployResponse`, not a new type) and a
    new `CreateDeployResponse.config_warnings` field threaded onto the wire — closing a review-noted
    CLI/API parity gap (`cli.main._resolve_scenario_or_config` always collects+prints expansion-time
    advisories; the API previously dropped them silently on the `config` path, and would have on the
    new `imprint` path too).

- **PART B — Grafana plugin (`o2/grafana/plugins/axellio-axrange-control-panel/`):**
  - `src/io/api.ts` — `Imprint*` interfaces (`ImprintListItem` / `ImprintSummary` /
    `ImprintTopologySegment` / `ImprintProvenanceRow` / `ImprintProvenance` / `ImprintDeployOptions`)
    plus five new `AxrangeApi` methods (`getImprints` / `getImprint` / `getImprintProvenance` /
    `planImprint` / `deployImprint`).
  - `src/types.ts` + `src/module.ts` — new `AxrangeControlOptions.initialView: 'topology' |
    'imprints'` panel option (default `'topology'`, so every dashboard built before this option
    existed keeps behaving identically).
  - Two new components under `src/components/imprints/`: `ImprintsScreen.tsx` (imprint list, stat
    tiles, provenance table, target-node picker, plan-preview, "Deploy as twin" action) and
    `ImprintTopologyPanel.tsx` (a confidence-shaded learned-topology render).
  - `src/components/AxrangeControlPanel.tsx` — an in-panel `RadioButtonGroup` Topology/Imprints
    toggle seeded by `initialView` (still operator-flippable live), a `contentHeight = height -
    VIEW_BAR_H` definite-height content wrapper so both screens' `height:100%` roots resolve, and
    `handleImprintDeployed(scenario, id)` — the handoff that, after a successful "Deploy as twin,"
    switches back to the Topology screen with the new deploy selected on the SAME live
    op-tree/status machinery an ordinary scenario deploy already uses (no second status UI).
  - `README.md` — the consumed-routes list gains the five new imprint endpoints.

**Reuse posture (the load-bearing constraint both parts honor):** no second orchestration path
exists anywhere in this slice. The imprint deploy source reuses `create_deploy`'s existing
validate→plan→lease→executor sequence unchanged; `imprint_plan`/`imprint_deploy` are pass-throughs,
never reimplementations; the UI's "Deploy as twin" reuses the existing deploy-status/op-tree screen
rather than growing its own. **The coupling rule holds structurally, unchanged from slice 1:**
axRange still imports nothing from `aximprint`; the backend still reads only the two checked-in
fixture files per imprint.

**Three fixes from a code-review round, all landed pre-commit:**

1. **Blank-canvas handoff bug.** `GET /api/scenarios/{id}/topology` builds its base node/edge graph
   ONLY from the scenarios catalog; the `deploy` query param drives just the per-node STATUS
   overlay, never the base graph. Since an imprint id isn't a scenario-catalog id,
   `handleImprintDeployed`'s jump to Topology 404'd the base graph and rendered a blank canvas next
   to a live, correctly-updating op-tree. Fixed with new `service._resolve_read_ir_path`, which
   falls back to materializing the imprint's twin_config (base-graph-only: no param overrides, no
   provenance load) when the scenarios-catalog lookup 404s; `_parse_ir_only` now calls it, so both
   `topology` AND node-detail get the fix in one place. Generalized as new `docs/LESSONS.md`
   **LES-196**.
2. **Debounced the plan-preview effect.** `ImprintsScreen`'s auto-preview effect fired a full
   validate+plan `POST` on every keystroke in the target-node field; fixed with a 400ms debounce
   (`debouncedTarget`).
3. **Missing exception chain.** `_resolve_imprint_scenario_path`'s `ImprintDataError → ApiError(400,
   ...)` branch raised without `from e`, losing the original traceback context on a 400; added.

**Two NITs deliberately left, not fixed (known/acceptable, not bugs):**
- The plan-preview shows an "✗ invalid" label when the only problem is an unset `DEFAULT_NODE` (a
  target-node-selection gap, not a genuinely invalid twin_config) — cosmetic, and matches how the
  same preview already behaves for a `config`-sourced plan.
- Deploy-ability is gated on provenance-sidecar validity, even though provenance is read-only
  metadata never consumed by the deploy path itself — defensible ("an imprint is one unit": a
  corrupt sidecar is itself a signal something about the imprint's generation is untrustworthy), not
  slated for a fix.

**Not a version-locked change:** no IR_SCHEMA / OP_CATALOG / VALIDATOR / ADAPTER / IMAGE_MANIFEST /
PLANNER schema change; no new op type (stays 40); no new validator rule (stays 114); no
`scenario_schema_version` bump.

**Verification:** 16 new tests in `tests/test_api.py` (13 for PART A — plan/deploy success,
unknown-imprint 404, validation-failure clean-422-not-500, write-token gating, and the three-way
mutual-exclusion permutations across `scenario`/`config`/`imprint` — plus 3 from the review's
handoff fix — topology/node-detail imprint-fallback resolution and a truly-unknown-id
still-404s regression). Full suite: **1906 passed / 12 skipped / 0 failed, 1918 collected** (was
1890 passed / 12 skipped, 1902 collected after slice 1). Plugin side: `npm run build` clean, `npm
run test:ci` jest 8/8, a scoped `tsc` shows only the pre-existing `TS2686` React-UMD baseline
finding (LES-042, unrelated to this change). `python3 scripts/doc-stats.py --check` is clean after
reconciling eleven stale `1902 pytest tests` → `1918 pytest tests` counters in `CLAUDE.md` (drift
from this slice's own 16 new tests).

**Status — gated live-verify still PENDING.** Track C is now code/UI-complete and
unit/build-verified, but the work order's §4 "Done when" (a real learned imprint rendering in the
Console AND its "deploy as twin" action standing a twin up, verified on `dev45` behind the standard
gated live-verify) has **NOT run** — "deploy as twin" creates real VMs, so this is deliberately held
for the gate, not skipped. Do not read this entry as Track C being fully closed; see the updated
STATUS line in `docs/WORK_ORDER_aximprint_console.md`.

**Affected docs:**
- `CLAUDE.md` — new blockquote appended directly after the Track C slice 1 blockquote (Current
  State); eleven `1902 pytest tests` → `1918 pytest tests` counter corrections (`scripts/
  doc-stats.py --check`-flagged, from this slice's own test additions).
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — new LES-196.
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_aximprint_console.md` — STATUS line updated; the two NITs recorded as residuals.
- `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` — handoff rewritten for this session.

**Affected code:** `axrange/api/service.py`, `axrange/api/dtos.py`, `axrange/api/app.py` (modified);
`tests/test_api.py` (modified, +16); `o2/grafana/plugins/axellio-axrange-control-panel/src/io/
api.ts`, `src/types.ts`, `src/module.ts`, `src/components/AxrangeControlPanel.tsx`, `README.md`
(modified); `o2/grafana/plugins/axellio-axrange-control-panel/src/components/imprints/
{ImprintsScreen.tsx,ImprintTopologyPanel.tsx}` (new). **Not yet committed** — staged in the working

## 2026-08-25 — aximprint integration — Track C slice 1: belief-store/provenance READ surface

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code). Design-of-record: `docs/design/aximprint-integration/`
(`WORK_ORDERS.md` Track C) + the routable ticket `docs/WORK_ORDER_aximprint_console.md`, both
committed+pushed at `d1e8630` on `wip/aximprint-integration` ahead of this entry.

**What changed:** The first of Track C's three scope items (`docs/WORK_ORDER_aximprint_console.md`
§2: UI / **read + provenance** / deploy action) — the backend read surface only:

- **New `axrange/api/imprints.py`** — a directory/file catalog reader (mirrors
  `axrange.twinconfig.registry`'s directory-scan idiom, LES-025) over `axrange/api/fixtures/
  imprints/<id>/`, one subdirectory per imprint holding exactly two files: `<id>.yaml` (a genuine
  `twin_config` document, parsed with the real `TwinConfigFile` model, not a hand-rolled dict walk)
  and `<id>.provenance.json` (a per-field provenance sidecar — required because `twin_config` is
  `extra="forbid"`, so provenance cannot live inline). Derives an `ImprintSummary`
  (hosts/segments/services/personas/confidence/out-of-vocab) and parses `ProvenanceRow` lists; two
  typed errors (`ImprintNotFoundError`/`ImprintDataError`) map to 404/400 in `service.py`.
- **New fixture `axrange/api/fixtures/imprints/corp-enterprise/`** — a 42-host, 4-segment sample
  imprint that validates through the REAL pipeline (`expand_twin_config` + `validate_full`, 0
  errors/0 warnings) and the real CLI (`te validate --config` → PASS), not a synthetic shape that
  merely satisfies this reader.
- **3 new read-only endpoints** — `GET /api/imprints`, `GET /api/imprints/{id}`,
  `GET /api/imprints/{id}/provenance` — plus 6 new DTOs in `dtos.py` (`ImprintListItemDTO`,
  `ImprintListDTO`, `ImprintSegmentDTO`, `ImprintSummaryDTO`, `ImprintProvenanceRowDTO`,
  `ImprintProvenanceDTO`) and 3 `service.py` functions, all honoring the `api/` boundary rule (clean
  DTOs only, never `axrange.api.imprints`'s dataclasses on the wire). `ApiSettings` gains
  `imprints_dir`/`AXRANGE_IMPRINTS_DIR` (same unset-means-package-default posture as
  `twin_configs_dir`).
- **Coupling rule honored, structurally:** `imprints.py` reads only the two artifact FILES an
  imprint ships. It never imports `aximprint` and adds no dependency on it — axRange stays a
  one-way consumer of aximprint's *output shape*, never its code.
- **No new op type; no version-locked schema change** (IR/PLANNER/OP_CATALOG/VALIDATOR/ADAPTER/
  IMAGE_MANIFEST all untouched) — purely additive read-only surface over the existing engine.

**Two must-fix defects caught in code review, fixed pre-commit:**

- **M1 — fail-loud coercion of author-supplied sidecar scalars.** The original draft let a
  malformed sidecar numeric field (`days_observed: "ten"`, a summary scalar authored as a string, …)
  reach a pydantic DTO uncoerced, outside `service.py`'s `try/except ImprintDataError` — an unhandled
  `pydantic.ValidationError` would have surfaced as an HTTP 500 instead of a clean 400. Fixed with
  `_coerce_int`/`_coerce_float`/`_coerce_optional_int`/`_coerce_optional_str` helpers that raise the
  module's own typed `ImprintDataError` at the point of coercion, inside `imprints.py`, before any
  value reaches a DTO. **This is an instance of existing LES-110** ("every coercion of an
  author-supplied field at an expansion boundary must raise the module's typed error, never a bare
  `ValueError`/`TypeError`") — no new lesson recorded for it; LES-110's `Scope:` already covers this
  class of module.
- **M2 — publish the id the lookup resolves by; cross-check any content-declared id.** The original
  draft derived the published `id` from `twin_config.id` (content) rather than the directory name
  the caller actually looked up by, and never checked the sidecar's own `imprint_id` against
  either — a renamed/copy-pasted fixture directory could silently break `list`→`get` round-tripping,
  or worse, serve one imprint's provenance under another's URL with no error raised anywhere. Fixed:
  the directory name is now the sole canonical id (`list_imprints`/`load_imprint_summary` always
  publish it), and both loaders cross-check it against `twin_config.id` and the sidecar's
  `imprint_id`, failing loud (`ImprintDataError`) on any disagreement. **New `docs/LESSONS.md`
  LES-195** records this as a general rule for any directory/file catalog reader, not just this one.

**Why:** Track C is the one net-new, greenfield thread of the aximprint↔axRange integration — it
turns a learned belief store into something an operator can browse before deciding to deploy it as
a twin. Building the read surface against a checked-in fixture (rather than waiting on Track A's
real compiled output) keeps this slice parallel-safe, per the work order's own §3: "depends only on
the schema pin + the output shape, never blocks on Track A finishing."

**Scope honesty — this is SLICE 1 of a 3-item Track, not the Track's completion.** Still open, per
`docs/WORK_ORDER_aximprint_console.md` §2/§4: the Grafana Console UI (`o2/grafana/plugins/`, the
imprint browser/stat-tiles/topology-preview/provenance-table already designed at `docs/design/
aximprint-integration/Console.dc.html`) and the "deploy as twin" action (handing the drafted docs to
the existing `te`/`tfc`/`tap` deploy verbs) are both separate, not-yet-built follow-on slices, and
neither is exercised by this entry's tests. `CLAUDE.md`'s new blockquote says exactly this, not
"Track C complete."

**Verification:** 47 new tests — 37 in `tests/test_imprints_reader.py` exercising the reader module
directly (discovery, all three id-mismatch permutations, every numeric-coercion failure mode, the
real-pipeline expand+validate proof) + 10 in `tests/test_api.py` over real HTTP (5 happy-path
endpoint tests, plus 5 M1/M2 regression cases — a non-numeric summary scalar, a non-numeric
`days_observed`, a non-numeric provenance confidence, an id-mismatch, and a list that skips a
mismatched entry — each asserted as a clean 400/200, never a 500). Full suite: **1890 passed / 12
skipped / 0 failed, 1902 collected** (was 1843 passed / 12 skipped, 1855 collected at HEAD before
this slice's 47 new tests — the exact figure `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` recorded on 2026-08-14).

**Affected docs:**
- `CLAUDE.md` — new brief blockquote (Current State, end of the Tech Stack blockquote sequence) +
  the eleven `1855 pytest tests` → `1902 pytest tests` counter-drift corrections `scripts/
  doc-stats.py --check` flagged (unrelated to this slice's own test additions — those lines had
  already gone stale from work between 2026-08-14 and now)
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — new LES-195
- `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` — handoff rewritten for this session

**Affected code:** `axrange/api/imprints.py` (new); `axrange/api/fixtures/imprints/corp-enterprise/
{corp-enterprise.yaml,corp-enterprise.provenance.json}` (new); `axrange/api/dtos.py`,
`axrange/api/service.py`, `axrange/api/app.py`, `axrange/api/settings.py` (modified);
`tests/test_imprints_reader.py` (new), `tests/test_api.py` (modified). **Not yet committed** — this
slice's code + docs are staged in the working tree on `wip/aximprint-integration`, pending a gated
commit.

Track C; `docs/design/aximprint-integration/Console.dc.html`.

## 2026-08-14 — exr AD kill-chain: clock-sync step 0 added, so Kerberoast is robust to the GAP-037 DC skew without manual intervention

**Authority:** user (asked to add the clock-sync step 0 to the exr spec).

Follow-on to the exr run entry below. `twin-configs/corp-enterprise/exr-ad-killchain.yaml` gained two
`technique:`-less steps (so neither emits a spurious ATT&CK truth marker):
- **step 0 (clock sync):** `timedatectl set-ntp false` → read the DC's `currentTime` via an anonymous
  RootDSE LDAP query (`ldapsearch`, already a chain dependency — no new package) → `date -u -s` the
  attacker to it. Fixes the `KRB_AP_ERR_SKEW` that blocked Kerberoast (GAP-037's 6h-slow DC) by
  matching the attacker to the target, the way a real operator would.
- **step 8 (cleanup):** `set-ntp true` + restart `systemd-timesyncd`, so the attacker's clock returns
  to real time and the exercise leaves the box as it found it.

**`set-ntp false` first is load-bearing** — verified live that without it, systemd-timesyncd reverts
the `date -u -s` within ~2s (the first attempt at this step silently reverted for exactly that
reason). Best-effort throughout (`; true`): an unreadable DC time leaves the clock as-is and the chain
still runs (Kerberoast then skews, as before the step existed).

**Live-verified** by a clean replay: a same-host overlap guard (GAP-034/LES-116) first refused the
re-run against the still-standing prior exercise ("destroy the prior run first") — working as
designed — so the prior run was `exr destroy`'d, then the replay
(`exr-corp-enterprise-890c29b2-12a99ba4`, `playout_hash b55787556e336a3d` — changed, as a content
edit should) ran the full chain **autonomously**: step 0 synced (06:06 UTC), **Kerberoast returned a
real `$krb5tgs$23$*svc-sql$…` with no manual fix**, DCSync still dumped krbtgt, and step 8 restored
the attacker to real UTC (confirmed 12:07). This is the attacker-side workaround LES-194 flagged;
the durable twin-level fix (GAP-037 w32time) — which also fixes the measurement-window corruption
this cannot — remains open.

## 2026-08-14 — `exr` AD kill-chain run on the standing `corp-enterprise` twin: DCSync krbtgt dumped; Kerberoast blocked then unblocked by the GAP-037 clock skew

**Authority:** user (asked to run `exr` on corp-enterprise). The pre-run traffic quieting and the
clock-sync workaround are `implementation (Claude Code)`.

Ran the AD kill-chain exercise (`twin-configs/corp-enterprise/exr-ad-killchain.yaml`, name-targeted)
against the standing `corp-enterprise-890c29b2` twin — the run that was **deferred 2026-08-05** on a
dev45 upstream-egress outage (since resolved). Deploy `exr-corp-enterprise-890c29b2-525fab2c`,
`playout_hash ef233724b347966c`, status COMPLETED.

**Pre-run judgment call:** the twin was still carrying the 2026-08-13 max-capture-rate load
(`tfc-maxrate-l5`), which ran a `loadgen` persona **on the attacker node** and flooded all three
segments incl. the 1-vCPU `gw` router on the attacker→DC path. Latency-sensitive impacket steps would
have been at risk, so the traffic was switched to the realistic `tfc-default` profile first — freeing
the attacker and giving the exercise normal background noise rather than a synthetic flood (the
intended exercise pairing). Reversible (`tfc deploy tfc-maxrate-l5.yaml`).

**Scorecard** (all 7 techniques' truth markers emitted locally; read from the attacker's activity log):
- **T1046 recon — ✅** nmap found the full DC service surface (53/88/135/139/389/445/464/636/3268 open, 3389 filtered).
- **T1557.001 LLMNR poison — ⚪ unavailable by design** (Responder not staged on an apt-only attacker; the spec documents this branch — the attempted LLMNR traffic still shows on the wire).
- **T1087.002 SMB enum — ◐** `nxc` absent (apt-only kit); the `smbclient -N` fallback listed no shares (anonymous SMB restricted — realistic).
- **T1087.002 LDAP enum — ✅** anonymous bind returned the naming contexts; the `(servicePrincipalName=*)` search was correctly refused ("a successful bind must be completed" — anonymous directory search disabled, realistic AD hardening).
- **T1558.004 AS-REP roast — ✅** a real offline-crackable `$krb5asrep$23$jsmith@CORP.AXR.LAB:…` (only `jsmith` is `asrep_roastable`; the other users correctly report `UF_DONT_REQUIRE_PREAUTH` not set).
- **T1558.003 Kerberoast — ◐ → ✅** `GetUserSPNs` authenticated as `svc-sql` and **found the SPN** (`MSSQLSvc/sql01.corp.axr.lab:1433`), but `-request` failed `KRB_AP_ERR_SKEW`. Root-caused to a **6h-slow DC clock (GAP-037)** and completed by syncing the attacker to the DC — then returned a real `$krb5tgs$23$*svc-sql$CORP.AXR.LAB$…`.
- **T1003.006 DCSync — ✅ (the headline)** replicated over DRSUAPI as `bob-admin` (Domain Admins, lab-default password) and dumped **krbtgt**: NTLM `…:d9a27d0c40a00c9ef3812e3d4a3ad0c2:::` plus the aes256/aes128/des Kerberos keys — golden-ticket material, the domain-admin objective. Matches the 2026-07-25 Stage-5 proof.

**The clock-skew finding upgrades GAP-037 from latent to proven.** GAP-037 (Windows guests ~6h
unsynced) had predicted "mysterious failures traceable only to clock skew" but noted the auth-failure
risk was "not yet observed." It is now observed, with a clean diagnostic signature: **NTLM-based
DCSync and no-preauth AS-REP roast both succeed while pre-auth-requiring Kerberoast fails on skew** —
because the domain is internally self-consistent (members sync from the DC) and only the
non-domain-joined attacker sees the gap. Recorded as **LES-194** (the signature + the fix: sync the
*attacker* to the DC, never "fix" the DC).

**Measurement side (secondary):** the 7 `[axr-truth]` markers were written to the attacker's local
`.truth.log` (joinable by technique+host+ts) but **not shipped to Loki** — this exr's `measurement`
block declares no sink and no `axr-truth-ship-*` unit ran (consistent with GAP-021: the truth shipper
needs a configured Loki endpoint + TE-host egress). The attack playout itself is the primary proof
and is complete; the Grafana coverage join is the 2026-07-25 Stage-5 concern, not re-exercised here.

**Standing state:** the exr run is **ephemeral but persists until an explicit `exr destroy`** (GAP-035,
no auto-reap) — left standing. The twin, tap and (now `tfc-default`) traffic are unchanged; the
attacker's clock was set back 6h for the Kerberoast proof and **restored** to real UTC afterward.
`corp-enterprise` and every unrelated resource untouched.

## 2026-08-14 — W2 live-verify on dev45 PASSED: incremental twin update proven on a router-bearing twin, and an SDN apply does NOT detach running taps

**Authority:** user (asked for the gated live-verify to run). The tfc-fixture correction below is
`implementation (Claude Code)`.

The mandatory gated run for `axrange te deploy --update`
(`docs/WORK_ORDER_twin_incremental_update.md` §6). It deletes real VMs, which is why it was gated.
**All four proofs pass**, on the `w2-incr-verify` twin (gw + h-a + h-b, Debian, deploy
`w2-incr-verify-8c4a3377`, plan `w2-incr-verify-71de705c`):

- **(a) add.** Uncommenting `h-c` and re-running with `--update` executed **only h-c's ops** — 31
  skipped as Unchanged. gw/h-a/h-b kept their vmids and their uptimes GREW (90/89/90s → 199/197/197s),
  i.e. nothing was restarted or re-cloned. h-c came up on the shared vnet with `10.140.0.12/24` and
  pinged the router at 0% loss. **This is the M1 proof:** the twin has a `kind: router` node, so
  before the 2026-08-11 fix its plan-wide `served_hosts` roster made every add look like a mutation
  and the update refused. It did not refuse.
- **(b) remove.** `--update --allow-destroy` removed h-c's VM and its mirror and scoped 8 op rows to
  `destroyed`. Neighbour uptimes went 199/197/197s → 625/623/623s — untouched. The **shared vnet
  survived** (still in `vnets.cfg` and still a live bridge), because h-a/h-b still hold it (§3.2).
- **(c) re-run.** Identical command again: "Nothing to add or remove -- already up to date",
  0 ops reset, 31 ok. Clean no-op.
- **(d) dangling siblings.** BOTH bound sibling deploys raised the §3.5 warning naming `h-c`, with
  the remediation — the `tfc` deploy *and* the `tap` deploy, not just one.

Also proven in the same run: **the removal refuses without `--allow-destroy`** ("would remove 8
op(s)") and executes nothing — h-c was still running afterwards; and **`plan_id` is identical across
both the add and the remove**, which is the safety property the whole design hangs on.

**A long-standing open question, now closed.** `CLAUDE.md` has listed "whether an SDN apply detaches
running taps" as open since 2026-05-29, and a full `te destroy` never exercised it (everything is
going away anyway). An incremental removal is the first operation that applies SDN to a **surviving**
twin. With a tap bound to the `lab` segment and a pre-removal baseline of 64 captured frames per 30
pings, the post-removal count was **64 — identical** — and live ICMP between the surviving hosts was
visible decapsulated at the sink. **An SDN apply does not detach running mirrors.**

**Incidental confirmations:** the 2026-08-14 gretap-qdisc fix applied itself to a brand-new gretap
(`tapg_de44b4` came up `pfifo`/`qlen 20000` with no manual step), and the single-peer
self-resolution fix resolved correctly on a live deploy — h-a/h-b/h-c each peered a NEIGHBOUR
(`.10→.11`, `.11→.12`, `.12→.10`), never themselves.

**Fixture defect found and corrected:** `deploy-dev45/tfc-w2-incr-verify.yaml` assigned the
`corp-workstation` persona, which cannot bind on this deliberately minimal twin — that persona has
hard peers `domain-controller`/`file-server`/`egress-proxy`/`mail-server` and the twin carries none
of them, so `tfc deploy` would fail-loud on the first unresolvable peer (correct behaviour, and
unrelated to the update path). The fixture had been authored and "validated offline", which did not
include resolving peer roles against the twin's actual role set. Swapped to `bastion-jump`, whose
only hard peer is `corp-workstation` — a role h-a/h-b/h-c hold themselves, which is also what makes
it a live exercise of the self-resolution fix.

**Blast radius:** the cluster VM inventory is **byte-identical** to the pre-run baseline. The
standing `corp-enterprise` twin, its tap and tfc deploys, the O2 services VM, both XPress sinks and
every `img-*` template were untouched throughout, including across the teardown. One cosmetic
finding, the known GAP-050 class: the teardown's tap cascade logs a `mirror-descope` warning for
h-c against an already-removed VM (`Configuration file … does not exist`) — best-effort, and the
result was still `DESTROYED`, 9 resources removed, 0 not cleared.

## 2026-08-14 — Single-peer resolution excludes the sender's own host: a mesh persona was talking to itself over loopback

**Authority:** user (asked to fix the `peer_scope: one` self-resolution trap, found during the
2026-08-13 dev45 max-capture-rate run). The skip-with-warning treatment of "the role's only holder
is me" is `implementation (Claude Code)`.

**The defect.** `_resolve_peer_sets` (the `peer_scope: all` path) has always excluded the instance's
own host — that is **LES-170**, written after exactly this reasoning: self-directed traffic "never
crosses a tap point, inflating the message count without producing a single capturable frame."
`_resolve_peers` (the single-peer path) never got the same guard. So a persona whose `peer_role` is
a role the **sender also holds** — any mesh — resolved to its own IP and talked to itself over
loopback.

The failure is completely silent. The guest logs `sent … fails=0`, `tfc status` reports the
instance applied and healthy, and **not one frame reaches any sensor**. Found live on dev45
2026-08-13: a 3-host DMZ mesh reported perfect health at 1.5 MB/fire while every capture stage read
exactly zero.

**It also silently defeated shipped content.** `domain-controller` emits `drsuapi` replication to
`peer_role: domain-controller` and its own comment states the intent — inter-DC traffic "only exists
when the twin has MORE THAN ONE domain controller … Optional: resolve-if-present." Correct, and
defeated: on a single-DC twin the candidate list contained the DC itself, so the `optional:` skip
never fired and the DC "replicated" to loopback. The author reasoned right; the resolver did the
wrong thing anyway.

**What changed:** `_resolve_peers` takes `exclude_host_id` (threaded from the call site, exactly as
`_resolve_peer_sets` already was) and filters the candidate list **before** the empty check, so:
- **≥2 holders** — resolves to a real other host (round-robin unchanged, now over the filtered list).
- **sole holder is self** — skipped with a warning, NOT self-peered and NOT raised on. This is a
  normal topology (a one-DC twin), so failing would break every single-DC twin's `tfc deploy`; the
  guest client already logs-and-skips an emit with no resolved peer.
- **no host holds the role at all** — still fail-loud, unchanged (LES-098/LES-001). Keeping these
  two cases distinct is the whole reason the filter runs before the empty check rather than after.

**No contract change:** no new op, no schema bump, no IR or persona-catalog change.

**Lesson candidate for the doc-writer stage:** LES-170's rule was implemented on only one of the two
peer-resolution paths. When a lesson names a behaviour ("fan-out peer resolution must exclude the
instance's own host"), check every path that resolves the same thing — the sibling function is where
the same bug survives.

**Verification:** 4 new tests (exclusion on the single-peer path; sole-holder-is-self skips with a
warning; that case does NOT fail a hard emit while a genuinely absent role still does; and the
shipped `domain-controller` persona across one-DC and two-DC twins). All four **proven to fail
without the fix**. Suite: **1843 passed / 12 skipped**. **Live-verified on dev45** non-disruptively
via `tfc plan` against the standing single-DC `corp-enterprise` twin, which now emits
`peer_role 'domain-controller' resolves only to this instance's own host … skipping` instead of
quietly self-peering.

## 2026-08-14 — `provision-app` refreshes the apt index before installing (a cloned guest inherits its template's stale cache)

**Authority:** user (asked to fix `update_cache` in the provision-app render, after the 2026-08-13
dev45 max-capture-rate deploy hit it). The no-`cache_valid_time` call is
`implementation (Claude Code)` — see "the fix that didn't work" below.

**The defect.** `render_playbook` emitted `ansible.builtin.apt: {name, state: present}` and never
refreshed the package index. A cloned guest carries whatever apt cache its **template** baked, so
once the distro rotates its indices every URL in that cache 404s and a perfectly installable package
fails. The error names the *package*, not the stale cache —
`"No package matching 'tree' is available"` — so it reads like a bad package list and sends the
operator looking in the wrong place.

Hit live on dev45 2026-08-13: **four Debian hosts** (dmz-web, attacker, egress-proxy, file-server)
failed `provision-app` on a fresh `corp-enterprise` deploy. An in-guest `apt-get update` (25.7 MB)
fixed all four. The controlled comparison in the same run: `mail-server`, whose cache was refreshed
pre-emptively, **passed the same op** while the un-refreshed hosts failed.

**LES-024 already recorded the rule** ("ensure `update_cache` first (a fresh clone's package lists
are empty)"). It was simply never implemented on this path — and notably, the *shell* installers
(`_render_pkg_install_or_fail`, the default exporter bootstrap) always did it correctly with
`apt-get update ... || true` then a fail-loud install. This was an inconsistency between the two
delivery paths, not a systemic omission.

**What changed:** `config_exec/render.py`'s `render_playbook` opens the play with a
`refresh apt cache` task (`update_cache: true`, `failed_when: false`) when `package_manager == "apt"`
and there is at least one action. It mirrors the shell pattern deliberately: the refresh is
**non-fatal**, so a guest with no egress but a usable cache still installs, and the loud, specific
failure stays on the install task where the operator can read *which package* failed.
apt-only — `ansible.builtin.package` has no `update_cache` parameter and would error on it.

**The fix that didn't work, and why it matters.** The first cut also set `cache_valid_time: 3600`,
which looked like a free optimisation (skip a redundant refresh). It is actively wrong here: it
trusts apt's update-success **timestamp**, not whether the index actually exists. The live A/B
caught it — with `/var/lib/apt/lists` emptied but a recent stamp, Ansible reported the refresh `ok`
without doing anything and the install failed on the empty index **exactly as before**, i.e. the fix
silently did not apply. Dropped; an unconditional refresh costs ~5s / ~25MB once per provision-app
op, which is the right trade against installing off a broken index. Pinned by a test assertion.

**No contract change:** no new op, no schema bump, no IR change. `provision-app` (OP-APP-020)
behaviour only.

**Verification:** 3 new tests (refresh present + first + non-fatal + no `cache_valid_time`; non-apt
gets none; empty action list emits none), and 4 existing tests updated — they indexed `tasks[0]`
for the install, which the inserted task shifts, so they now select install tasks by name. The new
behavioural test was **proven to fail without the fix**. Suite: **1839 passed / 12 skipped**.
**Live-verified on dev45 (dmz-web, 957670)** as a three-way A/B/C against a deliberately emptied
`/var/lib/apt/lists`: old render FAILED, new-render-with-`cache_valid_time` FAILED, final render
`ok=2 changed=2 failed=0` with the package actually installed. Guest restored afterwards (package
removed, playbooks deleted, index repopulated).

## 2026-08-14 — Mirror gretap gets a deep FIFO: the node-default qdisc was silently dropping captured frames

**Authority:** user (asked to fix the gretap qdisc in the adapter, after the 2026-08-13 dev45
max-capture-rate run found it). Implementation choices (single int knob, assert-on-every-run,
0 as opt-out) are `implementation (Claude Code)`.

**The defect.** `attach-gre-tunnel` created the mirror gretap with `ip link add … type gretap` and
never touched its queueing discipline, so the netdev inherited the **node's default qdisc** — on
PVE 9.x that is `fq_codel`, an AQM whose entire purpose is to **drop packets** to hold latency down.
That is correct behaviour for a congested uplink and exactly wrong for a capture path: the mirrored
copy is fire-and-forget, so a drop is lost evidence, and there is no sender to signal or back off.
Nothing in the chain reported it as an error — the frames simply never arrived.

**Measured on dev45 (2026-08-13):** at ~2.38 Gbit/s a 90 s window lost **9,349 frames** with
`fq_codel` and **zero** after replacing it with `pfifo limit 20000` — worth roughly a **60% increase
in lossless mirror throughput**. It also removed drops at the *sink* (`tap104i1`), not just at the
gretap: fq_codel's bursty release was overrunning the guest's virtio ring downstream.

**What changed:**
- **`adapter_proxmox/ssh.py`, `attach_gre_tunnel`:** after the existing idempotent `mtu … up`
  assert, issue `ip link set <dev> txqueuelen N` + `tc qdisc replace dev <dev> root pfifo limit N`.
  Placed in the **idempotent** block deliberately, not the create branch: the op *adopts* a
  pre-existing netdev (Model C, §2 check-then-act), so a gretap built before this change would
  otherwise keep `fq_codel` forever. Asserting on every run is also what removes the manual
  "re-assert the qdisc after every `tap deploy`" step the live run needed.
- **`adapter_proxmox/config.py`:** new `AdapterConfig.mirror_gretap_txqueuelen`
  (`MIRROR_GRETAP_TXQUEUELEN`, default **20000**). **`0` is an explicit opt-out** — leave the node
  default alone, for a node whose qdisc is managed elsewhere. Parsed defensively (malformed →
  default, negative → 0) rather than with a bare `int()`, because `load_config` is called from a
  dozen independent CLI/API/o2control entry points and a new raise there would surface as a raw
  traceback on paths that never expected one (**LES-090**).
- The qdisc **kind** is deliberately NOT operator-tunable: it would have to be interpolated into an
  ssh command string, and the set of kinds that make sense for a mirror target is exactly one. The
  one knob is an int, so it cannot inject.
- `adapter.conf.example` + `config.py`'s example-writer document the knob and the reasoning.

**No contract change:** no new op type, no changed op signature, no schema bump. `OP_CATALOG` stays
at 40 op types; `VALIDATOR` unchanged. This is `attach-gre-tunnel` implementation behaviour.

**Verification:** 4 new tests in `tests/test_adapter_proxmox.py` — create path, **adopt path**
(the one that matters), the `0` opt-out, and defensive config parsing. The two behavioural tests were
each **proven to fail without the fix**. Suite: **1836 passed / 12 skipped**.
**Live-verified on dev45 the same day:** the standing twin's gretap was manually reset to
`fq_codel` + `qlen 1000`, a plain `axrange tap deploy` was re-run, and the adapter restored
`pfifo limit 20000` + `qlen 20000` by itself with no manual step — exercising the adopt path against
a live capture. A following 90 s window read tc 133,368 pps ≈ gretap 133,268 ≈ sink 133,372 with the
whole sink path clean.

**Still open (the next ceiling):** with the sink NIC now multiqueue (`queues=8`, 2026-08-13) and this
qdisc fixed, the binding constraint is that **all tapped ports mirror into one single-queue gretap
netdev**; `tc mirred` starts logging `overlimits` past ~2.6 Gbit/s. Raising that means per-sensor
gretap fan-out or a multiqueue-capable tunnel device — not attempted.

---

## 2026-08-13 — corp-enterprise max-capture-rate run (dev45): ~1.5 Gbit/s / ~132 kpps sustained lossless; sink multiqueue clears 31,733 drops; a single gretap fan-in is the new ceiling

**Authority:** user.

**What happened.** A capture-rate ramp against the standing `corp-enterprise` twin (deploy
`corp-enterprise-890c29b2`, plan `corp-enterprise-4fc62c80`, `N_WORKSTATIONS=6`) mirrored to the
external `XPress-SW-A` packetXpress sink, run in two phases (started 2026-08-13, the multiqueue
phase ran past midnight into 2026-08-14). Seven "rungs" (R1–R7) stepped offered load from ~14 Mbit/s
to the twin's full generating capacity, each measured with a drop-accounting harness (`measure.py`)
differencing tc's mirrored-packet count, the gretap's tx count, and the sink's own capture counters.

**Result — Phase 1 (single-queue sink):**

| Rung | Senders | Offered | At `gre-cap` | pps | Window | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1–R3 | 3 | 14–628 Mbit/s | matching | up to 53.9k | 25 s | clean |
| **R4** | **3** | **1,521 Mbit/s** | **1,521 Mbit/s** | **131,868** | **240 s** | **sink path clean** |
| R4b (+corp) | 4 | 2,369 Mbit/s | 2,367 Mbit/s | 205,349 | 120 s | 5,965 sink drops (0.024%) |
| R5 (+srv) | 5 | 2,665 Mbit/s | 2,660 Mbit/s | 231,041 | 90 s | 31,733 sink drops (0.15%) |

R4 is the headline: **~1.5 Gbit/s / ~132 kpps sustained lossless** over a 240 s / 31.7M-packet
window — the tc mirror dropped 0 and the sink's vhost/NIC/decap stages were all clean (a separate,
tiny, non-rate-correlated 10-packet loss at the host's gretap tx, 0.00003%, is noted but unexplained
— the leading theory is occasional oversized/GSO frames exceeding the gretap's 1500 MTU, not
congestion, since 10 drops at 1.5 Gbit/s over 240 s vs. 2 drops at 2.6 Gbit/s over 90 s argues against
a rate cause; worth a look if bit-exact capture matters).

**Two ceilings found, in this order, each fixed the same day (own CHANGELOG entries above):**
1. The gretap's inherited `fq_codel` qdisc was silently dropping mirrored frames (an AQM that drops
   *by design*, wrong for a fire-and-forget capture copy). Fixed in the adapter (`attach-gre-tunnel`
   now asserts `pfifo limit N`) — worth roughly a 60% lossless-throughput increase on its own
   (measured separately at R4b: 9,349 drops → 0 at ~2.38 Gbit/s).
2. With the qdisc fixed, the **sink NIC** (`XPress-SW-A`, a 32-core box with a single-queue `net1`)
   became the wall above ~2.4 Gbit/s: every mirrored frame rode one virtio rx ring. **Fixed live,
   Authority: user** — `qm set 104 -net1 virtio=BC:24:11:70:84:02,bridge=o2v7dc98,queues=8`,
   hot-applied with no reboot (the NIC's MAC was held identical so the PCI slot — and the interface
   name — never moved, defusing LES-092's NetworkManager DHCP-profile trap; capture never stopped).
   At the same ~2.6 Gbit/s that previously cost 31,733 sink drops (0.15%), the sink read **zero**.

**Result — Phase 2 (multiqueue sink, same window):**

| Rung | Senders | Offered | pps | tc overlimits | sink drops | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R4 | 3 | 1,539 Mbit/s | 133,629 | 0 | **0** | zero-drop |
| R5 | 5 | 2,583 Mbit/s | 224,158 | 1,605 | **0** | 0.005% |
| R7 (every sender) | 12 | 2,748 Mbit/s | 235,250 | 325,446 | **0** | ~1.4% |

The sink stopped being the bottleneck at any rate the twin can produce. The ceiling **moved, not
vanished**: all tapped ports mirror into **one single-queue gretap netdev**, and `tc mirred` starts
logging `overlimits` past ~2.6 Gbit/s — per-sensor gretap fan-out or a multiqueue-capable tunnel
device is the next lever, not attempted here. **Generation is now the practical limit**: the
`scripted` persona driver's rendered client is a single-threaded loop, so one instance pegs exactly
one core — 5 Linux senders (~490 Mbit/s each, one core at 94%) drove ~2.45 Gbit/s; 6 Windows/
PowerShell senders added only ~80 Mbit/s combined (~13 Mbit/s each, ~40× weaker per host than a
Linux sender). Offered load scales by adding sending **hosts**, never by raising one host's
`volume:`.

**New content shipped** (all under the existing `tfc`/`tap`/persona vocabularies — no schema
change): 8 new personas chosen purely as load generators (`axrange/personas/catalog/loadgen-{corp,
l1,l2,l3,l4,mgmt,srv,win}.yaml` — catalog **28 → 36**), `twin-configs/corp-enterprise/
roles-loadgen.yaml` (a shared DMZ-mesh `loadgen` role), seven ramp profiles
(`tfc-maxrate-l1..l7.yaml`) and three tap plans of increasing width
(`tap-maxrate-{dmz,wide,all}.yaml`, 4/15/17 ports), and a **snapshot folder** `twin-configs/
corp-enterprise-maxrate/` — a dated *record*, not a live catalog entry (its config copy is
deliberately suffixed `.yaml.snapshot` so twin-config discovery doesn't double-list
`corp-enterprise`) — capturing the exact deployed IR, the drop-accounting harness, and the published
report (`report/capture-rate.html`).

**Two lab facts used during the run that are gitignored and do not travel with a fresh clone**
(recorded in the snapshot folder's own README so they aren't lost): (1) `deploy-dev45`'s
image-binding repoints — `corp-enterprise` references `ubuntu-server-22@v5`/`win11-finance@v4`
templates that don't exist on dev45 (28 of 32 nodes couldn't clone against the stock bindings);
repointed to templates 620/101 for this run, originals backed up; (2) the `adapter.conf`
`EXTERNAL_SENSORS` declaration for `XPress-SW-A` — required for the tap plans to resolve at deploy
time (`tap validate` reports "static OK" for an unknown sensor id regardless, GAP-052) and its
transport IP must be read live via QGA, never copied from `adapter.conf.example`.

**Left standing on dev45 (not torn down):** `te corp-enterprise-890c29b2` (completed) + `tap
tap-corp-enterprise-890c29b2-c9cab1c4` (the 4-segment/17-port `tap-maxrate-all.yaml` binding, so the
twin's ordinary AD background traffic stays captured alongside any future rung) + `tfc
tfc-corp-enterprise-890c29b2-b423b5cc` (parked at rung 4 — `tfc-maxrate-l4.yaml`, 3 senders, the
proven ~1.5 Gbit/s zero-sink-drop rate — after the ramp explored up through rung 7 to characterize
the ceiling). `XPress-SW-A`'s `net1` keeps `queues=8` in its live VM config (restore: `qm set 104
-net1 virtio=BC:24:11:70:84:02,bridge=o2v7dc98`, dropping `,queues=8`).

**No contract change:** no new op, no schema bump, no new validator rule — entirely new catalog
content plus the two adapter/render fixes and one persona-driver fix documented in their own entries
above.

**Verification:** live, on dev45, both phases. **1855 pytest tests** (unaffected by this entry
itself — see the three sibling 2026-08-14 entries for the code changes the run surfaced and their
own unit/live verification).

**Affected docs:** `CLAUDE.md` (Current State summary); `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` (handoff — the twin
is standing, not torn down).

**Affected code:** `axrange/personas/catalog/loadgen-*.yaml` (new, 8 files); `twin-configs/
corp-enterprise/{roles-loadgen.yaml, tfc-maxrate-l1..l7.yaml, tap-maxrate-{dmz,wide,all}.yaml}`
(new); `twin-configs/corp-enterprise-maxrate/` (new, snapshot folder).

## 2026-08-11 — Incremental twin update: `axrange te deploy --update` (W2)

**Authority:** user (2026-08-04) for the scope (`docs/WORK_ORDER_twin_incremental_update.md`) — add
and remove nodes on a standing `te` deploy; in-place mutation of a surviving node is explicitly OUT
of scope, refused loudly rather than redone. The M1 fix approach (move `configure-router`'s
`served_hosts` roster out of persisted `inputs_resolved` entirely, into a new planner-internal-only
`OpInstance.planner_hints`) is **Authority: user, 2026-08-11**.

**What changed:** `axrange te deploy --config <id> --update [--allow-destroy] [--yes]` (equally `te
deploy <scenario.yaml> --update`) reconciles an edited IR against a STANDING deploy instead of
minting a new one — closing `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-040 for its headline case (add/remove a
node), a documented dead-end since 2026-07-25 (LES-126).

- **The safety property the whole design hangs on:** `--update` pins the standing deploy's
  `plan_id` (never derives a fresh one) and rebuilds the DAG under it, so every unchanged op keeps
  its exact op id / VMID / VM name / zone / vnet / bridge, and the diff reduces to a set comparison
  over op ids (`axrange/planner/plandiff.py`, new — pure, no I/O). Four classes, a deliberately
  REDUCED subset of `docs/PLANNER.md` §9.1: **Unchanged** (skip, today's resume path), **Added**
  (run normally), **Removed** (swept via a new scoped teardown, gated behind `--allow-destroy`),
  **Mutated** (a completed op whose resolved inputs differ — **hard refusal, naming the op / field /
  old→new value**; §9.2's redo-rules table is NOT implemented — "a partially-correct redo table is
  worse than none"). A fifth bucket outside §9.1's table, **retry**, covers a row present on both
  sides but not yet `completed` — still walked by the ordinary resume path, never refused as
  Mutated.
- **Teardown grows a scoped variant** (`planner/teardown.py`'s `_sweep` extracted from
  `destroy_deploy`, reused by new `destroy_ops_scoped`) — the exact same reverse-order sweep,
  `_protected()` guard, and idempotent check-then-act handlers as a full `axrange te destroy`,
  applied to only the removed op-id set (LES-037: reuse, don't fork).
- **Barriers AND aggregate "commit" ops are re-evaluated, not just barriers.** A barrier whose
  predecessor set gained an Added op is force-reset even if already `completed`
  (`plandiff.barriers_to_reevaluate`, work order §3.3) — and, found only in round 3's review, the
  same treatment is needed for non-barrier ops with a catalog `after:` clause shaped `<name>[*]`
  (bare-star, not the ordinary `[$var@*]` scope-bound tail) — `apply-sdn-config` chief among them,
  whose `PUT /cluster/sdn` COMMITS what `create-vnet`/`create-subnet` merely STAGE. Without this,
  adding a network left its vnet staged-but-uncommitted (loud: the new host's `start-vm` failed
  "bridge does not exist"), and flipping `egress: true` on an already-gatewayed network added only
  `create-subnet` and reported every op COMPLETED on a segment with no gateway and no SNAT (silent —
  nothing failed on its own). `plandiff.ops_to_reexecute` generalizes the fix, computed from the
  live catalog (`list_actions_and_waits()`), not hand-maintained.
- **CLI:** `--update` prints the diff (`docs/PLANNER.md` §9.3's shape, grouped by entity —
  `host/h-new`/`net/dmz`, not one line per op) before executing, echoing the target `deploy_id` in
  the header BEFORE any destructive-removal confirmation prompt, and requires `y` (or `--yes`) when
  the removed set is non-empty; non-interactive stdin (EOF/Ctrl-C) aborts like an explicit "n"
  rather than a traceback. `--update`/`--fresh` are mutually exclusive; `--allow-destroy`/`--yes`
  without `--update` are refused as meaningless. API peer: `CreateDeployRequest.update`/
  `.allow_destroy`, same refusal semantics, a shared resolution helper
  (`api.service.resolve_deploy_plan`/`apply_update_tail`) used by both CLI and API (LES-037).
- **Recorded on success:** the deploy row's `ir_hash` advances to the edited IR's hash; `plan_id`
  stays pinned. `plan_id` becomes the twin's stable identity across updates, `ir_hash` its current
  generation — the documented consequence (work order §3.4) that a new
  `StateStore.deploys_matching(scenario_id=...)` query exists specifically to keep finding the
  standing deploy once `plan_id` no longer equals a freshly-derived `derive_plan_id(scenario_id,
  ir_hash)`.
- **Sibling tiers (`tap`/`tfc`/`exr`) are warned about, not auto-reconciled** (work order §3.5, an
  honest v1 limit): if a removed host is still referenced by a standing sibling deploy's ops, the
  tail logs a named warning ("re-apply it") rather than leaving a silent dangling binding
  (`o2control/cascade.py`'s new `siblings_for_te`, reused rather than a fourth hand-rolled copy of
  each tier's naming convention).

**Why:** GAP-040's operational cost was paid live on 2026-07-25 — the twin-model live-verify Stage 5
needed an attacker host on a standing 32-host `corp-enterprise` twin and could only choose between a
full ~30-VM redeploy or hand-provisioning outside the twin-config model (it took the second, and the
authored config and the live topology diverged as a result). `docs/PLANNER.md` §9 has specified a
full re-planning model since v1 and it had never been built; this closes the add/remove half.

**It took three code rounds, because each round's own green suite had certified a build that could
not do its job.** Round 1 built it (1802 tests green). Round 2's review found the **M1 blocker**:
`configure-router` emits a plan-wide `served_hosts` roster ("every non-router host sharing one of my
LAN nets") into the SAME `inputs_resolved` dict the diff fingerprints — so adding or removing *any*
host anywhere on a router's nets changed that fingerprint and hard-refused as Mutated, on **100% of
the 5 shipped twin-configs and 6 archetypes** (every one of them uses a router). Fixed by carrying
`served_hosts` in a new `OpInstance.planner_hints` dict instead — planner-internal, read only by
`pass3_ordering.py`'s edge computation, never serialized into `inputs_resolved_json`
(`plandiff._RETIRED_PERSISTED_INPUT_KEYS` tolerates the one-way migration: present on an
already-persisted OLD row, absent from a freshly-built op, without a false Mutated field). Round 3's
review found the `apply-sdn-config`/aggregate-op silent-success trap described above, plus a
tombstone bug (**M2**): a prior `--update`'s removal sweep marks a row `destroyed`, but nothing
excluded `destroyed` rows from the NEXT diff — so a removed op was reclassified `removed` again on
**every subsequent diff, forever**, hard-refusing an identical re-run without `--allow-destroy` and,
with it, re-issuing real teardown calls against already-gone cluster resources. Fixed by excluding
`status == DESTROYED` rows in `diff_plan` and by teaching `StateStore.reset_incomplete_ops` to leave
`destroyed` rows alone too.

**Also fixed by code review, all pinned by regression tests:** a non-clean removal sweep (a
protect-guard refusal, a resource that wouldn't clear) used to report `rc=0` — the executor's own
successful added-ops pass had already written the deploy `completed`, and nothing downgraded it when
the *removal* half then failed; now `apply_update_tail`'s `UpdateTailResult.clean` propagates to a
non-zero exit, an "INCOMPLETE" message, and a `FAILED` persisted status (mirroring `te destroy`'s
own posture) **(M3)**. The same-generation auto-heal (an unflagged redeploy of byte-identical
content after a PRIOR update had pinned the plan_id) checked `ir_hash` equality alone, which is also
true for an OLDER, already-`destroyed`-superseded row sharing that same plan_id by construction
(`deploy` → `--fresh` → `destroy` the newer one, both unedited) — without also requiring `plan_id !=
plan.plan_id`, the heal could silently revive a deploy_id `--fresh`/destroy had deliberately moved
past **(M4)**. And the "more than one standing deploy for this scenario" ambiguity refusal
originally ran unconditionally, before the code even checked whether `--update` was passed — so an
ordinary, non-destructive deploy call could be blocked by a 409 telling the operator to destroy two
LIVE twins just to mint a third; moved inside the `update:` branch only, where the destructive
single-target semantics actually need it, and it now lists every candidate `deploy_id` rather than
silently picking the newest **(M5)**.

**Validated against SHIPPED content, not only hand-written fixtures** — the review that found M1
also required a regression test against `target-environments/fabric-verify/scenario.yaml` (a real,
multi-router fabric archetype, six `configure-router` ops), pinning that adding a node no longer
refuses on any of them; **1802 green tests had certified a build that refused every router-bearing
scenario this repo ships**, since none of the hand-written unit fixtures happened to include a
router.

**No version-locked contract changed:** no `IR_SCHEMA`/`PLANNER`/`OP_CATALOG`/`VALIDATOR`/`ADAPTER`/
`IMAGE_MANIFEST` schema bump, no new op type, no state-store schema bump — `docs/PLANNER.md` §9
already specified this behavior; this is its reduced subset (a note pointing here was added there).

**Out of scope (work order §5), unchanged:** in-place mutation of a surviving node (§9.2's redo
rules); sibling-tier auto-reconcile; a twin generation counter/update history; `exr` replay-identity
interaction (`playout_hash()` still keys on the exercise spec, not the twin).

**Verification:** unit/integration — `tests/test_plandiff.py` (new, the pure classification/
re-evaluation logic) + `tests/test_twin_incremental_update.py` (new, end-to-end through the real CLI
path with a fake adapter, covering every work-order §6 unit/integration criterion). Suite verified
independently at the working tree: **1844 collected (1832 passed / 12 skipped)** at the point this
work finished. **Gated `dev45` live-verify has NOT been run** — the work order marks it mandatory
(this feature deletes real VMs) and it is the one thing left before this can be trusted against live
infrastructure; see `docs/WORK_ORDER_twin_incremental_update.md` §6 for the exact scripted sequence.

**Affected docs:** `docs/WORK_ORDER_twin_incremental_update.md` (STATUS → code/tests/docs DONE,
live-verify PENDING); `docs/PLANNER.md` §9 (note: a reduced subset is now implemented);
`docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-040 (→ Resolved (build; gated dev45 live-verify pending); new GAP-053 for
a twin-config bug found in passing, left unfixed); `CLAUDE.md` (counters + a new summary note);
`docs/LESSONS.md` (LES-178 through LES-187, and a forward-pointer appended to LES-126).

**Affected code:** `axrange/planner/plandiff.py` (new); `axrange/planner/{dag,pass1_emit,
pass3_ordering,state,teardown,__init__}.py`; `axrange/api/{service,dtos}.py`; `axrange/cli/main.py`;
`axrange/o2control/cascade.py` (`siblings_for_te`, new); `tests/test_plandiff.py` (new);
`tests/test_twin_incremental_update.py` (new); five existing test files touched for the shared-
signature changes.

## 2026-08-11 — Workflow tooling: adopt the v7 generic pipeline scripts, superseding the 2026-08-07 "keep the bespoke scripts" decision

**Authority:** user (2026-08-11).

**What changed:**
- Adopted the workflow-template **v7** generic, config-driven pipeline scripts in place of this
  project's bespoke `scripts/doc-stats.py` / `scripts/verify_tier.sh`-class tooling that the
  2026-08-07 entry had deliberately kept. A `/catchup` at session start found the v7 upgrade already
  sitting **uncommitted** in the working tree (the bespoke scripts had already been overwritten by
  it), so "keep the bespoke scripts" was no longer the live state of the repo regardless of what the
  docs still said.
- New **`.claude-doc-stats.json`** (checked in, repo root) reproduces axRange's own counters —
  pytest test count (`pytest --collect-only -q`), op-type count
  (`axrange.ops.list_actions_and_waits`/`list_barriers`), catalog-rule count
  (`axrange.validator.list_rules`) — through v7's generic `counters`/`claims`/`files` config model,
  instead of the bespoke script's hand-coded imports. Every counter command hardcodes
  `.venv/bin/python` (the system interpreter has neither `pytest` nor `axrange` importable) and runs
  as an argv list, never a shell string, so nothing goes through a shell.
- `scripts/inject_lessons.py`'s over-matching regression (surfaced by the v7 swap) was repaired —
  bare tokens that are also non-top-level directory names (e.g. `design`, `catalog`, `bindings`,
  `images`) still over-match against one subtree (`docs/design/*.md` shows a handful of matches with
  nothing removed); this is a bounded, known residual, not a full fix — a proper fix needs curation
  of the token list, not a smarter derived rule.
- `tests/test_pipeline_scripts.py` rewritten against the v7 script API.

**Why:**
- The 2026-08-07 decision was made because the bespoke scripts diverged meaningfully from the
  template's generic model at the time (custom op/rule counting, custom verify tiers) and the
  template's refresh script would have silently overwritten that divergence. By 2026-08-11 the
  divergence had already been overwritten anyway (found uncommitted in the tree at session start) —
  continuing to document "keep the bespoke scripts" while the working tree had already moved on
  would itself be the drift this project's doc discipline exists to prevent. Reconciling onto v7
  with an axRange-side config file (`.claude-doc-stats.json`) keeps the counter-drift enforcement
  this project relies on without hand-maintaining a diverging script.

**Supersedes:** CHANGELOG 2026-08-07 "Workflow docs aligned to template v5 (baseline + two optional
modes); bespoke pipeline scripts kept" — specifically its "keep the bespoke pipeline scripts" scope
decision. The docs/model framing that same entry established (baseline + two optional modes) is
unaffected and stays current.

**Verification:** suite verified independently at the working tree: **1844 collected (1832 passed /
12 skipped)** at the point this work finished (before the 2026-08-13/14 work above added its own
tests).

**Affected docs:** `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` (handoff — reflects the v7 tooling as the current state).

**Affected code:** `.claude-doc-stats.json` (new); `scripts/{claude-export.sh, doc-stats.py,
hooks.py, inject_lessons.py, verify_tier.sh}`; `.claude-workflow.env`; `tests/
test_pipeline_scripts.py`.

## 2026-08-07 — Workflow docs aligned to template v5 (baseline + two optional modes); bespoke pipeline scripts kept

**Authority:** user (asked to update the project's workflow to match the latest template, `/home/rocky/workflow` @ v5).

**What changed:**
- **`docs/WORKFLOW.md` reframed to v5's model:** the old single "## Modes" block became v5's
  "## The baseline (always on, no machinery)" (the four-point baseline — docs are truth, every
  decision gets an Authority marker, `SESSION_STATE.md` is the committed handoff, the user reviews
  every diff) + "## Two optional modes" (chat-bridge, pipeline). The onboarding-paths table gained the
  `--with-pipeline` flags and the `claude-add-pipeline.sh` row. The axRange-specific pipeline section
  (gated commands, the `verify_tier.sh` tiers) is preserved unchanged.
- **`.claude/agents/doc-writer.md`:** its handoff instruction still carried the retired solo-mode line
  ("Do not commit it — solo mode force-adds it onto the wip branch"); corrected to v5's — update
  `SESSION_STATE.md` and **commit it with the work**, it is the next session's starting point.
- `CLAUDE.md` was already v5-aligned (the `## Session Handoff` section + the solo-retirement note
  landed with the 2026-08-04 solo-mode retirement, commit `1bdec75`); no change needed.

**Scope decision (Authority: user):** update the **docs/model** to v5 and **keep the bespoke pipeline
scripts**. This project's `scripts/doc-stats.py` and `scripts/verify_tier.sh` are custom axRange
implementations (doc-stats imports `axrange.ops`/`axrange.validator` to count op-types/rules + does a
pytest collect; verify_tier runs `sdn_preflight.py`/`verify_resource_locks.py`) that **diverge from
v5's new generic + `.claude-doc-stats.json` model**. The template's `claude-refresh-scripts.sh` would
overwrite them with the generic versions and break the project's doc-drift enforcement and verify
tiers, so the scripts were deliberately left as-is. The v5 host-global mechanism split was likewise
not adopted — the project stays project-local. Only the docs/model changed.

**Why:**
- v5 removed solo mode entirely and reframed the workflow as one always-on baseline plus two optional
  modes. This project had already done the mechanical half (solo scripts deleted, `SESSION_STATE.md`
  promoted to a committed file, 2026-08-04), but its docs still described the old three-mode framing
  and one agent still carried a stale solo instruction. This closes that gap.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORKFLOW.md` — baseline/two-modes framing + onboarding table
- `.claude/agents/doc-writer.md` — session-handoff instruction

**Links:** template v5 (`/home/rocky/workflow` TEMPLATE_CHANGELOG 2026-08-04); CHANGELOG 2026-08-04
(this project's solo-mode retirement).

## 2026-08-05 — corp-enterprise bundle `dev45` live-verify: tap + tfc PROVEN, clean-cascade re-verified at scale, exr deferred on a dev45 egress outage

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code) — the gated bundle live-verify (WO-2 §6), user-authorized;
the scope reduction (verify tap+tfc, defer exr) was a user decision when a dev45 infrastructure outage
surfaced mid-run.

**What happened.** The full ~30-VM `corp-enterprise` bundle was stood up on `dev45` end to end. It
proved the bundle is sound; the only failures across the entire run trace to a **single external cause
— a sustained dev45 upstream-egress outage** (the mirror *and* Cloudflare both unreachable, confirmed
from multiple hosts/segments) that appeared *after* 377/383 ops had already installed their apps.

**Per-tier scorecard:**
- **te — deploys soundly.** 377/383 ops; the full AD domain stood up (Windows DC promotion + all **25
  workstation domain-joins**); `apply-sdn-config` completed cleanly (GAP-046 did **not** fire — it runs
  early, before the VM count climbs). The name-targeted twin config (new `name:`/`role:` + `extra=
  "forbid"` schema) deployed at 30-VM scale, `plan_id=corp-enterprise-86423faa` (unchanged — the
  re-authored content is IR-identical). The **only** incomplete ops were 2 `provision-app` app installs
  (`squid`, the `attacker` toolkit), blocked by the egress outage.
- **tap — ✅ PROVEN.** `tap deploy … --target corp-enterprise` resolved the twin **by name** against a
  `failed`-status-but-live deploy, bound both selectors, and **verify-capture confirmed LIVE capture**
  (`capture_verified: True`, sentinel token traversed tap → gretap → the standing `o2-suricata`
  sensor — real proof, LES-001). The external `Tcpdump-SW-A` binding loud-skipped verify-capture, as
  designed (GAP-026 pattern).
- **tfc — ✅ PROVEN.** 30/31 persona instances driven via the name-targeted profile. The one failure
  (`egress-proxy`) is a **downstream consequence of the same egress outage** — its `real-service`
  persona requires the `squid` daemon, which never installed — not a tfc defect (correct fail-loud).
- **exr — ⏸️ deferred.** The AD kill chain needs the attacker's toolkit, which needs the egress-blocked
  install. Cleanly deferred; not run.
- **cascade teardown — ✅ PROVEN CLEAN at scale.** One `te destroy` cascaded tfc + tap **once**, before
  the twin's own sweep, with **zero `Configuration file … does not exist` warnings** — the exact
  re-verification of the W1 **finding-#4** dead-duplicate-cascade-block deletion, now confirmed at
  30-VM scale (72 resources removed, 0 not cleared). Blast radius bounded: every protected row
  (o2-services, o2-suricata, external sinks, 6 templates) byte-identical afterward; dev45 back at the
  exact §2 baseline, zero corp-enterprise leftovers.

**Content fix made during the run (uncommitted → committed here):** `tap-default.yaml` and
`tap-reshard.yaml` referenced sensor id **`o2-tcpdump`**, a sensor **decommissioned 2026-07-26** (the
standing dev45 surface is suricata-only; the tcpdump sink is now the external `Tcpdump-SW-A`). WO-2's
"re-confirmed to resolve" check only verified the tap plans **parse**, not that their sensor ids
**exist** — `tap validate`'s static mode does not check sensor existence and printed "static OK." The
live-verify preflight caught it before deploy. Fixed `o2-tcpdump` → `Tcpdump-SW-A` (preserves the
deliberate two-sensor design: suricata IDS on server-vlan + raw tcpdump on the boundary) across both
plans + the README. **New GAP-052** tracks the static-validation blind spot; **LES-177** generalizes it.

**Operational notes (not code defects, logged for the trail):**
- **Windows domain-joins are the dominant cost** of an AD twin deploy — ~1–2 min each, effectively
  serialized, so 25 workstations alone are ~40+ min. `GAP-051`.
- **Background deploy jobs were reaped mid-run** several times (SIGTERM at a too-short timeout / context
  teardown), leaving a stale-`running` op + a still-held lease. `te deploy`/`tfc deploy` **resume-safe**
  recovery handled every case (reclaim the expired lease, reset failed/stale ops, continue) — the
  deploy was driven 343 → 377 across resumes with no data loss. The resume-safety is exactly what made
  the run recoverable.

**Verification status:** tap + tfc **PROVEN live** on dev45; clean cascade + bounded teardown **PROVEN**;
te **deploys soundly** (completion egress-blocked); **exr deferred** on the dev45 egress outage (not a
bundle issue). No code changed except the tap-plan content fix; **1737 pytest tests** unchanged. dev45
returned to baseline.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_corp_enterprise_bundle.md` — live-verify status → PARTIAL PASS (tap+tfc+cascade PASSED; exr deferred-on-egress)
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — new GAP-051 (Windows-join cost), GAP-052 (tap sensor-id existence unvalidated)
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — new LES-177
- `CLAUDE.md` — WO-2 note
