## 2026-08-04 — Exercise-tier name-targeting + the co-located `corp-enterprise` bundle (WO-2)

**Authority:** user (2026-08-04) — `docs/WORK_ORDER_corp_enterprise_bundle.md`; the exr code change
is flagged there (§4) as a reviewer-visible contract extension, not a slip.

**What changed:**

- **`exr` becomes the fourth (and last) tier with NAME-targeting.** `exercise.twin` was the last
  place still requiring a literal deploy id — the LES-072 staleness trap tap plans fixed 2026-07-25
  and `tfc` fixed under W1. Now `twin:` is OPTIONAL and accepts a scenario/twin-config **name**
  (resolved to its newest live `te` deploy at run time), with a `--target` override on `exr
  deploy`/`run` + the API body (precedence override > spec > error). The resolver **delegates to
  `taps.resolve_target_deploy`** (LES-037 — a third copy would drift), adapting only the error type,
  exactly as `traffic.resolve_traffic_target` does; the spec rebuild uses `dataclasses.replace()`
  (LES-173). **This also fixes a latent bug:** a name-authored exr run built `plan_id =
  exr-<name>-<token>`, which the twin-destroy cascade (searching `exr-<te_deploy_id>-*`) would
  MISS — a cascade-orphan. Resolving to the concrete id first makes the plan_id cascade-findable.
  `exr` **destroy is unchanged** — it targets the run's own `deploy_id`, never a twin name, so it
  needs no resolution (unlike tfc/tap, whose destroy-by-name gap LES-175 fixed). No IR/OP_CATALOG/
  VALIDATOR change; op count 40, rules 114 unchanged. **+4 exr regression tests** (name resolves,
  `--target` override beats spec, no-target errors, and the concrete-keyed plan_id that keeps a
  name-authored run cascade-findable).
- **The `corp-enterprise` deployment bundle is now complete and co-located** under
  `twin-configs/corp-enterprise/` — every tier's spec in one place, all name-targeted, portable
  (no minted deploy id anywhere): the twin config + `roles.yaml` + `tfc-default`/`tfc-busy` +
  four `tap-*` plans (all from the W1 migration) **plus** the AD kill-chain `exr` spec, **moved in**
  from the gitignored `deploy-dev45/` and re-addressed — `twin: corp-enterprise` (name, was a dead
  minted id) and `host: attacker` (corp-enterprise's dedicated attacker node, replacing the Stage-5
  `egress-proxy` GAP-040 workaround now that the node exists) — and a `README.md` indexing the files
  with the full deploy/inspect/teardown command sequence (standing-O2 precondition, `--images`/`--db`
  dev45 flags, the one-`te destroy` cascade). The orphaned `deploy-dev45/` copy was removed (moved,
  not duplicated) and its one inbound path reference (a `corp-enterprise.yaml` comment) repointed.

**Why:** an operator asked for "all the configs for all the tiers … in one place … from scratch using
the new schemas." The W1 migration had already re-authored the te/tfc/tap files into the new schema;
this closes the gap — the exr spec was orphaned outside the folder and welded to a dead deploy id, so
the bundle was neither complete nor portable. The exr name-targeting is what makes a checked-in exr
spec possible at all.

**Verification:** every bundle file parses/validates (twin config PASS with one benign OVR-020
warning; all tfc/tap/exr specs load and resolve their `target: corp-enterprise`). **1737 pytest
tests** (+4). **Gated `dev45` live-verify PENDING** — stand up the full ~30-VM bundle end to end
(te → tap → tfc → exr) and confirm the one-`te destroy` cascade leaves the standing surface / sinks /
templates untouched.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_corp_enterprise_bundle.md` — STATUS → DONE (code/content/docs); live-verify pending
- `CLAUDE.md` — CLI grid note (exr name-targeting), test count

## 2026-08-04 — Solo mode retired: the session handoff is a committed file, not a branch protocol

**Authority:** user

**What changed:**
- Removed `scripts/claude-checkpoint.sh`, `scripts/claude-restore.sh` and
  `scripts/claude-clean-wips.sh`, and the `wip/<base-sha12>/<label>` checkpoint-branch
  convention they implemented. Nothing on this repo used it: of the four `wip/*` branches,
  **none** were checkpoint-format — they are hand-named topic branches
  (`wip/restructure`, `wip/fabric-slice`, `wip/scenario-authoring-guide`,
  `wip/workflow-speedup`), so no in-flight work was stranded by the removal.
- **`docs/SESSION_STATE.md` is now a committed file**, not gitignored. It was ignored on the
  base branch only so `claude-checkpoint.sh` could force-add it onto a wip branch; with that
  gone the file simply travels with the repo. Removed the `.gitignore` entry and retitled the
  file's header.
- `docs/WORKFLOW.md`: dropped the "Solo Mode" section (79 lines) and reframed the opening as
  one always-on baseline plus two optional modes (chat-bridge, pipeline). The baseline is
  docs-as-truth + Authority markers + `SESSION_STATE.md` + the user's diff review.
- `CLAUDE.md`: "Cross-Session Handoff (Solo Mode)" → "Session Handoff"; the `/dev` pipeline's
  `doc-writer` stage still owns updating it at session wrap, and now commits it.
- `.claude-workflow.env`: dropped the solo stubs (`CLAUDE_REMOTE` / `CLAUDE_WIP_PREFIX`) and
  added commented `CLAUDE_EXPORT_EXCLUDE` / `CLAUDE_EXPORT_MAX_MB` / `CLAUDE_CHANGELOG_ENTRIES`
  stubs — `docs/` is ~1.9 MB here (CHANGELOG 672 KB, LESSONS 284 KB), which no longer fits a
  chat-bridge export.

**Why:**
- The machinery went unused while the convention thrived. Resuming was always *reading
  `SESSION_STATE.md`*, never running `claude-restore.sh`; parallel work happens on topic
  branches and in the `o2-build` worktree. What restore did is `git checkout <branch> &&
  git reset HEAD~1`; what the narrative file does — why the work is shaped this way, what is
  half-done, what is next — is the part a diff cannot reconstruct.
- The checkpoint model also assumed a pristine base branch you park work *off*. This repo works
  directly on `wip/restructure`, which that model never anticipated.
- Removing it deletes the most intricate git surgery in the workflow (cherry-pick + reset delta
  reconstruction, force-adding a gitignored file onto a branch, base-sha auto-detect,
  base-moved conflict recovery) — the code most likely to misfire at the worst moment.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORKFLOW.md` — solo section removed, baseline framing added.
- `CLAUDE.md` — "Session Handoff" section; three stale "Solo" references corrected.
- `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` — header now says committed.

**Affected code:**
- Deleted: `scripts/claude-checkpoint.sh`, `scripts/claude-restore.sh`,
  `scripts/claude-clean-wips.sh`.
- `.gitignore`, `.claude-workflow.env`.

**Supersedes:** the entry that added solo-mode cross-session handoff to this project.

**Note:** `.claude/worktrees/o2-build/` is a separate checkout (branch `worktree-o2-build`) and
still carries the old solo text in its own `CLAUDE.md` / `docs/WORKFLOW.md`; it picks this up
when that branch merges.

## 2026-08-04 — W1 (role/persona decoupling) `dev45` live-verify PASSED — all 8 proofs, plus four bugs found and fixed that the 1731-test suite could not catch

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code) — the gated live-verify authorized by the user (2026-08-04); the four fixes are `implementation (Claude Code)`, driven by the run.

**What happened:** the gated `dev45` live-verify of `docs/WORK_ORDER_role_persona_tier.md` (W1) ran
end to end against a throwaway 5-host twin authored in the new `name:`/`role:` schema (`deploy-dev45/
w1-verify/`) and **all 8 proofs PASS**. The headline (proof 5): a role+persona swap on a running host
touched **no VM** — worker vmid **707604 identical** before/after, guest uptime **+29.3s monotonic**
(no reboot), the twin's op-row set **md5-identical** (`f32334deb40be327054a16f290428d8f`) with zero
`clone-vm`/`start-vm`/`configure-*` re-run, the old persona unit gone and the new one running. Proof 4
captured **336 real packets** at `o2-suricata` matching `backup-server`'s exact port set (873/445/22),
corroborated by the peer's own sshd journal. Proofs 1–3 confirmed the `name:`/`role:` split, name-target
resolution, and the clean `role`/`role_baseline` JSON separation (no `(tfc; baseline: …)` decoration
leak, LES-149). Teardown returned dev45 to the exact §2 baseline — every protected row (o2-services,
external sinks, templates, o2 SDN) byte-identical.

**This is the entry's real point: a green suite proved nothing about four distinct bugs.** Each hid
behind exactly the name-target / minimal-twin path the 1731-test unit suite never exercised (LES-001's
family). All four were fixed mid-run, each (where behavioral) guarded by a regression test **proven to
fail without the fix**:

1. **Roles dropped on a name-target rebuild (W1 regression).** `apply_traffic_profile` — and the three
   CLI handlers `tfc plan`/`validate`/`info` — rebuilt `TrafficProfileSpec(target=…, assignments=…,
   replay=…)` when resolving a scenario NAME to a concrete deploy id, silently defaulting the new
   `roles` field to `[]`. So an assignment addressing a role only the profile's own `roles:` block
   assigns matched no host and hard-failed — on the exact name-target path proof 2 exercises. Fixed at
   all 6 reconstruction sites (2 tap-tier included) with `dataclasses.replace()`, which carries every
   field and is immune to the next one added (LES-037/**LES-173**). Invisible to the suite because every
   prior apply test used a literal deploy-id target, skipping the rebuild branch.
2. **Persona launch died with no `/var/log/axrange` (pre-existing latent).** The scripted/replay
   drivers launch through `systemd_daemon_start_block`, whose unit does `bash -c '<cmd> >LOG 2>&1'` —
   and nothing generic creates `/var/log/axrange` on a plain host (only sensor rendering / `provision-app`
   apt-logging did, as a side effect). A deliberately minimal twin was the first to expose it. Fixed
   with a leading `mkdir -p $(dirname <log>)` at that shared primitive (covers both Linux drivers at
   once) — the identical line the Loki-shipper block already had under GAP-020 (**LES-174**). Windows is
   unaffected (its log lives inside the already-created script dir). Golden launch-render SHA re-pinned
   deliberately + an explicit "mkdir precedes launch" assertion added.
3. **Destroy-by-name never resolved the twin (W1 gap + identical pre-existing tap-tier gap).**
   `destroy_traffic_profile`/`destroy_taps` built `find_deploy_for_plan(f"tfc-{target}")` from the
   **raw** target, so `tfc/tap destroy --target <name>` could never find a deploy created *by* name —
   the name-targeting work (2026-07-25 taps, W1 tfc) extended only the apply path, never the symmetric
   destroy (**LES-175**). Both fixed to resolve first (concrete id passes through unchanged, so the
   cascade path is byte-identical); regression tests for both tiers.
4. **Duplicate cascade block in `cmd_destroy` (pre-existing, LES-171 incomplete removal).** The
   2026-07-29 LES-171 fix added the correct before-sweep `cascade_destroy_siblings_for_te` call but
   **left the old after-sweep tap/tfc/exr cascade block in place** — so every `te destroy` ran the
   cascade twice, the duplicate pass firing against already-purged VMs and reproducing the exact
   `Configuration file … does not exist` wall LES-171 exists to prevent (harmless in outcome — the
   helpers are idempotent — but real duplicate work, and CLI-only, the precise drift the ONE-shared-
   helper design forbids; the API always called it once). Dead block deleted; no test asserted it
   (**LES-176**).

**Also found, not fixed (cosmetic):** the twin's own mirror-snippet teardown step
(`planner/teardown.py`, `delete_mirror_snippets`) has no pre-existence check, so an idempotent second
`te destroy` reports "N resource(s) removed" for the snippet-file deletes rather than 0 — an accounting
nuance on an `rm -f`-shaped op, not a state bug. Logged as `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` **GAP-050**.

**Verification:** all 8 proofs PASS on `dev45`; cluster confirmed back at the §2 baseline. **1733
pytest tests** (+4 regression tests since the W1 build's 1729; the log-dir fix re-pinned a golden SHA
and added shape assertions rather than a new test). No version-locked schema change beyond W1's already-
recorded `OP_CATALOG` 39→40; op count 40, rules 114, all unchanged by these fixes.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_w1_live_verify.md` — STATUS → DONE (PASSED)
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_role_persona_tier.md` — live-verify PENDING → PASSED
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — new LES-173/174/175/176
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — new GAP-050
- `CLAUDE.md` — W1 blockquote (live-verify PASSED + the four fixes)

**Links:** the W1 build entry below (status flipped from "live-verify PENDING" to PASSED).

## 2026-08-04 — Role and persona become `tfc`-owned: a standing twin can be re-personad and re-labelled without redeploying a node

**Authority:** user (2026-08-04) — the decoupling and the "no sixth tier" call were proposed by
implementation (Claude Code) in-session and accepted; the `OP_CATALOG` bump and `extra="forbid"` were
explicitly approved. Work order: `docs/WORK_ORDER_role_persona_tier.md` (W1).

**What changed:**

*The problem, correctly diagnosed.* An operator reported that changing a deployed node's persona
required destroying and redeploying it. **Personas were already a separate tier** (`tfc`, built
2026-07-24, live-verified 2026-07-25) — three unrelated things made it look otherwise, and all three
are fixed here:

1. **`twin_config.nodes[].persona` was INERT** — read only by a catalog typo-warning, never written
   to the IR, never reaching the traffic tier. Removed (26 shipped uses), completing the
   2026-08-04 inert-field sweep, which deliberately held this one back until its replacement existed.
2. **The published `hosts[].role` was baked into `clone-vm`'s persisted inputs**
   (`pass1_emit.py` writes `node_role`; `planner/inventory.py` reads it back). *That* is the literal
   reason a role change needed a re-clone: the label lived inside a provisioning op.
3. **A traffic profile's `twin:` was a REQUIRED literal deploy id** with no name resolution and no
   `--target`, so a checked-in profile was welded to one minted deploy id — the exact trap tap plans
   had fixed 2026-07-25, one day after `tfc` was built.

*Engine + tier:*
- **New `OP-TFC-020 assign-role`** (`OP_CATALOG` **39 → 40**, version-locked, user-approved): records
  a host's tfc-owned semantic role. Pure metadata — no adapter call, no guest exec, nothing on the
  wire — and **the only op in the catalog with empty `resource_categories`**, since giving it the
  `ansible-pool` slot to match its config-class siblings would burn a real throughput slot on a
  no-op. `test_config_ops_use_ansible_pool`'s invariant is restated precisely: config-class ops *that
  deliver into a guest* hold the pool.
- **`build_twin_inventory(..., role_overrides=)`** resolves `hosts[].role` as *tfc assignment →
  `clone-vm` baseline → None*, and publishes the twin's own value alongside as
  **`HostInfo.role_baseline`** so `te inventory` can render `role=file-server (tfc; baseline: ws)`.
  The map is passed IN, never looked up here: the engine must not import `o2control` (LES-025), so
  `o2control.traffic.effective_roles_for_twin` owns the "roles live in a deploy named `tfc-<twin>`"
  knowledge and the CLI/API thread it through.
- **Traffic profiles gain a `roles:` block** (`node:`/`selector:` addressing — addressing by `role:`
  is rejected as circular), **resolved and applied BEFORE assignments**, so an `assignments[].role`
  may reference a role the same profile assigns. That ordering is load-bearing, not stylistic, and is
  pinned by a test. A dropped role is retired by `_retire_dropped_roles`, mirroring
  `_stop_dropped_instances` — without it a de-scoped host would keep a stale role forever (the
  GAP-045 failure shape, pre-empted rather than rediscovered).
- **Target parity with tap plans:** `target:` (alias `twin:`) is now OPTIONAL and accepts a
  scenario/twin-config NAME resolved to its newest live `te` deploy, with `--target` on `tfc
  validate|plan|deploy|info` and the API body. `resolve_traffic_target` **delegates to
  `taps.resolve_target_deploy`** rather than reimplementing it (LES-037); only the error type is
  adapted. `tfc validate` gains tap-validate's two modes (a profile with no target static-lints and
  stops, rather than failing for a target it was deliberately authored without).
- **`roles_from: <path>`** shares one role map across profiles of a twin. See the correction note
  below — this exists *because* the design rebuttal that justified not building a sixth tier turned
  out to rest on a mechanism that didn't apply.

*Twin config:*
- **`nodes[].role` (required) splits into `name:` (required id stem) + `role:` (optional semantic
  baseline).** Migration is `role: X` → `name: X` + retain `role: X`, which leaves every node id
  byte-identical. Omitting `role:` is now a supported bare-bone authoring choice: the twin publishes
  no role and the profile supplies the whole map.
- **`TCBase` flips to `extra="forbid"`** (user-approved). A typo'd `presona:`/`imagge:` used to be
  silently absorbed — the mechanism that let five inert fields survive unnoticed. `_reject_removed_keys`
  still runs first so a *retired* key keeps its actionable message; `forbid` is the catch-all for keys
  that never existed.
- **`persona_check.py` retired** with the field it validated (its two consumers were
  `_persona_warnings` and `twin-config show`, both gone). Persona ids are now validated **fail-loud
  against the live registry** at `tfc validate|plan|deploy`, where they are actually authored — a
  stronger check than the filesystem-glob shim it replaces.

*Content:* 5 twin-configs migrated; 5 `tfc-default.yaml` profiles shipped carrying exactly the
persona intent the removed `persona:` fields claimed, plus `corp-enterprise/{roles.yaml,tfc-busy.yaml}`
demonstrating the shared map. Three configs turned out to have roles whose persona name **differs**
(`db-primary`/`db-replica` → `db-tier`, `nas-storage` → `file-server`, `mail-relay` → `mail-server`,
`corp-ws` → `corp-workstation`) — separations the pre-split single field could not express at all.

**Correction made during the build (recorded rather than quietly fixed):** work order §3.1 originally
rebutted the "a role tier would avoid duplicating the map across profiles" argument by claiming the
loader's existing YAML **include** support already solved it. It did not — `load_traffic_profile`
calls `load_yaml`, which loads exactly one file; includes are processed only by `parse_ir` against an
IR-shaped merge table. Because that rebuttal is load-bearing for the no-sixth-tier decision, the build
implemented the small thing that makes it true (`roles_from:`) rather than leaving the design argument
resting on a capability that didn't exist. §3.1 carries the correction inline.

**Verification:**
- **1729 passed / 12 skipped** (was 1717): +12. Covers roles-before-assignments sequencing, the
  circular-addressing rejection, `assign-role` composition and its empty `resource_categories`,
  `roles_from` sharing/mutual-exclusion/no-chaining, inventory precedence in all three directions,
  the closed vocabulary, and a shipped-profile persona-catalog cross-check.
- **All 5 shipped configs produce byte-identical `plan_id`/`ir_hash`** across the `name:`/`role:`
  split — now a permanent regression pin (`test_shipped_config_plan_id_survived_the_name_role_split`)
  with the pre-split values hardcoded. This is not cosmetic: `plan_id` keys VMIDs, VM names, the SDN
  zone, vnets and the underlay bridge, and finds a standing deploy (LES-126), so a change would
  orphan every existing deploy and renumber every VM.

**Verification status:** unit/suite-verified **+ gated `dev45` live-verify PASSED 2026-08-04** (all 8
proofs; see the 2026-08-04 live-verify entry above — the swap touched no VM: worker vmid identical,
uptime +29.3s monotonic, twin op-rows md5-identical. Four bugs the suite could not catch were found
and fixed during the run).

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/OP_CATALOG.md` — §5.7 (two ops), the `OP-TFC-020` block, summary table, 39 → 40 totals
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_role_persona_tier.md` — status DONE + the §3.1 correction
- `CLAUDE.md` — the role/persona blockquote

## 2026-08-04 — Inert-field sweep of the twin-config vocabulary: five fields that reached nothing are removed

**Authority:** user (2026-08-04)

**What changed:**

- **An audit traced every field in the twin-config vocabulary (`axrange/twinconfig/schema.py`) to a
  consumer.** Six reached nothing. **Five are removed here**; the sixth (`TwinConfigNode.persona`) is
  deliberately held for `docs/WORK_ORDER_role_persona_tier.md`, which ships the traffic profiles that
  carry its intent for real — removing it now would delete 26 nodes' worth of authored intent with
  nowhere for it to go.

  | Removed | Why it was inert | Shipped uses |
  |---|---|---|
  | `TwinConfigSegment.purpose` | zero references in `expand.py` | 18 |
  | `TwinConfigNode.identity_role` | **zero** references anywhere in `axrange/`, including its own package | 0 |
  | `TwinConfigConduit.policy` | zero references in `expand.py` — the conduit ENTRY is load-bearing (cross-checked against an implementing router, `expand.py:301-307`), its `policy:` string was not | 0 |
  | `TwinConfigIdentity.groups` | `expand.py` reads only the per-**user** `u.groups`; the top-level whole-domain list was never read | 0 |
  | `TwinConfigIdentity.ous` | same, for `u.ou` | 0 |

- **New `_reject_removed_keys` / `_REMOVED_TOP_LEVEL_KEYS` / `_REMOVED_SECTION_KEYS` /
  `_REMOVED_IDENTITY_KEYS` in `expand.py`.** The single hardcoded `tap_points:` check
  (`docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_eligibility.md` D3) generalizes into tables covering every retired key, checked
  on the RAW pre-substitution dict at each level (top-level / `segments[]` / `nodes[]` / `conduits[]` /
  `identity`). Without this, `TCBase`'s permissive `extra="allow"` would silently absorb a leftover and
  drop it with no signal (LES-111) — which is exactly how these fields came to look wired. Each message
  names where the intent belongs instead ("author a user's groups per-user in
  `identity.users[].groups`"), because a rejection that doesn't say that just relocates the confusion.
- **The `identity.groups` (removed) vs `identity.users[].groups` (wired) distinction is explicit** in
  the tables, the docstrings, and a dedicated regression test — it is the one thing an over-eager sweep
  would get wrong.
- **Shipped content:** all 18 `purpose:` strings across the 5 twin-configs became **YAML comments**, so
  no human-readable annotation was lost. `purpose:` was the one field where "inert" ≠ "harmful" — it
  read as documentation and *was* documentation, unlike `persona:`, which reads as configuration and
  configures nothing. It was removed anyway, per the user's instruction, with the text preserved.
- **Docstrings** in both modules were re-checked against current source (LES-164) and updated: the
  `expand.py` module docstring now points at the generalized tables rather than the single `tap_points`
  check, and `schema.py` gained a "no inert fields" note stating the rule for future fields.

**Why:**

- The user hit the class directly: `twin-configs/*/*.yaml` declares `persona: <id>` on 26 nodes, and
  that field is read by **one** thing — a catalog typo warning. Editing it changes nothing, anywhere.
  Reasonably read as "personas are embedded in the node spec," which is what motivated the whole
  role/persona decoupling design (see the two new work orders below). The user then asked for the rest
  of the class to be found and removed.
- This is the third time the project has retired a field that looked wired and was not — `tap_points[].labels`
  (GAP-030, dissolved) and the `tap:` flags (`docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_eligibility.md`) were the first two.
  The `_REMOVED_*_KEYS` tables exist so the fourth is a table entry, not a fresh one-off `if`.

**Verification:**

- **The removals are provably inert: every one of the 5 shipped configs produces a byte-identical
  `plan_id`/`ir_hash` before and after** (`sme-flat-d6ab9fe2`, `corp-enterprise-86423faa`,
  `datacenter-09528ee1`, `ics-ot-34fd6ef5`, `dmz-perimeter-dcf875e2` — compared against a stashed
  pre-change tree). This matters beyond tidiness: `plan_id` is keyed on `ir_hash` (LES-126), so a
  non-inert removal would have orphaned every standing deploy of these configs.
- All 5 configs still `te validate` PASS (against a real images root — the bare `--config` failure is
  pre-existing GAP-031, no shipped `images/` catalog).
- **1717 passed / 12 skipped** (was 1709/12): +8 tests — one per removed key, the falsy-value case, the
  message-names-the-replacement case, and the per-user-`groups`/`ou`-still-wired regression.

**Not done here (reported, not removed):** two fields in the **version-locked** Scenario IR were traced
to zero consumers in the same sweep — `Activity.adversary` and `Network.isis_area`. Removing an IR field
needs a schema bump and sign-off, so they are logged as `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` **GAP-049** instead.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — new GAP-049
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — new LES-172
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_role_persona_tier.md` — §4.6 records what landed here vs. what it still owns

**Links:** `docs/WORK_ORDER_role_persona_tier.md`, `docs/WORK_ORDER_twin_incremental_update.md`

## 2026-07-29 — Twin-destroy cascade ordering: siblings tear down BEFORE the twin's own sweep

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

- **`axrange te destroy` now runs its sibling-tier cascades FIRST, in reverse-of-create order —
  `exr` → `tfc` → `tap` → `te`.** They previously ran *after* the twin's own sweep, in the order
  `tap` → `tfc` → `exr`. Every sibling tier cleans up *through* the twin's live VMs, so the old
  order meant those cleanups could only ever fail: the tap tier's de-scope re-asserts a per-VM
  mirror hookscript (`qm set <vmid> --hookscript`) and the traffic tier's persona stop is a QGA
  `agent/exec` into the guest — both against VMs the twin's own step 1 had already purged.
- **New `axrange/o2control/cascade.py`** owns the order (and nothing else):
  `cascade_destroy_siblings_for_te()` + `CASCADE_ORDER`/`CASCADE_LABELS` (the sequence exported as
  data, test-pinned) + `SiblingCascade` (per-tier outcome: normalized results list + an optional
  UNEXPECTED-failure string). **Both** transports — `cli/main.py:cmd_destroy` and
  `api/service.py:destroy_deploy` — now call that one helper instead of each carrying its own copy
  of the three-cascade sequence, which is how the identical wrong order came to be duplicated in
  two places (LES-037). Per-sibling exception isolation (LES-117) moves into the helper unchanged;
  the three cascade helpers are still resolved **lazily** so the existing monkeypatch-based
  isolation tests keep working. Placed in `o2control` (not `planner/teardown.py`) because it is a
  cross-tier policy over the three playouts that package owns — the engine never imports
  `o2control` (LES-025).
- **Side effect of the move:** the cascades now run inside the destroy's `Heartbeat` window, so the
  scenario lease is renewed across the *whole* teardown rather than only the twin's own sweep.
  Lease-safe because every sibling deploy is keyed `scenario_id == plan_id` (`exr-*`/`tfc-*`/
  `tap-*`), never the twin's `scenario_id`.
- **Docs:** `docs/design/02-twin-model.md` had **codified the wrong order** ("sweeps its own
  resources and then cascades") — corrected in the tier table, the matrix bullets, and the ASCII
  diagram, with the *why* (siblings bind into live VMs) stated so it does not regress.
- **Tests:** +12 (1709 → **1721 collected**; 1709 passed / 12 skipped). New
  `tests/test_o2_cascade.py` (10) covers order, label coverage, exception isolation, return-shape
  normalization, and seam threading; `tests/test_cli.py` + `tests/test_api.py` each gain an
  end-to-end pin asserting the tap cascade's `delete_gretap` lands **before** the twin's first
  `destroy_vm` on a shared recording backend. The CLI pin was verified to **fail** against the old
  order before being kept.

**Why:**

- Reported from a real dev45 run: `axrange te destroy corp-enterprise-f525eed4` on a 32-host twin
  finished with **55 warnings** — 30 `mirror-descope … Configuration file
  'nodes/dev45/qemu-server/<vmid>.conf' does not exist` and 25 `persona … stop failed: HTTP 500
  Configuration file … does not exist` — while still printing `Result: DESTROYED` and exiting 0.
- **Why it hid for five live-verifies:** both cleanup loops are best-effort by design (they log and
  swallow, and do not count into `TeardownResult.errors`), so the wrong order never failed
  anything. The end state was even correct — the mirrors die with the VM's tap device and the
  gretaps live on the PVE host, so the cascade still removed them afterwards. The damage is that
  the de-scope and persona-quiesce paths are structurally unreachable on a twin destroy, and that a
  *genuinely* stuck teardown is now indistinguishable from a clean one. Recorded as **LES-171**.
- No version-locked contract changed: no IR/PLANNER/OP_CATALOG/VALIDATOR/ADAPTER/IMAGE_MANIFEST
  schema touch, no new op type, no state-store schema bump. Op count stays 39, rule count 114.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/design/02-twin-model.md` — tier table (Twin row), "The teardown protect/cascade matrix"
  bullets + diagram
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — new LES-171

**Not fixed here (observed in the same run, benign):** two of four gretaps reported `absent` rather
than `removed`. `absent` is the normal check-then-act "already gone" status — counted as neither
removed nor error — and gretap netdevs do not survive a node reboot (LES-012).

**Verification status:** unit/suite-verified (1709 passed / 12 skipped / 0 failed, 1721 collected).
**dev45 live-verify pending** — the next `te destroy` of a twin with a bound tap/tfc/exr deploy
should show the cascade summaries *above* the twin's resource lines and **no**
`Configuration file … does not exist` warnings.

## 2026-07-28 — Persona volume lever + UDP/broadcast/fan-out transports, and eight high-volume personas

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

*Driver layer (`axrange/personas/`, `axrange/o2control/traffic.py`):*
- **The volume lever.** `volume:` used to map to a message COUNT alone (`low: 2 / med: 8 / high: 24`), with every message a fixed ~40-byte tag over a fresh TCP connection and a hardcoded 0.2 s inter-message gap. It now resolves three knobs together — message count, **bytes per message**, and the gap — via `scripted._VOLUME_PROFILE`, plus a new **`bulk`** elephant-flow tier (`VOLUME_LEVELS` 3 → 4). An emit can pin `bytes:` explicitly to move payload size without dragging count and pacing along (`schema.Emit.bytes_per_msg`).
- **UDP.** New `emits[].transport` (`tcp`/`udp`), **derived from `proto`** when unset (`schema.default_transport`). A UDP emit is sent as datagrams chunked at a 1400-byte MTU instead of a TCP connect/send/close. **This changes the driven transport of pre-existing catalog emits** — `dns`, `ntp`, `snmp`, `syslog`, `netflow`, `ike`, `wireguard` were all being driven over TCP; a deliberate fidelity fix, overridable per-emit.
- **Segment-scoped peer roles.** `RESERVED_PEER_ROLES` gains `broadcast`/`multicast`, resolved by the driver to a literal wire address (255.255.255.255, or the proto's well-known group) rather than to any twin host — `o2control.traffic` stays ignorant of wire semantics. A `multicast` emit whose proto has no known group **fails loud** (LES-001) rather than substituting a plausible wrong address.
- **Peer fan-out.** New `emits[].peer_scope` (`one`/`all`); `all` fans the emit across every twin host holding the role via a new `_resolve_peer_sets`, **excluding the instance's own host** (self-directed traffic never crosses a tap point). Threaded as a new optional `peer_sets=` kwarg — `_resolve_peers` is untouched, so the existing hard-unresolvable-role fail-loud and every `peer_scope: one` binding are unchanged.
- **`burst:` cadence fix.** `burst:<dur>` hardcoded `interval_s = 86400.0` regardless of the authored duration, so `corp-workstation`'s `burst:30m` print emit fired **twice a day**, and `burst:30m` vs `burst:8h` differed only in spread, never in frequency. Both kinds now mean "fire every `<dur>`" and differ in shape: cyclic paces at the volume gap, burst fires back-to-back.
- Both client renderers (Linux python3, Windows PowerShell) rewritten for the above; the per-message log line became a per-fire summary (at `bulk` across a fanned-out peer set, per-message logging wrote more to disk than the persona wrote to the wire) that keeps the literal `sent` token so existing greps match, and adds a `fails=` counter.
- `KNOWN_PROTOS` +10: `sip`, `rtp`, `stun`, `mdns`, `llmnr`, `nbns`, `ssdp`, `dhcp`, `arp`, `beats`.

*Catalog content — eight personas (20 → 28), chosen by load shape, not by role:*
`voip-softphone` (constant-bitrate UDP, the only such shape) · `streaming-media-client` (sustained NS cover traffic) · `ci-build-farm` (bursty `bulk` WAN pulls) · `db-replica` (`real-service`, needs no driver work) · `log-shipper` (high pps / low bytes-per-packet — the profile that overruns a capture ring) · `broadcast-chatter` (the only broadcast/multicast persona) · `scanner-benign` (authorized scan; enormous flow count, trivial bytes) · `p2p-update-peer` (the only mesh — flow count grows with the square of the twin).

*Validator:* **`VALIDATOR` 112 → 114 rules** — new `PSN-100` (transport enum) and `PSN-110` (peer_scope enum); `PSN-070` widened for `bulk`. PSN 9 → 11.

**Why:**
- The `scripted` driver was documented as a v1 connectivity-shape emitter, and the deferral had a hard consequence nothing surfaced: a whole persona instance was capped near **5 tiny connections/second (~2 kbit/s)** — the gap bounded the persona, not the emit, since the client is a single-threaded loop over all emits. `volume:` could not express byte size at all, so "a 2 GB nightly backup" and "a DNS query" were the same traffic. No amount of *catalog authoring* could change that; the lever had to be in the driver.
- Two of the new personas close gaps rather than only adding volume. `broadcast-chatter`: `ad-domain-lab`/`purple-team-range` ship an LLMNR-poisoning step, but the twin carries no benign LLMNR/NBNS chatter for it to poison — the attack was detectable for the wrong reason (it was the only name-resolution traffic on the wire). `scanner-benign`: a range whose blue team only ever distinguishes "scan" from "not scan" teaches a habit that fails on a real network where sanctioned scanning runs on a schedule.
- `ci-build-farm`/`streaming-media-client` also give the `perimeter: wan` tap scope (built 2026-07-27) real traffic to capture, which it otherwise almost entirely lacked.

**Verification:**
- **Functional wire test** (not just render assertions): the rendered Linux client run against local UDP/TCP sinks produced **1269 datagrams / 1.77 MB in 4 s (~3.5 Mbit/s)** from one `volume: high` UDP emit, with exact TCP byte accounting (8 × 4096) on the TCP emit — against the ~2 kbit/s ceiling it replaces.
- Full suite green: **1709 passed / 12 skipped**. Two pins updated deliberately (both self-documenting as such): the Linux golden-render SHA256 in `test_persona_windows_port_review.py`, and the PSN id list.
- Every catalog persona still validates clean (28/28, zero PSN findings).
- **Not live-verified on `dev45`** — no on-cluster run yet. The TCP-byte caveat below is the specific thing a live run should check.

**Caveats / follow-ons:**
- **TCP byte volume only materializes against a real listener.** A refused connect is 2 packets, not 64 MiB. `ci-build-farm`/`streaming-media-client` reach their stated rates only where the peer role is backed by a `real-service` daemon; the guest log's `fails=` counter is the operator's tell. UDP emits have no such dependency.
- **Emits targeting the reserved `internet`/`egress` tokens put nothing on the wire** (they resolve to no peer IP and the client logs-and-skips) — pre-existing, and it silently affects 11 emits across the original catalog. The new personas route through `egress-proxy` instead. Worth a decision separately: either resolve those tokens to the twin's gateway, or make the skip louder.
- `parse_duration` has no sub-second unit, so `cyclic:` bottoms out at 1 s; RTP-style pacing is approximated by datagrams-per-tick rather than a true inter-packet interval.

---

## 2026-07-28 — The twin substrate becomes tap-agnostic: `tap:` IR flags, `record-tap-intent`, and the published tap-point catalog all removed (`docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_eligibility.md`, D1–D6 + STR-200)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code) for the build; the underlying D1–D6 decisions, the
`STR-200` validator rule, and all three version-locked schema bumps are **Authority: user,
2026-07-28** (recorded as such in `docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_eligibility.md` §3/§5 and repeated per bump
below).

**What changed:**
- **D1 — Tap eligibility is now the tap plan's sole authority over the twin's FULL topology.**
  `resolve_tap_point` (`axrange/planner/tappoints.py`) resolves against the twin's complete
  `hosts[].nets[]` attachment topology (sourced unconditionally from `attach-vm-to-vnet`), not the
  `record-tap-intent`-recorded subset. Any port a tap plan names is tappable — no allow-list, no
  hint, no default anywhere. `segment` now genuinely means "every port on the net," including the
  router's WAN uplink (`__wan__`), which is no longer a special case: it is carried in the same
  unconditional topology as any other port, so `perimeter: wan` resolves the ordinary way.
- **D2 — `tap:` removed from `IR_SCHEMA` entirely.** `nodes[].tap`, `nodes[].nets[].tap`, and
  `networks[].tap` (`axrange/ir/schema.py`) are gone. `scenario_schema_version`'s recognized set
  widens `{1, 2}` → `{1, 2, 3}` (enforced by `STR-020`, not just documented — see the review note
  below).
- **D3 — The named/labeled tap-point catalog is dropped.** `TwinConfigTapPoint`,
  `_apply_tap_points`, and `TapPointMaterializationError` (`axrange/twinconfig/{schema,expand,
  errors}.py`) are removed; a twin config's `tap_points:` key now fails loudly (error, not a silent
  no-op) instead of materializing into boolean `tap:` flags. Selector `labels:` matching is removed
  from the tap-plan grammar (`axrange/o2control/taps.py`) along with the field it matched on.
- **D4 — `OP-MIR-005 record-tap-intent` is removed** (`axrange/ops/registry.py`,
  `axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py`) — op count **40 → 39**. A `te` deploy now emits **zero** OP-MIR
  ops of any kind (not even a declarative bookkeeping one). The A2 tap playout's discovery
  (`axrange/o2control/taps.py` `_scan_te_inventory`/`_tapped_iface_set`) re-points to the
  inventory's full topology instead of this op's persisted output; the `tap_scopes` M3 WAN
  special-case it carried is removed with it, exactly as that order anticipated.
- **D5 — The published inventory drops `tap_points[]`.** `build_twin_inventory`
  (`axrange/planner/inventory.py`), `te inventory` (`axrange/cli/main.py`), the API DTO
  (`axrange/api/{dtos,service}.py`, `GET /api/deploys/{id}/inventory`), and `twin-config show`
  publish only `hosts[].nets[]` (full topology) — no browsable tap-point catalog.
  `derive_default_tap_points` survives as **internal**, unpublished resolution machinery;
  `tap_point:` bindings still resolve against its **derived** ids
  (`<net>`/`<net>-perimeter`/`<a>--<b>`/`<host>:<net>:<nic>`), computed on demand at bind time.
- **D6 — The `tap_host`/`tap_net` → `sensor` binding kind is removed** from `bindings:`
  (`IR_SCHEMA` §6) — it referenced "tapped nodes," a concept D2 removes. `REF-200` (which validated
  its references) retires with it; a stray one is now caught by `STR-100`'s pre-existing closed
  binding-kind set instead of a dedicated rule.
- **Validator (`docs/VALIDATOR.md`):** retires `STR-190` (the old `tap:`-is-a-boolean shape check),
  `NET-110` (tapped-segment-has-≥1-node), and `REF-200` (all three: never reused, marked
  deprecated). Adds **`STR-200`** (Pass 1, structural, error) — a `tap:` field anywhere on a
  node/leg/network is rejected loudly ("`tap:` is removed; author taps in the tap plan
  (scope+target selector)"), because Pydantic's `extra="allow"` IR model would otherwise silently
  drop a leftover `tap:` with no signal (LES-111 posture). Net rule count **114 → 112**.
- **Capacity guardrail (§7 of the work order):** a non-blocking warning at the shared
  `resolve_tap_plan_bindings` choke point fires when a resolved tap plan's port count or fan-out
  crosses a large threshold — full-topology eligibility makes "bind everything to one sensor"
  trivial to author, and every fan-out destination duplicates frames on the transport wire.
- **Content migration:** ~63 `tap:` flags stripped across 11 `target-environments/` files
  (`optrahealth-twin`, `enterprise-multisite`, `ot-ics-range`, `purple-team-range`,
  `fabric-verify`, `vuln-management-lab`, `dfir-hunt-lab`, `ctf-range`, `soc-detection-lab`,
  `ad-domain-lab`, plus `target-environments/README.md` prose) and `demo/twin.demo1.yaml`; the
  `tap_points:` block removed from all 5 `twin-configs/`. The shipped `corp-enterprise` tap plans
  (already `selector: {scope, target}`-only, no `labels:`/`tap_point:`) are unaffected.
- **Second-order fixes surfaced by the removal, not part of D1–D6 but required by it:**
  `axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py`'s `_emit_overlays` had `capture_sensor_id: str | None` threaded
  through on the assumption a `te` deploy might still have tap-derived sensor context; made
  unconditional (`str`, never `None`) — otherwise any scenario with an `overlays.defense[].
  observes` entry would raise, since that context source no longer exists at all. Separately,
  `axrange/imagebuild/conformance.py`'s `build_smoke_scenario_yaml` (a string-templated Scenario-IR
  generator, not checked-in content) still emitted a literal `tap: false` line — invisible to a
  content grep, but a hard `STR-200` failure of `axrange image build`'s own smoke-test scenario had
  it shipped (LES-162).
- **Three code-review rounds** (code-writer → tester → code-reviewer, iterated three times) found
  one **must-fix** — `STR-020`'s recognized-version-set widening was documented but not enforced
  until the actual check was updated in the same change (LES-161) — plus the `imagebuild`
  generator miss above (LES-162), stale `redo_on`/`description` prose on sibling ops
  (`install-ovs-mirror`, `attach-gre-tunnel`; LES-163), a stale "reverted earlier revision" docstring
  note in `inventory.py` unrelated to this ticket's own files (LES-164), and two test fixtures
  whose header prose still narrated a "tapped" premise after their `tap: true` flags were removed
  (LES-165). All closed before merge.

**Why:**
- A tap is deployed and destroyed independently of the twin (the `tap` tier), yet the twin config
  still authored tap-related content and the resolver was gated on it — `segment` could not mean
  "every port on the net," only "every port someone remembered to flag when the twin was deployed."
  GAP-028 had been "fixed" by hand-editing `tap: true` into eleven archetypes — the clearest symptom
  that this decision belonged entirely to the tap plan, not the twin substrate. The user's decision
  (2026-07-28) was the cleaner, more aggressive of two options considered: rather than demote
  `tap:` to an optional hint and keep `record-tap-intent` as a declared default, remove tap
  authoring from the twin substrate entirely — the tap layer owns 100% of tap semantics.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/IR_SCHEMA.md` §1.5/§3.1/§3.2/§6 — `tap:` field removed; `scenario_schema_version`
  recognized-set bump documented; `tap_host`/`tap_net` binding kind removed
- `docs/OP_CATALOG.md` §5.3/§5.8/§8 — `OP-MIR-005 record-tap-intent` removed; op count 40 → 39
- `docs/VALIDATOR.md` §5.1/§5.3/§5.6 — `STR-190`/`NET-110`/`REF-200` retired; `STR-200` added; rule
  count 114 → 112
- `docs/ADAPTER.md` — capture-plane note corrected (no more `record-tap-intent` bookkeeping op)
- `docs/design/06-taps-sensors.md` — tap-eligibility section rewritten (no `tap:` authoring, no
  published catalog, derived-not-published `tap_point:` ids); "current limits" gains the capacity
  guardrail
- `docs/design/02-twin-model.md` — `te inventory`'s description corrected (no more "tap points")
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — GAP-028 stays ✅ Resolved under the new mechanism (superseded, not
  reopened); GAP-030 ✅ Dissolved (the `labels[]` field it tracked as inert is gone, not merely
  unwired); GAP-029 WAN-egress half flipped to live-verified (below); GAP-028's live-verify note
  flipped to PASSED; new GAP-046/GAP-047 opened (pre-existing, unrelated bugs the live-verify run
  surfaced)
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — LES-161 through LES-166 added by the build; LES-167/LES-168 added by the
  live-verify (below)
- `CLAUDE.md` — counters re-derived (39 op types, 112 catalog rules, 1695 pytest tests, 14
  occurrences); new "twin substrate becomes tap-agnostic" blockquote; superseding pointers added to
  the "Sensor-agnostic `te_` substrate" and "Twin-centric re-frame" blockquotes (left in place, not
  rewritten); both blockquotes' live-verify-pending callouts flipped to PASSED (below)
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_eligibility.md` — status OPEN/USER-GATED → DONE (code/tests/docs); gated
  `dev45` live-verify PASSED 2026-07-28

**Affected code:** `axrange/ir/schema.py`, `axrange/ops/registry.py`,
`axrange/planner/{tappoints,inventory,pass1_emit,access}.py`, `axrange/o2control/taps.py`,
`axrange/twinconfig/{schema,expand,errors}.py`, `axrange/validator/catalog.py`,
`axrange/validator/checks/{structural,network,references}.py`, `axrange/api/{dtos,service}.py`,
`axrange/cli/main.py`, `axrange/adapter_proxmox/{adapter,config}.py`,
`axrange/imagebuild/conformance.py`; 11 `target-environments/*.yaml` + `README.md`, 5
`twin-configs/*/*.yaml`, `demo/twin.demo1.yaml`.

**Live-verify (dev45, 2026-07-28):** PASSED end-to-end (`docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_eligibility.md` §9). A
fresh focused twin `tap-elig-verify` (Debian router + WAN + `h-a`/`h-b` on VLAN net `lan`,
`scenario_schema_version: 3`, **zero `tap:` flags** anywhere in the IR) was captured to the
external tcpdump sensor `Tcpdump-SW-A`. Confirmed: **D2** — `scenario_schema_version: 3` validates
(`STR-020` accepts `{1,2,3}`); **D4** — the `te` deploy emitted **zero** OP-MIR ops (no
`record-tap-intent`/mirror/gretap/verify-capture) at plan and deploy; **D5** — `te inventory
--json` published no `tap_points` key, only `hosts[].nets[]` (the router's `__wan__` leg marked
external); **D1** — `tap deploy` resolved with no `tap:` anywhere in the twin: `segment: lan` bound
every port on the net including the router's own leg (`h-a`, `h-b`, `rtr/lan`, all rx-only) and
`perimeter: wan` bound `rtr/__wan__` (both directions). On-wire capture showed each segment
host↔host request/reply sequence exactly once (rx-only, no duplication despite 3 tapped ports) and
all four WAN-egress NAT legs in clean, matching counts (pre-NAT at the router's LAN rx, post-NAT at
WAN tx, reply at WAN rx, de-NAT at the host's LAN rx) — independently confirming `segment` also
captured the router's own port. Teardown swept the verify twin's 9 resources cleanly, leaving
`corp-enterprise` and its `tap`/`tfc`/`exr`, the O2 surface, external sensors, and image templates
all untouched. **Two pre-existing bugs, unrelated to this change, surfaced during the run and are
logged as new gaps rather than fixed here:** `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` **GAP-046** (`apply-sdn-config`'s
trailing cluster-wide SSH tap-reassert scan is unscoped and O(bridges × running-VMs); its hardcoded
60s timeout fails on a busy node even though the SDN apply itself succeeds) and **GAP-047**
(`OP-BAR-010 network-ready`'s declared predecessor on `apply-sdn-config` has referenced an
out-of-scope scope var since the original op-registry commit, so `_resolve_pattern` has always
silently dropped the edge — the barrier never actually gates on that op). Neither blocked the run
(both ops' real work completed; only the ancillary scan/edge is affected), but both are real,
pre-existing defects now tracked (`docs/LESSONS.md` LES-167/LES-168).

**Supersedes:**
- **`OP-MIR-005 record-tap-intent`** — CHANGELOG 2026-07-13 "feat: sensor-agnostic `te_` substrate
  — BUILD" (which introduced it as the te deploy's entire tap-emission surface). The op is removed
  outright, not repurposed; the sensor-agnostic-te scope shift it was part of (a `te` deploy stands
  up only its own components) is otherwise unchanged.
- **The `tap:` IR flags** — CHANGELOG 2026-06-19 "implementation: dynamic taps + capture heartbeat
  (verify-capture)" / "design: dynamic (declarative) tap points + capture heartbeat" (which
  introduced declarative `tap:` at host/leg/segment granularity). The flags are removed from
  `IR_SCHEMA` entirely, not demoted to a hint; the `verify-capture` heartbeat itself survives,
  composed entirely by the A2 tap playout instead.
- **The published `tap_points[]` inventory + named/labeled tap-point catalog** — CHANGELOG
  2026-07-24 "Tap points BUILT: semantic tap-point model + resolution (second twin-model build)"
  (which built `TapPointInfo`, the published `tap_points[]` field, and `id`/`labels[]`
  addressing). The catalog is dropped, not relocated; `tap_point:`/`selector:` (minus `labels:`)
  addressing survives, now resolved as a derived id against full topology rather than a lookup.
- **The `tap_host`/`tap_net` → `sensor` binding kind + `REF-200`** — CHANGELOG 2026-06-03 "axrange:
  per-tap sink routing implemented (IR sinks: + REF-200 + per-(bridge,sink) tunnels)" (the
  per-tap sensor-routing binding kind, authored under the pre-rename "sink" terminology). Removed
  per D6, forced by `tap:`'s removal; `REF-200` retires with it.
- **The `tap_scopes` M3 WAN special-case** — CHANGELOG 2026-07-27 "Tap scopes BUILT: `port` /
  `segment` / `perimeter` / `boundary` with derived direction" (which added `record-tap-intent`'s
  always-included WAN-port entry as a minimal, explicitly-reversible eligibility unblock,
  documented there as removable once this work order landed). Removed exactly as that entry
  anticipated — full-topology eligibility makes the special case unnecessary.

**Links:** `docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_eligibility.md`; `docs/design/06-taps-sensors.md`

## 2026-07-27 — Persisted op inputs refresh on re-execute; tap read-back surfaces filter to COMPLETED (GAP-044 + GAP-045)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- **`axrange/planner/state.py` / `executor.py`** — `set_op_running` gained optional keyword-only
  `inputs_resolved_json` / `inputs_from_outputs_json`; the status flip to `RUNNING` and the inputs
  refresh land in the **same UPDATE**. `_run_one` passes the current in-memory op's serialized inputs
  (via `op_to_row`, not a re-implementation). Supplying exactly one of the two kwargs now raises
  `ValueError` **before** any transaction opens, rather than silently wiping the other to `{}`.
- **`axrange/o2control/taps.py`** — both loops of `_bindings_summary_from_ops` and the loop in
  `tap_info_from_deploy` now filter positively to `status == COMPLETED`; `_delete_dropped_gretaps`
  marks every dropped pair's `attach-gre-tunnel` op `destroyed`, **unconditionally** — including
  pairs whose gretap *device* is deliberately KEPT under VLAN-zone aliasing.
- **`insert_ops` keeps `INSERT OR IGNORE` unchanged.** No schema bump (`SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 3);
  both columns already existed.

**Why:**
- **GAP-044**, found live on dev45: `INSERT OR IGNORE` is right for resume-safety, but it also meant
  `inputs_resolved_json` was written once at first apply and **never updated**. A re-applied deploy
  executed from the fresh in-memory plan — wire correct — while the database kept the first apply's
  inputs forever, so `tap status` reported capture direction that had not been true for days.
- A **blanket upsert** was the tempting fix and is wrong in the other direction: it would claim new
  inputs are live for an op a resume legitimately **skipped**. Persisting on execute is what makes
  the record honest in both directions.
- Persisting at attempt-start then makes the consumer filter **mandatory**: `set_op_running` fires
  before lock acquisition, so an op failing lock resolution or timing out records new inputs with
  `attempts=0` and no adapter call. **LES-113 already recorded this rule** and `traffic.py` already
  obeyed it — its `Scope:` line just never named `taps.py`, which is why it went unapplied.
- **GAP-045** (found reviewing the GAP-044 fix, reproduced on the fixed tree) is the same
  over-report in the *sensors* dimension: the gretap netdev was deleted but its op row never retired,
  so a de-scoped sensor was reported forever. GAP-044 could not close without it.

**Diagnosis corrected twice, worth recording:** the incident was first explained as "`tap info`
re-resolves, `tap status` is cached", then as "`tap info` isolates the fresh row". **Both were
wrong.** Both verbs read the identical persisted rows; `tap info` was equally stale and merely
scattered the defect across itemized rows instead of collapsing it into one confident aggregate
token. See LES-158.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — GAP-044 corrected + closed, GAP-045 added + closed
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — LES-158/159/160 added; **LES-113's `Scope:` widened** to name `taps.py`

**Verification:** 1709 passed / 12 skipped. Every new guard test was demonstrated to fail against the
specific wrong implementation it guards — including the unconditional `mark_op_destroyed`, whose
gated variant otherwise passes all 131 tap tests (LES-160). Gated `dev45` live-verify pending.

## 2026-07-27 — Docs consolidation: fold twin-model/networking-capture/taps-sensors briefs into `docs/design/`, retire all 19 design briefs, repoint inbound references

**Authority:** user

**What changed:**
- Folded `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_{twin_model,tap_scopes,o2_taps,dynamic_tap,sensors,sdn_capture,fabric,production_topology}.md` into three `docs/design/` files, rewritten present-tense/current-state (per the ongoing `docs/design/` vs. `DESIGN_BRIEF_*` consolidation; this is one of three parallel folding passes covering all 13 `docs/design/*.md` files).
- `docs/design/06-taps-sensors.md` — replaced the retired tap-scope vocabulary (`segment`/`boundary`/`host`/`leg`) with the current one (`port`/`segment`/`perimeter`/`boundary`), added the derived-direction rule (rx-only for multi-port sets, tx&rx for a single port), the WAN edge (`perimeter: wan`), the tap-eligibility gate (`tap:` authored at node/leg/network granularity, resolved once at deploy time into the pool every tap point draws from), and a "current limits" list (dropped traffic invisible to `boundary`/`perimeter`, VXLAN-bridged pairs with no joining host, no per-binding filtering).
- `docs/design/04-networking-capture.md` — added the VyOS-only constraint on the fabric slice, the tenant-VRF internet-egress limitation (not implemented), and a mechanism-level note that a tap's `tc` mirror direction is derived from whichever tap point addresses it (full table lives in 06).
- `docs/design/02-twin-model.md` — reviewed against `DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md`; already reflected the current five-tier model and needed no edit.
- **Final cleanup pass, same day:** folded the one remaining orphaned brief — `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_production_topology.md`'s live Tier-1 content (a single production host/VM running the control-plane API and Grafana/Loki/Prometheus as local containers under one bring-up, design-only/not built) — into `docs/design/05-o2-surface.md` as a short "Production topology" section; its dead Tier-2 "appliance data plane" framing was dropped, per the brief's own two supersession notes (first by the in-scenario sensors model, then by O2's standing sensors).
- **Deleted all 19 `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_*.md` files** (`git rm`) — the design-brief genre is retired; `docs/design/` is now the single design-of-record.
- Rewrote `docs/design/README.md`'s header (no longer "one of two" doc sets) and added a compact retired-brief → current-file mapping table (all 19 former briefs), plus a pointer to `docs/CHANGELOG.md`/`docs/LESSONS.md`/git history as where the reasoning trail now lives.
- Fixed the 9 dangling in-`docs/design/` cross-references that pointed at soon-to-be-deleted briefs (`05-o2-surface.md` ×2, `08-exercise-measurement.md` ×2, `11-api-ui.md` ×2, `12-ai-scenario-research.md`, `README.md`'s genre description) — repointed to `docs/CHANGELOG.md`/the mapping table rather than naming a dead file.

## 2026-07-27 — Tap scopes BUILT: `port` / `segment` / `perimeter` / `boundary` with derived direction (`docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_scopes.md`, M1–M8)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- Implements the design ratified the same day directly below this entry (`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_tap_scopes.md`) — see that entry for the model itself (the four scopes, derived direction, the WAN edge). This entry covers what shipped and what the build itself surfaced, beyond the design record. Code, tests, and three review rounds are complete.
- **`perimeter` generalized to take a net id in ALL cases**, not two special-cased spellings: `perimeter: corp-lan` (a segment's edge) and `perimeter: wan` (the twin's external edge) are one resolution rule — "every `kind: router` port attached to `target`" — with `wan` resolving through the same author-facing alias for the `__wan__` sentinel used everywhere else.
- **New leaf module `axrange/tapscopes.py`** — `TAP_SCOPES`, `direction_for_scope`, `scope_error_detail`, **zero imports**. Both `axrange/planner/tappoints.py` (re-exports it, for backward-compat call sites) and `axrange/twinconfig/expand.py` import from it directly. This is the concrete fix for **LES-097** ("tier/tag vocabularies must live in ONE exported table"): before this, the scope enum only existed inside `tappoints.py`, and `twinconfig/expand.py` cannot import `tappoints.py` (or anything in `planner/`) without violating **LES-025**'s no-import direction between those two modules — a shared leaf module is the only shape that satisfies both rules at once.
- **`verify-capture` made direction-aware.** rx-only broke the existing boundary heartbeat outright: it injected a sentinel from a bystander leaf on the segment and relied on the frame being *delivered to* the mirrored port (the tx half, now gone for multi-port scopes). Fixed by making the heartbeat aware of which ports it's proving are rx-only-mirrored: for an rx-only binding whose candidate ports are router-only by construction (`perimeter`'s and `segment`'s router legs), the sentinel now injects **from the router itself**, addressed to a **unicast on-segment target** so the directly-connected route selects the mirrored NIC (a broadcast target does not reliably exercise one specific egress NIC choice the same way). This audit — walking every *consumer* of "a frame arrives at this port," not just the mirror-composition code — is what caught the break; see LES entry below.
- **Gretap device naming is now twin-discriminated, but only for node-shared bridges.** `vmbr0` is the first **node-shared** bridge (every twin's router uses it for WAN egress) to enter `gre_port_for_bridge_sensor`'s `(bridge, sensor)` keyspace. Without a twin-discriminator, two different twins both tapping `perimeter: wan` to the same sensor would collide on one `(vmbr0, sensor)` device name, and either twin's teardown would tear down the other's live capture. **Twin-scoped bridges (`tebr_*`/`tev*`) keep byte-identical naming** — deliberately: renaming an already-deployed resource's device name is out of bounds for this change, and no twin-scoped bridge is shared across twins in the first place so no collision existed there.
- **`record-tap-intent` now always marks a router's WAN port tappable.** This is the deliberately minimal, reversible eligibility unblock — brief §9 open-decision (2), option (b): the WAN uplink has no `NetRef` in the twin IR to carry a `tap:` flag, so `perimeter: wan` could never resolve without *some* eligibility change. The larger structural fix (move tap eligibility out of the twin IR entirely, into the tap plan) is deliberately **not** in this build — it is `docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_eligibility.md`, left OPEN and user-gated. The special-case site in `record-tap-intent` carries a comment pointing at that work order so it gets *removed*, not layered on, when that work lands.
- **Known limit, stated plainly:** `perimeter: wan` cannot resolve on a twin deployed **before** this change landed. `record-tap-intent`'s op row is already `completed` on such a deploy, so the new WAN-tappable input never gets (re-)published into that op's persisted output — a resume only re-runs ops that changed inputs, and this op's declared inputs didn't change in a way the state store tracks (**LES-096**). The fix requires a `--fresh` redeploy of the twin, not a resume. The engine's error message when `perimeter: wan` fails to resolve on a stale twin now names this requirement explicitly rather than leaving an operator to guess.
- **Verified numbers:** `1693 passed / 12 skipped / 0 failed, 1705 collected` (`.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q`), re-derived directly rather than trusting a prior report (LES-050).

**Why:**
- Ships the SPAN-model redesign so the exactly-once capture guarantee (§3 of the design brief) is real on the wire, not just on paper — and the two structural gaps the design didn't fully anticipate (a vocabulary shared across a no-import module boundary, and a heartbeat mechanism that assumed tx delivery) had to be closed for the design to be safe to deploy at all.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_scopes.md` — status → DONE (code/tests/docs); gated dev45 live-verify PENDING
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_tap_scopes.md` — status → BUILT; records what shipped vs. deferred
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` §4.1, `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_taps.md` §10 — supersedes pointers updated to say BUILT
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-029 — WAN-egress half 🟡 → ✅ Resolved (build; live-verify pending), with the LES-096 `--fresh` caveat noted
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — LES-142..LES-150 (nine entries, triaged from eleven reviewer candidates; see below)
- `CLAUDE.md` — test counter refresh (1658 → 1705) + tap-scope vocabulary update in the `tap_points` BUILT blockquote
- `docs/OP_CATALOG.md` — `OP-MIR-020 install-ovs-mirror`'s `tapped_nics` entry shape documents the new `direction` key; `OP-MIR-005 record-tap-intent`'s decomposition note documents the always-included router WAN uplink entry. **No version bump** (see the version-lock ruling below).

**Affected code:**
- `axrange/tapscopes.py` (new)
- `axrange/planner/tappoints.py`, `axrange/planner/inventory.py`, `axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py`
- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/ssh.py` (per-gretap direction, `gre_port_for_bridge_sensor` twin-discrimination for `vmbr0`)
- `axrange/o2control/taps.py` (new scopes, `wan` alias, direction union per `(port, sensor)`, direction-aware `verify-capture` sentinel)
- `axrange/twinconfig/expand.py` (retired-scope rejection with an actionable message)
- `axrange/api/{dtos,service}.py` (`kind`/`external` on inventory DTOs)
- `axrange/cli/main.py` (M6 CLI surfaces: inventory renderer, tap-plan renderers, error messages)
- `twin-configs/datacenter/datacenter.yaml` (mechanical `scope: host` → `scope: port` migration, M7)

**Version-lock ruling — `OP_CATALOG.md` `tapped_nics.direction` (RESOLVED):**
- **Authority: user, 2026-07-27 — additive key within an existing input, NO version bump.** `install-ovs-mirror`'s `tapped_nics` entries gain a `direction` key ("rx"|"both"); `record-tap-intent`'s tapped set now always includes a router's WAN uplink entry. Both are now documented in `docs/OP_CATALOG.md` (§5.3, `OP-MIR-020`'s inputs + description, `OP-MIR-005`'s decomposition note) with no version bump — the op count stays 40 and the rule count stays 114.
- **Reasoning that made it additive, not a signature change:** the input's *name and type token* (`tapped_nics: nic-index-list:list`) are unchanged; the per-entry shape was always prose-documented in the op's comments/description, not a typed field list the contract enumerates field-by-field; two prior keys (`sensor_id`, `gre_port_input`) were added to this same per-entry shape the same way, for dynamic taps (2026-06-19), with no version bump either; and an absent `direction` defaults to `"both"`, which preserves the exact prior behavior for every already-persisted op and every already-completed deploy — nothing that validated or deployed under the old signature stops validating or deploying, and nothing changes for it (LES-096 posture).
- This was the one deliberately deferred deliverable from the original build pass (code-writer and code-reviewer had independently reached the same "additive" read but the ruling itself was the user's to make, per `CLAUDE.md` "Design Document Maintenance"'s version-locked-schema trigger) — now closed.

**Still pending, not silently missing:**
- Gated `dev45` live-verify — **requires a freshly deployed twin** (LES-096), not the standing `corp-enterprise` one. **Approved and starting separately** (2026-07-27); its results are a later documentation pass, not this one — the status stays PENDING everywhere it's recorded until that pass lands.
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_eligibility.md` — OPEN, user-gated, not started.
- BPF filtering (brief §9(3)) — deferred by the user, no work order yet.
- A `completed-unverified` terminal state for a heartbeat skipped by topology — floated by the reviewer as a follow-on, explicitly not built here.

**New lessons (`docs/LESSONS.md`):** LES-142 (rx-only direction change invalidates tx-dependent verifiers — the flagship finding), LES-143 (a node-shared bridge entering per-`(bridge, sensor)` naming breaks twin-uniqueness, LES-118's family), LES-144 (never re-key a safety predicate to a narrower question), LES-145 (a unicast sentinel needs ARP; a broadcast one does not), LES-146 (prove a topology-forced injector can actually run the sentinel), LES-147 (an empty `tc filter` action list is invalid syntax — omit it), LES-148 ("record instead of drop" must audit derived key domains), LES-149 (a shared render helper's default decoration can leak into a `--json`/DTO field, extends LES-037), LES-150 (a vocabulary/selector can silently widen when an upstream change enlarges the collection it iterates). One reviewer candidate — "narrowing a per-host resource set must actively revert the dropped members" — was judged already covered by the existing LES-140/LES-141 pair and did not get a new entry.

## 2026-07-27 — Tap scopes re-based on a realistic SPAN model: `port` / `segment` / `perimeter` / `boundary`, with direction derived from scope

**Authority:** user

**What changed:**
- The tap-point scope vocabulary is redefined (DESIGN only — not built). `segment`/`boundary`/
  `host`/`leg` becomes **`port` / `segment` / `perimeter` / `boundary`**: `leg` → `port` (rename,
  same `{host, net}` target), old-`host` (all legs of a host) **dropped** as a scope (it becomes N
  `port` bindings or a `select_tap_points` query), and **`perimeter`** added — "every router port
  attached to this net," i.e. everything a network exchanges with anything else.
- **Direction is now part of the model, derived from the scope, never authored:** rx-only for a
  multi-port set (`segment`, `boundary`), tx & rx for a single port (`port`) and for
  `perimeter`. Rationale: every tapped port currently mirrors BOTH directions
  (`adapter_proxmox/ssh.py:383-384` installs an `ingress` *and* an `egress` `matchall` filter), so
  any n-port scope delivers each frame to the sensor 2..N times — a host↔host frame twice, a
  broadcast once per host on the net. rx-only is the textbook SPAN de-dup recipe: each frame
  captured exactly once, at its point of entry.
- **`kind: router` legs stay INSIDE `segment` scope.** An in-flight change excluding them (same
  date, reverted before this entry) was superseded: under rx-only, excluding the router leg is not
  a de-dup, it is a coverage hole — the router's port is the ONLY place traffic arriving from
  another segment or the internet enters the net, and a host's own port mirrors only rx.
- **The `exclude:` clause considered for `segment` was deliberately dropped.** Under rx-only,
  exactly-once holds only at full coverage; excluding ports silently loses traffic from an excluded
  port to an included one (LES-001/LES-089 posture). A subset of a segment is authored as N `port`
  bindings instead.
- **The WAN edge becomes expressible** — `perimeter: wan`. `pass1_emit.py:869-882` already emits an
  `attach-vm-to-vnet` op for the router's uplink (`net_id: "__wan__"`, real `nic_index`, `vmbr0`);
  `inventory.py:344-345` was simply discarding it. The fix records it behind a typed
  `HostNet.external` marker (not name-sniffing), with `wan` as the author-facing alias for the
  `__wan__` sentinel. No adapter change: the uplink is `tap<vmid>i<nic>` like any other port.
  **Resolves the WAN-egress half of `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-029** (the VXLAN-bridged-pair half
  stays open).
- No new tap-plan addressing mode: all four scopes slot into the existing `selector:`/`tap_point:`
  shapes (`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_taps.md` §10), and the three shipped
  `twin-configs/corp-enterprise/tap-*.yaml` plans need **no edit** (they use only `segment` and
  `boundary`, both unchanged in spelling).

**Why:**
- A tap in axRange already *is* a SPAN session — the vnet is the switch, a VM NIC is a port, the
  sensor is the SPAN destination — but the scope vocabulary was named for topology objects rather
  than for the question each answers, and it had no notion of direction at all. Naming the four
  distinct questions ("this interface" / "this network" / "this network's edge" / "between these
  two networks") and deriving direction from the scope removes the duplication silently present in
  every multi-port capture today, at zero cost to the author-facing surface.
- This does NOT reverse the twin-model brief's "`direction` is NOT a field anywhere" (§4.1): that
  bans north-south/east-west *traffic taxonomy* from the model (pushed to free-form `labels[]`).
  Host-side rx/tx *port* direction is an orthogonal dimension no decision had covered, and it stays
  out of the spec surface here since it is derived, not written.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_tap_scopes.md` — NEW; the design of record for this model (§3 direction
  rationale, §4 the four scopes, §5 the WAN edge, §6 worked syntax examples, §9 open decisions)
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` §4.1 — its four scopes are superseded by the above
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_taps.md` §10 — addressing unchanged, scope values updated
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-029 — WAN-egress half resolved by design

**Supersedes:** `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` §4.1's `segment`/`boundary`/`host`/`leg` scope
set and its resolution as BUILT 2026-07-24 (`docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_points.md`, CHANGELOG 2026-07-24
"Tap points BUILT"). Selector/`tap_point:` addressing and `mode: fanout|exclusive` are NOT
superseded.

**Open (must be settled before build, `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_tap_scopes.md` §9):** (1) union of
directions per `(port, sensor)` when one port appears in two bindings aimed at the same sensor —
`mode: exclusive` does not cover this; (2) whether tap **eligibility** moves from the twin IR's
`tap:` flags to the tap plan (recommended) or `record-tap-intent` merely special-cases router WAN
ports — `perimeter: wan` cannot resolve under the current IR gate either way; (3) BPF filter
syntax + whether it is enforced mirror-side (`tc flower`, per-binding, limited grammar) or
sensor-side (full BPF, per-sensor only).
