## 2026-07-27 — Fix: `tap status <twin-id>` resolves the twin's tap deploy (parity with `tap info`)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- `axrange.o2control.taps.tap_status` now disambiguates its positional/`deploy_id` argument the same way `tap_info` does: a non-`tap-` deploy id (i.e. a TWIN/target id) resolves to the twin's applied tap deploy via `find_deploy_for_plan(f"tap-{id}")`, instead of being used directly. Fixes `axrange tap status <twin-id>` (and `GET /api/o2/taps?deploy_id=<twin>`) printing `target: (blank)` / `bindings: (none)`. A genuine tap deploy id, `--target`, and the bare no-arg default are unchanged.

**Why:**
- `tap status corp-enterprise-f525eed4` (twin id as the positional `[ID]`) read tap bindings off the te deploy (which has none) → empty output, while `tap info corp-enterprise-f525eed4` resolved correctly — an inconsistency between the two read verbs on the same argument. Both now DWIM a twin id. (`tap status` = concise per-net summary; `tap info` = per-interface detail.) Since both the CLI and the API concise path call the shared `tap_status`, the fix reaches both (LES-037). dev45-verified.

**Affected docs:**
- `axrange/o2control/taps.py` (`tap_status`); `tests/test_cli_lifecycle.py`.

## 2026-07-26 — Fix: `install-ovs-mirror` with EMPTY `tapped_nics` CLEARS the mirror (a narrowing tap re-apply's revert), never raises

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- `SshBackend._install_ovs_mirror` no longer raises `"no tapped NICs / gre_port resolved"` when `tapped_nics` resolves to empty. An empty desired set is the **revert** case the tap playout deliberately composes for a host DROPPED on a narrowing re-apply (`o2control/taps.py::_compose_ops` — "with EMPTY desired content … actively reverting it"). It now **clears** that VM's mirror instead: writes+registers an empty (no-op) hook so a reboot reinstates nothing, and `tc qdisc del … clsact` on each of the VM's live tap ifaces (new `_clear_ovs_mirror`).

**Why:**
- A narrowing re-apply (dev45 `corp-enterprise` `corp-final` **segment** fan-out → `tap-xpress` **boundary**) force-reset every dropped host's `install-ovs-mirror` op to an empty desired set; the adapter's hard raise on empty then **failed all 28 dropped hosts** and left the whole tap deploy `failed` — even though the 3 new bindings (server-vlan → XPress-SW, corp-lan↔dmz boundary → XPress-SW-A) resolved correctly. The "re-assert dropped hosts back to EMPTY" design the module docstring describes was silently broken by the adapter rejecting empty.
- **dev45 live-verify:** the exact failing `tap deploy … tap-xpress.yaml` now completes (31/31 `install-ovs-mirror`), the dropped `o2-suricata`/`Tcpdump-SW-A` gretaps de-scope, both XPress source-gretaps' TX climbs under traffic, and a dropped workstation's tap shows **0 clsact filters** (mirror cleared).

**Affected docs:**
- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/ssh.py` (`_install_ovs_mirror` + `_clear_ovs_mirror`); `tests/test_adapter_proxmox.py`; `docs/LESSONS.md` LES-141.

## 2026-07-26 — API: `GET /api/o2/taps` read endpoint (concise status + verbose `tap info`)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- Added `GET /api/o2/taps`, the read peer of `axrange tap status` (concise) / `tap info` (verbose), closing the API parity gap (the tap surface previously exposed only `POST` apply + `DELETE` destroy). One `?verbose=` option toggles the two shapes: concise → `TapPlanStatusDTO`; verbose → new `TapInfoDTO` (per-binding host/nic/sensors + gre ports + transport IPs + `capture_verified`, plus resolved external-sensor access facts). `?target=<twin>` / `?deploy_id=<tap-id>` resolve like the CLI; verbose passwords are MASKED unless `?secrets=true` AND an authorized bearer token (LES-134, same gate as `/api/deploys/{id}/access`).
- New `axrange.o2control.taps.tap_info()` resolver (the rich peer of `tap_status()`); both are called by the CLI verb and the API service (LES-037 — one resolver, two callers). New DTOs `TapInfoDTO`/`TapBindingInfoDTO`; service `o2_tap_status`/`o2_tap_info`.

**Why:**
- Requested API parity for `tap info`. Verified live against `deploy-dev45/state.db`: concise resolves the twin→tap deploy (3 net bindings); verbose returns 33 per-binding entries + external-sensor facts (masked by default, `Axr4nge-Lab!` + resolved mgmt IPs on authorized reveal).

**Affected docs:**
- `axrange/api/{app,service,dtos}.py`, `axrange/o2control/taps.py` (`tap_info`); `tests/test_api.py`.

## 2026-07-26 — Fix: `tap info <twin-id>` resolves the twin's tap deploy instead of printing an empty card

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- `axrange tap info <id>` now accepts a **twin/target id** (the te deploy id, e.g. `corp-enterprise-f525eed4`), not only a tap deploy id (`tap-<twin>-<hash>`). When the positional id is not itself a tap deploy, it resolves the twin's applied tap deploy via `find_deploy_for_plan(f"tap-{id}")` — the same resolution `tap status --target` already uses. An id that is neither a tap deploy nor a tapped twin now errors (exit 2) with a clear message instead of rendering a garbage card.

**Why:**
- `tap info corp-enterprise-f525eed4` printed `target: ? (status=unknown)` / `bindings: (none)`: the handler derived `target` from the deploy's `plan_id` (`corp-enterprise`, no `tap-` prefix → `"?"`) and read tap bindings off the te deploy, which has none. Addressing a tap by its twin is the natural operator gesture (the tap plan's `target` *is* the twin), so `tap info` must resolve it, not mis-read it.

**Affected docs:**
- `axrange/cli/main.py` (`cmd_tap_info`); `tests/test_cli_lifecycle.py` (two regression tests: twin-id resolution + non-tap/non-twin rejection). dev45-verified against `deploy-dev45/state.db`.

## 2026-07-26 — Standing tcpdump sensor as an EXTERNAL VM with a virtio-fs host share (per-cluster deviation) + pcap helper scripts — BUILT + dev45 live-verified

**Authority:** user

**What changed:**
- Established a per-cluster (dev45) option to run the standing O2 tcpdump sensor as a **hand-built external VM** rather than a synthesized deployable sensor — declared in `adapter.conf`'s `EXTERNAL_SENSORS` exactly like the `XPress-SW` packetXpress sink. **No engine source change** (config-only, the intended footprint): `default_surface_manifest()`, `SENSOR_TYPES`, the validator, and the archetypes are all untouched. "Removed from the deployable sensors" here means the operator simply does not stand up a synthesized tcpdump-sensor on that cluster's O2 surface; the external declaration provides the tap target.
- The external tcpdump VM's capture directory is a **virtio-fs share mapped from a directory on the Proxmox host** (host path `/var/lib/axrange/o2-pcaps/<sensor-id>/`, guest-mounted at `/var/lib/axrange/capture` via a PVE directory mapping + `qm set --virtiofsN dirid=<map>`). This makes pcaps **directly readable on the host filesystem** (no QGA reach-in) and **decouples pcap storage from the sensor VM's lifecycle** — captures survive a power cycle *and* a full VM destroy/rebuild.
- **Naming convention (Authority: user):** external sensors follow `<Type>-SW-<Letter>` — mirroring `XPress-SW`/`XPress-SW-A`, with the trailing letter as a per-instance tag so a lab can grow `Tcpdump-SW-A`, `-B`, … The first tcpdump sensor is `Tcpdump-SW-A`; its adapter.conf `id`, PVE VM name, and host share subdir all match.
- Added two operator-side helper scripts: `scripts/o2-pcaps-list.sh` (list `cap-*` with sizes/total, optional glob) and `scripts/o2-pcaps-fetch.sh` (download matched file(s) to a local dest; host-side glob resolution → copies the resolved list, not a re-globbed pattern; `cp` local / `rsync --files-from`→per-file `scp` remote; multi-file-into-one-file guard; `--dry-run`). Both take `--host`/`--dir`/`--sensor` (or `PCAP_HOST`/`PCAP_DIR`/`PCAP_SENSOR`) and run locally on the host or over ssh (fast-fail `ConnectTimeout`).
- Documented the tcpdump-as-external declaration + the trailing-tag convention next to `XPress-SW` in `adapter.conf.example`.

**dev45 live-verify (2026-07-26):** built `Tcpdump-SW-A` (vmid 260) by full-cloning `img-debian-12-cloud` (620) on PVE 9.2 — dual NIC (`vmbr0` DHCP for apt egress + `o2v7dc98` transport at `10.255.251.11`), `balloon 0` (virtio-fs needs a shared memfd backend), `virtiofs0 dirid=tcpdump-sw-a` → host dir `/var/lib/axrange/o2-pcaps/Tcpdump-SW-A`. Provisioned over QGA: virtio-fs mount (fstab `nofail`), `tcpdump` apt-installed, `gre-cap` flow-based gretap + tcpdump capture + disk/count prune watchdog as **persistent** systemd units (mirroring `render.py`'s `-G 3600 -C 100` + `cap-*` watchdog, not the transient `systemd-run` form). Verified: all units active, capture writing `cap-*`, the virtio-fs share is **bidirectionally** visible on the host, both helper scripts list+fetch over ssh, and `load_config` recognizes `Tcpdump-SW-A` as a teardown-protected external sensor (vmid 260 auto-added to `protected_external_vmids`). Registered in the real `adapter.conf`.

**dev45 follow-on — tap verify + synthesized-tcpdump decommission (2026-07-26, Authority: user):** wired a real tap from the standing 32-host `corp-enterprise-f525eed4` twin to `Tcpdump-SW-A` (server-vlan segment) and proved capture — `gre-cap` RX climbed and a file-server→DC ping showed **220 ICMP for the DC in the host-share pcap**, listed/pulled directly off the host via the helper scripts (pcaps grew to 15 MB). Then removed the synthesized `o2-o2-tcpdump` (135913) by destroying the two-sensor O2 surface and redeploying a **suricata-only** surface (`deploy-dev45/surfaces/suricata-only.yaml`, `o2-surface-025c0709`); the transport vnet `o2v7dc98` is a deterministic hash of the subnet, so it survived the destroy/redeploy and `XPress-SW`/`XPress-SW-A`/`Tcpdump-SW-A` auto-reconnected with no NIC re-point. Two painful gotchas were hit and recorded as **LES-139** (`--images` is the dir *containing* `images/`, not `images/` itself — a doubled path silently loads zero bindings and every clone fails "template_vmid missing"; and a resume re-uses the failed plan's inputs, so a fresh deploy is required) and **LES-140** (the tap tier is one-plan-per-twin full-replace — a single-binding "additive" apply de-scoped the XPress mirrors; restored from the captured `tap status`, XPress down ~1 min). Correct invocation on dev45: `--db deploy-dev45/state.db --images deploy-dev45`. (The synthesized `o2-o2-tcpdump` at vmid 135913 is gone; the standing surface now carries only `o2-suricata`.)

**Why:**
- Two real pains at once: an operator hand-copying pcaps out of the sensor guest over QGA, and a synthesized standing sensor losing every capture when its VM is rebuilt (related to the retention/persistence concern behind `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-025). A host-mapped virtio-fs share fixes both, and the already-live external-sensor machinery (teardown auto-protection via id/vmid; `verify-capture` deliberately skipped; tap plans target by `id`) means it costs zero engine code — a genuine one-off deviation, not a rearchitecture.
- The alternative (building virtio-fs into the adapter + sensor image + a live-verify gate) was deferred in favor of this config-only path; the door to a first-class deployable virtio-fs sensor stays open.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/CHANGELOG.md` — this entry
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — LES-135 (external+virtio-fs pattern), LES-136/137/138 (bash review lessons)
- `adapter.conf.example` — EXTERNAL_SENSORS block (tcpdump example + naming convention)

**Links:** `scripts/o2-pcaps-list.sh`, `scripts/o2-pcaps-fetch.sh`; `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_taps.md` §9 (config-external sensor as a tap target); `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-025, GAP-026.

## 2026-07-26 — `o2 info`/`o2 status` tier guard on the read path (parity with `o2 down`)

**Authority:** user.

**What changed:**
- `axrange/o2control/control.py`'s `surface_status` now raises `O2ControlError` (status 400) when
  called with an EXPLICIT `deploy_id` that resolves to a non-`o2`-tier deploy (e.g. a `te`-tier
  twin id) — mirroring the tier guard `tear_down_surface` already had. The `deploy_id=None`
  bare-default path (which resolves via `_latest_o2_deploy_id`, always `o2`-tier) is untouched, so
  A2 (`o2control/taps.py`)/A3 discovery is unaffected.
- `axrange/cli/main.py`'s `cmd_o2_status`, `cmd_o2_info`, and the pre-`--allow-destroy` status read
  in `cmd_o2_destroy` now catch `O2ControlError` and print a clean stderr message + non-zero exit,
  instead of letting the new raise traceback.
- `axrange/api/service.py`'s `o2_surface_status` now catches `O2ControlError` and re-raises as
  `ApiError`, so `GET /api/o2/surface?deploy_id=<te-id>` returns 400 (mirroring `o2_surface_down`)
  rather than a raw 500.

**Why:**
- A real operator passed a twin's deploy id (`corp-enterprise-f525eed4`) to `axrange o2 info` and
  got back `O2 surface <id> -- completed / sensors: (none)` — read as "sensors were lost" rather
  than "wrong tier of id entirely." The write-path siblings (`tear_down_surface`, the API's
  `o2_surface_up`/`o2_surface_down`) already refuse a non-`o2` id; the read path was the one gap in
  an otherwise-complete guard pattern.

**Affected docs:** none (no schema/behavior beyond the guard; see fix commit for detail).

**Links:** `axrange/o2control/control.py`, `axrange/cli/main.py`, `axrange/api/service.py`.

## 2026-07-26 — Control-plane API parity: access facts, O2 endpoint resolution, twin-config deploy/browse

**Authority:** user.

**What changed:**
- **Access facts:** `GET /api/deploys/{deploy_id}/access` → `AccessFactsDTO`, the API peer of
  `axrange te info`'s per-node access block (ip / login user / effective password / a paste-ready
  `ssh`/`rdp` hint). Passwords are **masked by default**; `?secrets=true` reveals the real values —
  and, when an API token is configured, that reveal is **itself gated on the bearer token**, so an
  unauthenticated reader can never pull effective passwords off a token-protected deployment.
- **O2 endpoint resolution:** `GET /api/o2/info` → `O2InfoDTO`, the API peer of `axrange o2 info` —
  resolved observability endpoint URLs (Grafana/Loki/Prometheus/Caldera/Wazuh, from the services-VM
  live IP) plus `EXTERNAL_SENSORS` facts (live name/IP resolution). Same masked-by-default /
  token-gated-reveal posture as the access-facts endpoint; kept as its own endpoint rather than
  folded into `SurfaceStatusDTO` so that shape stays stable and secret-free.
- **Twin-config browse:** `GET /api/twin-configs` and `GET /api/twin-configs/{config_id}` →
  `TwinConfigListDTO` / `TwinConfigDetailDTO`, the API peer of `axrange twin-config list|show`, via
  the same `axrange.twinconfig.registry` entry points (clean DTOs, never the pydantic `TwinConfig`
  model itself, per the `api/` boundary rule).
- **Twin-config deploy:** `CreateDeployRequest` gains `config` + `params` (mutually exclusive with
  `scenario`; 400 on both/neither), resolved through the same `materialize_twin_config` expander the
  CLI's `te deploy --config` uses. `CreateDeployResponse` gains `config_warnings: list[str]` —
  expansion-time advisories (e.g. an unknown persona id), always `[]` on the `scenario` path, so a
  caller reading the response never silently loses what the CLI already prints.
- **Shared helpers, not parallel implementations:** a new engine module
  `axrange/planner/access.py` holds `te_info_from_ops`/`fill_access_facts` (the CLI's `te info` and
  the new access-facts endpoint both call it — "one derivation, two thin renderers", mirroring
  `axrange/planner/inventory.py`'s existing pattern); `axrange.adapter_proxmox.resolve.
  external_sensor_facts` is the shared external-sensor live-resolution the CLI's `o2 info`/`tap info`
  and the new `/api/o2/info` both call; `_resolve_config_scenario_path`/`_validated_plan_for_path`
  in `axrange/api/service.py` let the twin-config deploy path reuse the same validate+plan sequence
  a literal scenario path uses.

**Why:**
- The API had fallen behind the CLI's `info`/`o2 info` and twin-config verbs added in the prior
  access-facts and twin-configs work — a caller driving axRange purely through the API (the AI core,
  CI, a future UI panel) had no way to read connection facts, resolved O2 endpoints, or the
  twin-config catalog, and no way to deploy from a twin-config id at all.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/design/11-api-ui.md` — endpoint table corrected (the tap trio is `POST`/`DELETE
  /api/o2/taps` only, no GET; `/api/scenarios` lists scenarios only; twin-configs get their own
  rows; removed non-existent bare `GET /api/scenarios/{id}`, `GET /api/pcaps/{id}`, and a targets
  "management" POST) and extended with the new access-facts / O2-info / twin-config rows, the
  twin-config `scenario`/`config` deploy note, and an Auth-posture paragraph on the masked-default /
  token-gated-reveal idiom.
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — `LES-134`.

**Affected code:**
- `axrange/planner/access.py` (new), `axrange/adapter_proxmox/resolve.py`
  (`external_sensor_facts`), `axrange/api/{app,service,dtos,settings}.py`.
- No version-locked schema (`IR_SCHEMA`/`PLANNER`/`OP_CATALOG`/`VALIDATOR`/`ADAPTER`/
  `IMAGE_MANIFEST`) changed.

**Links:** `docs/WORK_ORDER_access_facts.md`, `docs/WORK_ORDER_twin_configs.md`,
`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_api.md` (historical record; currency for the API design now lives in
`docs/design/11-api-ui.md`).

## 2026-07-26 — `twin-configs/` reorganized into per-config subfolders (config + its tap-plans)

**Authority:** user. **Content reorg + a small loader change** — no version-locked schema, no op
change.

Each twin-config now lives in its own subfolder `twin-configs/<id>/<id>.yaml` alongside its
tap-plans (`twin-configs/<id>/tap-*.yaml`), instead of a flat `twin-configs/<id>.yaml`. Discovery in
`axrange/twinconfig/registry.py` is now RECURSIVE (`list_twin_configs` uses `rglob`; a co-located
`tap-*.yaml` is dropped by the existing `twin_config:` shape filter), and `load_twin_config_raw`
checks `<id>/<id>.yaml` first then the flat `<id>.yaml` (back-compat retained — the flat layout
still resolves). `corp-enterprise/` ships three **portable** library tap-plans lifted from the
gitignored `deploy-dev45/tapplans/` and rewritten with a **scenario-NAME `target: corp-enterprise`**
(the name-resolution from `docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_target_resolution.md`, so no hardcoded deploy id —
the LES-072 anti-pattern): `tap-default.yaml` (server-vlan→o2-suricata, corp-lan↔dmz→o2-tcpdump),
`tap-reshard.yaml` (the swap), and a new `tap-xpress.yaml` (server-vlan→`XPress-SW`,
corp-lan↔dmz→`XPress-SW-A` — the external packetXpress sinks; `XPress-SW-A` must be declared in
`adapter.conf` `EXTERNAL_SENSORS` to bind). The other four configs have no matching tap-plans yet, so
their subfolders hold just the config. **Verified:** `twin-config list`/`show` + `te …--config`
resolve from subfolders; `tap validate` of `tap-default`/`tap-reshard` PASSES against the standing
`corp-enterprise` twin (name→live-deploy resolution end-to-end). New registry test for subfolder
discovery + tap-plan skip; test helper updated for the moved source path.

## 2026-07-25 — Predefinable tap plans: optional `target:`, `--target` override, name resolution (`docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_target_resolution.md`)

**Authority:** user (the feature — making tap plans predefinable — plus the two framing decisions:
dropping the `exclude_plan_id_prefix="tap-"` filter from the resolver per the code review, and
scoping `tap validate`'s no-target mode to semantics-only rather than a soft "best-effort" live
check). **Authority: implementation (Claude Code)** for the build itself. No `Supersedes:` —
nothing ratified is reversed, and no version-locked schema (IR/PLANNER/OP_CATALOG/VALIDATOR/ADAPTER/
IMAGE_MANIFEST) changed; the tap-plan YAML remains a `DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_taps.md` document, not a
version-locked schema.

**What changed:**
- A tap plan's `target:` is now **optional**. `TapPlanSpec.target: Optional[str]`; the previous
  hard "target is required" parse-time raise is gone.
- New resolver `resolve_target_deploy(store, target)` (`axrange/o2control/taps.py`): `target` is
  either an already-minted deploy id (a direct `store.get_deploy` hit wins) or a **scenario/twin-config
  name**, resolved to the newest **live** (non-destroyed) `te` deploy of that scenario —
  `deploys_matching(tier="te", exclude_destroyed=True)` filtered to `scenario_id == target` over the
  FULL tier-scoped candidate set, THEN structurally excluded via `is_tap_plan_deploy` (LES-082's
  discriminator — not a `plan_id`-prefix filter), newest-first by `updated_at` (LES-084). A resolved
  target that is itself a tap-plan playout is rejected with a clear `TapPlanError`, same as an
  unresolvable name.
- **`--target <deploy-id|scenario-name>`** added to `tap validate`/`tap plan`/`tap deploy`.
  Effective target = `--target` (or the API body's `target`) if set, else the plan's own `target:`,
  else neither — `tap deploy`/`tap plan` with no effective target error out BEFORE any state write.
- **`tap validate` forks on effective-target presence:** target present → resolve to a live active
  `te` twin (fails outright if destroyed/wrong-tier/itself-a-tap-plan-playout) and validate bindings
  against that twin's real inventory, as before. Target absent → **semantics-only** structural checks
  (well-formed selector/scope, non-empty `sensors`, valid `mode`, literal duplicate-addressing
  dedup) with no `StateStore`/inventory lookup — this is the mode a predefined/library plan (no
  `target:`) validates in; it does NOT catch two different selectors that would collide once bound
  to a real twin.
- API parity: `TapApplyRequest.target` is optional; `POST /api/o2/taps` 400s on an absent effective
  target, same posture as the CLI (there is no separate override field — the body's `target` IS the
  effective target).
- **Code review: one must-fix, two minor, all fixed** (see LES-132). The must-fix: an earlier draft
  added `exclude_plan_id_prefix="tap-"` to the resolver's `deploys_matching` call (mirroring
  `list-deploys --tier te`'s own query) — this both over-excludes a genuine `te` scenario coincidentally
  named `tap-*` and makes the following structural `is_tap_plan_deploy` check unreachable dead code.
  Dropped in favor of filtering to `scenario_id == target` over the FULL `tier="te"` set and letting
  the structural discriminator alone decide.

**Why:**
- A tap plan's `target:` was a literal, per-deploy-minted `te` deploy id — welded to one deploy
  instance, so a plan file couldn't be checked into a library the way `twin-configs/` are, and any
  unrelated IR edit to the target twin (even flipping an unrelated flag) minted a new deploy id and
  silently orphaned every tap-plan file hardcoding the old one (LES-072). Name resolution + the
  `--target` override let a tap plan travel with a scenario/twin-config by name instead of a deploy
  id, closing that welding gap for name-authored plans.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_target_resolution.md` — status header updated OPEN → DONE.
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_taps.md` — new §11 (already landed alongside the build).
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_points.md` — new §6 follow-on pointer (already landed alongside the build).
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — GAP-030 annotated with the partial closure (target-by-name is closed;
  label-based selector addressing remains open — this build was never that gap's consumer).
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — LES-132 (new, reviewer must-fix) and LES-072 (annotated as partially addressed
  for name-authored plans; a plan hardcoding a literal deploy-id `target:` remains subject to it).
- `CLAUDE.md` — brief note in the CLI/tap area; pytest counter reconciled to 1630 (true collected
  count via `doc-stats.py --check`, up from a stale 1599 quoted in 8 places).

**Affected code:**
- `axrange/o2control/taps.py`, `axrange/cli/main.py`, `axrange/api/dtos.py`.
- `tests/test_o2_taps.py`, `tests/test_cli_lifecycle.py`, `tests/test_api.py`.

**Verification:** full suite green — 1618 passed / 12 skipped / 0 xfailed, 1630 collected. **`dev45`
read-only live-verify PASSED 2026-07-25** — all 8 sub-checks against the standing `corp-enterprise`
twin: scenario-name resolution (`--target corp-enterprise` → `corp-enterprise-f525eed4`, DAG built),
`--target` override (a bad override name 404'd over the plan's valid baked target, proving precedence),
no-target error, `tap validate` target-present (resolved + validated against the twin's live
interfaces) vs. target-absent (semantics-only, no state lookup), and the two must-fix guardrails (a
tap-plan-playout id and a destroyed deploy both rejected with the exact clear messages). No mutating
verb run — `tap validate`/`tap plan` only; cluster untouched. Not exercisable live (no matching twin
on dev45): a genuine `te` named `tap-*` resolving by name — unit-verified only.

## 2026-07-25 — O2 layer taxonomy refined: two intents, three planes (watch / act / steer)

**Authority:** user. **Docs-only refinement** — re-files existing O2 components conceptually; no
component, code, or ratified technical decision changed (nothing to supersede).

The prior "two halves" framing (`DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md` §2: Orchestration / Observability / "bridges
both") was too coarse and mis-filed components: calling the whole stack "telemetry" swept in
**Grafana** (a *console*, not a store) and the attack/defense servers **Caldera** / **Wazuh**
(*orchestration*, not telemetry). Sharpened into **three functional planes** that place every
component unambiguously: **Observability ("watch")** — collectors (tcpdump/suricata/zeek sensors +
external `packetXpress` sinks + host agents + `[axr-truth]` markers) feeding the **Loki**/**Prometheus**
stores; **Orchestration ("act")** — **Caldera** (attack), **Wazuh manager** (defense), `o2control`
(playout); **Console ("steer + see", spanning both)** — **Grafana** + the control-panel plugin + the
API/CLI. Key corrections captured: Grafana is the console (stores nothing), **Wazuh is dual-role**
(agent = watch, manager = act), **Caldera** is pure orchestration, and only **Loki + Prometheus** are
pure telemetry backends. `DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md` §2 and the CLAUDE.md O2 blockquote updated.

## 2026-07-25 — Access facts & endpoint resolution (`docs/WORK_ORDER_access_facts.md` Parts A–D)

**Authority:** user (feature requested end-to-end; the two framing decisions — default password as a
FALLBACK, not a forced overwrite, and o2-services as a REGISTER-and-auto-resolve in adapter.conf,
not a manually-maintained URL list — were both user `AskUserQuestion` answers). No `Supersedes:` —
nothing ratified is reversed, and no version-locked schema (IR/PLANNER/OP_CATALOG/VALIDATOR/ADAPTER/
IMAGE_MANIFEST) changed.

**What changed:**
- **Part A — deploy-wide default password.** New `AdapterConfig.default_password` (`DEFAULT_PASSWORD`
  in adapter.conf/env; built-in default `Axr4nge-Lab!`, so an unset key is behavior-identical to
  before). The four hardcoded `"Axr4nge-Lab!"` constants (`adapter_proxmox/rest.py`'s Windows admin
  password, `config_exec/backend.py`'s safe-mode/user/domain-admin passwords) are gone, replaced by
  `explicit-input or cfg.default_password` (explicit — a binding's `cloud_init.password`, an
  `IdentityEntry.password`, an intentionally-weak AD account — still wins, unchanged precedence).
  **Linux cloud-init now also enables password login** where it was previously key-only:
  `_render_userdata` sets `lock_passwd: false` + `ssh_pwauth: true` + a `chpasswd` block for the
  `ci_user`, so console *and* SSH-by-password both work. A binding `cloud_init.password` overrides —
  carried as a pydantic **extra** field (`ManifestBase(extra="allow")`), **not** a new IMAGE_MANIFEST
  schema field, so no schema bump.
- **Part B — o2-services register + auto-resolve.** adapter.conf is the resolution source: new
  `O2_SERVICES_VMID`/`O2_SERVICES_NAME` (+ optional `O2_<SVC>_PORT` overrides, defaults
  grafana=3000/loki=3100/prometheus=9090/caldera=8888/wazuh=443-https) let `AdapterConfig.
  resolve_o2_endpoints()` derive all five endpoint URLs from the docker-host VM's live IP instead of
  an operator hand-copying each `O2_<SVC>_URL`. Per-service precedence: explicit `O2_<SVC>_URL` >
  services-VM-derived > `(not configured)`. New `axrange/adapter_proxmox/resolve.py` holds every
  best-effort (5s timeout, never-raise) live lookup used by `info`. Optional `services:` stanza on the
  surface manifest (`o2control/manifest.py` + `resolve_manifest_endpoints`) overrides adapter.conf for
  one `o2 info <manifest.yaml>` / `o2 deploy` echo only.
- **Part C — external sensors (packetXpress) resolve + display.** `o2 info`/`tap info` resolve each
  `EXTERNAL_SENSORS` entry's live VM name + mgmt IP (best-effort, multi-node); `tap info` resolves only
  the sensors actually bound in that plan (not every declared entry).
- **Part D — access sheet in `te info`.** Per node: management IP (router `wan_ip` > persisted net IP
  > best-effort live-resolve > `(unresolved)`), login user (`ci_user` for Linux / `Administrator` for
  Windows), effective password, and a paste-ready `ssh`/`rdp` access hint. A router with no
  `configure-cloud-init` op (VyOS — config goes over SSH `vbash` instead) shows `(key auth)` with NO
  password hint, since it has no default-password account to show. `--no-secrets` masks passwords in
  `te`/`o2`/`tap info` TEXT output; `--json` always carries the real value (operator-local). `tfc info`
  gained a one-line pointer to `te info` for access facts (its roster has no ip/os-family join by
  design).

**Security posture note — read before using outside an isolated lab:** setting `DEFAULT_PASSWORD` (or
even leaving it unset, since the built-in default is a known constant) turns ON SSH password login
lab-wide on every Linux node the moment `configure-cloud-init` runs, not just on nodes an operator
explicitly opted into. This is a deliberate lab-usability trade (console/SSH access without hunting for
an SSH key) but is a real posture change from the prior key-only default — documented at the knob in
`adapter.conf.example` and in `AdapterConfig.default_password`'s own docstring.

**Why:** real-use pain hit during the twin-model live-verify — finding the IP/user/password for a
deployed node, or the Grafana/Loki/Caldera/Wazuh URLs, required cluster spelunking or asking Claude;
`info` already knew most of the topology and should just show the access facts.

**Affected docs:** `docs/WORK_ORDER_access_facts.md` (status → DONE), `CLAUDE.md` (CLI/`info`-verb
bullet + new "Access facts & endpoint resolution" blockquote), `adapter.conf.example` (new
`DEFAULT_PASSWORD` / `O2_SERVICES_VMID`/`_NAME`/`O2_<SVC>_PORT` keys, self-documenting).

**Affected code:** `axrange/adapter_proxmox/{config.py,resolve.py (new),rest.py}`,
`axrange/config_exec/backend.py`, `axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py`, `axrange/o2control/manifest.py`,
`axrange/cli/main.py`.

**Verification:** reviewed (four fixes landed from the review round — see LES-128..131) and full-suite
tested: **1586 passed, 12 skipped, 0 xfailed (1598 collected)**; `doc-stats.py --check` clean.
**Gated `dev45` live-verify PASSED 2026-07-25.** Phase 1 (read-only, against the standing
`corp-enterprise-f525eed4` twin + `o2-surface-verify-4e8609d9` surface): `te info` access sheet showed
real per-node ip/user/effective-password/access-hint (`--no-secrets` masks, `--json` carries real
values); `o2 info` with `O2_SERVICES_VMID=102` resolved all five endpoints off VM 102's live IP
(`http://10.80.72.37:3000` … `https://10.80.72.37:443`) and an explicit `O2_GRAFANA_URL` override
changed only grafana (per-service precedence); `EXTERNAL_SENSORS` `XPress-SW` (vmid 103) resolved to
its live name + mgmt IP, and `tap info` showed only the plan's bound sensors; pointing
`PROXMOX_API_URL` at an unroutable host degraded gracefully (exit 0, no traceback — the LES-128
never-crash contract, proven on real sockets). Phase 2 (a throwaway single-Debian-host `te` deploy
`access-facts-verify-5a1239e0`): `te info` on the fresh node reported `ssh debian@10.67.0.10
(password=Axr4nge-Lab!)`, and a **password-only SSH login (pubkey auth disabled) succeeded** —
proving the new `chpasswd`/`ssh_pwauth` Linux cloud-init path actually enables password login, not
just that `info` prints it; `te destroy` by explicit deploy-id swept its 4 resources with the
standing twin / o2 surface / VM 102 / XPress 103 all left untouched.

## 2026-07-25 — Twin-model arc: dev45 live-verify Stage 5 (`exercise_tier` capstone) PASSED — AD kill chain + DCSync measured, ephemeral, replay-identical

**Authority:** mixed — the `exr` run itself and the content authored to drive it are `implementation
(Claude Code)` (the live-verify `docs/RUNBOOK_twin_model_live_verify.md` §"Stage 5" directs); the
twin-config incremental-add limitation is a `implementation (Claude Code)`-discovered finding, not a
user decision. No `Supersedes:`.

**What changed:** ran Stage 5 of the twin-model live-verify — the last gated obligation in
`docs/WORK_ORDER_exercise_tier.md` §4 — on the standing, already-instrumented `corp-enterprise-f525eed4`
twin (Lab + `te` + `tap` + `tfc` all left up from Stages 1–4). Every Stage-5 done-criterion PASSED:

- **The full AD kill chain succeeded** as an `exr` run against the live Windows DC (`10.62.10.2`,
  `corp.axr.lab`): recon (nmap, `T1046`) → AS-REP roast (`$krb5asrep$…jsmith`, `T1558.004`) → Kerberoast
  (`GetUserSPNs` → `MSSQLSvc/sql01…:1433 svc-sql`, `T1558.003`) → **DCSync (`T1003.006`):
  `krbtgt:502:aad3b435…:153915d1…:::` + AES keys dumped over DRSUAPI** (full domain compromise / golden-
  ticket key material). The LLMNR-poisoning step ran but stayed **attempt-only** — no real hash was
  captured, because there is no script-staging primitive to land a listener like Responder on the
  attacker host (see GAP-041).
- **A4 measurement confirmed:** `[axr-truth] run=… technique=… host=egress-proxy ts=…` markers were
  emitted for every technique and landed in the standing Loki (VM 102), queryable and joinable by
  technique+host+window — the truth-marker half of the coverage view worked exactly as designed.
- **`exr destroy` was clean and ephemeral:** the exr run went `destroyed`; the twin, `tfc`, and `tap`
  deploys all stayed `completed` — the exercise tier touched only its own tier, confirming the
  `exr`-cascade isolation (LES-117) from the other side.
- **Replay was identical:** re-running the same spec produced a **byte-identical `playout_hash
  2be5d3ae0008bec7`**, the product's core determinism claim.
- **The same-host overlap guard fired correctly:** a second concurrent `exr` run targeting the same
  host was refused ("destroy the prior run first"), enforcing the sequential-replay discipline
  documented as the guard's actual scope (LES-116 — sequential-only, not a concurrency lock).

**The significant finding — twin-configs have no incremental add (GAP-040):** `corp-enterprise` (the
twin-config) has no attacker node, and editing the config to add one doesn't extend the standing
deploy — `derive_plan_id(scenario_id, ir_hash)` keys plan identity on the IR **content hash**, so any
edit (even purely additive) computes a new `plan_id` and would trigger a whole new ~30-VM deploy rather
than a resume/extension of the standing twin. Plan-diff/re-planning is explicitly unbuilt
(`docs/PLANNER.md` §9). **Workaround used (Authority: user):** the impacket toolkit was hand-provisioned
onto the existing `egress-proxy` host over QGA, and the vulnerable AD accounts (`svc-sql` Kerberoastable
SPN, `jsmith` AS-REP-roastable, `bob-admin` reused as the Domain-Admins/DCSync target) were created on
the live DC via QGA `New-ADUser`; the `exr` spec's `host:` points at `egress-proxy` rather than a
dedicated attacker node. `twin-configs/corp-enterprise.yaml` does carry an authored `attacker` node
(added this session) for a **future fresh deploy** — it was not exercised live this session, so the
config and this run's actual topology intentionally diverge; the config's own comment + this entry are
the record of why.

**Why:** closes the last gated live-verify obligation in the twin-model arc's build-out
(`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` §9) — all six work orders are now both code-complete AND
live-verified (Stages 1–5); only Stage 6 (`sme-flat`, a second twin-config) remains open. Surfaces the
first real limitation of the twin-config model under a live, iterative exercise-authoring workflow.

**Affected docs:** `docs/RUNBOOK_twin_model_live_verify.md` (Stage 5 checked off), `docs/
DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` (built-status extended to Stage 5 PASS), `docs/WORK_ORDER_exercise_tier.md`
(gated live-verify PENDING → PASSED, egress-proxy/incremental-add caveat noted), `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md`
(GAP-040, GAP-041 added), `docs/LESSONS.md` (LES-126, LES-127), `CLAUDE.md` (twin-model arc
live-verified-through-Stage-5 status).

**Affected code:** none — Stage 5 was content + cluster ops (twin-configs, a new exercise spec,
hand-provisioning over QGA), no engine code change.

**Content added this session:** `twin-configs/corp-enterprise.yaml` (an `attacker` node + a vulnerable-
identity extension — `svc-sql` SPN, `jsmith` no-preauth — authored but not deployed this run, see the
incremental-add finding above); `deploy-dev45/exr-ad-killchain.yaml` (new — the 7-step AD kill-chain
exercise spec, `host: egress-proxy`, `twin: corp-enterprise-f525eed4`, technique-labeled, measurement
`run_label: ad-killchain-s5`).

**Verification:** full suite **unchanged — 1529 passed, 12 skipped, 0 xfailed (1541 collected)** (no

count unchanged (114).

**Supersedes:** none.

## 2026-07-25 — Twin-model arc: dev45 live-verify Stages 1–4 PASSED (7 fixes surfaced and driven)

**Authority:** mixed — the per-fix Authority marker is called out inline below; the live-verify run
itself (finding + diagnosing) is `implementation (Claude Code)`.

**What changed:** ran the staged live-verify batch (`docs/RUNBOOK_twin_model_live_verify.md`) for the
twin-model arc on `dev45` against a **corp-enterprise twin (32 hosts)**. **Stages 1–4 PASSED**; Stage 5
(`exr` capstone) and Stage 6 (`sme-flat` twin-config) remain open for a follow-on session. The run
drove **seven code fixes**, all landed in this pass:

- **Stage 1 (Lab/`o2`):** `o2 deploy` stood up the 2-sensor surface, 24/24 ops, both sensors standing
  (`o2-tcpdump`/`o2-suricata`); the standing-sensor capture-supervision fix (LES-085/LES-086,
  `systemd-run --unit=` transients + the prune watchdog) held up live under real capture.
- **Stage 2 (twin/`te` + `role:` + twin-config):** `te deploy --config corp-enterprise` reached
  **401/401** after 3 resumes (two transient first-boot apt-churn failures, unrelated to the fixes
  below, plus the dataset-guard fix). `te inventory` published all 32 hosts carrying **`role`**
  end-to-end (the Traffic-tier `node.role` field + twin-config expander + role inventory, live-proven
  for the first time). DC promoted; `win11-finance` workstations domain-joined. **Content finding, not
  a code bug:** `corp-workstation01..25` (19 chars) truncate to the 15-char NetBIOS limit, so all 25
  collapse into **one** AD computer object — see GAP-036.
- **Stage 3 (`tap_points`):** semantic tap-point selector addressing (segment + boundary scopes),
  per-sensor routing, a **boundary tap point on a real twin** (a first), heartbeats from both a Linux
  AND a Windows injector, and a re-shard (moving a tap point to a different sensor in place) — all
  PASS. Drove fixes #2, #3, and #7 below.
- **Stage 4 (`traffic`/`tfc` + `persona_library`):** `tfc deploy` reached **28/28**; a Windows persona
  Scheduled Task ran and **survived the launching QGA session**; a schedule override took effect; real
  busy-corp SMB sessions from Windows workstations to the DC/file-server were captured at the tap.
  Drove fixes #1, #4, #5, and #6 below.

**The 7 fixes:**

1. **Dataset synthetic-size guard raised 100 MiB → 2 GiB** (`axrange/config_exec/render.py`).
   **Authority: user.** `corp-enterprise`'s 2 GiB crown-jewel PII dataset is legitimate (the seeder
   delivers an in-guest *generator script*, never a huge QGA payload) — the guard stays a sanity
   ceiling, just a bigger one.
2. **`verify-capture` boundary injector moved off the router** (`axrange/o2control/taps.py`). A
   `boundary`-scoped tap point had been injecting the sentinel from the router, whose broadcast reply
   routes out the WAN rather than the mirrored leg. Now injects from a single-homed leaf on the
   segment, so the L2 flood actually reaches the router's mirrored port. Authority: implementation.
3. **Windows `verify-capture` sentinel** (`axrange/config_exec/render.py`
   `render_capture_sentinel_windows`; `backend.py` OS-dispatch; `axrange/o2control/taps.py` threads
   gateway/os through). An all-Windows segment couldn't run the POSIX shell sentinel; added a
   PowerShell path using `[Net.NetworkInformation.Ping]::Send` with the token in the ICMP payload,
   targeting the gateway (unicast, not a .NET broadcast — Windows has no broadcast-ping primitive).
   Authority: implementation.
4. **Persona optional emit-peers** (`axrange/personas/schema.py` `Emit.optional`,
   `axrange/validator/checks/personas.py` PSN-090 refinement, `axrange/o2control/traffic.py`
   resolution, catalog updates on `corp-workstation`/`power-user-dev`/`domain-controller`/`vip-exec`).
   **Authority: user.** PSN-090 had over-tightened `requires[]` to demand every `emits[]` peer role;
   now an `emits[].optional: true` peer (dns-resolver/print-server/…) is skipped-if-absent with a
   warning, while a hard (non-optional) peer still fails loud if missing. This **refines** PSN-090 (no
   catalog rule-count change — still 9 PSN rules, 114 total).
5. **Windows persona drivers** (`axrange/personas/drivers/{base,scripted,real_service,__init__}.py`,
   `axrange/config_exec/render.py` `scheduled_task_start_block`, catalog bindings on 11 real-service
   personas). **Authority: user.** `os_family` dispatch across the drivers: `scripted` on Windows
   launches a PowerShell TCP-connect client as a durable Scheduled Task (`-ExecutionTimeLimit 0`, so it
   survives the launching QGA session exactly like the Linux `systemd-run --unit=` pattern);
   `real-service` on Windows checks `Get-Service`/`Get-Process`; fixed an `os_family` leak in
   `_stop_instances` that assumed Linux unconditionally; unknown `os_family` now fails loud instead of
   silently no-op'ing. **This resolves GAP-032** (real-service personas now have bindings).
6. **Traffic-profile `schedule` override wired** (`axrange/o2control/traffic.py`,
   `axrange/personas/drivers/scripted.py`). **Authority: user.** The assignment-level `schedule` field
   existed in the schema but was inert; now `24x7` → emit gate `always`, `business-hours` → the
   `business_hours` gate, unset → cadence-derived (byte-identical to the prior behavior), and a
   malformed value fails loud at parse time rather than being silently ignored.
7. **Gretap de-scope over-deletion — a capture-plane data-loss bug** (`axrange/o2control/taps.py`).
   **The deepest find.** A keyed gretap netdev is named per `(underlay-bridge, sensor)` and is
   **shared** across every vnet on a VLAN zone that routes to that sensor. The de-scope drop-diff was
   working at `(net, sensor)` granularity, so it deleted a dropped pair's gretap device even when a
   still-needed pair used the *same* device — a re-shard or narrowing silently tore down **live
   capture** while the run still reported `completed` and `verify-capture` still read green (the
   heartbeat had already run and passed before the delete). Fixed to diff at gretap-**device**
   granularity: delete a device only if no remaining needed pair references it; log `KEPT` otherwise.
   Authority: implementation. **This is the root cause of the apparent "verify-capture false-green"
   symptom** — the heartbeat was accurate at the moment it ran; the de-scope pass tore the path down
   immediately after. Not a standing gap — root-caused and fixed in this pass.

**Why:** clears five of the six twin-model work orders' gated live-verify obligations against real
content (`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` §9) and surfaces the last mile of correctness bugs that only
show up under a real 32-host twin with real Windows hosts, real cross-OS personas, and a real re-shard
— none of which any unit/fixture test exercised.

**Affected docs:** `docs/RUNBOOK_twin_model_live_verify.md` (Stages 1–4 checked off), `docs/
DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` (built-status), `docs/WORK_ORDER_{tap_points,persona_library,
traffic_tier,twin_configs}.md` (live-verify PENDING → PASSED/partial), `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md`
(GAP-031/GAP-032 resolved; GAP-036..039 added), `docs/LESSONS.md` (LES-118..125), `CLAUDE.md` (test
count reconciled to the real collected total; twin-model live-verify status).

**Affected code:** `axrange/config_exec/render.py`, `axrange/o2control/taps.py`,
`axrange/o2control/traffic.py`, `axrange/personas/schema.py`, `axrange/personas/drivers/*`,
`axrange/validator/checks/personas.py`, `deploy-dev45/` content (twin-config param sets, tap plans,
traffic profiles used for the run).

**Verification:** full suite **1529 passed, 12 skipped, 0 xfailed (1541 collected)**; `doc-stats.py
--check` clean after this pass. Op count unchanged (40); catalog rule count unchanged (114 — PSN-090
is a text/behavior refinement, not a new rule).

**Supersedes:** none (all seven fixes are additive/refinement corrections to code that was unit-tested
but never live-exercised; the PSN-090 refinement narrows an implementation-authored rule's text, not a
ratified decision reversal).

## 2026-07-24 — Exercise tier (`exr`) BUILT: attack/activity/adversary/measurement move OUT of the `te` deploy (sixth and LAST twin-model build — arc COMPLETE)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code). This is a **scope-shift** order — Authority: user
(2026-07-23, `docs/WORK_ORDER_exercise_tier.md`) authorized starting it; the build itself, and the
`Supersedes:` reconciliation below, are `implementation (Claude Code)`.

**What changed:** the last of the six twin-model work orders (`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` §2/§8)
lands. A bare `te deploy` now stands up **only its own environment** — infra, apps, identity, data,
and defense overlays stay, but **attack playout, adversary agents, and measurement are pulled out**
into a new ephemeral **Exercise tier (`exr`)** run against an already-instrumented twin. This
**completes the five-tier twin model**: Lab (`o2`) → Twin (`te`) → Traffic (`tfc`) + Tap (`tap`) →
Exercise (`exr`), all six work orders (`tier_split`, `tap_points`, `persona_library`, `twin_configs`,
`traffic_tier`, `exercise_tier`) now built.

- **`axrange/o2control/exercise.py` (new)** — the `exr` playout: a standalone `tier="exr"`
  **ephemeral** deploy binding to a `te`, composed by hand (like `taps.py`/`traffic.py`) and reusing
  `run_deploy_executor`/`destroy_deploy` — no second executor, no new op type. Reuses the existing
  `OP-ACT-010/020/030` (`deploy-behavior-agent`/`start-activity`/`wait-actor-ready`) and
  `OP-OVL-070 install-agent` (`agent_kind=adversary`) verbatim; a fresh `deploy_id` per run.
  `playout_hash()` reuses the existing `ir_hash` column (no schema change) so replay-identity is
  assertable: same spec + same seed ⇒ same hash. A same-host overlap guard refuses a second live
  `exr` run whose target hosts overlap an already-running one on the same twin — this serializes the
  sequential replay loop but is **not** a concurrency lock (see LES-116 / GAP below).
- **`axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py`** — `_emit_activity` and the `overlays.adversary` install-agent
  sweep are **removed from the `te` path**. A `te` deploy's own DAG no longer contains any `OP-ACT`
  or adversary `OP-OVL-070` instance. Identity/data/defense overlays and the exporter/Wazuh
  default-agent sweep (`scenario.default_agents`) **stay** in `te` — only activity/adversary/
  measurement moved, exactly as the brief's "Key change" list specifies.
- **`axrange/validator/{catalog,checks/references}.py`** — new **`REF-260`** (WARNING, additive,
  113→114 catalog rules): fires when a `te`-tier deploy's IR still declares `activity:` and/or
  `overlays.adversary:`. Shipped archetypes (`ad-domain-lab`, `purple-team-range`,
  `soc-detection-lab`) keep validating (warning only) — they become **`exr`-consumed content**, not
  edited.
- **`axrange/ops/registry.py`** — emission-context notes on `OP-ACT-010`/`OP-OVL-070` (descriptive
  only, no signature/count change): both ops now say they are composed by the standalone `exr`
  playout, never emitted by the `te` planner.
- **CLI + API:** `axrange exr {deploy|run, status, destroy}` + `POST/GET/DELETE /api/exr`; a `te
  destroy` cascades its bound `exr` run (alongside `tap`/`tfc`) — each sibling cascade is now
  individually try/excepted so one sibling's unexpected failure can't skip the others or abort the
  parent's own teardown (LES-117).
- **Measurement:** A4 (`axrange/measurement/`) is untouched — `exr` threads an informational
  run-id/technique window through, it does not reimplement or query Loki itself.
- **Fixed in the same pass, not a new gap:** the exercise spec's top-level `seed` field is wired into
  `activity.seed` (or fails loud if that would silently conflict) rather than being an inert value
  that never reaches the determinism hash (LES-115).

**Why:** the accreted design had range DNA — attack/activity embedded in the `te` deploy. The
twin-centric re-frame (CHANGELOG 2026-07-23) treats the twin as purely *what the environment is*;
attack/defense/measurement is *what happens to it*, run and replayed independently, against traffic
(`tfc`) and capture (`tap`) that both **outlive** the exercise. This is the last piece needed for the
product's core loop: replay the same seed against the same twin and measure a delta after a defense
tuning.

**Verification:** unit/suite-verified — **1423 passed, 12 skipped, 0 xfailed (1435 collected)**;
catalog rules 113→**114** (`REF-260`); op types **unchanged at 40** (no new op — `exr` composes
existing ops by hand, the same reuse pattern as `taps.py`/`traffic.py`). **Gated `dev45` live-verify
PENDING** (`docs/WORK_ORDER_exercise_tier.md` §4 — run the AD kill chain as an `exr` against a
`corp-enterprise` twin with `tfc` busy + `tap` bound, confirm truth markers + detections join by
technique in Loki, confirm a clean `exr destroy` leaves twin/`tfc`/`tap` up, then replay the same
seed and confirm an identical playout).

**Affected docs:** `CLAUDE.md` (counters; twin-centric-reframe blockquote extended to mark the whole
arc code-complete; corrected statements that a `te` deploy runs attack/activity/adversary — false as
of this build); `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` (§2/§8 exercise tier BUILT; capstone header — all
six twin-model orders built); `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_attack_defense.md` (reconciliation pointer —
adversary emission moved out of the `te` substrate, §2/§3 rows superseded-in-direction, history not
rewritten); `docs/VALIDATOR.md` (`REF-260`, REFERENCE_INTEGRITY 25→26, 113→114 total);
`docs/OP_CATALOG.md` (`OP-ACT-010`/`OP-OVL-070` emission-context note — op count unchanged at 40);
`docs/SLICE_ACTIVITY.md` + `docs/SLICE_OVERLAY.md` (pointer note — the machinery is unchanged, the
trigger moved to the `exr` tier); `docs/WORK_ORDER_exercise_tier.md` (STATUS: OPEN → DONE except the
gated live-verify, PENDING); `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` (two new gaps — the concurrent-double-apply
TOCTOU and no-auto-reap); `docs/LESSONS.md` (LES-115/116/117); `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` (arc-complete
narrative).

**Affected code:** `axrange/o2control/exercise.py` (new), `axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py` (activity/
adversary emission removed from the `te` path), `axrange/validator/catalog.py` +
`axrange/validator/checks/references.py` (`REF-260`), `axrange/ops/registry.py` (emission-context
notes), `axrange/cli/main.py` (`axrange exr {deploy,run,status,destroy}`), `axrange/api/{app,dtos,
service}.py` (`/api/exr`), `axrange/planner/teardown.py` (per-sibling cascade isolation for
`tap`/`tfc`/`exr`), `tests/test_exercise_tier.py` (new).

**Supersedes:** this reverses the ratified decision that attack/activity/adversary/measurement are
emitted **inside** the `te` (engine) deploy. Specifically, it supersedes:
- **CHANGELOG 2026-07-11** "feat: the O2 build arc — A1 control, A2 taps, A3 attack/defense, A4
  measurement, activity schedule executor" §4 (A3 Stage A) — `overlays.adversary`/`AdversaryEntry` →
  `OP-OVL-070 install-agent` was, until this entry, **emitted by the `te` (engine) deploy path**
  (Stage A, "`te_`, engine deploy"). It is now emitted only by the standalone `exr` playout.
- **CHANGELOG 2026-07-09** "design: A3 — attack/defense as O2 servers + substrate agents (manual
  playout)" — the closed taxonomy `overlays = identity + data + defense + adversary` and `activity =
  TE-internal automated behavior` placed both squarely **inside the TE substrate** ("the AD kill
  chain stays here"). `overlays.adversary` and `activity` no longer emit from `te`; identity/data/
  defense remain as described.
- **CHANGELOG 2026-06-12** "implementation: OP-ACT inline-steps activity engine (first activity
  slice)" and **CHANGELOG 2026-06-24** "feat(activity): the AD attack chain (OP-ACT) — LIVE-VERIFIED
  on dev45" — both landed and verified the OP-ACT engine, and the AD kill chain specifically, as
  **`te`-deploy-emitted** activity (`planner/pass1_emit.py`'s `_emit_activity`, gated on the `te`
  deploy's own `mirror-ready`). The OP-ACT machinery itself is unchanged and unreverted — its
  *content* (the inline-steps engine, the AD kill chain authored on `ad-domain-lab`/
  `purple-team-range`) is unaffected — but its **trigger** moves from "part of every `te` deploy that
  declares `activity:`" to "run explicitly via `axrange exr deploy`/`run`."

## 2026-07-24 — Traffic tier (`tfc`) BUILT: persona binding onto a deployed twin's host roles (fifth twin-model build)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code) — except the `role:` IR field / `scenario_schema_version`
1→2 bump this build depends on, which is **Authority: user** (2026-07-24, explicitly approved as a
version-locked schema change; see the separate entry directly below for that half).

**What changed:**
- Built `docs/WORK_ORDER_traffic_tier.md` (code + tests + docs done; the gated `dev45` live-verify —
  `tfc deploy` a profile onto a `corp-enterprise` twin, confirm standing traffic on the wire at a
  co-located tap, confirm a `tap` re-apply doesn't disturb it — is still **pending**). The last of the
  three builds parallel on the `tier_split`/`tap_points`/`persona_library`/`twin_configs` keystones
  (`exercise_tier` is the one remaining twin-model order, and is user-gated — see
  `docs/SESSION_STATE.md`).
- New **`axrange/o2control/traffic.py`**: the Traffic-tier playout, modeled closely on
  `axrange/o2control/taps.py` (A2) — a **third standalone playout**, not a second orchestrator. Binds
  to an **already-deployed** `te`-tier twin (nothing to emit from an IR walk), so it composes a `Plan`
  **by hand** out of `OpInstance`s using the one new op's catalog metadata, and runs it through the
  **same engine executor** every other lifecycle uses (`run_deploy_executor`/`release_deploy_resources`
  from `axrange/api/service.py`; teardown reuses `planner.teardown.destroy_deploy` verbatim). Uses
  `tier="tfc"` — a real first-class deploy tier (`docs/WORK_ORDER_tier_split.md` already added it to
  `_VALID_TIERS`), so destroy discovery is a plain tier-filtered query, no structural discriminator
  needed (unlike `tap`, which reuses `tier="te"`).
- New op **`OP-TFC-010 drive-persona`** (`axrange/ops/registry.py`; 39→40 op types, 33→34 action/wait):
  config-class (`idempotency_class=CONFIG`), `resource_categories=("ansible-pool", "vm:$vmid")`,
  `cleanup_strategy=REDO`. Adapter-side execution is **not** `axrange.config_exec.ConfigBackend` — that
  would create a real import cycle (`axrange.personas.drivers` already imports `config_exec.render`'s
  `systemd_daemon_start_block`). Instead a new **`PersonaBackend`** (`o2control/traffic.py`) — its own
  small `Adapter`-protocol backend, handed to `run_deploy_executor` as both the `adapter=` and
  `config_backend=` slots (valid because `drive-persona` is always config-class, so the `adapter=` slot
  is never actually dispatched — a deliberate fail-loud trip-wire if a future tfc op ever isn't
  config-class).
- **Traffic-profile spec** (`{twin, assignments:[{role|node, persona, intensity, schedule, seed}],
  replay:[{template, onto_role, orig_ip, iface}]}`), parsed/loaded like a tap-plan. Resolution
  (`resolve_traffic_bindings`) against the twin's published inventory (`hosts[].role`, never twin
  internals): every assignment's `role`/`node` must match ≥1 host; every persona must be catalog-known
  with `requires[]` satisfied and every non-reserved `emits[].peer_role` resolvable (`egress`/`internet`
  reserved tokens skip resolution); a zero-match `peer_role` or `requires[]` gap is **fail-loud**
  (`TrafficPlanError`), never silently skipped. **Round-robin fan-out** for hub roles: when >1 host
  shares a `peer_role` (e.g. two domain controllers), candidates are `host_id`-sorted and picked
  `candidates[index % len(candidates)]` by the persona instance's position among its assignment's
  hosts — deterministic (same inputs ⇒ same peer, so a replay is identical) but spread, not always the
  same DC.
- **Idempotent apply with a de-scope pass.** Every apply force-resets every composed op to `pending`
  (mirrors `taps.py`'s `install-ovs-mirror` M1 fix) so a changed profile is genuinely re-asserted. A
  **separate** `_stop_dropped_instances` pass (new `StateStore.mark_op_destroyed`, one-op-at-a-time,
  distinct from the full-deploy `mark_destroyed`) stops + de-scopes every previously-`completed`
  instance the new apply's resolved set no longer contains — closing a real bug class caught in code
  review: force-reset alone re-runs ops still in the plan but says nothing about ones the plan
  *dropped*, so a narrowed re-apply or a persona swap on one host (`instance_id =
  f"{host_id}-{persona_id}"`, so a swap changes the systemd unit name) would otherwise leave the
  old/dropped instance running forever — for a swap, running **alongside** the new one, both emitting
  simultaneously. `tfc status`/`info` filter to `status == COMPLETED` so a dropped instance vanishes
  from what's reported "currently active." See `docs/LESSONS.md` LES-112/LES-113.
- **Thin surfaces:** CLI `axrange tfc {validate,plan,deploy,status,info,destroy}` (grid-consistent with
  `te`/`o2`/`tap`); API `POST/GET/DELETE /api/tfc` (clean DTOs — `TrafficApplyRequest` /
  `TrafficPlanStatusDTO` / `TrafficDestroyRequest`/`Response`, never `o2control.traffic`'s dataclasses
  or raw op/state-store shapes). A `te destroy` (CLI + `DELETE /api/deploys/{id}`) cascades to a bound
  `tfc` deploy via `cascade_destroy_tfc_for_te`, best-effort, never overriding the twin's own result.
- **`role:` field resolution** (see the schema entry below): `HostInfo.role` in the published twin
  inventory now sources from `clone-vm`'s new `node_role` input (unconditional op, fires for every
  cloned host regardless of tap status) instead of always `None` — **resolves `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md`
  GAP-027**. An earlier revision hung this on the tap-gated `record-tap-intent` op additively; reverted
  in the same build for the coupling reason `axrange/planner/inventory.py`'s module docstring now
  documents at length (editing an unrelated `tap:` declaration must never change a host's published
  role). A twin config's `nodes[].role` (`docs/WORK_ORDER_twin_configs.md`) propagates through the
  expander for free.
- **Final suite: 1370 passed, 12 skipped, 0 xfailed (1382 collected)**; 40 op types (34 action/wait + 6
  barriers); 113 catalog rules (unchanged — no new validator rule needed for `role:`).

**Why:**
- Closes `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` §7's "composition" gap: personas (the vocabulary, §5) and
  twin configs (role→persona defaults, §6) existed but nothing could yet **bind** a persona onto a
  running twin or **override** a default per run. Traffic and Tap stay two independent sibling tiers
  (§3) — this build touches no sensor/tap machinery at all, by design (§3's boundary: "do not stand up
  sensors or taps here").

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/CHANGELOG.md` — this entry + the paired schema-bump entry below.
- `docs/IR_SCHEMA.md` §1.5, §3.3 — `node.role` field + the `scenario_schema_version` 1→2 note (see
  entry below).
- `docs/VALIDATOR.md` §5.1 — `STR-020` now recognizes `{1, 2}`.
- `docs/OP_CATALOG.md` §4, new §5.7 `OP-TFC`, §8 — `OP-TFC-010 drive-persona`; op-count headline
  39→40 (34 action/wait + 6 barriers).
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` §7 — composition is now BUILT (code/docs-complete; live-verify
  pending).
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_traffic_tier.md` — STATUS: OPEN → DONE except the gated live-verify (PENDING).
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — GAP-027 marked Resolved; new gaps logged for real-service persona binding
  content and replay-pcap-template curation.
- `CLAUDE.md` — counters (1382 collected tests; 40 op types) + a proportionate `tfc`-tier mention.
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — LES-112 (re-assert vs. de-scope are separate obligations), LES-113
  (`reset_incomplete_ops` reverts `destroyed` rows too — correctness must rest on plan-absence + an
  actually-stopped driver, not a persisted status alone).

**Affected code:**
- `axrange/o2control/traffic.py` (new), `axrange/ops/registry.py`, `axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py`
  (`clone-vm`'s new `node_role` input), `axrange/planner/inventory.py` (`HostInfo.role` real
  resolution), `axrange/planner/state.py` (`mark_op_destroyed`), `axrange/twinconfig/expand.py`
  (`nodes[].role` propagation), `axrange/o2control/__init__.py`, `axrange/cli/main.py`,
  `axrange/api/{app,dtos,service}.py`, `tests/test_traffic_tier.py` (new), `tests/test_api.py`,
  `tests/test_ops_registry.py`.

## 2026-07-24 — `role:` IR field + `scenario_schema_version` 1→2 (version-locked schema change)

**Authority:** user (2026-07-24) — a version-locked contract change (`docs/IR_SCHEMA.md`), confirmed
per the CLAUDE.md "Design Document Maintenance" risk trigger before implementation.

**What changed:**
- New optional `Node.role: Optional[str]` field (`axrange/ir/schema.py`) — a persona-assignable role
  tag (e.g. `corp-workstation`, `domain-controller`), what the Traffic tier (`tfc`, entry above) binds
  a persona onto. **Parsed regardless of the declared `scenario_schema_version`** — a `version: 1` IR
  with `role:` gets it too; it is not gated on the version bump below.
- `scenario_schema_version`'s recognized set widened `{1}` → `{2}` → `{1, 2}` (`STR-020`,
  `axrange/validator/checks/structural.py`). Purely additive/backward-compatible: every existing
  `scenario_schema_version: 1` IR — every shipped `target-environments/*` and `twin-configs/*` — keeps
  validating completely unchanged, verified across the entire surface of the bump (no other version
  gating exists in the codebase). `2` exists so an author may explicitly mark an IR as "authored with
  `role:` in mind"; declaring it is optional, never enforced.

**Why:**
- The Traffic tier needs a real, first-class way to identify which twin hosts a persona can bind onto,
  distinct from `kind` (a topology-shape tag: host/router/switch) and from any identity/overlay role.
  A version bump on a version-locked schema is exactly the risk trigger CLAUDE.md's "Design Document
  Maintenance" section calls out for a confirm-first — the user explicitly authorized this one.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/IR_SCHEMA.md` §1.5 (versioning note), §3.3 (node field table + example).
- `docs/VALIDATOR.md` §5.1 (`STR-020` table row + note).

**Affected code:**
- `axrange/ir/schema.py` (`Node.role`), `axrange/validator/checks/structural.py` (`check_str_020`).

**Supersedes:** none — additive only; no earlier CHANGELOG entry claimed `scenario_schema_version`
would stay pinned to `1` forever (IR_SCHEMA.md's own §1.5 always allowed future bumps).

## 2026-07-24 — Twin configs BUILT: parameterized IR template expander + starter catalog (fourth twin-model build)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- Built `docs/WORK_ORDER_twin_configs.md` (code + tests + docs done; the gated `dev45` live-verify —
  deploy `sme-flat` + `corp-enterprise` from configs — is still **pending**). Parallel wave with
  `persona_library` (directly below) on the `tier_split` (2026-07-23) + `tap_points` (2026-07-24)
  keystones.
- New **`axrange/twinconfig/`**: `schema.py` (typed, permissive `TwinConfig`/segment/node/tap-point
  models — deliberately separate from `axrange.ir.schema`, never imported by it); `params.py` (a
  `${NAME:default}` param-substitution engine — declared `twin_config.params:` + `--param k=v`
  overrides, resolved before any IR dict exists); `expand.py` (the **one and only** place a
  twin-config becomes a plain Scenario-IR `dict` — the result flows through
  `axrange.ir.parser.parse_ir` / `axrange.validator.validate_full` / `axrange.planner.build_plan`
  completely **unchanged**, so **no `IR_SCHEMA` version bump**); `registry.py` (catalog loader);
  `persona_check.py` (a soft advisory check of a node's `persona:` against the persona catalog,
  tolerant of an absent catalog — `[warn]`, never blocks); `errors.py` (typed
  `TwinConfigShapeError`/`TwinConfigParamError`/`TapPointMaterializationError`).
- New top-level content dir **`twin-configs/`** (parallel to `target-environments/`, content not
  code) carrying the five `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` §6.2 starter configs: `sme-flat`,
  `corp-enterprise`, `datacenter` (its `FABRIC` param keeps the fabric slice opt-in), `ics-ot`,
  `dmz-perimeter`.
- **Tap-point materialization**: `_apply_tap_points` turns each declared `tap_points[]` entry into
  the real underlying IR `tap:` flag(s) — network-wide for `segment`, whole-node for
  `boundary`/`host`, one leg for `leg` — at expansion time, so a declared tap point structurally
  cannot fail to materialize; an unresolvable one (unknown segment/node/leg, or a `boundary` naming
  two segments no node actually joins) raises `TapPointMaterializationError` at **expansion** time,
  the earliest point such a bug can be caught, not a later live-verify surprise. Verified via real
  `build_plan`+`Executor` round-trips for all 5 configs.
- CLI: `axrange te {validate,plan,deploy} --config <id> [--param k=v]` (mutually exclusive with the
  scenario positional; one shared `_resolve_scenario_or_config` seam so the rest of validate/plan/
  deploy is untouched) + `axrange twin-config list|show <id>` (out-of-grid, like
  `list-deploys`/`serve`/`image build`). `--config` defaults the images-root to the catalog dir's
  parent / cwd.
- **Closes the deferred `${N:default}` deploy-time-parameters follow-up** from
  `docs/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` Q2 — see that entry, updated in the same pass.
- **Deferred, explicitly, not silently:** a config's `tap_points[].id`/`labels`/`scope` are consumed
  only to set the IR's boolean `tap:` flags, then **dropped** — they are not propagated to the
  IR/inventory, so `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` §4.1's label-based tap *selection* stays inert
  for config-sourced twins (the same limitation `tap_points` already logged in general terms,
  `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-028/GAP-029; see the new GAP-030 for this config-specific instance).
  Activating it needs a version-locked `IR_SCHEMA` `tap_points:` field — deferred to `traffic_tier`,
  the consumer that will actually select by label.

**Why:**
- A twin config gives an author a parameterized template for a whole class of realistic
  environments (`N_WORKSTATIONS`, `DOMAIN`, `OT_PROTOCOL`, `FABRIC`, …) instead of hand-writing every
  `te`-tier archetype from scratch, while guaranteeing every expansion is a **real** Scenario IR
  that goes through the unchanged validator/planner — no shadow validation path, no schema bypass.
  This is the twin-model arc's §6/§7 build, sequenced parallel with `persona_library`/`tap_points`
  per `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` §9.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_twin_configs.md` — STATUS: OPEN → DONE except the gated live-verify (PENDING)
- `docs/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` — Q2's `${N:default}` deploy-time-parameters follow-up marked addressed
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` — §6/§7 built-status header
- `docs/CONVENTIONS.md` — `twin-configs/` content dir + `twin-config`/`te --config` CLI noted
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — new GAP-030 (config tap-point labels stay inert) and GAP-031 (no
  top-level `images/` catalog for `--config` from repo root)
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — LES-109, LES-110, LES-111
- `CLAUDE.md` — counters (1311 pytest tests) + a proportionate mention of twin configs

**Affected code:**
- `axrange/twinconfig/` (new), `twin-configs/*.yaml` (new), `axrange/cli/main.py`,
  `tests/test_twinconfig_catalog.py`, `tests/test_twinconfig_cli.py`, `tests/test_twinconfig_expand.py`,
  `tests/test_twinconfig_params.py`, `tests/test_twinconfig_persona.py`, `tests/test_twinconfig_registry.py`,
  `tests/test_twinconfig_tap_materialize.py`

## 2026-07-24 — Persona library BUILT: schema + registry + PSN validator category + drivers (third twin-model build)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- Built `docs/WORK_ORDER_persona_library.md` (code + tests + docs done; the gated `dev45`
  live-verify — one **scripted** + one **replay** persona — is still **pending**). Third build on
  the `tier_split` (2026-07-23) keystone, parallel with `tap_points` (2026-07-24, directly below)
  and `twin_configs` (directly above).
- New **`axrange/personas/`** registry submodule — engine vocabulary, modeled directly on
  `axrange/sensors/`: `schema.py` (typed `Persona`/`Emit` model — `id`/`os_family`/`drive`/
  `emits[]`/`requires[]`/`knobs`), `registry.py` (loader), `catalog/*.yaml` — the 20-persona
  starter catalog from `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` §5.2 (`corp-workstation`,
  `domain-controller`, `plc`, `iot-device`, … through `bastion-jump`), and three **drivers**
  (`drivers/{base,scripted,replay,real_service}.py`) exposing a uniform start/stop/heartbeat
  interface the future Traffic tier drives.
- Drivers launch via **detached, deterministically-named transient units**
  (`systemd-run --unit=axr-persona-<safe> --collect`) with a fail-loud `is-active` check — never
  `nohup … &`, which is reaped the instant the launching QGA session ends (the GAP-020/GAP-025
  failure class). Payloads deliver base64-in-command (the PVE-9.2 wide-char path,
  `GuestAgent.write_file`). The **replay** driver's pcap staging uses a new bytes-native
  `write_binary_file` — `write_file` is UTF-8-lossy and would corrupt binary pcap bytes (LES-106).
  Scripted render is byte-stable given a seed (the established determinism pattern).
- New **`PSN-010..090`** validator rule category — **9 rules** (**catalog rules 104 → 113**):
  id/os_family/drive/proto/peer_role/cadence/volume shape checks (`PSN-010..070`), `PSN-080`
  (`requires[]` satisfiable against a supplied twin role set — a no-op without one, mirroring
  CLU-060's cluster-context dependency), and `PSN-090` (a code-review fix splitting `PSN-050`'s
  presence-only check: every non-reserved `emits[].peer_role` must be declared in the persona's own
  `requires[]`). Rule *definitions* live in `axrange/validator/catalog.py`; *execution* is a new
  `axrange/validator/checks/personas.py` that reads the persona registry directly — **validator →
  registry**, the same direction `checks/references.py`'s REF-210 already uses for
  `axrange.sensors`. This is a **decided boundary call** (the first cut had the reverse import;
  code review flipped it — LES-108): `axrange.personas` stays a pure leaf with zero
  `axrange.validator` imports, verified by an ast-based test inspecting `sys.modules`.
- 10 of the 20 catalog personas' `requires[]` were corrected (to satisfy the new PSN-090) so all 20
  validate clean.
- **Deliberately not done:** the `role:` IR field `axrange/planner/inventory.py` flags as this
  work's job (`hosts[].role` is `None` in the published twin inventory until it lands) is a
  version-locked `IR_SCHEMA` change the work order never requested — it belongs to a later binding
  layer (`traffic_tier`/`twin_configs`), not an omission of this build.

**Why:**
- The Traffic tier (`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` §2/§5) needs a shared, reusable library of
  benign behavioral archetypes before it can bind anything onto a twin's published hosts. Personas
  are deliberately **twin-agnostic and attack-free** — attacker TTPs live in the Exercise tier — so
  the same library composes across every twin regardless of the exercise run against it.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_persona_library.md` — STATUS: OPEN → DONE except the gated live-verify (PENDING)
- `docs/VALIDATOR.md` — new §5.12 PSN category + rule-count math updated 104 → 113
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` — §5 built-status header
- `docs/CONVENTIONS.md` — `axrange/personas/` engine-vocabulary registry noted
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — LES-106, LES-107, LES-108
- `CLAUDE.md` — counters (113 catalog rules; 1311 pytest tests) + a proportionate mention

**Affected code:**
- `axrange/personas/` (new), `axrange/validator/checks/personas.py` (new),
  `axrange/validator/catalog.py`, `axrange/validator/finding.py` (`RuleCategory.PERSONA`),
  `tests/test_personas.py`, `tests/test_catalog.py`, `tests/test_sensor_exec.py`
