## 2026-07-24 — Tap points BUILT: semantic tap-point model + resolution (second twin-model build)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- Built `docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_points.md` (code/tests/docs done; the gated `dev45` live-verify is
  **still pending**, see that work order's updated STATUS line). Second build on the `tier_split`
  keystone (2026-07-23), realizing the topological tap-point model ratified in the
  "Tap points are topological, not directional" entry directly below.
- **New `axrange/planner/tappoints.py`** — a tap point is `{ id, scope, target, labels[] }` with
  four scopes: **`segment`** (all traffic on one network), **`boundary`** (a conduit between two
  segments), **`host`** (every leg of one host), **`leg`** (one host↔net interface). `direction`/
  EW-NS is deliberately **not a field** anywhere in this module. `derive_default_tap_points` groups
  the mechanical `host:net:nic` inventory ids (`axrange/planner/inventory.py`, itself unaffected by
  the earlier keystone build's `record-tap-intent` sourcing) into candidate semantic points;
  `resolve_tap_point` resolves a point to a concrete interface set against a `TwinInventory`;
  `select_tap_points`/`TapPointSelector` match on `id`/`scope`/`target`/`labels`. New typed errors
  `TapPointShapeError` (malformed `target` for a given `scope`, raised at `TapPoint` construction,
  not deferred to a bare `ValueError` inside resolution) and `TapPointResolutionError` (a `boundary`
  point whose joining host exists but has no tapped leg on either side — an actionable failure, not
  a silent empty match). `TapPoint` is `id`-hashable (identity is the `id`, not the field tuple — a
  `leg`/`boundary` `target` dict/tuple would otherwise hash "by every field," the wrong notion for a
  catalog whose ids are the contract). A `TapIndex` precomputes the twin's tapped-interface set and
  host-leg map once per resolve pass instead of every matched point recomputing them. Engine-side
  (`axrange/planner/`, not `o2control/`, per LES-025) — pure data transformation over an
  already-built `TwinInventory`, no adapter/cluster call.
- **Tap-plan binding (`axrange/o2control/taps.py`).** `TapBindingSpec` gains `tap_point`/`selector`/
  `mode` (`fanout` default | `exclusive`); a binding now addresses its target via exactly one of the
  legacy `tap:` interface id, an author-declared/auto-derived `tap_point:` id, or a `selector:` —
  `parse_tap_plan` enforces the exactly-one-of at parse time and requires the selector carry **at
  least one effective criterion** (`has_criteria`) so an all-unset selector can never silently match
  every tap point in the twin. `mode: exclusive` is now enforced **across all bindings** post-
  resolution (a second, overlapping binding — fanout or exclusive — targeting an interface an
  `exclusive` binding already claims is a parse-time-adjacent `TapPlanError`, not a silent extra
  fan-out). The previously dead-ended dropped-gretap warning (`log=lambda _m: None`) now threads
  into `TapPlanStatus.warnings` (`log=warnings.append`) so an orphaned-gretap skip actually surfaces
  to an operator instead of vanishing. A `segment` tap point's **partial coverage** (fewer tapped
  legs than hosts attached to the net) also appends to `TapPlanResolution.warnings`.
  `resolve_tap_plan_bindings` maps `InventoryError`/`TapPointShapeError`/`TapPointResolutionError` to
  a uniform `TapPlanError(400)`. No new capture mechanism — reuses the existing per-tap sensor
  routing (1:N fan-out / per-tap exclusivity, gretap keying) verbatim; this adds addressing
  vocabulary only.
- **API/CLI.** `POST /api/o2/taps` now routes through `parse_tap_plan` — closes a hole where a
  binding with no addressing key (an empty selector reaching the API DTO directly, bypassing the
  file-parser's checks) would have mirrored the entire twin. `direction` is deleted outright from
  `TapPointInfo` (`axrange/planner/inventory.py`) / `TapPointInventoryDTO` (`axrange/api/dtos.py`) /
  the service mapping / the `te inventory` CLI render — it was always `None`, zero shipped
  consumers, kept as dead weight from the keystone build; removed rather than left inert.
- **`tap`-as-deploy-tier: decided NOT to promote it.** `tap` stays a resource **name-shape tag**;
  tap-plan deploys keep `tier="te"` plus the load-bearing `te → tap` cascade (LES-094) and
  `o2control/taps.py`'s own structural discriminator (its recorded ops are always a subset of
  `attach-gre-tunnel`/`install-ovs-mirror`/`verify-capture`, and its `plan_id` is always
  `tap-<target-te-deploy-id>`). `naming.py`'s `TIER_PREDICATES` already carries a `tap` row (added by
  the keystone build for exactly this future option); `TIER_SHAPE_CHECKS` deliberately does **not**
  — `deploys.tier` is never `"tap"` today. `destroy_deploy` still fails closed on an unrecognized
  tier either way (LES-098). Reasoning (scope discipline — promoting `tap` means re-verifying the
  full six/seven-tier teardown matrix, real separate work; no behavior gap the promotion would close;
  fail-closed posture is unaffected regardless) is recorded in `taps.py`'s module docstring **and**
  now mirrored into `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_taps.md` and `docs/CONVENTIONS.md` §2, per this build's own
  work order §5 — a module docstring isn't where the next reader looks.
- **Archetype content (user-approved), closing a real coverage hole the review caught mid-build:**
  the auto-derivation logic initially only emitted a `boundary` tap point when ≥1 of its joining
  legs was already tapped — on every shipped `target-environments/` archetype, the multi-homed
  node that actually *joins* two segments (`conduit`/`fw`/`core`/`gw`/`hq-core`/the fabric leaf and
  border nodes) was never itself declared `tap: true` (only the leaf hosts on either side were), so
  **`boundary` derived to nothing, everywhere** — including `ot-ics-range`'s `idmz-conduit`, the
  design brief's own headline capture point (§6.2) and this work order's own gated live-verify
  subject. Fixed two ways: (1) `derive_default_tap_points` now emits a `boundary` point for **every**
  segment pair a host joins regardless of tap status, and `resolve_tap_point` raises the actionable
  `TapPointResolutionError` above when nothing on either leg is wired — "exists, fails loud with a
  fix instruction" instead of "doesn't exist, no signal"; (2) tapped the boundary-forming nodes
  themselves across eight archetypes so the failure mode never fires against shipped content:
  `ot-ics-range` `conduit`, `soc-detection-lab` `fw`, `purple-team-range` `core`,
  `dfir-hunt-lab`/`vuln-management-lab` `gw`, `enterprise-multisite` `hq-core`+`br1-rtr`+`br2-rtr`,
  `optrahealth-twin` seven access/backbone routers, `fabric-verify` `leaf-1`/`leaf-2`/`border-1`.
  Deliberately skipped pure-P2P underlay spines (they derive-but-raise loudly, which is correct for
  a link that has no host-side leg to tap). Node-level `tap: true` only; zero new validator findings
  on any edited IR. New test `tests/test_target_environments_boundary_taps.py` proves
  `derive_default_tap_points`/`resolve_tap_point` yield a non-empty `boundary` interface set against
  these **real** IRs (a full `build_plan` + `Executor` round trip against a fake adapter, not a
  synthetic fixture) — see LES-103 below.
- **Known, honestly-documented limits, not silently swallowed:** a **WAN-egress boundary**
  (`corp-enterprise`'s `wan-egress`, `dmz-perimeter`'s `perimeter`) is inexpressible — `__wan__` is
  excluded from `TwinInventory.hosts[].nets[]` entirely, and today's `tap:` IR extension has no
  `NetRef` for a router's synthetic WAN uplink to carry `tap: true` on — so this gap has **no
  signal at all** (not even the actionable-error placeholder `boundary` now gets), distinct from
  `fabric-verify`'s `border-1↔wan-core` boundary, which **is** expressible today because `wan-core`
  there is an ordinary declared network, not the synthetic `__wan__` uplink. A **VXLAN-bridged
  segment pair** (`fabric-verify`'s `va↔vb`, joined only via the underlay fabric, no single host
  with legs on both) is likewise not derivable as a `boundary` point — there is no joining host to
  key off. Tracked as new `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-029.
- Full suite: **1155 passed, 12 skipped, 0 xfailed** (1167 collected, up from 1068 at the keystone
  build).

**Why:**
- Closes **GAP-028** (`tap_points[].id` was a mechanical `host:net:nic` interface id, not a
  semantic, author-declared namespace) with the grouping/resolution layer `docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_points.md`
  promised. The mid-build boundary-derivation fix is the direct, positive case for LES-103 below: a
  catalog derived from optional author declarations (`tap:`) can publish a silent hole across an
  entire content library, and checking it against the actually-shipped `target-environments/` IRs
  — not just synthetic unit fixtures — is what caught it before the gated live-verify would have.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` — built-status header extended: §4.1 tap-point model now BUILT.
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_points.md` — STATUS: OPEN → **DONE except the gated live-verify (PENDING,
  dev45)**.
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_taps.md` — tap-point vocabulary + the `tap`-as-tag-not-tier decision folded

- `docs/CONVENTIONS.md` §2 — the `tap` tap-plan-deploy mechanism clarified as `tier="te"` +
  structural discriminator, not a distinct deploy-tier value.
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — GAP-028 closed (✅ Resolved); new GAP-029 (WAN-egress boundary +

- `CLAUDE.md` — test counter 1068 → 1167 at every `doc-stats.py`-policed site; proportionate mention
  of the tap-point vocabulary.
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — LES-101 through LES-105 (below).

**Affected code:**

- `axrange/planner/inventory.py` — `direction` field removed from `TapPointInfo`.
- `axrange/o2control/taps.py` — `tap_point`/`selector`/`mode` binding, cross-binding `exclusive`
  enforcement, dropped-gretap + partial-coverage warnings wired to `TapPlanStatus.warnings`.

## 2026-07-23 — Tap points are topological, not directional (drops the EW/NS tap taxonomy)

**Authority:** user

**What changed:**
- The `tap` tier's organizing axis is now the **tap point** — a named capture *place* identified by
  `{ id, scope, target, labels[] }`, where `scope` is one of **`segment`** (all traffic on one
  network), **`boundary`** (a conduit between two segments, or segment↔WAN), **`host`** (every leg of
  one host), or **`leg`** (one host↔net interface). See `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` §4.1.
- **`direction` is no longer a field.** East-west / north-south may appear as an optional `labels[]`
  value and remains selectable (`bind everything labelled ns to sensor-b`), but nothing in the
  schema, planner, or teardown may key off it.
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_directional.md` → **renamed** `docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_points.md` and rewritten
  around the new model; the twin-config catalog's tap descriptions (§6.2) and the `twin_config` /
  `twin_inventory` schemas (§6.1, §4) were re-expressed in tap-point terms.
- The persona catalog's `emits[].direction` (§5.1/§5.2) is **unchanged** — there it describes the
  character of a *flow*, which is a real traffic property, not a capture-point taxonomy.

**Why:**
- Direction doesn't survive contact with the topologies the twin model targets. A **flat** twin
  (`sme-flat`) has no north-south/east-west split at all yet still needs tap points; a **segment**
  carries both lateral and boundary-crossing traffic on one wire, so a single point would have to be
  both; a **multi-homed router** leg's direction depends on which neighbour you reason from, making
  the label ambiguous exactly where capture matters most (the IT/OT conduit).
- The high-value capture points operators actually name are **places** — `idmz-conduit`, `corp-lan`,
  `db-vlan` — not compass headings. Scoping tap points topologically makes the common case
  (`segment`) the default and keeps direction available as a label where it genuinely describes the
  point.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` — §2 (Tap row), §2.1, §3, §4 (inventory shape), **new §4.1**,
  §6.1 (twin-config schema), §6.2 (all five catalog entries), §9 (build arc)
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_tap_points.md` — renamed from `_tap_directional.md`, rewritten
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — GAP-028 re-scoped to the topological namespace
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_{tier_split,persona_library,twin_configs,exercise_tier}.md`, `CLAUDE.md` — cross-refs

**Supersedes:** the "**Tap** (`tap`) — gains a **directional EW/NS taxonomy**" line in the
2026-07-23 *Twin-centric re-frame* entry below. The five-tier model, the `tap` tier's role as the
capture *sink*, and its independence from `tfc` are all unchanged — only the taxonomy within the tier
is replaced.

## 2026-07-23 — Twin-centric re-frame: five tiers (add `tfc` traffic + `exr` exercise), personas, twin configs

**Authority:** design (chat session)

**What changed:**
- Re-centered the platform on the **digital-twin pipeline**: *stand up the Lab once → deploy a Twin → instrument it (Traffic + Tap) → run Exercises → replay*. This is the frame; nothing is built yet.
- **Five tiers** formalized (extends the four-tag model in `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md` §6 / `docs/CONVENTIONS.md` §2):
  - **Lab** (`o2`) — standing, shared; now explicitly **includes the sensors** (capture receivers) alongside the telemetry stack + optional Caldera/Wazuh servers.
  - **Twin** (`te`) — topology + apps + identity + data only (*what the environment is*); publishes an inventory (persona-assignable host roster + EW/NS tap points).
  - **Traffic** (`tfc`, **NEW**) — a traffic profile: persona→node assignments + pcap replay; the benign background *source*; outlives exercises.
  - **Tap** (`tap`) — gains a **directional EW/NS taxonomy** and decouples from `te` emission; the capture *sink* bound to Lab sensors; outlives exercises.
  - **Exercise** (`exr`, **NEW**) — attack + defense tuning + measurement; ephemeral; replayable via seed.
- **Attack/activity/measurement move out of the `te` deploy into `exr`**; **sensors stay standing in the Lab**; **Traffic and Tap are two separate tiers by design** (source vs sink — independently re-shard capture without disturbing traffic, and re-profile traffic without disturbing capture).
- Introduced the **persona library** (benign behavioral archetypes; `scripted` / `replay` / `real-service` drivers) and **twin-config parameterized IR templates** (`corp-enterprise`, `datacenter`, `ics-ot`, `dmz-perimeter`, `sme-flat`).
- Authored **six sequenced `/dev` work orders**: `tier_split` (keystone) → `persona_library` ∥ `tap_directional` ∥ `twin_configs` → `traffic_tier` → `exercise_tier`.

**Why:**
- The accreted design carried **cyber-range DNA** (attack/activity embedded in the `te` deploy; sensors+taps as coupled O2 surface arcs). The product goal is a **digital twin** — fidelity, realistic-looking traffic, telemetry for analysis. The re-frame removes that impedance mismatch while **reusing** the existing engine, tier tags, gretap/`tc` capture mechanics, activity engine, and A4 measurement rather than reversing them.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` — **NEW**, design of record (five-tier model, persona schema + catalog, twin-config schema + catalog).
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_tier_split.md` · `_persona_library.md` · `_traffic_tier.md` · `_tap_directional.md` · `_twin_configs.md` · `_exercise_tier.md` — **NEW**, the build arc.

**Supersedes:** *(in direction only)* the in-scenario-sensor / embedded-attack framing in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §0 and `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md`. This entry **extends** the tier model (adds two tags, re-homes tier ownership) and **moves no code**; the actual scope-shift (pulling attack/activity out of `te`) lives in `WORK_ORDER_exercise_tier.md`, which is **user-gated before landing** per `CLAUDE.md` "Design Document Maintenance." `CONVENTIONS.md` §2 and `CLAUDE.md` are intentionally **not** updated to claim the new tiers as built — each work order updates them at build time.

**Links:** —

## 2026-07-23 — Tier split KEYSTONE: `tfc`/`exr` tags, six-tier teardown matrix, twin inventory

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- Built `docs/WORK_ORDER_tier_split.md` in full — the **keystone** every other twin-model work order depends on. No behavior moves yet (attack/activity still emits inside `te`; taps still bind via the existing A2 playout): this is pure substrate.
- **Two new tier tags, `tfc` (Traffic) and `exr` (Exercise)**, added to `adapter_proxmox/naming.py` alongside `te`/`o2`/`tap`/`img`: `_VALID_TIERS` extended, new `tfc_*_for_component`/`exr_*_for_component` producer families (double-keyed on `(plan_id, host_id)` for the VM-name producer, unlike `o2`'s single-arg shape, because `tfc`/`exr` are multi-deploy tiers where a bare `host_id` would collide across concurrent deploys), `is_tfc_*`/`is_exr_*` + aggregate `is_tfc`/`is_exr`/`is_te`/`is_tap` predicates. All producers/predicates for the tier vocabulary are now exported as **`TIER_PREDICATES`** (5, including `tap`) and **`TIER_SHAPE_CHECKS`** (4 deploy tiers) — a single owned table, not duplicated per-consumer — with import-time coverage asserts. A new public `check_tier()` wrapper lets outside callers (`planner/state.py::create_deploy`) fail loud on a bad tier at the point of the mistake.
- **`planner/teardown.py`'s `_protected()` guard generalized from a hard-coded `img`/`o2` special case to a six-tier matrix** derived from `naming.py`'s new exports: `img` + any operator-declared protected-external name are refused unconditionally by every tier; every other tier is refused unless it's `own_tier` or an explicitly cascade-allowed foreign tier (today only `te → tap`, and it's load-bearing — `o2control/taps.py` stamps its tap-plan playout deploy `tier="te"` too, since `naming.py` has no separate `"tap"` deploy-level tier). `_shape_checks_for_tier` now fails **closed** (all-`False`) on an unrecognized tier instead of silently borrowing `te`'s shapes, and `destroy_deploy` validates `deploy.tier` up front and raises the typed `TeardownError` before touching a single resource, rather than letting an unrecognized tier reach `underlay_bridge_for_plan` mid-sweep and raise a bare `ValueError` after some resources are already gone and before the lease is released.
- **`state.py`**: no schema bump — `deploys.tier` already has no CHECK constraint (SCHEMA_VERSION 3), so `tfc`/`exr` work as free-form values. `create_deploy` now calls `naming.check_tier()`.
- **New `axrange/planner/inventory.py`** publishes the design brief's §4 `twin_inventory` contract — `build_twin_inventory(store, deploy_id)` returns `hosts[]` (`vm_name`, `vmid`, `pve_node`, `vm_status`, `os`, `role`, `nets[]` — every attached leg of every host, not just the first) + `tap_points[]` (`id`, `location`, `bridge`, `iface`, `nic_index`, `host_id`, `sensor_id`) + the full `net_id -> bridge` map + `vlan_underlay` + `deploy_status`. Every fact is sourced from **unconditional** ops (`clone-vm`, `attach-vm-to-vnet`, `create-ovs-bridge`) — deliberately **not** from `record-tap-intent`, which only fires for a host with ≥1 tapped NIC and would otherwise publish an all-`None` roster for an untapped twin, re-coupling Traffic to Tap. `role` is always `None` today (a real `role:` IR field is `persona_library`'s job); `os` is `None` whenever no `manifests` registry is passed or the image is unresolvable — never fabricated. New read paths: `axrange te inventory [ID] [--json]` (CLI) and `GET /api/deploys/{id}/inventory` (API, clean DTOs only, never the engine dataclass directly). `o2control/taps.py::_scan_te_inventory` re-expressed as a thin adapter over `build_twin_inventory` (one derivation, not two that can drift) — its refactor also **restored** `net_bridge`'s full key domain (an intermediate cut had narrowed it to only tapped nets, which would have silently orphaned a gretap on the drop path of a resumed/edited-`tap:` redeploy).
- `OP-MIR-005 record-tap-intent`'s signature is **unchanged** — an intermediate cut had added `host_*` fields to it; reverted once review showed the facts belonged on unconditional ops instead. **No version-locked contract (`IR_SCHEMA`/`PLANNER`/`OP_CATALOG`/`VALIDATOR`/`ADAPTER`/`IMAGE_MANIFEST`) changed at all, no schema-version bump anywhere.**
- Full suite: **1056 passed, 12 skipped, 0 xfailed** (1068 collected total, up from 932).

**Why:**
- The 2026-07-23 design entry above ratified the five-tier twin model; this entry is the keystone build every other twin-model work order (`persona_library`, `tap_directional`, `twin_configs`, `traffic_tier`, `exercise_tier`) depends on. Landing the tier tags + guard matrix + published inventory first — before any tier's actual lifecycle exists — means the later orders bind against a stable, already-guarded contract instead of each having to re-derive naming/teardown correctness on its own.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_tier_split.md` — flipped OPEN → DONE.
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_twin_model.md` — §2/§2.1/§4 substrate marked built; §5–§7 (personas, twin configs, composition) remain unbuilt design.
- `docs/CONVENTIONS.md` §2 — `tfc`/`exr` added to the tier-tag table (tags/name-shapes/guard only — the Traffic/Exercise **lifecycles** are not built).
- `CLAUDE.md` — test counter (932 → 1068 at all 7 `doc-stats.py`-policed sites); brief mention of the new tier substrate + `te inventory` verb.
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — new GAP-027 (published `hosts[].role` is `None` until `persona_library` lands a real `role:` field) and GAP-028 (`tap_points[].id` is a mechanical `host:net:nic` interface id, not the design brief's semantic author-declared tap-point namespace).
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — LES-093 through LES-100 (naming-producer degenerate-input round-trip; cascade-exception ownership audit; conditional-op-derived contracts inherit their condition; additive op inputs never reach an already-completed op; tier vocabularies need one owned table with fail-closed unknowns; fail-closed-at-the-decision-point isn't fail-closed end-to-end; a consolidation refactor can narrow a derived map's key domain even when every field matches; a module-level `assert` invariant is unenforced under `python -O`).

**Supersedes:** none — additive; reverses nothing.

## 2026-07-21 — External sink nodes: teardown protection + config-declared tap target (XPress-SW)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed — three landings:**
- **1. Teardown protection for externally-managed sink nodes.** `axrange/adapter_proxmox/naming.py`
  gains a fifth, non-tier protection bucket — `is_protected_external(name, extra)` + a built-in
  `PROTECTED_EXTERNAL_NAMES = frozenset({"XPress-SW"})` — for operator-managed nodes created BY HAND,
  entirely outside axRange's deploy lifecycle, that no producer here ever names and no tier-tag can
  describe. `planner/teardown.py`'s `destroy_deploy` checks it UNCONDITIONALLY in `_protected()`,
  same posture as `is_img`: refused by every tier's destroy, including `o2`'s own. New `adapter.conf`
  keys `PROTECTED_EXTERNAL_NAMES` (comma-list) / `PROTECTED_EXTERNAL_VMIDS` (comma-list), parsed by
  `adapter_proxmox/config.py` into `AdapterConfig.protected_external_names/_vmids` and threaded through
  every teardown caller (`cli/main.py cmd_destroy`, `api/service.py destroy_deploy`,
  `o2control/control.py tear_down_surface`, `o2control/taps.py destroy_taps`).
- **Review-hardened with a vmid-based protection axis.** The name guard can only fire once
  `backend.vm_name()` resolves a display name — a resolution failure previously fell through to the
  ordinary vmid-range check, a fail-OPEN gap. `destroy_deploy` now takes `protected_vmids` and checks
  it FIRST in the VM loop, before any name resolution is attempted, so a naming hiccup can never
  unprotect a declared node. Also added: `.strip()` normalization on both the target name and every
  `extra` entry, and a `_record_guard` helper consolidating the three previously-duplicated
  guard-violation append+log call sites.
- **2. Config-declared EXTERNAL sensor as a tap target.** `adapter_proxmox/config.py` adds
  `ExternalSensor` (`id`/`vmid`/`transport_ip`/`type="packetxpress"`/`capture_iface="gre-cap"`) and
  an `EXTERNAL_SENSORS` adapter.conf key (a JSON list), parsed into `AdapterConfig.external_sensors`.
  `o2control/taps.py` gains `_external_to_inventory_entry`/`merge_external_sensors`, threaded through
  `resolve_tap_plan_bindings` and every `tap validate`/`plan`/`info`/`apply` entry point (CLI + API
  `o2_tap_apply`): a tap plan can now name a sensor that is **not** in A1's standing inventory, and
  its `transport_ip` (straight from config) is what the keyed gretap's `remote` is aimed at.
  **Declaring an external sensor auto-protects it** — its `id` folds into `protected_external_names`,
  its `vmid` into `protected_external_vmids`, no double-declaration needed. This partially un-defers
  the "packetXpress VM sensor" item in `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_taps.md` §8: a config-declared external
  packetXpress **sink** as a tap target now exists, even though the full VM-sensor design (image,
  in-scenario decode, downstream shipping) remains its own deferred item.
- **3. Loud "capture UNVERIFIED" warning (LES-001 family).** Because an external/operator-managed
  sink can't be QGA-read, `_compose_ops` skips composing a `verify-capture` heartbeat for any binding
  pair whose sensor resolves to `deploy_id == ""` (the `merge_external_sensors` marker for "no A1
  backing, never reach into it") — but the skip is now loud, not silent: a `warnings` collector
  threads through `TapPlanResolution` → `TapPlanStatus`/`TapInfo` → `TapBindingInfo.capture_verified`
  → CLI (`[warn] capture UNVERIFIED for external sensor ...` on `tap apply`/`tap validate`/`tap plan`;
  `capture: UNVERIFIED (external sink)` inline on `tap info`) → API (`TapPlanStatusDTO.warnings`).
  Also landed in this pass: a top-level `ConfigError` handler around `cli/main.py:main()`'s entire
  dispatch (a malformed `EXTERNAL_SENSORS` now fails clean on every command path, not just the two
  handlers that already caught it locally); a collision warning when a declared external `id` shadows
  a standing A1 sensor of the same id (A1 wins, but silently would have hidden a misconfiguration);
  blank `id`/`transport_ip` rejected at parse time (`ConfigError`, not a gretap aimed at nowhere).

**Why:**
- axRange's own tooling (the planner/executor/adapter) has no automated reach into a hand-provisioned
  appliance like packetXpress's `XPress-SW` — it is never a deploy target and never should be — but
  operators need to route scenario taps to it anyway. The design keeps axRange's automated side to
  "point a gretap's `remote` at a config-given IP," never provisioning or reaching into the node
  itself through the engine, while still giving it the same blast-radius protection a golden template
  gets. (An operator-granted SSH credential to the node, as happened on dev45, is a legitimate manual
  config/verify channel — it just isn't one any axRange op or `verify-capture` drives.)
- LES-001 (capture must never look silently green) applies just as hard to a path axRange
  *intentionally* can't verify as to one it's merely not yet verifying — a `tap apply` that reports
  `completed` with no proof of a live path to an external sink is exactly the trap that lesson exists
  to prevent, so the skip had to become a surfaced warning, not a quiet `continue`.
- The vmid-hardening and the loud-warning mechanics were both code-review findings during this same
  arc, not separately planned work.

**Live-verify status — PASSED end-to-end on dev45, 2026-07-21.** unit/suite-verified (932/932 pytest
tests), plus a full live run closing `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-026. **Reachability + declaration:**
the capture NIC was added to the real XPress-SW VM (vmid 103) — `qm set 103 -net1
virtio,bridge=o2v7dc98` — confirmed host-side (`tap103i1` UP, on bridge `o2v7dc98`, forwarding) and
enumerated inside the guest immediately as `ens19`, no reboot needed. The operator granted **SSH
access** to XPress-SW (`fxadmin@10.80.72.40`, its management IP, passwordless-sudo) — XPress-SW is
Rocky Linux 10.2, NetworkManager-managed, not a black box. Using that channel, the actual cause of the
initial unreachability was found and fixed: NetworkManager had auto-created a DHCP profile for the
hot-added `ens19` that sat stuck `activating` (no DHCP server on the isolated transport net), leaving
it IP-less; a static NM profile (`ipv4.method manual` + `ipv4.addresses 10.255.251.20/24` +
`ipv4.never-default yes`) fixed it, persistently. `adapter.conf` declares it:
`EXTERNAL_SENSORS=[{"id":"XPress-SW","vmid":103,"transport_ip":"10.255.251.20",
"type":"packetxpress","capture_iface":"gre-cap"}]`, validated to parse and auto-protect XPress-SW by
name + vmid. **End-to-end capture-through-sink — PROVEN:** a `tap-verify` TE
(`tap-verify-09a5090a`, 23/23 ops — only `record-tap-intent` emitted, confirming the
sensor-agnostic-te substrate) was deployed, then a tap-plan binding `h-a → [XPress-SW]` applied
(`tap deploy` → `tap-tap-verify-09a5090a-4bb7e9ab`, completed); the loud
`[warn] capture UNVERIFIED for external sensor 'XPress-SW' ...` warning surfaced as designed (LES-089
confirmed live, not just unit-tested). `tcpdump -i gre-cap` on XPress-SW over SSH caught **44
decapsulated frames** while 20 pings ran on the tapped host — 20 matched ICMP echo request/reply pairs
(`h-a 10.62.0.10` ↔ `h-b 10.62.0.11`) plus corp-net ARP: the mirror path (tap → keyed gretap →
XPress-SW's `gre-cap` decap) works end-to-end through a real external sink. **Teardown protection,
positively confirmed:** `tap destroy` (1 resource) then `te destroy tap-verify-09a5090a` (7 resources)
both reported DESTROYED cleanly; XPress-SW was inspected directly afterward (`op_attempts` table +
`qm`, not just "no warning logged") and found completely untouched — still `running`, `ens19` still at
`10.255.251.20`, `gre-cap` still up. The standing O2 surface was also left untouched. Nothing remains
open on this feature; GAP-026 is closed.

**Affected docs:**
- `CLAUDE.md` — pytest counter (904→932, all seven occurrences); the "packetXpress VM sensor: its own
  design, deferred" note reconciled to note a config-declared external sink now exists.
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_taps.md` — new §9 on config-declared external sensors.
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — `LES-088`..`LES-092`.
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — new `GAP-026`.

**Affected code:**
- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/naming.py` — `is_protected_external`, `PROTECTED_EXTERNAL_NAMES`.
- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/config.py` — `ConfigError`, `ExternalSensor`, `_parse_external_sensors`,
  `AdapterConfig.protected_external_names/_vmids/external_sensors`.
- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/__init__.py` — exports `ConfigError`, `ExternalSensor`.
- `axrange/planner/teardown.py` — `destroy_deploy(protected_names=, protected_vmids=)`,
  `_record_guard`, vmid-first protection check.
- `axrange/o2control/taps.py` — `merge_external_sensors`, `_external_to_inventory_entry`,
  `TapPlanStatus.warnings`, `TapInfo.warnings`, `TapBindingInfo.capture_verified`.
- `axrange/o2control/control.py`, `axrange/api/service.py`, `axrange/api/dtos.py` — protected-list
  threading + `TapPlanStatusDTO.warnings`.
- `axrange/cli/main.py` — `--adapter-config` on `tap validate`/`tap info`; warning rendering on
  `tap apply`/`tap validate`/`tap plan`/`tap info`; top-level `ConfigError` handler in `main()`.
- `adapter.conf.example` — `PROTECTED_EXTERNAL_NAMES`, `PROTECTED_EXTERNAL_VMIDS`, `EXTERNAL_SENSORS`
  documented with the XPress-SW manual runbook.
- `tests/test_adapter_proxmox.py`, `tests/test_cli_lifecycle.py`, `tests/test_o2_taps.py`,
  `tests/test_teardown.py` — new/updated coverage. Full suite: 932 pytest tests, green.

## 2026-07-20 — Hook invocation cwd-independence (LES-067 fix)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code). **Tooling change** — `.claude/settings.json` only; no product/schema change.

**What changed:**
- All five hook commands in `.claude/settings.json` (`SessionStart`; `PreToolUse` on
  `Edit|Write|MultiEdit` → `inject`; `PreToolUse` on `Bash` → `commit-guard`; `PostToolUse` on
  `Edit|Write|MultiEdit` → `compile`; `Stop` → `stop`) now anchor the repo root instead of invoking
  `scripts/hooks.py` and `.venv/bin/python` by a path relative to the invoking shell's cwd:
  `sh -c 'R=$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null || echo .); PY=$([ -x "$R/.venv/bin/python" ]
  && echo "$R/.venv/bin/python" || echo python3); exec $PY "$R/scripts/hooks.py" <verb>'`.
  `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` resolves the repo root from ANY cwd inside the worktree (falls
  back to `.`, matching prior behavior, outside a worktree). `scripts/hooks.py` itself needed no
  change — it was already cwd-independent (`REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent`,
  subprocess `cwd=REPO`); the bug was purely in how `settings.json` invoked it.
- Updated the `settings.json` `$comment` to describe the fix and drop the stale "Known follow-up
  (LES-067)" note the 2026-07-17 entry below had left in place.

**Why:**
- LES-067 (`docs/LESSONS.md`) flagged that a Claude Code subagent whose cwd ≠ repo root (observed
  across four subagents in one pipeline run, one with cwd `docs/`) got every `Edit`/`Write` call
  silently rejected — `scripts/hooks.py inject` resolved against the wrong directory and errored
  before the tool call could proceed. A `doc-writer` stage in particular routinely runs with cwd set
  to a subdirectory, making this a recurring pipeline hazard rather than a one-off.

**Verification:**
- Functional, not a pytest surface (harness config only; suite not run by design). From
  `cwd=/home/rocky/axr-product/docs`: the OLD-style command reproduced the bug
  (`python3: can't open file '.../docs/scripts/hooks.py'`, exit 2); the NEW-style command resolved
  correctly and exited 0 for verbs `session-start`, `inject` (with real `tool_input` stdin on
  stdin), and `commit-guard` (non-commit Bash passthrough).

**A second lesson surfaced while landing this fix:** an intermediate edit briefly produced a
malformed hook command (a dropped space, `hooks.py"inject`) on both the Edit/Write and Bash
`PreToolUse` matchers at once — since a non-zero-exit `PreToolUse` hook BLOCKS the tool call it
gates, this simultaneously locked the session out of Edit, Write, and Bash (Read still worked).
Recovery required a user-run `!`-prefixed shell command, the one channel that bypasses the model's
own `PreToolUse` hooks. Recorded as `docs/LESSONS.md` LES-087 (new) — editing a hook `command`
string in `.claude/settings.json` is itself a self-lockout hazard.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — LES-067 marked RESOLVED (resolution note appended); new LES-087 (self-lockout
  hazard).
- `docs/CHANGELOG.md` — this entry.

**Affected code:**
- `.claude/settings.json` — all five hook commands + the `$comment`. (Already committed to the
  working tree before this doc pass; `scripts/hooks.py` unchanged.)

## 2026-07-17 — Doc-drift hook: enforce at commit, advisory (non-looping) at stop

**Authority:** user (2026-07-17). **Tooling change** — `.claude/settings.json` + `scripts/hooks.py`; no product/schema change.

**Problem.** The `Stop` hook ran `doc-stats --check` on every turn-end and emitted a blocking
drift nudge whenever CLAUDE.md's quoted pytest count ≠ the live count. During a multi-stage pipeline
the live count changes repeatedly (each subagent adds tests) while the count is reconciled **once**, at
the end, by the doc-writer stage — so drift legitimately exists for dozens of turns. With no
`stop_hook_active` guard, the one condition re-fired on every turn-end, eventually tripping the
consecutive-block cap and force-overriding the turn (a wedge). Raising `CLAUDE_CODE_STOP_HOOK_BLOCK_CAP`
only lifts the ceiling; it doesn't address the policy mismatch.

**Fix (enforce where it's load-bearing).** Doc/source consistency only actually matters at **commit
time**, not at every turn-end. So:
- **New `commit-guard` (`PreToolUse` on `Bash`)** — HARD-blocks a `git commit` (`permissionDecision:
  deny`) while `doc-stats --check` reports drift; non-commit Bash calls pass through untouched.
- **`stop` is now an advisory one-shot** — it backs off immediately when `stop_hook_active` is set, so
  it can never loop, and its message says drift is fine to leave until the doc-writer stage reconciles it.

Net: transient drift during a pipeline no longer wedges turns, and stale counters still cannot be
committed. Verified: `stop` silent under `stop_hook_active`; `commit-guard` denies on induced drift,
allows when clean, ignores non-commit Bash. **Known follow-up (LES-067):** hook commands still invoke
`scripts/hooks.py` by a repo-root-relative path (silent Edit/Write failure for a subagent whose cwd ≠
repo root); anchor via `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` when next hardening the hook block.

## 2026-07-17 — Standing O2 sensor capture supervision + retention fix (tcpdump/zeek reaped by QGA session end; pcap rotation overhaul)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- **Root cause found (dev45 investigation, 2026-07-17):** the standing O2 `tcpdump-sensor` was
  **not capturing at all**. `deploy-sensor`'s in-guest render (`axrange/config_exec/render.py`)
  launched tcpdump and zeek via a plain background `nohup … &`. A process launched this way is
  **reaped the moment the qemu-guest-agent exec session that issued the command ends** — the exact
  same failure class GAP-020 identified and fixed for the shared `export.ship: loki` shipper and
  worked around for suricata (`-D`, self-daemonizing), but never fixed for tcpdump/zeek, whose
  `deploy-sensor` render still used the old `nohup` pattern.
- **Part 1 — supervision fix.** tcpdump and zeek now launch as detached, deterministically-named
  transient systemd units: `systemctl reset-failed U; systemctl stop U; systemd-run --unit=axr-sensor-<safe>
  --collect bash -c 'exec <tool> …'`, followed by a fail-loud `systemctl is-active --quiet U || exit 1`
  guard that dumps the unit's journal + log file on failure (`_systemd_daemon_start_block`,
  `axrange/config_exec/render.py`). `--collect` lets a re-`o2 up` cleanly replace a prior unit with
  no `pkill`+reap race — there is no `pkill` anywhere in the new path at all. suricata's `-D`
  self-daemonize path is unchanged.
- **Part 2 — retention overhaul (user requirement, 2026-07-17): pcaps must persist across tap
  removal *and* sensor restart/re-up, size-bounded by an oldest-first prune, not reset by a redeploy.**
  Replaced the old cyclic `-C 100 -W 50` ring (which silently discarded all prior capture history on
  every redeploy, resetting to file `00`) with timestamped, **accumulating** pcaps under a stable
  per-sensor directory: `/var/lib/axrange/capture/<safe>/cap-%Y%m%d-%H%M%S.pcap` (`-G 3600 -C 100`,
  `-W` dropped). Retention is now the job of a new in-guest disk-%/file-count prune watchdog
  (`axr-cap-watchdog-<safe>.service`/`.timer`, 60s tick, `_render_capture_pcap_watchdog`) **ported
  from the standing appliance's own `o2/tcpdump-sensor/bin/capture-watchdog`** rather than
  reinvented: prunes oldest-first by mtime once disk usage on the capture filesystem is `≥ HIGH`%
  (default 85, back down to `< LOW`%, default 70) **or** the file count exceeds `MAX_FILES` (default
  2000, back down to at most that many), always keeping the newest `MIN_KEEP=3` files untouched
  (protects tcpdump's currently-open file). New optional `adapter.conf` keys
  `CAPTURE_HIGH_WATER_PCT` / `CAPTURE_LOW_WATER_PCT` / `CAPTURE_MAX_FILES`
  (`AdapterConfig.capture_high_water_pct`/`capture_low_water_pct`/`capture_max_files`,
  `axrange/adapter_proxmox/config.py`); `load_config` clamps `low < high` if an operator sets them
  the wrong way round (parity with the appliance's own sanity guard). The watchdog never runs on tap
  teardown or sensor restart — `axrange/o2control/taps.py` only touches tc/gretap/mirror config via
  the adapter, never the sensor VM's filesystem, so removing a tap (or the whole tap plan) leaves
  every prior pcap in place; only the 60s timer ever deletes anything.
- **Two review-caught bugs fixed in the same arc (see `docs/LESSONS.md` LES-085/LES-086):**
  (a) the watchdog's prune glob was initially `cap-*.pcap`, which **misses** tcpdump's `-C`
  size-rotation files (`cap-<ts>.pcap1`, `.pcap2`, …) — tcpdump appends the size-rotation counter
  *after* the `.pcap` extension, not before, so under sustained traffic the bulk of the rotated
  files were invisible to an extension-anchored glob and retention silently pruned nothing while the
  disk filled. Fixed to the extension-agnostic `cap-*` (safe because the capture directory holds
  only this sensor's pcaps). (b) the original `-G 300` (5-minute) rotation interval proliferated
  near-empty files on an **idle** mirror (288/day) faster than a disk-%-only watchdog could ever
  notice (tiny files barely move disk usage) — fixed to `-G 3600` (hourly, caps an idle link to 24
  files/day) plus the new file-count trigger as a second line of defense.
- `verify-capture` (`OP-MIR-040`) is **unaffected** by any of this — it reads the wire live over the
  decap interface, never the pcap files on disk.
- No IR/PLANNER/OP_CATALOG/VALIDATOR/ADAPTER/IMAGE_MANIFEST schema change; no `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump.

**Why:**
- A standing O2 sensor that silently captures nothing defeats the entire O2 observability arc (A2
  tap playout, A3 attack/defense, A4 measurement all assume the sensor is actually recording) — this
  was a live-verified regression waiting to be found, not a hypothetical.
- The user explicitly required capture history to survive both a tap-removal (A2 `tap destroy`) and
  a sensor restart/re-`o2 up`, ruling out the old cyclic ring's implicit "redeploy resets capture"
  behavior.

**Affected docs:**
- `CLAUDE.md` — pytest counter (881→904, all six occurrences) + a new summary blockquote for this fix.
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — new `GAP-025` (Resolved (build); dev45 live-verify pending), cross-referencing GAP-020.
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — `LES-085`, `LES-086`.

**Affected code:**
- `axrange/config_exec/render.py` — `_systemd_daemon_start_block`, `_render_capture_pcap_watchdog`,
  `render_sensor_setup` (tcpdump-sensor + zeek-sensor branches).
- `axrange/config_exec/backend.py` — `ConfigBackend._deploy_sensor` threads the new retention knobs
  through to `render_sensor_setup`.
- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/config.py`, `adapter.conf.example` — new `CAPTURE_HIGH_WATER_PCT` /
  `CAPTURE_LOW_WATER_PCT` / `CAPTURE_MAX_FILES` keys.
- `tests/test_sensor_exec.py`, `tests/test_adapter_proxmox.py` — new/updated coverage. Full suite:
  904 pytest tests, green.

**Live-verify status:** unit-verified only. dev45 live-verify still pending — see `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md`
GAP-025 and `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` for the plan (deploy a tapped TE → confirm capture runs under
systemd + survives a restart/re-up + pcaps persist across `o2 tap destroy`).

## 2026-07-16 — CLI lifecycle unification: `axrange {te,o2,tap} {validate,plan,deploy,status,info,destroy}`

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- Restructured the `axrange` CLI from its accreted shape (bare `deploy`/`status`/`destroy`; `o2
  up`/`down`; `o2 tap apply`/`destroy`) into a **uniform grid**: three deployable-object tiers (`te`,
  `o2`, `tap`) × one verb set (`validate`, `plan`, `deploy`, `status`, `info`, `destroy`). Spec is a
  positional argument on `validate`/`plan`/`deploy`; `status`/`info`/`destroy` take an optional id,
  defaulting to the latest deploy **of that tier**. `list-deploys` (+ new `--tier {te,o2,tap}`
  filter), `serve`, and `image build` stay outside the grid (cross-cutting/different lifecycle).
- **D1 — full symmetry (user decision, 2026-07-16):** no bare top-level lifecycle verbs survive.
  `axrange deploy scenario.yaml` no longer exists as such — it is `axrange te deploy scenario.yaml`.
  **One-cycle deprecation shims** cover every verb that changed spelling — bare
  `deploy`/`validate`/`plan`/`status`/`destroy` → `te <verb>`; `o2 up` → `o2 deploy`; `o2 down` →
  `o2 destroy`; `o2 tap apply` → `tap deploy`; `o2 tap destroy` → `tap destroy` — each printing a
  one-line stderr deprecation notice (`note: 'axrange deploy' is deprecated; use 'axrange te
  deploy' (removed next release)`) and then dispatching to the canonical handler. Marked `#
  DEPRECATED shim` in `axrange/cli/main.py` for removal next cycle; **not** a permanent dual
  surface. Every in-repo caller (`docs/RUNBOOK_o2_live_verify.md`, `.claude/agents/live-verifier.md`)
  was swept to the canonical form in this same change so the repo itself never depends on a shim.
- **New `info` verb** (all three tiers): a read-only projection over persisted op rows — distinct
  from `status` (op-tree completion) — showing resolved config **+ runtime access facts** (per-host
  IPs/subnets/router WAN IP/SSH user/taps for `te`; standing sensors + observability endpoints for
  `o2`; resolved tap→sensor bindings + GRE keys for `tap`). Two-tier behavior: `info <spec>` with
  nothing live shows resolved config only; `info [id]` on a live deploy adds access facts. Derived
  entirely from existing persisted op rows (`axrange/planner/opview.py`, new — `op_fields` duck-types
  `OpInstance`/`OpRow` so the same reader works pre- and post-deploy), no new data plane. `--json` on
  `info` (and `status`) emits a stable machine shape via `dataclasses.asdict()` through one shared
  `_print_json` helper — never a hand-rolled dict literal.
- **D2 — `O2_*` observability-endpoint keys in `adapter.conf`** (user decision, 2026-07-16): the
  server-stack endpoints (Grafana/Loki/Prometheus/Caldera/Wazuh, `docs/WORK_ORDER_o2_server_stack.md`)
  live on a dedicated VM outside the deploy state store, so `o2 info` needs an authoritative,
  non-derived source. Added optional flat `O2_GRAFANA_URL` / `O2_LOKI_URL` / `O2_PROMETHEUS_URL` /
  `O2_CALDERA_URL` / `O2_WAZUH_URL` keys to `adapter.conf.example` (mirroring the existing `BUILD_*`/
  `NETWORK_SENSOR_*` flat-key convention), loaded into `AdapterConfig.o2_endpoints`
  (`axrange/adapter_proxmox/config.py`). `o2 info` prints whichever are configured and "(not
  configured)" for the rest — deliberately **not** guessed from a sensor IP.
- **`te` vs. `tap-<te>` read-path disambiguation.** A tap-plan playout deploy is stamped `tier="te"`
  (the state-store schema has no separate `"tap"` deploy-level tier — `tap` is a name-shape tag on
  individual resources, not a deploy tier) and is named `tap-<target-te-deploy-id>`, so a naive
  `tier="te"` query for "the latest TE" would wrongly surface a tap-plan playout. Every read path that
  picks a default/latest TE (`te status`, `te info`, `te destroy`, `list-deploys --tier te`) now
  excludes the `tap-` `plan_id` prefix (`StateStore.deploys_matching(..., exclude_plan_id_prefix=
  "tap-")`) **and** `list-deploys --tier tap` cross-checks candidates against a structural op-name
  discriminator (`o2control.taps.is_tap_plan_deploy` — the persisted op set is a non-empty subset of
  exactly `{attach-gre-tunnel, install-ovs-mirror, verify-capture}`), because the `tap-` prefix alone
  is a naming convention a real TE scenario could coincidentally collide with. The empty-set case is
  explicitly guarded (`_is_tap_shaped_op_names` requires non-empty — `set() <= ANY_SET` is vacuously
  true).
- **API parity (work order §3.7) — explicitly DEFERRED.** `axrange/api/` is untouched by this change;
  the CLI grid was the deliverable. Bringing `/api/o2/surface`-style endpoints to the same
  `info`/tier-grid shape is follow-on work, not part of this change.
- Full suite green: **867 pytest tests** (was 797 before this change's additions).

**Why:**
- The CLI had grown three different verb vocabularies for the same underlying concept
  ("idempotently reconcile a spec to the cluster") — `te` bare/top-level, `o2` noun-grouped
  `up`/`down`, `tap` buried under `o2` with `apply`/`destroy` — and no `validate`/`plan`/`info`
  surface at all for `o2` or `tap`, even though the machinery those verbs need
  (`compose_surface_ir`/`build_plan_and_ctx` for `o2`; `parse_tap_plan`/`resolve_tap_plan`/
  `_compose_ops` for `tap`) already existed. This was a surface/UX unification over existing
  machinery, not new engine capability.

**Affected docs:**
- `CLAUDE.md` — CLI surface bullets updated from `axrange validate | plan | deploy [--fresh] |
  status | list-deploys | destroy | serve | image build` to the tier-grid form; pytest counter
  797→867 across all quoted occurrences.
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_cli_lifecycle.md` — STATUS moved OPEN → DONE (build), as-built note added.
- `docs/RUNBOOK_o2_live_verify.md`, `.claude/agents/live-verifier.md` — swept to canonical
  `te`/`o2`/`tap` verb forms.
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — LES-082, LES-083 (see below).

**Spec:** `docs/WORK_ORDER_cli_lifecycle.md` (D1/D2 both resolved therein, user, 2026-07-16).

**Affected code:**
- `axrange/cli/main.py` (verb grid, deprecation shims, `info` command handlers, `_print_json`,
  `_latest_te_deploy`) — largest single-file change (~1300 lines).
- `axrange/planner/opview.py` (new) — `op_fields`, the shared OpInstance/OpRow duck-typing reader.
- `axrange/planner/state.py` — new `deploys_matching` (tier + prefix/exclude-prefix filtered,
  unbounded, per LES-053's "tag-filtered query, never a capped scan" rule).
- `axrange/o2control/{control,taps,__init__}.py` — `is_tap_plan_deploy`, `_is_tap_shaped_op_names`,
  `tap_status`, `tap_info_from_resolution`/`tap_info_from_deploy`.
- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/config.py` (+ `naming.py` comment) — `o2_endpoints`.
- `adapter.conf.example` — `O2_*` key block.
- `tests/test_cli_lifecycle.py` (new), `tests/test_o2_taps.py` (extended).

**Live-verify:** not required per the work order (CLI surface change, unit/integration-provable); a
smoke `te deploy && te info && te destroy` on `dev45` is optional polish, not a gate, and was not run
as part of this build.

## 2026-07-16 — CLI bare-default deploy-targeting safety fix (dev45 smoke)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- Ran the optional dev45 smoke the CLI-lifecycle-unification entry above left un-run: `te deploy →
  te info → te status → te destroy` against the minimal single-host `deploy-dev45/scenarios/
  blast-check.yaml` TE. The new `te info` card **worked and was verified correct** against the live
  guest over QGA (resolved config + runtime access facts matched reality) — the smoke's positive
  result for the `info` verb stands as originally intended. But the run also caught a **HIGH-severity
  safety bug** in the new bare-form defaulting: a bare `axrange te destroy --allow-destroy` could
  print `Result: DESTROYED` against a stale/unrelated deploy while the just-created VM stayed live.
- **Three-part root cause**, all in `axrange/planner/state.py`'s "latest deploy" queries:
  1. "Latest deploy" was ordered by `started_at DESC`. A resumed/revived deploy keeps its *original*
     `started_at` (only `updated_at` moves on a status transition), so it sorted **behind** an
     unrelated deploy that merely started more recently — a bare-default lookup picked the wrong row.
  2. `find_deploy_for_plan` revived a `destroyed` deploy **in place** (it matched on the deterministic
     `plan_id` regardless of terminal status), silently resurrecting a tombstone's `deploy_id` instead
     of minting a fresh one.
  3. (found during review of the fix for #1/#2) Because `mark_destroyed` bumps `updated_at`, a
     just-destroyed tombstone jumps to the **top** of the corrected `updated_at`-ordered "latest"
     query — so a bare `te destroy` run immediately after a destroy would resolve to the tombstone
     (a safe no-op, but a *misleadingly reassuring* "DESTROYED" printed while a genuinely newer, live
     deploy of the same tier sat untouched).
- **Fix, two rounds:**
  - Round 1 (root causes 1–2): every "latest of tier" query — `latest_deploys`,
    `latest_deploy_for_tier`, `deploys_matching`, `find_deploy_for_plan` — now orders by
    `COALESCE(updated_at, started_at) DESC`, not `started_at` alone. `find_deploy_for_plan` gained
    `exclude_destroyed: bool = False`; the four **deploy-creation resume sites** (`cli.main.cmd_deploy`
    (`te deploy`), `api.service.create_deploy`, `o2control.control.stand_up_surface`,
    `o2control.taps.apply_tap_plan`) now call it with `exclude_destroyed=True` — a `destroyed` top
    match is treated as no-match, so the caller's existing "no match → mint a fresh deploy_id" branch
    fires automatically (the same branch `--fresh` already uses). Resume-safety for
    completed/failed/halted/running deploys is unchanged. Teardown and read-only lookups
    (`destroy_taps`'s target lookup, `cascade_destroy_for_te`) deliberately keep the default — they
    must still be able to see a tombstone (idempotent-no-op path).
  - Round 2 (root cause 3, code-review finding): the **destroy** bare-defaults specifically (`te
    destroy`, `o2 destroy`, `tap destroy`) now prefer the newest **non-terminal** deploy of the tier
    (`exclude_destroyed=True` threaded through `latest_deploy_for_tier`/`deploys_matching`/
    `_latest_te_deploy`/`latest_tap_plan_deploy`). When the only candidate is a tombstone, the CLI
    prints an explicit `"latest te deploy <id> is already destroyed; nothing to do (pass an explicit
    deploy_id ...)"` and exits 0 — never a misleading "DESTROYED". `status`/`info` bare-defaults are
    **unchanged** — they deliberately keep the tombstone on top, since inspecting what you just
    destroyed is the whole point of running `status`/`info` right after a destroy.
- No state-store schema-version bump: both rounds reuse the existing NOT-NULL `updated_at` column and
  only add query parameters (`exclude_destroyed`) — no new column, no new table, no migration.
- Full suite green: **881 pytest tests** (up from 867 at the CLI-lifecycle-unification commit).

**Why:**
- A destroy verb that can report success against the wrong target while the intended target survives
  live is a blast-radius-relevant correctness bug, not a cosmetic one — exactly the class of thing a
  gated live smoke exists to catch before an operator hits it for real. The `te info` card's
  correctness was never in question; the smoke's value here was entirely in exercising the new
  bare-default *targeting* logic end-to-end against real, time-ordered rows, which no unit test
  happened to construct in a way that exposed the `started_at`-vs-`updated_at` ordering bug.

**Affected docs:**
- `CLAUDE.md` — pytest counter 867→881 across all quoted occurrences; CLI-surface bullet notes the
  dev45 smoke ran (deploy/info/status/destroy verified; bare-default bug found + fixed).
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_cli_lifecycle.md` — as-built note updated: the smoke WAS run (2026-07-16), the
  `info` card verified correct on-cluster, and it drove this safety fix. STATUS stays DONE.
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — GAP-023 (`delete_mirror_snippets` always reports "removed", never
  "absent", on an idempotent re-destroy — cosmetic reporting inaccuracy) and GAP-024 (`--images`
  pointed at the `images/` subdirectory instead of its parent root silently yields an empty manifest
  registry + a false `REF-120` validate failure) — both minor findings from the same smoke, unrelated
  to the safety bug above.
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — LES-084.

**Affected code:**
- `axrange/planner/state.py` — `latest_deploys`, `latest_deploy_for_tier`, `deploys_matching`,
  `find_deploy_for_plan` (ordering fix + `exclude_destroyed` param).
- `axrange/cli/main.py` — `cmd_deploy` (te), `_latest_te_deploy`, `cmd_destroy`, `cmd_o2_destroy`,
  `cmd_tap_destroy` (destroy bare-defaults + the explicit "already destroyed" message).
- `axrange/api/service.py` — `create_deploy` (`exclude_destroyed=True` on its resume lookup).
- `axrange/o2control/control.py` — `stand_up_surface` (same).
- `axrange/o2control/taps.py` — `apply_tap_plan`, `latest_tap_plan_deploy` (same, plus the destroy
  bare-default).
- `tests/test_planner_state.py`, `tests/test_o2control.py`, `tests/test_cli_lifecycle.py` — coverage
  for both rounds.

**Live-verify:** dev45 smoke `te deploy → te info → te status → te destroy` against
`deploy-dev45/scenarios/blast-check.yaml`. `te info` verified correct on-cluster over QGA; the smoke
found and drove the fix of the bare-default targeting bug documented above (now fixed and
unit/integration-covered — not separately re-run live post-fix, per the same "CLI surface change,
unit/integration-provable" gating this work order was built under).

## 2026-07-15 — O2 server-stack live bring-up + A3/A4 gated live-verify: PASSED (dev45 VM 102)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- Ran the §2/§5 gated live bring-up `docs/WORK_ORDER_o2_server_stack.md` left PENDING (the entry
  directly below this one landed only the code-side fixes). **Result: all three blockers resolved,
  both deferred live-verifies (A3, A4) now PASSED.**
- **Stood up a dedicated O2-services VM** (work order §2): VMID `102` `o2-services` on `dev45`,
  cloned from template `620` (`img-debian-12-cloud`), 4 vCPU / 8 GB / 60 GB, DHCP `10.80.72.37`,
  rootful Docker CE 29.6.1 + compose v2. All 5 compose services (prometheus / loki / grafana /
  caldera / wazuh-manager) came up `Up (healthy)` and are reachable from the orchestrator.
  Snapshotted (`o2-stack-healthy`) as a rollback-able known-good surface.
- **GAP-016 (Caldera) → Resolved (live-verified).** The already-committed `yarl==1.9.4` pin (see the
  entry below) built clean on the real VM (build smoke-gate → 200) and Caldera serves 200 there;
  PROVEN end-to-end by A3 (a Sandcat agent downloaded from Caldera and beaconed).
- **GAP-017 (Wazuh) → Resolved (live-verified), fix EVOLVED past the code-fixes entry below.** The
  committed filebeat-stub config alone did NOT hold on the real VM: filebeat 8.x rejects a
  bind-mounted config it doesn't own (a uid=0 ownership check) — a git-tracked file necessarily
  carries the HOST's ownership, which git cannot control — so the stub alone still crash-looped the
  container exactly like the original missing-file symptom. The DURABLE fix (now landed): an empty
  s6 `down` file (`o2/wazuh/filebeat-down`, mounted at `/etc/services.d/filebeat/down:ro`) that
  disables the filebeat service at the s6 supervisor level before it ever reads the stub — the work
  order's own named alternative. Also surfaced and fixed on the same VM: the default `kvm64` CPU
  model lacks the **x86-64-v2** baseline Wazuh 4.9.2's glibc needs (`Fatal glibc error: CPU does not
  support x86-64-v2`) — fixed with `qm set 102 -cpu host` + reboot. With both fixes, Wazuh ran stable
  (all daemons up, `:55000` API responding 401); PROVEN by A3 (`agent1` enrolled, ID `001`, Active).
- **GAP-018 → Resolved (live-verified).** The rootful-docker VM eliminates the whole
  rootlessport-orphan class (clean iptables port publishing, no stale-port footgun).
- **A4 (`docs/WORK_ORDER_measurement.md` §3) → PASSED**, after finding and fixing two real bugs in
  the never-before-live-verified `export.ship: loki` path (sensors design Phase C2), tracked as new
  **GAP-020**: the shared `_render_loki_tail_shipper` bootstrap (`axrange/config_exec/render.py`)
  opened with a `pkill -f '<pattern>'` where the pattern (derived from `shipper_path`, e.g.
  `ship-<id>.py`) is a literal substring of the delivering wrapper's OWN filename
  (`ship-<id>.py.sh`) — the bootstrap self-killed its own wrapper before it ever launched the
  shipper; separately, a plain `nohup ... &` launch was reaped once the QGA exec session that
  delivered it ended. Fixed by dropping the `pkill` entirely and launching as a detached, named
  `systemd-run --unit=<id> --collect` transient (idempotent restart via `systemctl stop` /
  `reset-failed` on the same unit, no `pkill` anywhere) — fixes both the sensor shipper
  (`axr-ship-<id>`) and the truth shipper (`axr-truth-ship-<id>`). 5 new regression tests
  (`tests/test_activity_exec.py`, `tests/test_sensor_exec.py`). Re-proven live: `deploy-sensor` now
  auto-starts the shipper (`systemctl is-active` → `active`). Also hit (new **GAP-021**, resolved
  for this verify with an open design question left standing): the activity truth-marker shipper
  runs on the TE host and needs egress to reach the O2 Loki, which the verify scenario's TE host
  initially lacked — fixed for the run by `egress: true` (Simple-zone SNAT) on the verify network;
  whether truth markers should instead route via the always-present transport/sensor plane (so an
  egress-isolated TE can still get A4 coverage) is recorded as an open design question, not decided
  here. **Result:** both streams landed in Loki (`{job="axrange-sensor"}` suricata alert
  `sid=9000001` + `{job="axr-truth"}` `technique=T1595 host=atk1`), joinable by
  technique+host+window; the "Attack coverage (A4)" Grafana dashboard renders against the Loki
  datasource.
- **A3 (`docs/WORK_ORDER_attack_defense.md` §4) → PASSED.** Deployed a verify TE with
  `overlays.adversary` (Sandcat → Caldera `10.80.72.37`) + `overlays.defense` (Wazuh-agent → manager
  `10.80.72.37`). Sandcat enrolled in Caldera (paw `ahkojg`, host `agent1`, group `red`); the Wazuh
  agent enrolled in the manager (ID `001`, Active). **New GAP-022 (Open, Low):** the Prometheus
  exporter→Prometheus-server half of A3's three agent→server pairs was descoped — there's no
  mechanism that auto-adds a newly-deployed TE host to `o2/prometheus/prometheus.yml`'s static
  scrape targets (a file-based-SD follow-on, not a live-verify blocker).
- Full pytest suite: **797 passed** (was 792 before the sensor-agnostic-`te` build; +5 GAP-020
  regression tests since the code-fixes entry below).

**Why:** closes out the O2 build arc's last two gated live-verify deferrals (A3, A4) purely on
axRange-adjacent infra work (a dedicated standing VM + two container-image config fixes), none of
which touched engine/`o2control` code beyond the GAP-020 render.py fix the live-verify itself
surfaced. All six of the O2 arc's gated `dev45` live-verifies (A1, A2, sensor-agnostic-te, SCHED,
A3, A4) are now PASSED.

**Affected docs:** `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` (GAP-016/017/018 → Resolved (live-verified), evidence +
the filebeat-fix evolution + the CPU x86-64-v2 finding added; new GAP-020/021/022),
`docs/WORK_ORDER_o2_server_stack.md` (status → DONE), `docs/WORK_ORDER_measurement.md` (§3 header →
PASSED), `docs/WORK_ORDER_attack_defense.md` (§4 header → PASSED), `docs/LESSONS.md` (LES-078..081
below), `CLAUDE.md` (O2-arc status block: A3/A4 flipped from DEFERRED to PASSED; GAP-016/017/018
Resolved; standing O2 surface now live on dev45 VM 102).

**Affected code:** none beyond what the entry below already recorded as uncommitted at the time
(`o2/wazuh/filebeat-down` (new), `o2/compose/docker-compose.yml` (the `filebeat-down` mount),

## 2026-07-15 — O2 server-stack bring-up: Caldera/Wazuh/Prometheus code fixes (GAP-016/017/018), GAP-016 root cause overturned

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- Three code-side fixes to the O2 compose/attack-defense stack under `o2/` (`docs/WORK_ORDER_o2_server_stack.md`
  §3), **no `axrange/` engine change**:
  1. **§3.2 GAP-017 — Wazuh filebeat crash-loop.** New `o2/wazuh/filebeat-stub.yml` (empty
     `filebeat.inputs`, `output.file`, no indexer) mounted read-only into the `wazuh-manager` service
     in `o2/compose/docker-compose.yml`. `wazuh/wazuh-manager:4.9.2`'s s6 init auto-starts filebeat
     expecting a wazuh-indexer this manager-only stack doesn't have; with no `filebeat.yml` at all it
     hard-exits `code=1` and s6 tears the whole container down. The stub makes filebeat idle
     harmlessly instead.
  2. **§3.1 — `o2/prometheus/prometheus.yml` render-artifact revert.** A prior live session had baked
     a concrete `10.80.72.52:9100` into the file, clobbering the `__CAPTURE_HOST__:__CAPTURE_NODE_EXPORTER_PORT__`
     template placeholders; reverted to the committed template. Folded into the same pass: a
     five-service compose healthcheck audit against LES-064 found **all five already valid**
     (prometheus/loki/grafana use busybox `wget` — `grafana/loki:3.3.2`'s `distroless/static:debug`
     base DOES bundle busybox incl. `wget` via the debug layer, so it is not a LES-064 violation
     despite looking like one; wazuh uses `wazuh-control`+grep; caldera uses python3 urllib) and a
     port audit found `PROM_PORT=9091` (host override) vs. compose `${PROM_PORT:-9090}` (default)
     consistent, no mismatch.
  3. **§3.3 GAP-016 — Caldera "every request 500s", root cause OVERTURNED.** The work order and
     GAP-016 as previously written hypothesized an `apispec`/`webargs` version skew breaking
     `aiohttp-apispec`'s middleware. A live in-container diagnosis (podman, 2026-07-15) **disproved
     that** — `apispec==6.10.0`/`webargs==8.7.1` serve fine — and found the real cause: **`aiohttp==3.9.3`'s
     `BaseRequest.url` passes the raw `Host: <host>:<port>` header straight into
     `yarl.URL.build(host=...)`, and `yarl>=1.13` rejects any host containing `:`**
     (`ValueError: Host 'localhost:8888' cannot contain ':'`) — on every request, before
     routing/middleware ever runs. `yarl` is an unpinned transitive of `aiohttp` (not in
     `requirements.txt`), so it resolved to `1.24.2`. Fix: pin `yarl==1.9.4` (new `RUN pip install`
     in `o2/caldera/Dockerfile`), validated end-to-end in a fresh podman build (`GET /` → 200,
     `/api/docs/swagger.json` → 200, several `/api/v2/*` routes → 200). Added a build-time HTTP
     smoke gate (python3 `urllib`, probes `GET /` in a retry loop, fails the `docker build` on a 500)
     per §3.3 step 3 — locally build-validated (the heredoc-in-`RUN` parses under podman
     5.8.2/buildah's classic parser; the smoke gate printed `-> 200 (OK)`). Kept `CALDERA_VERSION=5.0.0`
     with the `yarl` pin (validated, minimal, reversible) rather than bumping to 5.3.0 — see
     "Why" below.
  - **A second, separate bug surfaced during the same diagnosis:** unauthenticated
    `GET /api/v2/health` returns 500 on Caldera 5.0.0 — an upstream app bug (`IndexError` on
    `access[0]` for anonymous callers on an `authentication_exempt` route with an empty permissions
    tuple), unrelated to the `yarl` fix. This is why the build smoke gate and any future compose
    healthcheck must probe `GET /` (anonymous-safe, serves the login page) and **not** `/health`.
    Caldera 5.3.0 re-guards `/health` upstream.
  - Two additional Dockerfile/compose changes present in the same working tree, precondition fixes
    made in the same bring-up arc (not new this pass, recorded here for completeness since they were
    still uncommitted): building the `magma` UI plugin (`npm ci`/`npm run build`, `nodejs npm` added
    to the image) — without it `app/api/rest_api.py` crashes on every boot serving an unbuilt
    `plugins/magma/dist/assets/`; and changing the Grafana `../grafana/plugins` compose bind-mount
    from `:ro` to read-write — `GF_INSTALL_PLUGINS` (`frser-sqlite-datasource`,
    `yesoreyeram-infinity-datasource`) writes into that same path at container init, and a `:ro`
    mount there made Grafana fail to start on every boot (`failed to extract plugin archive:
    ... read-only file system`).
- **Not part of this pass, still PENDING:** the §2 dedicated O2-services VM stand-up (rootful
  docker/podman, GAP-018's actual fix) and the §5 gated live bring-up + A4/A3 re-run. **The code
  fixes above are locally build-validated only** (in-container/podman diagnosis on the author box) —
  they have not yet been exercised end-to-end against a real, reachable O2-services host, and A3/A4
  remain DEFERRED per the 2026-07-15 batch live-verify entry above.

**Why:** unblocks the two of three GAP-016/017/018 blockers that are image/config defects traveling
with the container (host-independent) regardless of where the O2-services VM ends up living; GAP-018
itself still needs the §2 host move. The GAP-016 root-cause overturn matters beyond the fix itself —
a documented hypothesis (`apispec`/`webargs` skew) that "sounded right" and was never actually
disproven turned out to be wrong; the true cause was an unpinned transitive dependency three levels
removed from anything the original diagnosis looked at. `CALDERA_VERSION` stays at `5.0.0` with the
`yarl==1.9.4` pin (validated, minimal) rather than jumping to `5.3.0`; bumping to 5.3.0 (whose
`aiohttp==3.10.11` fixes this at the source via `authority=` instead of `host=`) is recorded as a
cleaner future migration option, not a decision made now.

**Affected docs:** `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` (GAP-016 root cause corrected, apispec/webargs → yarl;
GAP-016 + GAP-017 status → Resolved (code) — live-verify PENDING; GAP-018 unchanged Open, the
prometheus.yml revert noted as related hygiene; new note on the Caldera `/health` anon-500),
`docs/WORK_ORDER_o2_server_stack.md` (status blockquote updated: §3 code fixes DONE + locally
validated with the corrected root cause; §2/§5 VM bring-up + A4/A3 still PENDING), `docs/LESSONS.md`
(LES-074..077 below).

**Affected code:** `o2/caldera/Dockerfile` (yarl pin, magma build, build-time smoke gate),
`o2/compose/docker-compose.yml` (wazuh filebeat-stub mount, grafana plugins bind-mount rw),
`o2/wazuh/filebeat-stub.yml` (new), `o2/prometheus/prometheus.yml` (template placeholders restored).
**Uncommitted** at the time of this entry — the orchestrator handles the commit gate.

**Live-verify:** PENDING — the §2 dedicated-VM stand-up and the §5 gated A4/A3 re-run have not run.
Do not treat this entry as closing GAP-016/017/018 or flipping `WORK_ORDER_attack_defense.md` /
`WORK_ORDER_measurement.md` to PASSED.

**Supersedes:** the GAP-016 root-cause text in the previous entry below ("`apispec`/`webargs`
unpinned" hypothesis) — corrected, not reversed in direction (the symptom and fix-location were
right; the cause was not).

## 2026-07-13 — feat: sensor-agnostic `te_` substrate — BUILD (`docs/WORK_ORDER_sensor_agnostic_te.md`)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- Built the design decided in the "sensor-agnostic `te_` substrate" entry immediately below (same
  date). New op **`OP-MIR-005 record-tap-intent`** (`axrange/ops/registry.py`) — declarative,
  `idempotency_class: infra`, `resource_categories: ()`, `after: ()`, **no adapter call**. A `te`
  deploy now emits exactly this one OP-MIR op per tapped host (`axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py`) instead
  of `attach-gre-tunnel` / `install-ovs-mirror` / `verify-capture`, and emits **no `mirror-ready`
  barrier of its own** (`start-activity` and other `mirror-ready`-gated ops simply see zero
  predecessors for a `te`-only deploy — the gate is a no-op until a tap playout runs).
  `attach-gre-tunnel`/`install-ovs-mirror`/`verify-capture` stay in the catalog with **unchanged
  signatures**; they are now emitted exclusively by the **A2 tap playout**
  (`axrange/o2control/taps.py`).
- **A2's discovery source flipped**: `_scan_te_inventory` now reads `record-tap-intent`'s
  `tapped_nics` output instead of `install-ovs-mirror`'s `tapped_nics` input (same shape, new
  producer — A2's own composition of `attach-gre-tunnel`/`install-ovs-mirror`/`verify-capture`
  against the discovered set, and its `qga_hosts` injector-candidate derivation from
  `wait-os-ready`/`clone-vm`, are otherwise unchanged). A2 is now the SOLE emitter of the three real
  OP-MIR install ops — not a re-point layered over te-side mirroring, which no longer exists.
- **`SENSOR_TRANSPORT_IP` / `SENSOR_<ID>_TRANSPORT_IP` / `SENSOR_SSH_HOST` / `SENSOR_SSH_USER` /
  `SENSOR_SSH_KEY` / `SENSOR_CAPTURE_IFACE`** and the `sensors: dict[str, str]` map are **removed**
  from `AdapterConfig` (`axrange/adapter_proxmox/config.py`) and `adapter.conf.example`. `--sensor-ip`
  / `sensor_transport_ip` are dropped from the te deploy path (`axrange/cli/main.py`,
  `axrange/api/service.py`, `axrange/api/dtos.py`).
- **The `SENSOR_SSH_*` "no vmid ⇒ SSH fallback" branch is deleted, not just unreachable**:
  `run_ssh_sensor` (`axrange/adapter_proxmox/ssh.py`) and `_default_sensor_confirm` /
  `sensor_confirm_fn` injection (`axrange/config_exec/backend.py`) are gone entirely.
  `ConfigBackend._verify_capture` now raises loud (`verify-capture-no-sensor-vmid`) if no
  `sensor_vmid` is threaded into the op — the QGA read is the only path, no silent fallback.
- **`axrange/imagebuild/conformance.py`**'s smoke-test deploy (always untapped, no capture) dropped
  its now-nonexistent `sensor_transport_ip` argument.
- Added `tests/test_o2_taps.py::test_record_tap_intent_producer_consumer_roundtrip` — builds a real
  plan, persists it through the **real** `op_to_row`/`barrier_to_row`/`StateStore.insert_ops`
  serialization path (not a hand-built fixture dict), and asserts `_scan_te_inventory`/
  `_tapped_iface_set` recover exactly the `(host, net_id, nic_index)` triples `pass1_emit` resolved —
  closing the exact class of drift LES-065 was written about, generalized from a text log-line format
  to a dict-shaped op `inputs_resolved`/`outputs` contract (LES-068, added this entry).
- **Full suite: 794 → 792 tests** (net -2: the round-trip test above and REF-250's test coverage
  (`test_catalog.py`) added, against a larger number of emission-shape tests updated/removed in place
  rather than counted as new — `test_planner_executor.py`, `test_sensor_routing.py`,
  `test_sensor_exec.py`, `test_dynamic_tap.py`, `test_planner_dag.py`, `test_ops_registry.py`,
  `test_adapter_proxmox.py`, `test_activity_exec.py`, `test_overlay_exec.py`).
- **One code-writer judgment call, deliberate and in-scope of the work order's §7 "out of scope"
  note:** **`NETWORK_SENSOR_*` / `deploy-network-sensor` (Option C, the appliance-adjacent network
  sensor) is explicitly KEPT**, untouched — it is a separate, already-superseded/back-compat mechanism
  (`config.py` now carries an explicit code comment saying so), not part of the A1/A2 tap-playout
  sensor plane this work order retired. Do not read its survival as an oversight.
- **Code-review fix, landed in the same build (F1/F2 — bigger in scope than the work order's own §3
  "revisit `catalog.py`" note anticipated):** the work order flagged that `NET-110` (tapped-network-is-
  capturable) needed a look "now that `verify-capture` leaves the te deploy." Code review went further
  than a prose fix — it found that **Sensors-design Phase B/C in-scenario sensor VM synthesis**
  (`sensors:` → a synthetic node + `deploy-sensor`, `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_sensors.md`) was **still firing
  for an ordinary `te` deploy**, which is now a **silent-blind trap** (LES-001/004 pattern): a te-emitted
  sensor VM would receive ZERO mirrored traffic (no attach-gre-tunnel/install-ovs-mirror for it any
  more) and get NO heartbeat to catch it, because verify-capture no longer runs inside a te deploy
  either. Fix: `axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py`'s sensor-synthesis block (and the `deploy-sensor`
  emission loop with it) is now **`tier == "o2"`-gated** — it fires only inside
  `axrange.o2control.control.stand_up_surface`'s standing-surface deploy (`build_plan_and_ctx(tier="o2")`),
  never for a `te`. A `te` deploy that still declares `sensors:` now synthesizes **no sensor VM at
  all**. Paired with two validator changes (new rule ID, `list_rules()` 103→104):
  - **`REF-250`** (new, WARNING) — fires whenever `sensors:` is non-empty on a `te`-tier IR, telling
    the author capture now requires `axrange o2 up` + `axrange o2 tap apply` post-deploy, not this
    block. WARNING not ERROR so the shipped `target-environments/` archetypes (`ad-domain-lab`,
    `purple-team-range`) keep validating.
  - **`NET-110`** retargeted — its old part (b), "every tapped interface has a reachable sensor" (a
    `tap_host`/`tap_net` binding or the implicit primary), is **removed**: that was a te-side "which
    sensor does this tap route to" check that no longer applies (a te never routes a tap to any sensor
    at plan time now). `REF-250` covers the general case as a warning instead — no double-report, no
    silent drop. `NET-110` keeps its part (a): a tapped segment still needs ≥1 node attached.
  This is a materially bigger scope closure than the work order's own text anticipated — it retires
  the entire per-scenario in-scenario-sensor-VM model (Phases A–C of `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_sensors.md`),
  not just the tap-wiring detail, completing the O2 direction's "sensors become standing" clause for
  real. `docs/VALIDATOR.md` version-locked schema bump: rule count 103→104, REF 24→25.
- **Content side-effect, corrected from an earlier draft of this entry (the tier-gating fix above
  changes it materially):** `ad-domain-lab` and `purple-team-range` (`target-environments/`) declare
  `sensors:` in their IR. Before this build, a bare `axrange deploy` of either synthesized a sensor VM
  and wired its taps automatically. **After this build, a bare `axrange deploy` synthesizes NO sensor
  VM at all** (tier-gated to o2, above) and the `sensors:` block only produces a `REF-250` warning at
  validate time. Both archetypes still validate and deploy cleanly, but they no longer self-capture —
  capture now requires `axrange o2 up` (stand a sensor) + `axrange o2 tap apply <te>` (bind the te's
  taps to it) as separate, explicit post-deploy steps. No archetype content was changed to reflect
  this; it is a documented follow-on for whoever next runs those archetypes end-to-end.

**Why:** closes the O2-direction gap recorded in the design entry below and finishes **GAP-012**
(`docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md`) by removing the `SENSOR_SSH_HOST` dependency `verify-capture` used to have.
The code-review fix closes a silent-blind trap (LES-001/004 pattern) the work order's own scope hadn't
anticipated: leaving Phase B/C sensor synthesis unconditional would have produced a scenario that
"deploys clean" while its declared sensor captures nothing, with no heartbeat to catch it.

**Affected docs:** `CLAUDE.md` (counters + a new summary blockquote + sensors-bullet tier-gating
note), `docs/OP_CATALOG.md` (`OP-MIR-005` spec block + table row + re-summed §8 total, EMISSION
CONTEXT notes on `verify-capture`/`install-ovs-mirror`/`attach-gre-tunnel`/`deploy-sensor`, a doc-
hygiene gap noted for 5 pre-existing ops with no YAML block), `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_dynamic_tap.md`
(in-deploy emission marked RETIRED for the `te`), `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_taps.md` (A2 now the sole

`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_sensors.md` (banner updated: supersession LANDED for the `te` tier, Phase B/C

**Affected code:** `axrange/ops/registry.py`, `axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py`,
