**Live-verify:** gated, PENDING (work order §5) — `axrange o2 up` → `axrange deploy <tapped-te>`
(confirm no gretap/mirror/verify-capture; `record-tap-intent` present in state) → `axrange o2 tap
apply <te>` (confirm A2 discovers the sensor from A1, `verify-capture` passes over QGA with no
`SENSOR_SSH_*` set anywhere) → teardown cascade, idempotent re-runs.

**Supersedes:** none (additive build on the design entry below; no ratified decision reversed).

**Follow-up:** `docs/WORK_ORDER_o2_sensor_rules.md` (opened 2026-07-13) — the `tier == "o2"` gating
above took `ad-domain-lab`/`purple-team-range`'s per-scenario `suricata_ad.rules` AD-detection
ruleset offline with it (it lived on the now-unsynthesized in-scenario sensor VM); the standing O2
sensor A2 binds a `te`'s taps to has no equivalent ruleset yet, so the AD purple-team detection loop
(CHANGELOG 2026-06-24 "AD DEFENSE overlay") is dark until that work order lands.

## 2026-07-13 — design: sensor-agnostic `te_` substrate — A2 owns all tap/sensor wiring; `SENSOR_*` leaves `adapter.conf`

**Authority:** user (direction) — 2026-07-13

**What changed (decision; build is `docs/WORK_ORDER_sensor_agnostic_te.md`, OPEN):**
- A `te_` deploy will stand up **only its own components** — no `install-ovs-mirror` /
  `attach-gre-tunnel` / `verify-capture`, no `SENSOR_*` read from `adapter.conf`, no `--sensor-ip`.
  All tap→sensor binding + capture verification becomes the **A2 tap playout** (`axrange o2 tap
  apply`), which already discovers the sensor's `transport_ip` **and** `vmid` from **A1's standing
  inventory** and reads the sensor over **QGA** (never `SENSOR_SSH_*`).
- **The discovery+inject mechanism the user asked for already exists** — A1 (`o2control/control.py`)
  exposes a `state.db`-derived sensor inventory (`SensorInventoryEntry{sensor_id,type,transport_ip,
  deploy_id}`), and A2 (`o2control/taps.py`) resolves each tap binding against it. This decision
  *removes the legacy alternative* (static `SENSOR_*` in `adapter.conf` + in-deploy tap emission)
  rather than adding a new mechanism.
- **The `te` stays sensor-agnostic; A2 owns discovery** (the user's chosen fork, 2026-07-13) —
  rejected the alternative of injecting a discovered sensor IP into the te deploy's `ClusterContext`
  at standup, because that keeps te↔sensor coupling and two tap code paths. The sensor-agnostic te
  matches "the engine stands up only a scenario's own components."
- **One additive piece is required** (not pure subtraction): today A2's `_scan_te_inventory`
  discovers *what is tappable* from the te deploy's own `install-ovs-mirror` ops — so A2 currently
  rides on top of inline emission. Removing inline emission strands that discovery source, so the te
  deploy must persist a **`record-tap-intent`** artifact (the dynamic-tap-resolved `(host,net,nic)`
  tappable set, no adapter action) for A2 to read instead. Proposed as a new `OP-MIR-005`.

**Why:**
- In the O2 model capture sensors are **standing O2 substrate** (A1), not per-scenario config. A
  static `SENSOR_SSH_HOST`/`SENSOR_TRANSPORT_IP` in `adapter.conf` is drift-prone (it caused GAP-012)
  and conceptually wrong — the sensor's identity should be *discovered* from the standing surface,
  which A1/A2 already do. Removing the static path closes GAP-012 by eliminating the dependency.

**Supersedes:** completes the O2-direction supersession of in-deploy tap emission already stated in
`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md` (§ "Surface-layer direction — O2": *"taps become a separate config
playout"*). The dynamic-tap slice's inline emission (`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_dynamic_tap.md`) is retired
by the work order, not by this entry.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_sensor_agnostic_te.md` — new, the build spec
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — GAP-012 annotated (resolution path)
- (on build) OP_CATALOG (+version), VALIDATOR (+version if rules change), dynamic_tap / o2_taps /
  o2 §6 briefs, ADAPTER, `adapter.conf.example`, CLAUDE.md

## 2026-07-13 — resource ownership tags (`te_`/`tap_`/`o2_`/`img_`): dev45 live-verify PASSED

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- Ran the gated `dev45` live-verify for `docs/WORK_ORDER_resource_tags.md` §5 — the last open item on that
  work order. **Result: PASS.** The tier-tagged naming + destroy-guard build (landed 2026-07-08) is now
  proven end-to-end on a live cluster.
- **Create side:** a tapped `tap-verify` deploy produced resources carrying the correct tier tags —
  `te_` zone `te7d90c8`, vnet `tev19f4c`, underlay bridge `tebr_7d90c8`, VMs `te-7d90c8-h-a`/`-h-b`;
  `tap_` source gretap `tapg_8fd2ab` + per-tap `tc` clsact mirrors (hooks `axrange-mirror-<vmid>.sh`)
  redirecting to that gretap. Verified directly on-cluster (`qm list`, `pvesh get /cluster/sdn/{zones,vnets}`,
  `ovs-vsctl show`, `ip link show type gretap`).
- **Destroy side:** `axrange destroy` swept all **8** `te_`/`tap_` resources of the deploy (0 not cleared);
  post-destroy the cluster showed no `te-*` VMs, empty SDN zones/vnets, no `tebr_*` bridge, no `tapg_*`
  gretap. The `img-*` golden templates (100/101/620) and unrelated VMs (`8100 ragx-dev`, `9000/9351/9353`)
  were **untouched** — blast-radius containment held. Idempotent re-destroy was clean (`absent`/`DESTROYED`).
- **Migration + template rename (§5) were non-events:** dev45 carried zero old-named (`axr`/`vn`/…) live
  resources (nothing to sweep), and its three axRange templates were already born `img-*` via `axrange
  image build` after the naming change landed — so no `qm set --name` migration was needed. Bindings
  resolve templates by `template_vmid` (not name), confirmed intact.

**Why:**
- Closes the only outstanding item on `WORK_ORDER_resource_tags.md`; the tags + destroy guard now carry a
  live end-to-end proof, not just unit/suite verification.

**Orthogonal findings (NOT tag defects; logged, not fixed here):**
- `verify-capture` requires `SENSOR_SSH_HOST` in `adapter.conf` to read a sensor's pcaps; unset in this
  run ⇒ the op failed (and halted `mirror-ready`) *after* every tagged resource was already created.
  Environmental — `tap-verify` has no standing capture sensor. No bearing on the tag proof.
- The **default-ON exporter agent** (`install-agent`, `scenario.default_agents` default-ON since
  2026-07-11) apt-installs and needs egress; the minimal `tap-verify` scenario has no router/NAT, so the
  agent timed out (600s) and retried. Logged as a product gap (`docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md`).

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_resource_tags.md` — status header → DONE
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md` §6 — status → LIVE-VERIFIED; work order marked fully closed
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — new gap for the default-ON-agent egress assumption
- `CLAUDE.md` — resource-tags live-verify note flipped from pending to verified

## 2026-07-11 — feat: the O2 build arc — A1 control, A2 taps, A3 attack/defense, A4 measurement, activity schedule executor

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code), EXCEPT the default-ON default-agents decision (A3
Stage A, §3 below), which is **Authority: user** (2026-07-11).

**What changed (all five `/dev` Pipeline work orders from the 2026-07-10 handoff, closing the O2
design arc into a built one):** implemented, code-reviewed, and integration-verified (**794 pytest
tests**, up from 583) — **NONE of the five are live-verified yet; every gated `dev45` live-verify
below is still PENDING**, alongside the pre-existing `WORK_ORDER_{resource_tags,sink_rename}.md`
live-migrations.

**⚠️ Operator action required:** `axrange/planner/state.py`'s `SCHEMA_VERSION` moved **2→3** (a new
`deploys.tier` column). Any `state.db` from before this change fails loud (`StateStoreSchemaError`)
under the new guard, same pattern as the earlier v1→v2 migration (CHANGELOG 2026-07-07) —
**re-initialize (delete/move) `state.db` on any cluster that ran deploys before 2026-07-11.**

**§1 — A1: O2 control (`axrange/o2control/`, `docs/WORK_ORDER_o2_control.md`).** The keystone: a
new submodule that stands up the standing O2 sensor surface as an `o2_`-tagged **persistent** deploy
by reusing the engine (planner+adapter+state) as a spec-executor (LES-037). `tier` is now a
deploy-scoped parameter threaded through plan/deploy/naming/state/executor/teardown (default `te`;
`o2control` passes `o2`) — the SCHEMA_VERSION bump above. New naming shape `o2z<5hex>` (SDN zone).
`planner/teardown.py`'s `_protected()` guard is refined so an `o2`-tiered destroy CAN sweep its own
`o2_` resources (`axrange o2 down`), while every `te`/`tap` destroy still can never touch `o2_`/
`img_` — a symmetric positive tier-name-shape gate added on the VM-name path too, not just the
existing range/`_protected` pair. New `latest_deploy_for_tier` tier-filtered discovery query (+
`deploys_by_tier` index) — the standing deploy is found by tag, not by scanning a capped recent-N
list (it's typically the OLDEST row). CLI `axrange o2 up|status|down`; API `POST/GET/DELETE
/api/o2/surface`.

**§2 — activity schedule executor (`docs/WORK_ORDER_schedule.md`, `docs/SLICE_ACTIVITY.md` §11).**
`Schedule.interval {min,max}` + un-reserved `Activity.seed` (additive IR — stays
`scenario_schema_version: 1`, the fabric/interval-additive precedent). The jittered cadence is a
**seeded Python sleep-loop**, NOT a systemd timer — `RandomizedDelaySec` isn't seedable, which would
break "same seed ⇒ same schedule" replay. Delivered via a written launcher script executed as
`ga.exec(vmid, ["bash", launch])` — the pkill-then-relaunch logic must live in that file, never
inlined into the invoking `sh -lc` command, because the inline command's own argv would contain the
scheduler path substring and self-match its own `pkill -f <scheduler>` (a real self-kill bug caught
during build). Per-activity `act-<id>` keying; overnight-window wrap handled; 1s interval floor.
Validator: **ACT-050 lifted** for seeded schedules (only warns when there's no `interval` to seed);
new **ACT-060** (interval shape) + **ACT-070** (seed-recommended).

**§3 — A2: tap config playout (`axrange/o2control/taps.py`, `docs/WORK_ORDER_taps.md`).** A tap-plan
spec `{target, bindings:[{tap, sensors}]}`; playout reuses the OP-MIR ops (keyed gretap + `tc`
chained-`mirred`, LES-011) via the engine executor; 1:N fan-out vs. per-tap exclusivity; a distinct
GRE key per (bridge, sensor); every resource `tap_`-tagged. Realized as a **separate `tap-<te>`
deploy**, so `destroy taps` is structurally scoped (an op-name-subset guard). `force_reset_ops`
makes every apply re-assert (Model-C no-op when unchanged; correct on narrow/revert — fixes a real
traffic-mis-routing bug a content-hashed op_id would otherwise mask, since the resume-skip executor
treats a completed op_id as done regardless of whether the underlying binding narrowed). A de-scope
pass drops removed sensors' gretaps and re-asserts the source mirror so it never dangles pointing at
a torn-down tunnel. **A TE destroy cascades its standalone tap-plan** (`cascade_destroy_for_te`,
called from `axrange destroy`) — this reconciles `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_taps.md` §5's "still cascades"
claim for the separate-deploy design. CLI `axrange o2 tap apply|destroy`; API `POST/DELETE
/api/o2/taps`. **Deferred, not silently dropped:** no `o2 tap status` verb (out of scope per the
work order); the **packetXpress** VM sensor type stays its own, separate design.

**§4 — A3: attack/defense — substrate agents + O2 servers (`docs/WORK_ORDER_attack_defense.md`).**
**Stage A** (`te_`, engine deploy): a new `overlays.adversary` IR block + `AdversaryEntry` (additive)
→ Sandcat (Caldera agent) install via new **`OP-OVL-070 install-agent`** (config-class over QGA,
mirroring `install-defense-agent`'s render/backend shape so that op stays defense-only); a
Prometheus node-exporter default; `axrange/overlays/default_agents.py` (the default-agent
registry). **`Scenario.default_agents: Optional[bool] = None` is DEFAULT-ON** — unset or explicit
`true` both mean baseline Wazuh defense + node-exporter on every `kind: host` TE node; explicit
`false` is the scenario-level opt-out; per-node `default_agents_off` scopes it further.
**This default-ON call is `Authority: user` (2026-07-11)**, not an implementation default. Dedup
against explicit `overlays.defense` entries covering the same tool (`hosts_with_explicit_defense_tool`,
LES-034-shaped — every entry's every `installed_on:` host, not just the first); the DC gets a
domain-scoped defense agent, correctly gated on `domain-ready[$domain]` the same way its authored
counterpart is (replicating the conditional-scope derivation, not just the emission). Adversary
targets are restricted to `kind: host` nodes (Sandcat installs over QGA — a router/switch target
would hang at deploy, not fail at validate). New validator rules **REF-220** (adversary entry has an
`id`), **REF-230** (every `targets:` entry resolves — a fan-out, every binding checked, LES-034),
**REF-240** (every resolved target is `kind: host`). Pass-3 router-egress gating extended to cover
the default agents too. **Stage B** (`o2_`, stood up by A1): Caldera + Wazuh-manager docker-compose
services under `o2/compose/` (+ `o2/caldera/Dockerfile`, built from a pinned `CALDERA_VERSION` git
tag — MITRE ships no published image; `--insecure` HTTP contact mode to match Sandcat's plain-HTTP
beacon origin; Wazuh manager on `1514/tcp`, the correct protocol for the Wazuh 4.x agent-enrollment
default, not the historical UDP; a `python3 -c urllib…` healthcheck, since `wget`/`curl` aren't
guaranteed on a slim base). Kept **human-gated** (not wired into `axrange o2 up`) — consistent with
the existing standalone Grafana/Loki/Prometheus stack and the engine-standalone boundary. Manual
playout only (no attack automation) — by design, per the A3 thesis. The `engine: atomic|caldera`
OP-ACT-compiling ladder from `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_adversary_overlays.md` remains **out of scope /
unbuilt**.

**§5 — A4: measurement & scoring (`docs/WORK_ORDER_measurement.md`).** `technique:` becomes a
free-form key on inline activity steps (additive — steps are already `dict[str,Any]`, no schema
class change) + `AdversaryEntry.techniques` (informational). Ground truth: a
`[axr-truth] run=<id> technique=<T> host=<h> ts=<..>` marker is emitted per technique-labeled step
by `render_activity_runner`, shipped to Loki via a new shared `_render_loki_tail_shipper` helper —
the existing sensor `export.ship: loki` shipper was refactored to reuse it, behavior-preserving
(reuses `AdapterConfig.loki_url`; a round-trip test guards the render-then-reparse wire format so
the two sides can't drift silently). A run-id is minted per execution. New `axrange/measurement/`
leaf subpackage (`join_coverage` — truth × detections joined on technique+host+Δ-window;
`technique_for_detection` — self-label off a Wazuh/Suricata rule's own ATT&CK id, else a small
`FALLBACK_RULE_TECHNIQUE_MAP`) — the engine never imports it (LES-025). A Grafana coverage dashboard
(`o2/grafana/dashboards/attack-coverage.json`) shows the truth timeline + detection timeline +
per-run-id technique-coverage matrix. **Out of scope (follow-ons):** the automated scorer
(`o2control`), the replay-delta (run1 vs run2 — depends on the seeded activity schedule, §2), and an
ATT&CK-Navigator export.

**Why:** closes the O2 design arc (A1→A4 + A2 + schedule, `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2*.md`,
2026-07-08→07-10) into a built one. An operator can now stand up a standing sensor surface, bind a
deployed target environment's taps to it independently of both, get default red/blue
instrumentation baked into every TE host, and see a measurable per-technique detection-coverage view
— the "validate defenses" proof the platform exists for, still gated on a live cluster run.

**Affected docs:** `CLAUDE.md` (Current State/Implementation Status, counts); `docs/IR_SCHEMA.md`
(`overlays.adversary`/`AdversaryEntry` §8.4, `scenario.default_agents`/`node.default_agents_off`
§2/§3.2, `schedule.interval`/`Activity.seed` §5.5/§5.6, `technique:` §5.3); `docs/OP_CATALOG.md`
(`OP-OVL-070 install-agent`, 37→38 op types); `docs/VALIDATOR.md` (REF-220/230/240, ACT-060/070,
98→103 rules, per-category table re-sum per LES-050); `docs/ADAPTER.md` (tier-as-deploy-parameter,
`o2z<5hex>` naming, the SCHEMA_VERSION 2→3 note); `docs/CONVENTIONS.md` §2 (the `o2z<5hex>` shape +
tier-as-deploy-param); `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md` §6/§7 (own-tier teardown refinement, containment/
cascade realized); `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_control.md`, `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_taps.md`,
`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_attack_defense.md`, `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_measurement.md`,
`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_adversary_overlays.md`, `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_defense_overlays.md` (marked
BUILT/REALIZED); `docs/SLICE_ACTIVITY.md` §11 (marked BUILT, systemd-timer correction);
`docs/SLICE_OVERLAY.md` (adversary landing note); the five `docs/WORK_ORDER_*.md` (STATUS: DONE
headers); `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` (GAP-014, exporter-dedup case-sensitivity).

**Affected code:** `axrange/o2control/` (new: `control.py`, `manifest.py`, `compose.py`,
`taps.py`), `axrange/overlays/` (new: `default_agents.py`), `axrange/measurement/` (new:
`coverage.py`, `detections.py`), `axrange/ir/schema.py` (`ScheduleInterval`, `Schedule.interval`,

## 2026-07-10 — design: A2 — the tap config playout (`tap_` tier) — closes the O2 design arc

**Authority:** user (this design session)

**What changed (design, not built):** `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_taps.md` — the last open O2 gap. The `tap_`
tier's own lifecycle: a standalone **tap-plan** (`tap → [sensors]` bindings) binds a deployed TE's
tappable interfaces to standing O2 sensors, reusing the engine's **OP-MIR** ops via an **O2-control
playout** against A1's sensor inventory. Independently **apply/destroy/redeployable** — re-point a TE's
taps at a different sensor config without redeploying the TE or the sensors.

**The binding matrix** expresses both operator-named configs: **1:N fan-out** (one tap → many sensors,
chained `mirred` → N keyed gretaps) and **per-tap exclusivity** (each tap → its own sensor,
per-(bridge,sensor) key). **VM sensor types** (packetXpress, tcpdump/suricata/zeek) are interchangeable
gretap endpoints (the gretap terminates in-guest — a VM, not a container). Fan-out **duplicates** on the
transport wire (a surfaced capacity/MTU cost).

**Forks (user → recs):** F1 standalone tap-plan, IR-defaulted (the `tap:` markers supply the tappable
set); F2 reuse OP-MIR via a playout mini-run; F3 `tap → [sensors]` list; F4 `tap_`-scoped idempotent
apply/destroy (the standalone `destroy taps` verb lands here — was flagged out-of-scope in
`WORK_ORDER_resource_tags.md` §7, blocked on this).

**Open:** the **packetXpress** VM sensor type (its own design); the tap-plan schema; source- vs
sensor-side fan-out at high width. Depends on A1 (inventory) + the shipped `tap_` naming.

**Affected docs:** `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_taps.md` (new); pointers in `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md` §7 + `CLAUDE.md`.

- `python scripts/doc-stats.py --check` → clean ("No counter drift in the policed docs.").

  `scenario_ai_research/**`).

  paths (`scenarios/`, `observability/`, `sink/`, `DESIGN_BRIEF_ai_scenario_sidecar.md`).
- Counters re-derived from source, not copied cross-doc: **98 validator rules** (91 IR-side across
  10 categories + 7 CLU, with the FAB category = 8 rules — see the VALIDATOR.md structural catch
  below), **37 op types** (31 action/wait + 6 barriers), **583 pytest tests**.

**The one real structural catch:** `docs/VALIDATOR.md`'s headline text claimed 98 total rules, but
its own §5.1–§5.10 per-category tables summed to only **90** — the FAB category (8 rules,
`FAB-010..080`, already ratified in `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_fabric.md` §9 on 2026-06-29) had never gotten
a rule table of its own, only a mention in the prose count. A new **§5.11 FAB table** (8 rows,
transcribed from `DESIGN_BRIEF_fabric.md` §9) was added, the worked-example footer's "90 rule
definitions (83 IR-side + 7 CLU)" was corrected to "98 rule definitions (91 IR-side + 7 CLU)", and
the `§5.1–§5.10` cross-reference was updated to `§5.1–§5.11`. This is exactly the class of drift
`scripts/doc-stats.py` cannot catch — it polices whole-document headline numbers against `list_rules()`,
not a doc's own internal table-sum-vs-headline consistency — so it was only caught by hand-summing
the category tables during the audit.

**Why:** Six weeks of O2-direction, tier-tag, sink-rename, and archetype-count decisions had
accumulated real drift (per-doc detail in `docs/WORK_ORDER_doc_audit.md` §3's probe) across docs
that `doc-stats.py`'s automated policing doesn't reach (prose "not built" claims, un-policed
counters, cross-ref integrity, genre-appropriate pointer vs. rewrite). A full audit closes that gap
in one pass and produces the audit-table record so future sessions can trust `docs/*.md` again
without re-verifying it themselves.

**Affected docs:** all 41 `docs/*.md` (table above); this CHANGELOG entry; `docs/LESSONS.md` (new
LES-050); `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` (gitignored, not committed).

## 2026-07-09 — terminology: retire "sink" — appliance tokens (`capture`/`gre-cap`) + full prose sweep

**Authority:** user (the rename scheme + `docs/WORK_ORDER_sink_rename.md`, 2026-07-09); implementation (Claude Code) for the two-stage build.

**What changed:** completes the `sink`→`sensor`/`capture` retirement that the code-level `SINK_*`→`SENSOR_*` rename (the 2026-06-23 "sensors" design entry, CHANGELOG) started but deliberately deferred (the 2026-07-08 physical-`o2/`-restructure entry, CHANGELOG, scoped the appliance's internal tokens + pervasive prose out explicitly). Two stages, per `docs/WORK_ORDER_sink_rename.md`:

- **Stage 1 — appliance tokens + code defaults** (`o2/**`, `axrange/**` comments/defaults, `pyproject.toml`, `scripts/*.py`):
  - Runtime config path `/etc/capture-sink/` **and** a pre-existing drifted variant `/etc/capture-o2/tcpdump-sensor/` (found in different scripts) unified to **`/etc/capture/`**, config file **`capture.conf`**; runtime state dir `/var/lib/capture-sink/`→`/var/lib/capture/`; doc dir `/usr/share/doc/capture-sink`→`/usr/share/doc/capture`.
  - Env override `CAPTURE_SINK_CONF`→**`CAPTURE_CONF`** (read by `capture-start`/`capture-watchdog`/`capture-healthcheck`).
  - Adapter-side capture-iface default `gre-sink`→**`gre-cap`** (`adapter_proxmox/config.py`, `config_exec/backend.py` fallback) — now matching the tcpdump-sensor's own already-documented `gre-cap` example (`o2/tcpdump-sensor/README.md`, `docs/tcpdump-sensor/README.md`); the two sides previously disagreed on the default device name.
  - o2 observability env family `SINK_HOST`/`SINK_TEXTFILE_DIR`/`SINK_CONF`/`SINK_HEALTHCHECK_BIN`/`SINK_NODE_EXPORTER_PORT` (+ their `__SINK_*__` sed placeholders in `prometheus.yml`) → **`CAPTURE_*`**; Prometheus `job_name`/`component` `capture-sink-health`/`capture-sink`→`capture-health`/`capture`; the dashboard's `uid` `axr-capture-sink-health`→`axr-capture-health` and `tags` entry `capture-sink`→`capture`.
  - `pyproject.toml` package-exclude `"sink*"`→`"o2*"`; `scripts/sdn_preflight.py` flag `--sink-ip`→`--sensor-ip`; the lesson/commit **area tag** `sink`→`o2` in `scripts/inject_lessons.py`'s `_MODULES` set (matching `CLAUDE.md`'s already-`o2` Git Policy area-prefix list).
  - **Note on scope:** several of the work order's listed renames (`sink.conf.example`→`capture.conf.example`, the health-exporter script, the systemd units, the dashboard file) turned out to already be at their target filenames from the 2026-07-08 physical-`o2/`-restructure commit — this pass closed out their remaining internal `sink` content (env-var names, comments, doc-path references, the dashboard's `uid`/`tags`) rather than re-renaming already-correct paths. The one genuine filename rename Stage 1 itself needed was folded into the post-review fixups below.
  - **Bonus latent-bug fix:** `o2/tcpdump-sensor/install.sh` created its doc directory at `/usr/share/doc/capture-sink` but copied the README to a *different, never-created* path (`/usr/share/doc/capture-o2/tcpdump-sensor/README.md`, missing `-D`) — the README-copy step would fail on every fresh install. Both normalized to `/usr/share/doc/capture/`.
- **Stage 2 — context-sensitive prose sweep** (~346 occurrences across `docs/*.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `target-environments/**`, `tests/fixtures/**`): "the sink"/"capture sink" (the appliance) → "the tcpdump-sensor"/"the capture appliance"; "sink" (receiver/role) → "sensor"; "capture sink" (the plane) → "capture"; "sink-network-sensor Suricata" → "appliance-adjacent network sensor Suricata". The version-locked specs (`OP_CATALOG`/`IR_SCHEMA`/`PLANNER`/`ADAPTER`) carried **stale prose only** (the code was already `sensor`) — aligned with **no schema version bump**.
- **Post-review fixups:** `git mv tests/test_sink_routing.py tests/test_sensor_routing.py` (a stranded filename a content-only grep can't catch — 583 tests still collect under the new name); restored the literal `` `SINK_TRANSPORT_IP` `` in `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-012 as a diagnostic dead-key (an operator greps exactly this in a stale `adapter.conf`); un-quoted a loose CHANGELOG citation in `docs/IR_SCHEMA.md` and `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md` (both had paraphrased *inside* a quoted historical title — fixed to a by-date descriptive pointer instead); fixed a pre-existing "network-sensor sensor" double-word in `axrange/config_exec/render.py`, caught by the same pass.

**Acceptance state (grep-verified 2026-07-09 — this is the allowlist for future greps):** zero live "sink" in-scope except six deliberate carve-outs:
- `docs/CHANGELOG.md` — frozen history, never rewritten (includes this entry, which necessarily narrates the retired term).
- `docs/WORK_ORDER_sink_rename.md` — the spec that names the retired term, filename included (marked done below, not deleted).
- `scenario_ai_research/**` — out-of-scope sidecar-branch content.
- `docs/CONVENTIONS.md:34` — a meta-reference listing `sink`/`sidecar` as examples of superseded terms.
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-012 — the literal legacy `SINK_TRANSPORT_IP` key an operator greps in a stale `adapter.conf`.
- `docs/LESSONS.md` LES-046..049 (added by this same pass) — lesson bodies describing the retirement itself are explicitly permitted to name the historical term; that's the subject of the lesson, not residue.

Also note: `gtcap0` (the network-sensor decap-iface *example* in `docs/SLICE_OVERLAY.md`/LES-012) was **intentionally left alone** — it was never `gre-sink`, so it's out of scope for this rename; don't "fix" it later.

**Verified:** full pytest suite **583 passed** (unchanged baseline — no counter moved: 37 op types, 98 catalog rules, 583 tests); `python scripts/doc-stats.py --check` clean; acceptance grep clean modulo the six carve-outs above.

**Deferred:** the **dev45 appliance live-migration** — `/etc/capture-sink/`→`/etc/capture/`, `gre-sink`→`gre-cap`, the systemd/env-var renames strand existing installs until reinstalled or migrated (LES-044 shape; the actual live-verifier pass should record its own `LES-NNN` per `docs/WORK_ORDER_sink_rename.md` §4 when it runs) — is deferred by the user, same as the resource-ownership-tags dev45 live-verify (CHANGELOG 2026-07-08) remains deferred.

**Not changed:** any version-locked schema (`OP_CATALOG`/`IR_SCHEMA`/`PLANNER`/`ADAPTER` prose only, no version bump); the `wip/ai-scenario-sidecar` branch name (an unrelated "sidecar"); CHANGELOG history.

**Affected docs:** `docs/WORK_ORDER_sink_rename.md` (marked DONE), `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md` §7 (terminology-cleanup bullet marked done), `docs/LESSONS.md` (LES-046..049, new), and the ~50 `docs/*.md` files + `CLAUDE.md` + `target-environments/**` + `tests/fixtures/**` swept in Stage 2 (file list: `docs/WORK_ORDER_sink_rename.md` §3).

**Affected code:** `o2/**` (installer, systemd units, exporters, dashboards, compose, config examples — 24 files), `axrange/adapter_proxmox/config.py`, `axrange/config_exec/backend.py`, `axrange/config_exec/render.py`, `axrange/ir/schema.py`, `axrange/ops/registry.py`, `axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py`, `axrange/sensors/registry.py`, `axrange/validator/catalog.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `scripts/inject_lessons.py`, `scripts/sdn_preflight.py`, `adapter.conf.example`, `tests/test_adapter_proxmox.py`, `tests/test_dynamic_tap.py`, `tests/test_teardown.py`, `tests/test_sensor_routing.py` (renamed from `test_sink_routing.py`), `tests/fixtures/ir/valid_two_sensor/scenario.yaml`. 77 files changed total (755 insertions, 719 deletions).

**Links:** `docs/WORK_ORDER_sink_rename.md`; the 2026-06-23 "sensors" design entry (CHANGELOG — the code-level rename this completes); the 2026-07-08 physical-`o2/`-restructure entry (CHANGELOG — the deferral this entry closes).

## 2026-07-09 — decision: keep "Scenario IR" — formalize scenario (spec) vs. target environment (deployment), do NOT rename

**Authority:** user (option C)

**What changed:** resolved the long-deferred "Scenario IR concept rename." **Decision: do not rename.**
Instead formalize the distinction in `docs/CONVENTIONS.md`:
- **Scenario (IR)** = the authored spec (the versioned YAML recipe); the version-locked contract term
  stays **"Scenario IR"**, and code/API keep `scenario` identifiers (`/api/scenarios`, `scenario_id`,
  `scenarios_dir`).
- **Target environment (TE)** = the deployed instance — the running, agent-instrumented world a
  scenario stands up (the `te_` tier + the `target-environments/` folder).
- A scenario **defines** a target environment; `target-environments/` is the library of scenario IRs.

**Why not rename:** the IR is a *designed exercise* (carries `activity` + `overlays.adversary/defense`,
not just a static world); "scenario" isn't a superseded term (unlike `sink`/`sidecar`); and a full
rename is **~1,400 occurrences** that would **break the version-locked schema + the `/api/scenarios`
contract** the SPA consumes — cost far above the clarity gain. Supersedes the earlier "rename deferred
until content sheds attack/defense" notes (the O2 redesign kept that content in the substrate IR).

**Affected docs:** `docs/CONVENTIONS.md` (new "Scenario vs. target environment" section) +
`docs/IR_SCHEMA.md` (a pointer note under Status).

  `sink.conf`→`capture.conf`, `CAPTURE_SINK_CONF`→`CAPTURE_CONF`, **`gre-sink`→`gre-cap`** (unifies with

  renamed files; `sensor_capture_iface` default moves in lockstep.
- **Stage 2 (prose sweep, context-sensitive):** "the sink"/"capture sink" → **tcpdump-sensor** (appliance)
  / **sensor** (receiver) / **capture** (plane); align the stale `sink` prose in the version-locked specs

**Flags:** the appliance-token rename **strands live installs** (LES-044-shape hazard — reinstall on
dev45, record a LES); grep gate = zero `sink` outside CHANGELOG history + the unrelated
`wip/ai-scenario-sidecar` branch. ~600 occurrences / ~75 files — the largest terminology sweep yet.

## 2026-07-09 — design: A4 — measurement & scoring (ATT&CK coverage, purple-team Tier 4)

**Authority:** user (this design session)

**What changed (design, not built):** `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_measurement.md` fills the
**explicitly-missing Tier-4 detection scoring** (`DESIGN_BRIEF_defense_overlays.md`: *"did the defense
catch the attack? ❌ missing"*). A4 = an **ATT&CK-technique coverage matrix + %** per attack run.

**Data spine:** both sides land in **Loki**, joined by `technique + host + [ts, ts+Δ]`:
- **Attack truth** — the runner emits one `[axr-truth]` marker per step (`run-id`/`technique`/`host`/
  `ts`); the only new stream. Activity steps carry an ATT&CK **`technique:`** label; Caldera/Atomic
  supply native mapping via their operation report.
- **Detections** — Suricata/Wazuh/audit → Loki (shipped, via Alloy); self-label with ATT&CK where the
  rule carries it, else a rule→technique fallback map.

**Decisions:** manual-first **Grafana coverage dashboard** (automated scorer in `o2control` = follow-on);
**single-run coverage now, stamped with `run-id`** so the **replay-delta** (run1 pre-defense vs run2
post-defense) is a clean next step. **Load-bearing tie-in:** the delta is only meaningful if RUN1/RUN2
run the *identical* attack — guaranteed by **seeded activity** (`SLICE_ACTIVITY.md` §11). So A4 + seeded
activity = the measurable "validate defenses" proof the platform exists for.

**Status:** small new mechanism (the `technique:` label + ground-truth marker to Loki + the Grafana
dashboard); reuses Alloy→Loki + Grafana + the A3 detections. No `te_`/engine change — A4 is an O2-side
read. **Follow-ons:** automated scorer, replay-delta, ATT&CK-Navigator export.

**Affected docs:** `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_measurement.md` (new); pointers in `CLAUDE.md`,
`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_defense_overlays.md` (Tier-4 closed).

## 2026-07-09 — design: activity `schedule:` executor — windows + seeded jittered cadence + multiple-per-node (SLICE_ACTIVITY §11)

**Authority:** user

**What changed (design, not built):** `docs/SLICE_ACTIVITY.md` §11 specs the deferred `schedule:`
piece of the O2 activity model (`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_attack_defense.md`). Three capabilities:
1. **Multiple activities per node** — already legal IR (ACT-040 caps one host-binding *per activity*,
   not one activity per host); each compiles to a **per-activity runner keyed by activity id**
   (`act-<id>`), no collision.
2. **Windowed** scheduling — the existing `Schedule` `days`/`start`/`end`/`once` fields (when it may run).
3. **Seeded jittered cadence** — new `schedule.interval: {min, max}` (durations) fires at
   `random(min,max)` within the window for realism.

**Load-bearing decision:** the jitter is drawn from a **seeded RNG** using `Activity.seed` (today
reserved — ACT-050 rejects it; this gives it its purpose). **Same seed ⇒ same schedule** — realism
*and* reproducibility, without which the O2 replay loop (replay identical activity after adding
defense) can't attribute a detection delta. Compiles to a per-activity seeded runner / systemd timer
with `RandomizedDelaySec`; no op-catalog signature change (render/backend + two schema fields).

**Affected docs:** `docs/SLICE_ACTIVITY.md` §11 (new) + §2/§3 deferral notes now point at it.

## 2026-07-09 — design: A3 — attack/defense as O2 servers + substrate agents (manual playout)

**Authority:** user (this design session)

**What changed (design, not built):** `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_attack_defense.md` settles how red/blue
lives in the O2 world. **Thesis:** the TE substrate is a living, agent-instrumented *world*; the
attack/defense/telemetry **servers live in O2**; red/blue **playout is operator-driven, manual for
now** (syncing automation is future).

**Closed taxonomy (converged over the session):**
- **`overlays` = identity + data + defense + adversary.** Defense is *retained* as an overlay (blue
  **host** agents on TE nodes); the old catch-all `overlays.defense` dissolves (host → agent, network
  → O2 sensors).
- **`activity`** = TE-internal automated behavior — spanning **benign traffic AND scripted attacks**
  (the AD kill chain **stays** `activity`, no migration) + schedule + tools.
- **Attack is dual-mode:** internal `activity` (engine-run, deterministic) **or** external Caldera
  (manual, interactive). `overlays.adversary` (Sandcat) only needed for the Caldera path.
- **Three default agent→server pairs (instrumentation spine):** Prometheus exporter→**Prometheus
  server**; **Sandcat** (`overlays.adversary`)→**Caldera server**; **Wazuh agent**
  (`overlays.defense`)→**Wazuh server**. O2 substrate gains the Caldera + **Wazuh** servers.

**Supersedes-in-direction:** the "emit into the deploy DAG" assumption of
`DESIGN_BRIEF_adversary_overlays.md` + `DESIGN_BRIEF_defense_overlays.md`, and this brief's own earlier
A3 sketch (attack/defense as IR content O2 *executes*).

**Status:** near-real (Wazuh host agent, Prometheus server, `activity`); new/design-forward (Caldera +
Wazuh servers as O2 components via A1, Sandcat install, the default-agent-set mechanism); deferred
(playout automation; **A4** measurement & scoring).

**Affected docs:** `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_attack_defense.md` (new); pointers in `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md`,
`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_{adversary,defense}_overlays.md`, `CLAUDE.md`.

## 2026-07-08 — resource ownership tags: implementation (`te_`/`tap_`/`o2_`/`img_` naming + destroy protect-guard)

**Authority:** user (the tagging scheme, per the 2026-07-08 design entries below); implementation (Claude Code) for the build and the protect-guard extension beyond the work order's minimum ask.

**What changed:**
- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/naming.py` producers now emit **tier-tagged** deterministic names (function names unchanged, only outputs): SDN zone `zone_id_for_plan` → `te<6hex>`; SDN vnet `vnet_id_for_net` → `tev<5hex>`; underlay/per-net bridge `underlay_bridge_for_plan`/`bridge_name_for_net` → `tebr_<6hex>`/`tebr_<sanitized>`; VM `vm_name_for_host` → `te-<6hex>-<host>`; source gretap `gre_port_for_bridge[_sensor]` → `tapg_<6hex>`; per-tap mirror `mirror_name_for_vmid` → `tapm-<vmid>`; golden template `img_vm_name_for_image` (new) → `img-<image-slug>`.
- `is_axr_*` predicates fully replaced by `is_te_*`/`is_tap_*`; new `is_o2_*`/`is_img_*` predicates + `is_o2`/`is_img` aggregate guards. New `o2_vnet_id_for_component`/`o2_bridge_name_for_component`/`o2_vm_name_for_component` producers are defined for the `o2` tier's shape but **not called by any live emission path** — the O2 control layer that will actually create `o2_*` resources is separate, unbuilt work (`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md` §3/§7).
- Tier mutual-exclusivity is structural: every regex is fully anchored with a disjoint literal prefix/position (e.g. `tev<hex>` vs `te<hex>` differ at index 2 — `v` is never a hex digit) — see the `naming.py` module docstring for the full argument; `test_adapter_proxmox.py` round-trips every producer against every predicate.
- `bridge_name_for_net` gains an empty-net-id `"x"` fallback so a degenerate `net_id` can't sanitize to a bare `tebr_` that its own `is_te_bridge` predicate would then reject (code-review finding).
- `planner/teardown.py` gains a hard `_protected()` guard: refuses + records a `guard-violation` on any `is_o2`/`is_img` match, checked BEFORE the positive `te`/`tap` shape-matches, applied uniformly across **every** resource kind — gretap, vnet, zone, bridge, **and the VM path** (new `vm_name()` added to the `TeardownBackend` protocol + `ProxmoxTeardown.vm_name()`; mirror-snippet cleanup is gated by a `protected_vmids` set so a protected VM's snippets are skipped too). This is broader than the work order's literal ask (which scoped the guard to the non-VM resource kinds) — extended to the VM path as defense-in-depth, since a te-VMID-range false-positive would otherwise be the only thing standing between a `te` destroy and an `o2`/`img` VM. The `te`→`tap` cascade remains structural and unchanged (tap ops are ops of the `te` deploy). The te-VMID range check remains the secondary VM guard.
- `imagebuild/builder.py:_vm_name` now delegates to `naming.img_vm_name_for_image`; newly-built golden templates get `img-<slug>` names. Version is no longer embedded in the name (accepted as a parameter, unused) — multiple versions of one image share the golden name and are distinguished by VMID, not name.
- `adapter_proxmox/rest.py`'s live OVS tap-reassert shell scan updated `grep '^vbr_'` → `grep '^tebr_'` (a hardcoded prefix string outside `naming.py`'s direct callers — see LES-045).
- Tests updated (`test_adapter_proxmox.py`, `test_teardown.py`, `test_planner_executor.py`, `test_sink_routing.py`) + added (protect-guard refusal cases, producer/predicate round-trips, tier mutual-exclusivity). Suite: **583 pytest tests**, all green.

**Why:** makes `destroy` blast-radius intrinsic (sweep by tier tag + `deploy_id`) instead of shape-matched, and makes the TE/tap/O2/img seam explicit in every resource name — `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md` §6.

**Does NOT reverse a ratified decision** (no `Supersedes:`) — this *implements* the design already ratified in the two 2026-07-08 design entries below (`docs/WORK_ORDER_resource_tags.md`).

**Migration hazard (recorded, not yet acted on):** any deploy still on a cluster under the old `axr`/`vn`/`vbr_`/`gre_`/`mir-` names is **not** recognized by the new `te_`/`tap_` guards — a `destroy` against it will report success having swept nothing, leaking the old-named resources. Before deploying this change to a live cluster, destroy all pre-rename deploys with the **pre-rename** code (or use a clean cluster) and record what was swept (`docs/WORK_ORDER_resource_tags.md` §5). See LES-044.

**Status:** implemented, code-reviewed, unit/suite-verified. **Live-verify on `dev45` — including the `axr_*`→`img-*` template rename — is pending**, gated per the work order (§5 is a gated live-verifier stage, not run in this pass).

**Affected docs:** `docs/ADAPTER.md` §4 (new Model-C naming table), `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md` §6 (marked implemented), `CLAUDE.md` (pytest count 583; O2 blast-radius-tiers blockquote), `docs/LESSONS.md` (LES-043..045; LES-028 example refreshed).

**Affected code:** `axrange/adapter_proxmox/naming.py`, `axrange/planner/teardown.py`, `axrange/adapter_proxmox/teardown.py`, `axrange/adapter_proxmox/rest.py`, `axrange/imagebuild/builder.py`, `axrange/cli/main.py`, `tests/test_adapter_proxmox.py`, `tests/test_planner_executor.py`, `tests/test_sink_routing.py`, `tests/test_teardown.py`.

## 2026-07-08 — design: the O2 control component (`axrange/o2control/`) — a thin engine client

**Authority:** user (forks F1/F2/F3), decomposition Claude Code.

**What changed (design, not built):** `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_control.md` designs the linchpin of the
O2 surface layer — the component that stands up the standing observability/orchestration surface.

**Thesis:** **not a second orchestrator.** The engine already knows how to deploy sensors (today via
an in-scenario `sensors:` block → `OP-MIR-050`); the O2 control component **reuses the engine
(planner+adapter+state) as a spec executor**, feeding it a surface spec that lands as a **persistent
`o2_`-tagged deploy**. It only owns *who triggers standup* and *the standing lifecycle*, not new
deploy mechanics.

**Forks resolved (user → my recommendations):**
- **F1 = (b)** — logic in a new **`axrange/o2control/`** submodule (engine-consuming, stays in the
  `axrange/` package), exposed by thin `api/` endpoints + `cli/` verbs (config_exec pattern). Distinct
  from the non-Python top-level `o2/` appliance dir.
- **F2 = generic executor** — refines `DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md` §1: the engine executes any spec (TE =
  `te_`, O2 surface = `o2_`); engine stays standalone (O2 calls it, not vice-versa).
- **F3 = reuse `sensors:`** — the surface manifest reuses the ratified `sensors:` IR shape; no new schema.

**Scope:** A1 = compose surface spec → stand up (`o2_` persistent deploy) → inventory/status → explicit
teardown. **Follow-ons (plug into A1's inventory):** A2 tap playout, A3 attack/defense playout, the
engine→O2 migration staging.

**Cross-dependency flagged:** the **tier tag becomes a deploy-scoped parameter** (default `te_`;
`o2control` passes `o2_`) threaded into `naming.py` — refines `docs/WORK_ORDER_resource_tags.md`.

**Affected docs:** `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2_control.md` (new), `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md` §1 (refinement).

## 2026-07-08 — design: resource ownership tags — three blast-radius tiers (`te_*` / `tap_*` / `o2_*`)

**Authority:** user

**What changed (contract, not code yet):** recorded in `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md` §6 a tier-tag model
for every engine/control-created resource, so `destroy` blast-radius is *intrinsic* (sweep by tag +
id) rather than shape-matched (`is_axr_*`). Three tiers:
- **`te_*`** — a target-environment deploy (zones, vnets, VLAN underlay bridges, VMs).
- **`tap_*`** — the tap-binding (per-tap mirrors, source-side gretap netdevs) — the seam between a
  TE and an O2 sensor; its own tier, neither `te` nor `o2`.
- **`o2_*`** — standing O2 surface (standing sensor/sink VMs, their transport vnets/bridges, the
  sensor decap iface).

**Cascade / containment:** `tap ⊂ te` for teardown (a `te` destroy also removes its taps); `tap` is
**independently** destroyable + redeployable against a running TE + standing sensors (touches neither
`te_*` nor `o2_*`); `o2` is orthogonal (never removed by a `te`/`tap` destroy). Resolves the earlier
"is the mirror/gretap `te` or `o2`?" question: it's `tap`.

**Status:** the standalone tap lifecycle needs the **tap config playout (not designed yet** — §7). The
`te_*`/`o2_*`/`tap_*` **naming tags + `te`→`tap` destroy cascade** are implementable now (supersede the
`axr`-prefix + `is_axr_*` shape-matching in `naming.py`/`teardown.py`) — pending go-ahead.

**Affected docs:** `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md` §6 (new) + §7 (taps deep-dive references the tiers).

  operator flag pointing at any dir. The `/api/scenarios` route name and the version-locked
  **"Scenario IR"** schema term are **unchanged** — those stay "scenario" until the IR content
  sheds attack/defense (the O2 refactor), so the schema is churned once, not twice.

**Why:**
- Aligns the folder with the O2 direction (`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md`): as attack/defense +
  observability move to the O2 surface, the deployable IR becomes the *target environment* you
  stand up and attack. Chose `target-environments/` over bare `targets/` — `targets` already means
  the cluster deploy-target nodes (`GET /api/targets`, `TargetListDTO`).

**Verification:** all scenarios `axrange validate` PASS at the new path; full suite **576 passed**.

**Affected docs:** `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md`, and the path-reference files listed above.

## 2026-07-08 — design: O2 — the Observability-&-Orchestration surface layer (TARGET, documented)

**Authority:** user

**What changed (direction, not code):**
- Recorded the **O2** target architecture in `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md`: three separated
  lifecycles — the **design plane** (`scenarios/`/`aiscenario/`), the **standalone engine**
  (`axrange/`, plan→deploy→destroy of the scenario-internal components only), and **O2**, the
  **surface layer** stood up separately on request. O2 has two halves — **orchestration/control**
  (UI/canvas + a control component) and **observability** (sensors `tcpdump`/`suricata`/`zeek`,
  sink, Grafana/Loki/Prometheus) — with Grafana and the control-plane API bridging both.
- **Engine stays standalone; O2 is the surfaces around it.** "Surface" = either a standalone
  appliance dir (`sink/`, `observability/`, relocatable under a future `o2/`) or engine-*consuming*
  code that stays in the package (the API). `axrange/sensors/` is neither — it is a registry the
  engine depends on, so it stays until sensor standup moves out of the engine, then moves with it.
- **Sensors become standing O2 components** stood up separately by the control layer (like the
  sink), not VMs the engine deploys per scenario. **Attack/defense** becomes O2 playout against a
  standing target environment. **Taps** become a separate config playout (deep dive **deferred**).

**Why:**
- A range is two systems: the *environment under test* (engine) and the *instrumentation/control
  around it* (O2). Coupling them re-provisions observability inside every scenario. The sink
  already works the standing way; O2 generalizes that pattern.

**Supersedes (as *direction* — current implementation stands until the refactor lands):**
- 2026-06-23 "design: sensors — unified typed in-scenario capture nodes" + Phase B/C — sensors
  move from in-scenario VMs to standing O2 surface components.
- 2026-06-19 "dynamic taps + capture heartbeat" — tap emission moves from inside the scenario
  deploy to a separate config playout.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_o2.md` (new), `CLAUDE.md` (orientation pointer)

**Follow-ons:** taps deep-dive; folder rename `scenarios/` → `target-environments/` (TE), with the
version-locked "Scenario IR" schema-concept rename deferred; the staged engine→O2 refactor.

## 2026-07-08 — feat(ui): SPA canvas layout ported as 7th topology-renderer bake-off entry

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code) — implements a user-ratified design
(`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_ui.md` §12.5.1, Authority: user, 2026-07-07).

**What changed:**
- Implemented DESIGN_BRIEF_ui.md §12.5.1's six touchpoints, 1–4 plus the jest test (5–6 —
  Grafana build/view and deleting the untracked `ui/` working-tree dir — are left for a gated
  live-verify/cleanup step, not this pass).
- New `observability/grafana/plugins/axellio-axrange-control-panel/src/topology/layouts/canvas.ts`
  — `canvasLayout(topo): Record<string, Pos>`, a faithful port of the retired SPA's
  `computeLayout` (recovered from the annotated tag `retired/ui-viewer-spa:ui/src/canvas/layout.ts`)
  translated to the plugin's `Topology`/`Pos` types: a deterministic, dependency-free bipartite
  layout — networks placed on a horizontal spine, non-network nodes positioned at the centroid of
  their attached network(s), orphan nodes parked in a separate row. Confirmed (per §12.5.1's
  "VERIFY FIRST" instruction) that the original algorithm computes purely from topology, not from
  persisted drag/canvas coordinates — nothing to re-derive.
- Registered in `registry.ts`: `{ value: 'canvas', label: 'SPA Canvas Layout (original ui/ port)' }`
  added to `RENDERER_OPTIONS`, `canvas: makeGraphRenderer(canvasLayout)` added to `RENDERERS`.
- Manifest entry appended to `observability/grafana/topology-eval.manifest.json`
  (`key: canvas`, matching the registry `value`, `enabled: true`).
- Eval dashboard regenerated via `gen-topology-dashboards.py` →
  `observability/grafana/dashboards/topology-eval/canvas.json` (loaded, `topologyRenderer: canvas`
  pinned; nothing hand-edited).
- New jest suite `canvas.test.ts` — 8 cases (determinism, spine/upper-row placement, network
  centroid, orphan handling, switch-node coverage, empty topology) — all green. `npm ci` +
  `npm run test:ci` for the plugin passed (8/8 new, existing suite unaffected).
- Code review: CONFIRMED CORRECT, no must-fix — faithful port, edge-direction verified against
  `layered.ts`'s contract, registry lists (`RENDERER_OPTIONS`/`RENDERERS`) kept in sync.
- The renderer bake-off is now **seven** methods (was six): `tree`, `sugiyama`, `cluster`,
  `interactive`, `nodegraph`, `honeycomb`, `canvas`.

**Why:** evaluate the original SPA layout algorithm head-to-head against the deterministic
`layered.ts`-driven layouts (`tree`/`sugiyama`) that superseded it when the SPA was retired
(§12.4), without reviving any SPA runtime code — purely additive to the existing bake-off harness.
Additive and reversible (flip `enabled: false` in the manifest + drop the two registry entries to
park it); touches no IR/planner/adapter/API contract — entirely inside the Grafana panel plugin +
bake-off harness.

**Affected docs:**

- `observability/grafana/plugins/axellio-axrange-control-panel/src/topology/layouts/canvas.test.ts` (new)

## 2026-07-07 — Code-review-driven fix batch across `axrange/` (8 areas, 505 → 570 tests)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:** a whole-`axrange/` code review (7 scoped reviewers) surfaced correctness bugs and
dedup opportunities; fixed across 8 areas over several waves, verified by an adversarial review pass.
No new op types or validator rules — these are fixes, not features (still **37 op types, 98 catalog
rules**). Full suite **505 → 570 passing** (both deterministic and randomized order).

- **Planner / state (the headline fix):**
  - The `ops` table PRIMARY KEY changed from `op_id` alone to composite **`(deploy_id, op_id)`**;
    `op_attempts` gained a `deploy_id` column; `StateStore` methods `get_op` / `set_op_running` /
    `complete_op` / `fail_op` / `halt_op` / `start_attempt` / `attempts_for_op` are now deploy-scoped.
    A new `SCHEMA_VERSION=2` + `PRAGMA user_version` guard fails loud (`StateStoreSchemaError`) on an
    old v1 DB; fresh-DB init is atomic (DDL + pragma in one script transaction) so a concurrent opener
    never sees a half-created schema.
  - **This RESOLVES the long-standing LES-007 `--fresh` caveat** ("`--fresh` does not re-run ops
    marked `completed` under a prior deploy") AND its previously-undocumented other half: a `destroy`
    after `--fresh` used to sweep **nothing** yet report DESTROYED (a live-resource leak). Two deploys
    of one plan now hold independent op rows. **Operator-facing migration note:** an existing
    `state.db` from before this change must be re-initialized (delete/move the file) — the guard fails
    loud with a clear message rather than misbehaving silently.
  - **Heartbeat split-brain abort:** `Heartbeat.lost` (a `threading.Event`) is now set on
    **lease-renewal failure alone** (the race-free single-writer signal), and the executor polls it
    between ops and aborts; wired at the sole `Executor` construction site (`run_deploy_executor`). An
    earlier per-resource-key holder-diff detection was dropped as racy — it false-positived on a
    legitimate concurrent different-scenario deploy taking a shared correctness key.
  - `LockResolutionError` now fails the op cleanly (terminal `failed` + subtree halt) instead of
    crashing the executor with the op stuck `running`.
  - Efficiency/dedup: the resume short-circuit uses an in-memory `_completed_ids` set (no per-op DB
    round-trip); `dag.topological_order()` uses a heapq (byte-identical order); teardown reuses
    `adapter_proxmox.naming.is_axr_*` predicates and releases resource locks only on a clean sweep.

- **Validator:** `CAP-020` now checks **every** app→host binding (was last-wins, false-passed an
  unsupported install action on a multi-host app); `NET-080` unions `net:` + `nets:` when counting a
  router's attachments (was rejecting a valid single-LAN `kind: router` that used `net:` instead of
  `nets:`); `STR-130` flags a double-action-key install (e.g. `{pkg: nginx, script: setup.sh}`);
  `REF-120` skips when the manifest registry is empty (fixes a false-fail through
  `validate_structural`'s Pass 1+2 entry point, which intentionally runs with an empty
  `ManifestRegistry()`); shared `node_kind()` helper factored out.

- **`config_exec`:** the sidecar-Suricata renderer brought to parity with `deploy-sensor` via a shared
  `_suricata_start_block` (stale-pidfile clear + fail-loud `pgrep` start-check — was a silent-dead
  daemon on redeploy); zeek pkill/pgrep now key off an argv-present token
  (`-U /run/zeek-<id>.status`); a new `_ps_dq_escape` handles the AD `-Filter` double-quoted
  PowerShell context (backtick/`$`/`"` escaping AND apostrophes like `WS01$`); choco
  package/version validation + quoting; a truncation-collision dedupe suffix for Sam/local usernames;
  a `verify-capture` cross-node guard.

- **`ir`/`manifest`/`ops`/`sensors`:** the `fabric:` include-merge is fixed (was dropping
  `fabric_asn`/`tenant` from a fragment and emitting a bogus `INC-010`); `_collect_ids` is now scoped
  to the known ID-bearing sections (IR_SCHEMA §1.4) with per-fragment attribution (free-form `id:`
  keys, e.g. `inline.steps[].id`, no longer false-trip `INC-010`); a shared
  `ir/yaml_util.load_yaml_raw`; `current_version()` warns when no version is `current`;
  `ops/registry.py` docstring counts corrected (37/31/6/98) and `consumed_by` drift reconciled;
  `InstallSupport.supports()` dead-clause simplified; the `ir` YAML loader switched `rt` → `safe`
  (nothing consumed ruamel's round-trip line/comment metadata; safe mode is also faster).

- **`adapter_proxmox`:** disk-resize now only swallows shrink/same-size wording (was hiding real
  storage-exhaustion errors); the boot-disk key is resolved from live config (fallback prefers
  `scsi0`); `clone-vm` name-guards VMID adoption (`vmid-collision-foreign`); new reusable
  `zone_exists` / `bridge_exists` / `vnet_exists` / `subnet_exists` / `vm_status` + `is_axr_*`
  predicates + `mac_for_nic` + `apply_vyos_sets` (VyOS envelope dedup, LES-005 order preserved);
  `_build_ipconfig_body` dedup; deterministic per-NIC MAC on attach; the dormant `input_data` param
  removed from `GuestAgent.exec`/`shell_exec`.

- **`imagebuild`:** a first-build smoke-test failure now clears `template_vmid` (new
  `binding_io.clear_template_vmid`) instead of leaving a broken pointer at a template that never
  sealed; a non-zero sysprep exit fails the seal; cloud-init `errors[]` is checked; reuses
  `adapter_proxmox.GuestAgent` (LES-025); a local-storage guard; the smoke-scenario id is salted with
  the build VMID.

- **`api`/`cli`:** adapted to the deploy-scoped `StateStore` API; `--fresh` correctness is now
  root-fixed by the composite PK (regression-tested); `destroy` wraps teardown in a `Heartbeat` (was
  holding a fixed-TTL lease with no renewal); shared engine helpers `build_plan_and_ctx` /
  `run_deploy_executor` / `release_deploy_resources` (CLI+API dedup, LES-025); constant-time API-token
  compare; a lazy `create_app` export (PEP 562) so the CLI imports without FastAPI; the vmid match
  uses a canonical `op_name == "clone-vm"` comparison; a shared `default_db_path()`.

**Why:** a scheduled whole-project code review pass (7 scoped reviewers across the `axrange/`
submodules) to catch correctness bugs and duplication accumulated across many incremental slices, and
to close out the long-standing `--fresh` correctness gap flagged in LES-007/CHANGELOG 2026-06-04
before it caused a live-resource leak in the field.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/PLANNER.md` — §7.2 (composite `(deploy_id, op_id)` PK, schema-version guard), §6.2/§8.4
  (heartbeat lease-loss abort).
- `docs/VALIDATOR.md` — notes on the `CAP-020`/`NET-080`/`STR-130`/`REF-120` corrections (rule count
  unchanged, still 98).
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — `LES-007` annotated RESOLVED; `LES-030`–`LES-036` added.
- `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` — new entries for KNOWN-DEFERRED items found but intentionally not fixed
  this pass (Pass-4 CLU rules unwired, SSH pooling, imagebuild sysprep post-check, STR-120/CAP-030
  lenient-skip, multi-node placement hardcoding).
- `CLAUDE.md` — pytest count 533 → 570 (three lines); `--fresh` caveat marked RESOLVED (Iteration-0
  paragraph + Immediate Next Actions item 3).

**Affected code:** `axrange/planner/{state,executor,locks,dag,teardown}.py`,
`axrange/validator/checks/{capability,network,structural,references,fabric}.py`,
`axrange/config_exec/{render,backend}.py`, `axrange/ir/parser.py`, `axrange/ir/yaml_util.py` (new),
`axrange/manifest/loader.py`, `axrange/ops/registry.py`, `axrange/sensors/registry.py`,
`axrange/adapter_proxmox/{rest,ssh,naming,guest_agent}.py`,
`axrange/imagebuild/{builder,binding_io,conformance,proxmox_build}.py`,
`axrange/api/{service,settings,app,__init__}.py`, `axrange/cli/main.py`.

**Supersedes:** the CHANGELOG 2026-06-04 `--fresh` finding and the LES-007 workaround ("clear the
state DB"), which are now root-fixed rather than worked around.

**Verification:** full suite 570 passing (deterministic and randomized order); `doc-stats.py --check`
clean after the CLAUDE.md count fix. **NOT yet committed / NOT live-verified** — pending user go-ahead
before a live deploy on `dev45` exercises the schema-v2 state.db against real cluster state (see
`docs/SESSION_STATE.md`).

## 2026-07-07 — Live-verify follow-up: two fixes found on `dev45`, composite-PK fix confirmed end-to-end (570 → 576 tests)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:** live-verified the composite-PK `(deploy_id, op_id)` fix from the same-day code-review
batch (entry above) end-to-end on `dev45`, and fixed two further bugs the live-verify surfaced. Full
suite **570 → 576 passing**. No new op types or validator rules (still 37 op types, 98 catalog rules).

- **Fix A — `REF-120` mode-gating (validator), correcting the same-day fix above.** The code-review
  batch's `REF-120` fix ("skip when the manifest registry is empty") was too broad: it made
  `axrange validate` PASS over a genuinely-empty/misconfigured image registry (e.g. a wrong `--images`
  path) instead of failing loudly, so a deploy then failed cryptically at `clone-vm: template_vmid
  missing`. Corrected: `check_ref_120` now skips only when `ctx.skip_unknown_image_lifecycle` is
  `True`, a flag `validate_structural` sets (canvas/shape-only mode, Pass 1+2 only, which
  intentionally runs against an empty `ManifestRegistry()`); `validate_full` (the deploy path) leaves
  the flag `False`, so an empty/misconfigured registry now fails `REF-120` loudly at validate time, as
  it should. Files: `axrange/validator/engine.py` (`_build_context` gained the kwarg;
  `validate_structural` sets it `True`), `axrange/validator/checks/references.py` (skip gated on the
  flag, not on registry emptiness).
- **Fix B — disk-size unit normalization (adapter).** The IR uses binary units for `resources.disk`
  (`10Gi`), but PVE's `/resize` endpoint's `size` param wants decimal-suffix form (`10G`) and 400s on
  `10Gi` (`{"errors":{"size":"value does not match the regex pattern"}}`). This had been silently
  swallowed by the pre-batch blanket `except AdapterError: pass`, so disk resize **never actually
  worked** for any `Gi`-unit scenario — the same-day batch's disk-resize fix (only swallow
  shrink/same-size wording, per the entry above) stopped masking the error and exposed it live.
  Corrected: new `_normalize_pve_disk_size()` in `axrange/adapter_proxmox/rest.py` strips the binary
  `i` (`10Gi`→`10G`, `512Mi`→`512M`, preserves a leading `+`, raises on an un-coercible value), wired
  into `op_configure_vm_hardware`. Sibling `resources.ram` was already normalized via `_parse_mem_mb`;
  disk had no equivalent until now.

**Why:** the code-review batch's headline fix (composite-PK `--fresh`/destroy correctness) needed a
real cluster to prove out the schema-v2 migration and the full deploy→`--fresh`→destroy lifecycle
against live Proxmox state, not just unit tests. Live-verify is exactly the stage that catches a
too-broad validator skip and a silently-swallowed adapter error that unit tests, run against mocks,
had no way to surface.

**Live-verify result (dev45):** a minimal non-tapped 2-host scenario. Deploy #1 → COMPLETED (21 ops).
Deploy #2 `--fresh` → a new `deploy_id` with 42 independent op rows (not aliased to Deploy #1's) + 18
attempts (genuinely re-ran every op, not the old silent no-op) → COMPLETED. `axrange destroy` of the
`--fresh` deploy → 7 resources removed, cluster-confirmed VMs/vnet/bridge actually gone (not the old
false-DESTROYED leak). Idempotent re-destroy clean. This confirms the composite-PK fix end-to-end,
closing out the "NOT yet live-verified" caveat on the entry above. Fixes A and B were both discovered
and fixed during this same live-verify run.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/VALIDATOR.md` — `REF-120` note revised: skip is gated on `skip_unknown_image_lifecycle`
  (caller-declared mode), not on registry emptiness.
- `docs/LESSONS.md` — `LES-040` and `LES-041` added.
- `docs/SESSION_STATE.md` — handoff updated: batch + both live-verify fixes complete, live-verified on
  `dev45` 2026-07-07, full suite 576 green, ready to commit (still uncommitted).
- `CLAUDE.md` — pytest count 570 → 576 (three lines).

**Affected code:** `axrange/validator/engine.py`, `axrange/validator/checks/references.py`,
`axrange/adapter_proxmox/rest.py`.

**Supersedes:** the "NOT yet committed / NOT live-verified" caveat on the same-day code-review-batch
entry above (composite-PK fix is now live-verified); the same-day batch's own `REF-120` fix (registry
emptiness → mode-flag gating).

**Verification:** full suite 576 passing; `doc-stats.py --check` clean; live end-to-end deploy →
`--fresh` → destroy cycle on `dev45` as described above. **Still NOT committed** — pending user
go-ahead.

## 2026-07-07 — UI: add the SPA canvas layout as a 7th topology bake-off renderer; retire the `ui/` working-tree copy

**Authority:** user

**What changed:**
- **Design-only decision** (implementation delegated to the orchestrator's subagents — no code/manifest/registry edits in this session). Grafana is the project's front-end UI (`observability/` — Grafana + Loki + Prometheus + the `axellio-axrange-control-panel` plugin). The plugin's topology view is a **pluggable renderer bake-off** (`registry.ts` `RENDERER_OPTIONS`/`RENDERERS` + `topology-eval.manifest.json` + `gen-topology-dashboards.py`) with six methods: `tree`, `sugiyama`, `cluster`, `interactive`, `nodegraph`, `honeycomb`.
- **Add a 7th renderer option: `canvas` — `SPA Canvas Layout (original ui/ port)`** (key/label user-confirmed 2026-07-07; registry `value` + manifest `key` + dashboard tag use these verbatim). When the `ui/` SPA was retired (2026-06-25) its node components were ported but its layout algorithm (`ui/src/canvas/layout.ts`) was not — it was superseded by the deterministic `layouts/layered.ts` driving `tree`/`sugiyama`. This brings that original layout back as a first-class, first-class-evaluatable renderer so it can be compared head-to-head against the layouts that replaced it. It's a graph-family renderer (a pure `(topology) => positions` layout fn reusing `TopologyFlow`/`useFlowGraph` + the already-ported `EntityNode`/`NetworkNode`), so no standalone component.
- **The untracked `ui/` working-tree dir is unwanted code and is to be removed** (build artifacts only). The layout *source* seed is preserved at the annotated tag `retired/ui-viewer-spa` (`ui/src/canvas/layout.ts`) — the port origin.
- Spelled out the exact subagent touchpoints (port `layout.ts`→`layouts/canvas.ts`; add to `RENDERER_OPTIONS`+`RENDERERS`; append the manifest entry; re-run the dashboard generator; build/restart; delete `ui/`). `control-console.json` default stays `tree` — additive only.

**Why:**
- The user designated Grafana as the front-end and wants the retired SPA's topology layout preserved as one of the selectable rendering methods rather than lost with the SPA. Adding it to the existing bake-off is purely additive, reuses the established renderer contract, and is reversible (flip `enabled: false` + drop the registry entry).

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_ui.md` — new §12.5.1 "Add the SPA canvas layout as a 7th bake-off renderer".

**Links:** `observability/grafana/topology-eval.manifest.json`, `observability/grafana/plugins/axellio-axrange-control-panel/src/topology/registry.ts`, tag `retired/ui-viewer-spa`.

## 2026-07-07 — Workflow overhaul: chat-bridge optional, risk-based design gate, background subagent pipeline, LESSONS store, verification tiers

**Authority:** user

**What changed:**
- **Chat-bridge mode is now optional / user-invoked — not the home of design.** `docs/WORKFLOW.md` restructured into three optional modes over a shared spine — *Solo* (default), *Chat-bridge*, *Pipeline* — with a new "Modes" preamble and a "Pipeline mode" section. `CLAUDE.md` "Collaboration With Claude (Chat)" → **"Collaboration & Design Authority"**: any surface can make design decisions; Claude Code advances design by default and records it; `implementation (Claude Code)` is a first-class origin for *design* decisions.
- **The surface/category design gate is replaced by a generic risk trigger.** `CLAUDE.md` "Design Document Maintenance": pause-and-confirm **only** when a change reverses a ratified decision, changes a version-locked contract schema, or shifts scope/direction — otherwise proceed and record. The Git-Policy `design:` commit note was reworded to match.
- **Stale gates swept:** the dead "Questions for the User Before Starting" (4 sink-bring-up questions) → a generic "Starting Work" note; three dangling "What NOT to Do" references fixed (`OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` Q13, `SLICE_CONFIG.md` ×2).
- **Background subagent pipeline** (`.claude/`, git-tracked): five agents (`code-writer` / `tester` / `live-verifier` / `code-reviewer` / `doc-writer`), a `/dev <task>` command, and `settings.json` — a permission allowlist (reads/tests/preflight auto-run; `deploy`/`destroy`/`image build`/`commit`/`push`/manual-`qm` gated; inbound git denied) plus four hooks (SessionStart lessons-index, PreToolUse lesson-inject, PostToolUse py_compile, Stop drift-check). Live-verify is the human-gated stage; the orchestrator relays `DECISION NEEDED:` items to the user and resumes workers with the answer. `code-review` covers correctness **+ optimization/efficiency/duplication from whole-project context**.
- **`docs/LESSONS.md` (`LES-NNN`) institutional-memory store** — 28 seed lessons mined from the CHANGELOG (bug-classes, silent-success traps, platform/tool gotchas, ordering invariants, dead-ends, reversal hazards, reuse rules, patterns). Fed forward two ways ("Both"): a `PreToolUse` hook auto-injects the entries whose `Scope:` matches each edited file (`scripts/inject_lessons.py`), and agents grep it. `code-reviewer` proposes new lessons; `doc-writer` records them. `doc-writer` captures **what was learned**, not just what was built.
- **Friction-killers:** `scripts/doc-stats.py` (compute / `--check` volatile counters from source — caught the 429/483→**495** test-count drift, now fixed); `scripts/verify_tier.sh` (the required, cluster-free, non-mutating middle gate: fast tests + validate + plan + `sdn_preflight` + `verify_resource_locks`); a **fast/slow pytest split** (`slow`/`live` markers; the inner loop `pytest -m "not slow"` is **~14s** vs the full **~55s / 495 tests** — one 40s API retry-backoff test dominated); the `doc-writer` refreshes `SESSION_STATE.md` at wrap.

**Why:**
- Design had matured into code (the 2026-06-11 entry already made routing per-thread), but the docs still read chat-first and gated Claude Code from advancing design — friction the user kept overriding by hand. Keying the discipline on *risk/reversibility* and *recording* rather than *surface/category* removes the false gate while keeping the safety net (Authority markers + `Supersedes:` + the user's diff review).
- The user wants implementation to run in the background (code → test → review → docs → gated live-verify) while they focus on design, interrupting only when a decision is needed, with hard-won knowledge captured once and fed forward so the same bug/duplicate is not repeated elsewhere.

**Affected docs:**
- `CLAUDE.md` — "Collaboration & Design Authority" (rewrite), "Design Document Maintenance" (risk-gate), Git Policy note, "Starting Work" (replaces the sink questions), stale counters fixed.
- `docs/WORKFLOW.md` — three-optional-modes restructure + Pipeline mode.
- `docs/LESSONS.md` (new), `docs/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` + `docs/SLICE_CONFIG.md` (dangling-ref fixes).

**Affected code:**
- `.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/agents/*.md` (5), `.claude/commands/dev.md`, `scripts/{doc-stats.py, inject_lessons.py, verify_tier.sh, hooks.py}`, `pyproject.toml` (pytest markers), `tests/{test_api, test_planner_locks, test_dynamic_tap}.py` (`@pytest.mark.slow`), `.gitignore`.

**Supersedes:** the chat-first framing of `docs/WORKFLOW.md` and the surface/category design gate in `CLAUDE.md` "Design Document Maintenance"; completes the routing relaxation begun in the 2026-06-11 entry ("Project-wide scope-boundary gates removed").

**Verification:** fast subset `pytest -m "not slow"` → 492 passed in ~14s; full suite → 495 passed; `doc-stats.py --check` clean after the fix; `inject_lessons.py` scopes correctly (ssh.py → LES-005/011/025); hook dispatcher emits well-formed context. Branch `wip/workflow-speedup`. Live pipeline (`/dev`) smoke-run pending.

## 2026-07-01 — GAP-004 RESOLVED: SDN `create-*` idempotency guard (already-exists vs already-defined)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed.** Fixed a deploy-time robustness gap surfaced while redeploying the fabric rig on dev45:
`create-subnet` hard-failed on a redeploy with PVE's `sdn subnet object ID '…' already defined`, which
**halted the whole SDN apply** (→ vnets never materialized → VyOS routers got no DHCP lease →
`configure-router` hung). Root cause: the four SDN `create-*` ops tolerate an "already present" collision
as success (Model C), but the guard matched **only** the string `"already exists"` — PVE 9.2 says
`"already defined"` for **subnets** — and `create-subnet` (unlike its siblings) had **no** check-then-act
pre-check, so it relied entirely on the wrong-string backstop.

- **Fix:** a shared `_is_already_present()` guard (broadened wording: already exists / defined /
  registered) applied to all four `op_create_*` backstops, **plus** a `_subnet_exists()` GET pre-check on
  `create-subnet` so it check-then-acts like `create-zone`/`create-vnet`/`create-ovs-bridge` and no longer
  reaches the POST in the normal resume case. +3 regression tests; suite **493**.
- **Impact:** restores `deploy` resume-safety for any scenario with a `sensors:` block; a narrow
  sensor-path miss no longer cascades to wedge the fabric path. Full trail: `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-004.

---

## 2026-07-01 — GAP-003 RESOLVED: cross-leaf symmetric-IRB routes (duplicate L3VNI Router-MAC fix)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed.** Root-caused and **fixed** GAP-003 — cross-leaf symmetric-IRB routing now works
end-to-end on dev45 (VyOS 1.5 / FRR 10.6.1): **`ha`→`hb` cross-leaf ping 4/4, 0% loss, ttl 62**, routed
through the L3VNI. This closes the fabric slice's headline symmetric-IRB use case.

- **Root cause (found via `debug bgp updates` on the receiving leaf):** the leaf *received* each peer's
  Type-5 but dropped it on input — `rcvd UPDATE about RD …:10999 [5]:…[10.1.20.0] … -- DENIED due to:
  self mac`. **All linked-clone leaves auto-derived the *same* `br<l3vni>` bridge MAC**
  (`d6:2b:80:fd:94:79`), which FRR uses as the L3VNI **Router-MAC** and stamps into every Type-5 it
  originates. FRR's RMAC loop check then DENIES any inbound Type-5 whose RMAC == the local RMAC — so each
  leaf rejected all peers' Type-5 as its own. Type-2/3 have no RMAC-self check → unaffected (which is why
  the failure looked Type-5-specific + symmetric, and misled two earlier diagnoses).
- **Fix:** `fabric_render._l3vni_rmac` emits a **unique per-leaf MAC** on `br<l3vni>`, keyed off the
  loopback (`02:<vni_hi>:<vni_lo>:<loopback last 3 octets>`, LAA unicast; e.g. `10.255.0.3` →
  `02:2a:f7:ff:00:03`). Distinct from the L2VNI `_anycast_mac` (intentionally shared). +2 tests
  (`test_irb_symmetric_gateway` asserts the RMAC line; `test_l3vni_rmac_unique_per_leaf`). Suite **490**.
- **Live-verify (dev45):** applying the exact per-leaf MACs the renderer now emits made `leaf-1` install
  `10.1.20.0/24 via 10.255.0.4 br10999 onlink` (reverse symmetric), and `ha`→`hb` ping 4/4. The baked
  `_l3vni_rmac` reproduces those distinct MACs for the rig (offline-verified: leaf-1 `02:2a:f7:ff:00:03`,
  leaf-2 `02:2a:f7:ff:00:04`) — closed loop: distinct RMAC ⇒ Type-5 accepted ⇒ cross-leaf route.
- **The trail (two prior misdiagnoses, now corrected):** the RT-binding fix (`route-target both`,
  2026-06-30) was correct and necessary but NOT the blocker; the "pure-RR spines don't reflect Type-5"
  re-scope (2026-07-01, below) was also wrong — the RR *did* reflect it; the drop was on the receiving
  leaf's RMAC-self check. `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` GAP-003 → **Resolved**; `docs/FABRIC_VERIFICATION.md` §9
  + `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_fabric.md` §3.5 updated; renderer comments corrected.

**Net:** the fabric slice is now **live-verified end-to-end incl. cross-leaf symmetric IRB** on dev45.
Remaining fabric follow-on: tenant-VRF internet **egress** (Finding C). Full FRR trace + trail in
GAP-003.

## 2026-07-01 — [SUPERSEDED by the entry above] GAP-003 clean-deploy live-verify: RT-binding was a MISDIAGNOSIS; real blocker = Type-5 reflection

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed.** Redeployed the `fabric-verify` rig on dev45 (PVE 9.2, VyOS 1.5 / **FRR 10.6.1**) with
the corrected `route-target both` to chase GAP-003 (cross-leaf symmetric-IRB routing). The clean deploy
**overturned the original diagnosis** and isolated the real blocker:

- **The L3VNI route-target binds correctly — GAP-003's original "RT doesn't bind on VyOS 1.5" was
  WRONG.** On a clean atomic commit, `show bgp l2vpn evpn vni <l3vni>` shows `Import`+`Export Route
  Target = <asn>:<l3vni>` on **both** leaves, and each leaf **originates its Type-5 with the correct RT +
  Router-MAC**. The earlier "operational RT empty" reading was an artifact of (a) a `grep` that dropped
  the *indented* RT value line and (b) diagnosing a **partially-applied** config left by the invalid
  `route-target vpn both` line. **The committed `route-target both` renderer fix is validated + effective.**
- **The real blocker: the pure route-reflector spines don't reflect EVPN Type-5 (L3VNI) routes to the
  client leaves on FRR 10.6.1** — Type-2/3 (L2VNI) reflect fine; the RR holds each Type-5 as `best` but
  `Advertised to peers:` stays empty, so the peer leaf installs no cross-leaf route and `ha`→`hb` returns
  `Destination Net Unreachable` (ha→its-own-gateway works). Ruled out live: RT binding, update-group
  split, convergence ordering (coordinated FRR restart), spine `advertise-all-vni`, hard clears. The only
  difference between the working and failing routes is the EVPN type → an FRR-10.6.1 RR-without-L3VNI
  behaviour (matches FRR #4044 / #16209 / #20271).
- **GAP-003 re-scoped** (`docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md`): RT-binding root cause corrected → **resolved**; residual
  is the Type-5 reflection issue, with four candidate fixes recorded (spines participate in the tenant
  VRF/L3VNI; separate VTEP vs peering loopbacks; full-mesh leaves; FRR bump) — a **design call**, not yet
  chosen. Renderer comments + `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_fabric.md` §3.5 + `docs/FABRIC_VERIFICATION.md` §9 updated.

**Net:** the fabric control plane, the `route-target both` fix, and intra-leaf IRB (host→anycast-gateway)
are all **proven on hardware**; cross-leaf symmetric-IRB routing remains open behind an FRR/topology issue
(GAP-003, re-scoped) that needs a design decision. Teardown clean (30 resources). No code change this pass
beyond doc/comment corrections (the `route-target both` fix was already committed 2026-06-30).
